# GameNight OS Backlog

This file is the SOURCE OF TRUTH for project scope. The Excalidraw project map is a
rendering of it, nothing more: every zone on the map maps to one heading below, so the
map can always be redrawn from this file alone (see MAP PROTOCOL). If the two ever
disagree, this file wins.

Section order below is deliberate and matches the map's zone order. Do not rename or
reorder headings without updating MAP PROTOCOL in the same commit.

## MAP STATUS (update every session, no exceptions)
Read this FIRST, before any other work. The redraw rule is driven by this counter, not by
anyone's memory of how many sessions have happened.

    Last map redraw:                    2026-08-23 (the night flow session, before its own work)
    Shipped sessions since that redraw: 0
    Redraw due at:                      3

    THE RULE, applied to the two numbers above and to nothing else:

      counter >= 3   reconcile this file and redraw the map BEFORE this session's own
                     work, then set the date to today and the counter back to 0.
      counter <  3   do the requested work, and increment the counter by 1 in the same
                     commit as any session that ships.

    The test is what the counter READS when a session starts, never what it will read
    when that session ends.

## MAP PROTOCOL
How and when the Excalidraw project map gets redrawn. Written so any session reproduces
the same map from this file, with or without the previous drawing's checkpoint.

**When:** when a session STARTS and READS the counter at 3 or more. That is the whole
test, and it is about the number the session finds, never about the number it will leave
behind: a session that reads 2 is below the threshold, does its work, and increments to 3,
and the NEXT one owes the redraw. Two sessions have now lost time to that ambiguity, so it
is worth stating twice.
A "shipped session" is one that
delivers a feature, pack, or fix set (doc-only passes do not count). Reconcile this file
FIRST, then draw the map from the reconciled file, then reset MAP STATUS. Never draw from
memory of a previous map.

**Zone-to-heading mapping (fixed):**
| Zone | Title on map | Source heading |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | SHIPPED — FOUNDATION | ## SHIPPED — FOUNDATION |
| 2 | SHIPPED — GAME PACKS | ## SHIPPED — GAME PACKS |
| 3 | NEXT UP (queued) | ## NEXT UP (queued) |
| 4 | FEATURES TO ADD | ## FEATURES TO ADD |
| 5 | BUG FIXES | ## BUGS |
| 6 | IDEAS — NOT SOLIDIFIED | ## IDEAS (not solidified) |

Every ZONE maps to a heading; not every heading is a zone. DEFERRED, STANDING RULES,
ADDING A PACK, CASINO GROUP, DECISION LOG and OPEN DECISIONS are reference sections that
are deliberately not drawn — they are reading material for a session, not scope. Adding
another reference section does not change the map; adding or renaming one of the SIX
above does, and must update this table in the same commit.

**Layout (fixed, so redraws are stable):** 3 columns x 2 rows. Columns at x=40, 560, 1080,
each 480 wide. Row 1 at y=95 (height 2650), row 2 at y=2770 (height 1200). Zone header text
15px below zone top, fontSize 22. Items 440 wide, 40 tall, 46px step, first item 50px below
zone top; give a taller box (52-70) to any item whose label wraps past one line. Row 1 grew
from 540 to 730 on 2026-07-27 (SHIPPED FOUNDATION reached 14 items and overflowed its
zone), from 730 to 800 on 2026-07-28, when the completed pre-pack cleanup item grew
to a three-line box and left only 10px of slack, from 800 to 860 on the third 07-28
pass, when the two newly rendered shipped sessions needed exactly the 92px of slack zone 1
had left, from 860 to 920 on 2026-07-29, when zone 1's remaining 42px was 4px short of
the 46px one more item costs, from 920 to 990 on 2026-07-30, when zone 2 took three
multi-line casino items in one pass, from 990 to 1120 on 2026-08-02, when zone 2 took
declarative modifiers and Casino Run, from 1120 to 1400 on 2026-08-09, when zone 2 took
the Board Game pack, the team primitive and the title-night layer in one pass, from 1400 to
1600 on 2026-08-10 when zone 2 reached 22 items, from 1600 to 1950 on 2026-08-17 in two
steps within one pass: 1800 took zone 2's two new items, and reading the file back showed
that left only 82px, which does not fit the Poker item the SAME session was about to ship,
so it went to 1950, from 1950 to 2150 on 2026-08-19, when zone 2 measured 138px of slack
against a NEXT UP whose top three are all packs: 138 fits one of those three and not three,
and from 2150 to 2400 on the SECOND 2026-08-19 pass, when zone 2 landed on 114px against the
same unchanged queue and the same arithmetic gave the same answer, and from 2400 to 2650 on
2026-08-23, when zone 2 landed on 158px against that same unchanged queue — the raise the
08-22 pass declined at 270px and wrote down as owed by the next one.
Each time it was raised pre-emptively rather than
waiting for the next entry to overflow it, since the fix is mechanical and the trap would
have landed on whoever added the next line.
ROW 2 GREW FOR THE FIRST TIME on 2026-08-02, 530 to 660: it had been a fixed height through
six passes because only row 1 was filling up, and zone 5 overflowed it by 20px once the
open bugs were all rendered. It grew again the same day, 660 to 720, on the stage 2 pass,
when a fifth Watch trap left zone 5 with 18px, and again on 2026-08-03, 720 to 840, when
zone 5 went genuinely OVER (764px of content in a 720px zone) rather than merely tight. Both times every row-2 zone was raised
together for the same reason row 1's are.
When a zone outgrows its height again, raise EVERY zone in its row together (there are
THREE per row, not two: the stage 2 pass raised FEATURES and BUG FIXES and left IDEAS
behind at the old height, which the generated-file check below caught immediately), and if
it is row 1, push row 2 down by the same amount so the columns stay aligned.
ROW 2 GREW AGAIN on 2026-08-17, 840 to 1020: zone 5 went to 15 items (a new OPEN for Mario
Kart's TV, two entries flipping to FIXED, and three new Watch traps out of the Tabletop
repair) and needed 920px of its 840. Row 2 also MOVED, 1720 to 2070, because row 1 grew and
the columns stay aligned. AND AGAIN on 2026-08-19, 1020 to 1200: zone 5 reached 17 and was
the first zone to be measured genuinely OVER since 2026-08-03, by 10px, which is the smallest
overflow this file has recorded and would have been invisible to anyone sizing by eye. Row 2
moved 2070 to 2270 with row 1.
A ZONE THAT SHRINKS DOES NOT SHRINK ITS ROW, and 2026-08-04 is the first pass where that
came up: zone 5 dropped four aged-out FIXED items and needs 572px of its 840. The height
stays, because the queue below it (a pack into zone 2, four items into zone 3) will refill
it, and a row that grows and shrinks with every pass moves every zone under it for no
reader's benefit. Raise a row when a zone is over; leave it when a zone is under.

**Colors:** zones 1-2 green (#d3f9d8 zone / #b2f2bb items / #15803d header). Zone 3 yellow
(#fff3bf / #ffd8a8 / #b45309); the first three items get strokeWidth 2 and a leading number,
they are the committed next sessions. Zone 4 blue (#dbe4ff / #a5d8ff / #2563eb). Zone 5 red
(#ffc9c9 zone / #b91c1c header): OPEN bugs #ffc9c9, the chosen FIX #ffd8a8, "Watch" traps
#fff3bf, FIXED items #b2f2bb. Zone 6 purple (#e5dbff / #d0bfff / #6d28d9). Newly shipped
items in zones 1-2 get #c3fae8 + "(NEW)" until the next redraw.

**Cameras:** open 600x450 on the title, then 800x600 per zone in reading order (1-6), close
on a panorama covering the full canvas (1600x4020 at the current zone heights; the canvas
itself measures 1560x3970, read off the generated file rather than assumed. This line read
2410 for four passes after the heights it describes had moved, which is why a redraw checks
it against the file). Title fontSize 30 at y=15, subtitle 18 at y=56 with the month/year.

**Reconcile step (do this before drawing):** move finished items from NEXT UP into the right
SHIPPED section with a one-line summary and date; renumber the top three of NEXT UP; move
fixed bugs to FIXED in BUGS and drop anything stale; add anything new discussed since the
last pass; then draw.

**Committed file copy (always, added 2026-07-19):** the live MCP canvas does not render in
Claude Code sessions (unlike regular Claude chats), so the map must ALSO always exist as
`project-map.excalidraw` at the repo root — open it at excalidraw.com. It is generated, not
hand-drawn: `scripts/generate-project-map.mjs` holds the zone/item data and emits the file
with this protocol's layout and colors. On every redraw: update the item data in that script
to match the reconciled headings, run `node scripts/generate-project-map.mjs`, and commit the
regenerated file in the same commit as the reconcile. If the MCP canvas is unavailable in a
session, regenerating the committed file alone still counts as the redraw.

## SHIPPED — FOUNDATION
- [x] HOME SAYS WHY A CREW IS WORTH HAVING (2026-08-22; WRITTEN UP 2026-08-23 BY THE RECONCILE THAT FOUND IT MISSING). "Recommended for accurate tracking per person.", five lines in `apps/web/src/pages/Home.tsx`. Crews was the one heading on Home with no hint line under it while Quick play directly below has had exactly that pattern since it shipped, so this is the counterpart to that line rather than a new kind of thing: quick play runs through a hidden personal crew, which is why its results are not attributed to the people who actually played, and a real crew is what gives each person their own record.
  **IT IS HERE BECAUSE THE RECONCILE FOUND IT, AND THAT IS THE FIRST DRIFT ANY PASS HAS FOUND.** The 2026-08-22 pass recorded "THE RECONCILE FOUND NO DRIFT" and was right on the day it was written. This commit and the casino default-bank one in SHIPPED — GAME PACKS both landed AFTER that session's own counter-to-3 BACKLOG commit, and neither wrote itself up, so two shipped changes existed in the repo and nowhere in this file. **THE LESSON IS ABOUT WHERE THE BACKLOG COMMIT SITS IN A SESSION**, not about either change: a session that keeps shipping after its BACKLOG commit still owes an entry, and the only thing that catches it is the next reconcile walking the log rather than trusting the file. Walk `git log` against this file at every redraw, not just the headings against the repo.
- [x] PARTNER STATS, AND A REAL STATS BLOCK ON HOME (2026-08-22). Two read-only surfaces over data the ledger already held, and the session that shipped them ALSO SHIPPED TWO THINGS WRONG AND CORRECTED BOTH THE SAME DAY, which is most of what is worth reading here.
  **WHO YOU WIN WITH.** `side` has been in the ledger since 2026-08-02 and `meetingOutcome` has classified a shared side as `together` for just as long, but nothing anywhere said who you win most WITH: the team primitive changed what was RECORDED without changing anything anyone SAW. `partnersFor` is ONE SQL self-join (`match_participants` to itself on `(match_id, side)` with `user_id <> user_id`), beside `finishAggDeep` and never inside `finishAgg`, wired into the three profile-side endpoints and deliberately not into the crew leaderboard. It does NOT repeat `countLastPlace`'s unbounded IN list, which is a Watch trap in BUGS; Postgres receives five bound parameters. `feedAgg`, `finishAgg`, `ResultRow` and `resultCols` are byte-identical, and a characterization test written BEFORE the change pins `finishAgg` byte-for-byte, which is what makes "the leaderboard did not move" evidence rather than a claim.
  **THE BUG: IT COUNTED CO-OP RUNS AND DEALT FACTIONS AS PARTNERSHIPS.** `side` means three different things (see DECISION LOG) and only one of them is this feature. One eight-person Casino Run yields 28 pairings with identical outcomes; one twelve-player Werewolf yields 36 villager pairings per game, dealt rather than chosen. "Who you win with" was silently becoming "who else turns up to Werewolf and Casino Run", with doubles ping pong lost in the noise and nothing erroring. Now excluded: co-op BY SHAPE (a match where nobody was on a different side), factions BY PACK off the registry. The block says TEAM GAMES on screen, and the empty state is correctly the common one.
  **THE HOME BLOCK, and the second thing corrected.** The panel first hand-rolled its rows and re-derived streak, nights, show rate and main from the raw payload. All of those already exist as standalone modules because two screens use each; Home is now the third CONSUMER rather than a second spelling. Two components were DROPPED rather than forked for not fitting, measured rather than judged.
  **NO SCHEMA CHANGE, no new endpoint, no extra request.** `alias` and `ne` are now re-exported from `@gamenight/db`, because the rule there is that nothing else imports drizzle-orm directly.

- [x] THE EVENT TV GETS STATS, AND FIVE OVERFLOWS GET LADDERS (2026-08-22). One feature plus five density ladders, structured so every ladder was its own commit and the session could stop green at any boundary. It did not stop: all five landed, plus a regression found on the way, and `KNOWN` in `scripts/tv-fit.mjs` is EMPTY for the first time since it was written.
  **"STATS ON THE TV" WAS PARTLY SHIPPED ALREADY and the backlog line did not say so**, which is the finding that changed the whole shape of the work (see DECISION LOG). `EventTvPage` has had a `NightSoFar` component since the one-TV-button session: tonight's standings on the left, latest results on the right, between every game. What was missing was not a panel, it was (a) a ladder behind two hardcoded slices, (b) LIFETIME standings anywhere on any TV, and (c) a harness case for a screen nobody had ever measured.
  **THE SCREEN WAS OVER AT THE CAPPED SIZE, which is two bugs stacked.** `recap.players.slice(0, 8)` and `recap.games.slice(-6)` meant the between-games face measured 1232px IDENTICALLY at 8, 12 and 16 players: a twelve-person night dropped four people off a television with nothing on screen saying so, AND it still ran 152px over with them hidden. Now on `event-tv-band.ts` / `[data-eband]`: every count from 4 to 24 fits, TWELVE players show in full where eight was the ceiling, and past twelve the list prints how many it is holding back. The "+N more" line costs height, so the slice reserves a row for it, which was measured rather than reasoned (12 rows fit; 12 rows plus the line ran 18px over).
  **LIFETIME CREW STANDINGS, ALTERNATING WITH TONIGHT'S every 12s.** The plan said to read `/api/groups/:id/stats`; THAT ENDPOINT CANNOT SERVE THIS SCREEN, because `statsRouter.use(requireAuth)` gates it and then checks crew membership, and a television is the one screen in this app that is reliably NOT signed in. So the same data goes through the same `newAgg`/`feedAgg`/`finishAgg` on the endpoint the screen ALREADY calls, in the `Promise.all` that was already there. `stats.ts` is not modified at all. **THE ROTATION IS 12 LINES AND ITS DEPENDENCY ARRAY IS THE WHOLE TRICK**: `useLiveRefetch` re-fetches this screen on every pack message, so depending on the payload would clear and recreate the interval every few seconds and a 12s timer restarted every 4s NEVER FIRES. It depends on a boolean that changes once in a crew's life. The band takes the LARGER of the two lists, or the screen fits tonight and overflows twelve seconds later with nobody holding the device.
  **THE FIVE LADDERS, before and after, measured at 1920x1080 against the lowest painted pixel:** Ping Pong 1126 -> 864 at seven (back button was 3px into the rail, now clear by 216); Board Game and Card Table 1256/1236 -> 894/915 at twelve, ONE ladder for both packs; Mario Kart 1179 and 1447 -> 794 and 869 at twelve and sixteen; the bracketed TV at sixteen pairs 1309/1377 -> fits in all four states with sixteen solo and eight pairs untouched; Beerio's Grand Prix 1148/1717/2286 -> fits at every count from four to sixteen. Each is a separate BUGS entry with its own numbers.
  **HARNESS FIRST, FEATURE SECOND, and the first commit was allowed to fail.** `/e/:id/tv` had no case in `tv-fit.mjs` at all despite being colour-swept for weeks; Card Table had no twelve-player case even though BUGS said it was the same bug as Board Game's; Beerio's Grand Prix had no case at all. Adding all three BEFORE touching anything is what found that Card Table is 20px taller than Board Game under Tabletop, that the event TV was over even at its cap, that Grand Prix had never fitted at ANY count, and that the 08-21 on-deck work had shipped a 48px regression while its closeout said tv-fit passed.
  **EVERY LADDER GOT A NEGATIVE CONTROL** and one of them caught itself: the bracketed TV's pairs control reported "FITS, so this check is blind" until the neutraliser was taught to pin `--gn-tv-chip-max` back as well. That is the second time a control in this file has caught itself (Beerio's did on 08-15) and it is the entire argument for having them.

- [x] BOTH BRACKETED TVs SHOW WHAT IS NEXT, NOT ONLY WHAT IS READY (2026-08-21). With a bye in play the room read a winners R2 matchup at the top of the on-deck column while the actual next race was a losers R1 one whose entrants were still being decided, and nobody in that match knew they were up. **THE ORDERING WAS NOT THE BUG, which is the finding.** `compareRoundOrder` has sorted depth first and side second since 2026-08-15, so losers R1 already sorted above winners R2; the losers R1 CARD did not exist yet, because both boards filtered the deck down to matches with two real seats (`m.playable` on the shell, `isRealPlayer(m.a) && isRealPlayer(m.b)` on Beerio). The fix is what ENTERS the list, and it went in `bracketboard.ts` for the reason that module's header already gives: two boards, one set of rules.
  **THREE CLASSES, and the third is the one to defend.** `ready` is both seats real, unchanged. `pending` with ONE seat known is always eligible, because somebody already knows they are playing next. `pending` with NEITHER seat known is eligible only when every feeder it waits on is playable right now, which is deliberately LOCAL and NON-RECURSIVE: follow the chain any further and every match in the bracket qualifies on day one, which is a list of the whole night rather than a list of what is next. Kept local it is exactly what puts losers R1 on screen at the start of a night reading "Loser of Ana vs Ben" and "Loser of Cal vs Dee", which is the most warning this board can honestly give.
  **THE EMPTY SEAT NAMES ITS FEEDER**, and falls back to a position ("Loser of Winners R1 #2") when the feeder is itself undecided and there is nobody to name. Round titles come from the payload and from `BR.groups`, so Final, Semifinals, Winners Final and Losers Semis all read correctly with no second naming rule. The shell needed `MatchDef.a`/`b` on the wire: the engine has always known where a seat comes from and `deriveView` dropped it, so `aFrom`/`bFrom` are now projected onto each match. Beerio needed no payload change and, checked rather than assumed, its own `SlotSource` union is byte for byte the shared one, so the vendored file was not touched at all.
  **NO BAND METRIC MOVED, and the fit is a measurement rather than a hope.** A pending card is EXACTLY the height of a ready one because the feeder label rides in `.gn-tvm__nm` / `.beerio-tvm__nm`, both already one line with an ellipsis, so placeholders spend from the existing 4-card slice instead of extending it. Pending reads as pending through colour and border alone, from tokens. `tv-fit`: PASS, every case fits, and the 16-pairs entries are OVER by 229 and 297, the pre-existing KNOWN numbers to the digit. `theme-sweep`: 25 differences, ALL of them new rules appearing, and ZERO pre-existing values moved, which is the honest form of "Arcade is unchanged". `screens-baseline`: UNCHANGED, 27 snapshots, and NOT re-pinned, because that harness pins the pack TVs (Board Game, Card Table, Social Deduction) and has never covered the shell's `/tv/:id` at all.
- [x] DELETING A CREW OR AN EVENT STOPS DESTROYING ITS HISTORY (2026-08-20). Deleting a crew, or a single event, deleted that crew's entire recorded ledger and then returned a 500 saying it had failed. The user retried, got the same 500, and had no way to learn that anything had been deleted at all.
  **THE ROOT CAUSE IS NOT "SOMEBODY FORGOT TWO TABLES".** It is a HAND-MAINTAINED CASCADE LIST THAT WAS NOT DERIVED FROM, OR CHECKED AGAINST, THE SCHEMA IT COVERS. This schema has no `ON DELETE CASCADE` anywhere (every foreign key is a plain `references()`, and that stays, see DECISION LOG), so both delete handlers wrote their cascade out by hand. Neither list had ever included `smash_sessions`, and neither learned about `game_sessions` when it shipped on 2026-07-16 described as "additive, so `smash_sessions` is untouched and Smash keeps working". Additive is true of reads and writes and FALSE of deletes: a new child table is a new obligation on every hand-written cascade in the app, and nothing said so and nothing failed when it was missed. Four months, two handlers, zero warnings.
  **THE NON-TRANSACTIONAL SEQUENCE IS WHAT MADE IT DATA LOSS RATHER THAN A 500.** Both tables declare `event_id ... notNull().references(() => events.id)`, so Postgres refused the `DELETE FROM events` line. The deletes were sequential awaits on the shared `db` with no transaction, so `match_participants`, `matches`, `brackets`, `rsvps` and `event_attendance` were already COMMITTED and gone by the time that line raised. `async-safe.ts` turned the rejection into a 500, the crew was still standing, and its whole ledger was not.
  **TRIGGER CONDITION: the crew or event has ever had a session pack STARTED on it.** Twelve of the fourteen tiles write a `game_sessions` or `smash_sessions` row AT SETUP, before a single result is recorded, so an abandoned setup screen was enough to arm it. A crew that only ever ran brackets or Beerio deleted cleanly, which is exactly why this survived: the happy path was real, it was just not the common one.
  **THE FIX IS ONE LIST, CHECKED BY A TEST THAT DERIVES ITS OWN REQUIREMENT.** `apps/server/src/cascade.ts` is now the only cascade in the app, and `apps/server/tests/cascade-integrity.test.ts` parses `packages/db/src/schema.ts` into a foreign key graph, takes the inbound transitive closure of `groups` and of `events`, and asserts the module covers each closure plus its root in a reverse topological order computed from that same graph. Nothing about the required set is written in the test. A table added tomorrow carrying `group_id` or `event_id` joins the requirement the moment it is written, and the next missing table is a red gate instead of a foreign key violation halfway through a destructive sequence. The test was written FIRST and proven red against the unmodified handlers, naming both missing tables for both cascades, before a handler was touched. Negative controls prove both halves still bite.
  **BOTH HANDLERS ARE NOW ONE TRANSACTION EACH.** Either the whole crew goes or none of it does, so the 500 became an honest one. Safe because the driver is `drizzle-orm/node-postgres` over a real `pg.Pool`, confirmed by reading it: a pinned connection, a genuine BEGIN, COMMIT on return and ROLLBACK on throw. The cascade functions take the transaction handle, typed off the driver rather than named, so the compiler now refuses a plain `db`. Broadcast, auth and lookup all stay outside: broadcasting a deletion that then rolls back tells every connected phone about a thing that still exists.
  **AND THE EVENT CASCADE STOPPED DOING N+1**, which was in scope because of the transaction rather than as tidying: an N+1 loop inside a transaction holds its locks across every round trip. One `DELETE ... WHERE match_id IN (SELECT id FROM matches WHERE event_id = $1)` replaces one DELETE per match. `beerio_sessions` and `beerio_hof` are correctly absent from both cascades and the test does not ask for them: both are keyed by a text room code and carry neither `group_id` nor `event_id`, so neither can block a delete. NO SCHEMA CHANGE AND NO NEON SQL.
- [x] A SECOND TOURNAMENT ON THE SAME NIGHT (2026-08-19). An event that had run one tournament could never run another, permanently, and the reason was one guard plus three reads that all quietly agreed with it. `brackets.ts` refused to create a bracket when the event had ANY bracket, without looking at its status, and there is no bracket delete route, so a completed tournament sealed the night. **THE RULE NOW:** another tournament is allowed once every bracket on the event is COMPLETED, and never two non-completed at once (see DECISION LOG for why the second half is the load-bearing one). `canStartBracket` is that rule as a pure function, tested without a database, the same split `tv.ts` uses between reading rows and deciding.
  **THE THREE READS WERE THE DANGEROUS HALF, not the guard.** `tv.ts`, `events.ts` and the guard itself all selected brackets by `event_id` with `.limit(1)` and NO `orderBy`, which was safe only because the guard made a second row impossible. Relaxing the guard alone would have failed in the worst possible place and silently: Postgres hands back whichever row it likes, the resolver filters it as completed, and the TV sits on the night-so-far lobby while a live tournament is being scored, with nothing erroring anywhere. All three moved in the same session. `TvCandidates.bracket` became `brackets[]` and the resolver loops and filters exactly as it already did for pack sessions; the event page prefers the non-completed bracket and falls back to the most recently touched completed one, so the tile offers the final bracket of the tournament that just finished rather than an arbitrary earlier one.
  **THE TIEBREAK HAD TO BECOME TOTAL.** Two brackets share the `"bracket"` key in `TIEBREAK`, so timestamp plus key stopped being a full answer the moment a night could hold two of them; the last comparison is now the candidate's own id. Two rows genuinely can carry the same millisecond, and without that the winner falls out of iteration order and the TV flips between two screens on consecutive refetches, which reads as a broken television rather than as a subtle bug.
  **THE TILE OFFERS BOTH ON A COMPLETED BRACKET**, opening the final one and starting a new one, with the start tiles saying "a second tournament" so nobody taps one expecting the old one. While a bracket is live or in setup the tile is unchanged, because the server still refuses a second and offering what will be refused is worse than not offering it. `brackets_event_idx` on `event_id` ships with it: three reads now need every row, so none of them can stop at the first.
  **THREE THINGS VERIFIED RATHER THAN ASSUMED**, all of the fail-silently kind. `event_session_changed` still broadcasts on the new bracket, without which the TV never learns the second tournament exists. A second bracket's ledger row cannot collide with the first's: `materialize` keys its `matches` row on `bracketId` and sets no `externalKey` at all, which is what makes two tournaments a night safe for lifetime stats. And both land in ONE leaderboard tab, because `bracketGameName` pins `games.name` to "Tournament" and `stats.ts` buckets on that name, so the second `games` row folds into the same tab instead of splitting the crew's history in two.
- [x] QUICK PLAY IS ONE ROUTE, AND THE SECOND ENTRANT SCREEN IS GONE (2026-08-18). The tournament was the one tile out of thirteen that did not run the shared setup screen, and the gap was invisible because nothing failed: `QuickPlayPage.tsx` had its OWN entrant list, so when the roster screen shipped on 08-17 quick play silently stayed behind rather than breaking. **THE RULE, now stated and checked:** a quick play route MINTS CONTEXT AND STOPS. `POST /api/quickplay/tournament` creates the personal crew and a live event, returns `{ eventId }`, and the client opens the pack's own screen at `?event=<id>`. That is what the twelve session packs always did, and it is why they never drifted. **WHAT LANDED:** the tournament route beside the `SESSION_PACK_KEYS` loop (it has no registry entry, because it is not a pack, it is what a pack-less night runs on); both Home tiles opening `/tournament?event=<id>&format=<f>`; `QuickPlayPage.tsx` DELETED, with `/quick` surviving as an address that mints an event and REPLACES itself (pushing would leave a route behind whose whole job is to mint another event, so Back would collect empty nights); the setup screen's copy rewritten for a crew of one, which is now its second most common case rather than an edge; and `quickplay-parity.test.ts`, which pins THERE IS EXACTLY ONE ENTRANT SCREEN by source scan, in the style `pack-screens.test.ts` and `copy-rules.test.ts` already use. Every scan carries a control, because three of them pass by finding nothing and would pass forever against an empty walk. Quick play gained crew-member entrants, the member/guest distinction, the seeding shuffle, team entrants, `normalizeEntrants` validation and the entrant cap all at once, by running the screen they were built on rather than by porting any of them.
- [x] THE TOURNAMENT GETS A SETUP SCREEN, AND THE BRACKET GETS TEAM ENTRANTS (2026-08-18). Two things that were priced separately and shipped together, because the first is what the second needed. `/tournament?event=<id>&format=<f>` matches every pack's address shape and holds the roster block Board Game and Card Table have had since they shipped: prefill from the yes RSVPs in answer order, add anyone in the CREW who never answered, type a guest, shuffle the seeding. **A TEAM ENTRANT IS ONE SLOT**, which is the decision that made the doubles half small: `entrants.length` stays the number of SLOTS, so `buildStructure`, `seedOrder`, `computeBracket` and `placements` were not touched at all. What the third kind cost was everything that READS an entrant, and `entrants.ts` now makes the kind stop mattering. Full writeups, including the yes-RSVP fallback that stays as insurance, are the two `[x] SHIPPED` entries under FEATURES TO ADD.
- [x] TABLETOP REPAIR, AND THE CLOTH REACHES ITS FIRST PACK (2026-08-17). The shipped theme did not look like the approved renders, and the reason was one file. **`apps/web/src/textures/felt.webp` ON MAIN WAS 39 BYTES OF ASCII** reading `<base64-encoded-webp-from-user-image-1>`: a placeholder, never replaced. Vite inlines anything under 4KB as a data URI, the browser could not decode it, the background layer dropped silently, and Tabletop painted a flat green gradient on every screen. **NOTHING CAUGHT IT AND THAT IS THE FINDING.** `theme-sweep.mjs` compares computed values and the computed value of a broken `url()` is the same string as a working one; `felt-variance.mjs` had never been run against a build that carried the file. Fourth instance of this repo's recurring shape, a registration nothing verifies. `theme-contrast.test.ts` now reads the file's magic number (RIFF/WEBP) and a 5KB floor, so the tile is asserted rather than the reference to it.
  **THE GENERATOR COULD NOT RUN, WHICH IS WHY NOBODY REPLACED IT.** `scripts/generate-felt-tile.mjs` declared `const sd` twice in one injected browser scope and died with a SyntaxError before drawing a pixel. Renamed to `sdDec`. It then correctly REFUSED TO WRITE, and the guard was right: `--gn-dim` on the lit crown landed at 4.37 against its 4.5 floor. **THE GUARD'S OWN CONSTANTS HAD TO MOVE WITH THE LAMP**, which is the more interesting half. The revised lamp is a pool with its centre on the screen and its first stop at full strength, so the brightest surface a person reads against is now exactly `--gn-felt-lit` rather than 85% of it; `FELT_CROWN` and `crown()` in the test both moved to the full value, which is stricter than what either measured before. `--gn-dim` paled to `#ddd3bd` (ink-to-dim gap 1.45 to 1.31) and TARGET_SD came down 6.4 to 5.4. **AND THE TILE ITSELF HAD A SECOND DEFECT THAT ONLY A SCREENSHOT AT THE PAINTED SIZE COULD SHOW.** The field carries two low-frequency sinusoids for slow unevenness, at amplitudes 0.30 and 0.18, and the second is diagonal at half the tile's period. A 512px tile painted at 150px is downscaled 3.4x, which averages the fine fibre away and leaves those two almost untouched, so normalising on the total deviation meant the surviving structure was nearly all sinusoid and the screen filled with regular diagonal streaks every 75px. Cloth read as brushed metal. Caught by screenshotting the built bundle at the size the stylesheet paints rather than inspecting the tile at 1:1, where both versions look like felt, which is why it had shipped once already. The amplitudes are now a fifth of what they were. **RESULT: tile 512x512, decoded grey 109..146, sd 3.86, 19.5KB, worst relative cost 16.7% of the 20% allowed, every gated pair clear.** TARGET_SD came down 6.4 to 4.0 in two steps, and the second is worth reading: the same deviation built out of fine fibre has far longer tails than one built out of smooth sinusoids, and this guard's worst case is the single most extreme pixel, so 5.4 of the new field runs 104..153 where 5.4 of the old ran 112..144. Bytes went 9.8KB to 19.5KB because noise is what a lossy codec cannot compress. Measured in a real browser on the built bundle, `felt-variance.mjs` reports the cloth at a best-channel sd of 4.48 against a 2.5 floor.
  **THE CARD ARGUMENT WAS ABOUT THE WRONG VARIABLE.** The long `.24` versus `.5` debate was trying to stop the card reading as a hole cut in the cloth by making it more transparent. A panel reads as a hole because nothing CASTS. Given a drop, a contact edge and a lit top lip it reads as card stock at any alpha, which freed the alpha to be chosen for contrast: at `rgba(22,15,8,.62)`, SEVEN OF THE EIGHT parity exemptions in `theme-contrast.test.ts` closed and were deleted. Only `--gn-p2 on --gn-surf` survives, and that one is felt green against Arcade's neon teal rather than anything about the card. The 2px warm-brown border went with it: `--gn-card-border`, `--gn-card-shadow` and `--gn-card-pad` are tokens now, carrying Arcade's shipped values, so the card is the last shell surface a theme can re-make without an override.
  **THE RAIL IS REBUILT AND IT IS THE BIGGEST VISUAL CHANGE.** James's two complaints had one cause each. It read as PIPING because `--gn-rail-timber` is a symmetric gradient with its highlight at 50%, which is the shading of a cylinder; a plank is flat across its face and catches light only at its two eased edges. The corners DID NOT JOIN because four sides on one box are four background layers each sized to 100% of its own axis, so the top layer runs the full width including both corner squares, the left layer runs the full height, and they overlap: a butt joint. Background layers cannot be masked individually, so the frame is now FOUR ELEMENTS inside `.gn-rail`, each `clip-path`ped to its trapezoid from the outer corner to the inner one. That is a real mitre, and `--gn-radius-rail` goes to 0px because a mitre and a rounded outer corner are mutually exclusive. Four `<i>` children in `index.html`, added unconditionally; under Arcade every plank token is `none` and `--gn-rail-w` is 0px, so all four clip to zero area and paint nothing. Grain moved from 94deg to 179/89deg so it runs ALONG the timber, and the four sides carry different tones because one lamp above a table means the top rail faces it and the bottom does not.
  **BOARD GAME IS THE STAGE 4 WORKED EXAMPLE, and it answers the identity question for the remaining eight.** Ping Pong proved a pack can carry its own token block; what it never had to answer is what happens when a pack's base material IS the shell's. Board Game is walnut and brass and so is Tabletop, so under a Tabletop shell the pack DISSOLVED. The answer is not a different material per pack, it is THE SAME CLOTH AT A DIFFERENT TINT: one tile, one rail, ten tables in one room. Three tints were rendered; olive won (tobacco still dissolves, slate drifts into Deduction's cold territory). **THE MECHANISM, which is the part that generalises:** a pack CANNOT read `--gn-texture`, because a custom property containing a var() resolves where it is DECLARED, so the shell's bakes in the shell's `--gn-felt-lit` and a pack reading it would light its own table with the shell's green. That is the `--gn-bevel-btn` trap. A pack composes its own layer list out of the geometry tokens plus its own colour. **`--gn-felt-tile` is the one structural addition**, a token holding nothing but the `url()`, because the file was named only inside `--gn-texture` and nine packs would each have had to name it again.
  **FILES:** `index.css`, `index.html`, `boardgame.css` (rewritten, and it joins `copy-rules.test.ts`'s tokenised list), `generate-felt-tile.mjs`, `tv-fit.mjs`, `theme-contrast.test.ts`, `copy-rules.test.ts`, `felt.webp`. **VERIFICATION:** 893 tests pass; typecheck and build clean; the Arcade sweep reports 3473 differences and every single one is an ADDITION belonging to the four new rail planks, with ZERO changed and ZERO removed values; three of the new tokens were negative-controlled by mutation and the sweep reported all three; `tv-fit.mjs` is byte-identical outside Board Game's own three rows, which each improved by 20px. Deploy: the push, no schema.
- [x] CLEANUP AND CORRECTNESS PASS (2026-08-15, phases 1 to 4; the audit is `AUDIT-2026-08.md` at the repo root and is the record of what was found, fixed and deliberately not fixed). Phase 1 was an audit that changed no code. Phases 2 to 4 shipped in eleven commits, one logical change each, every one gated on typecheck plus build plus the full test run. TESTS 648 to 767, zero failures, one todo left failing on purpose. ENTRY BUNDLE 244.57 kB / 77.66 gzip to 224.86 / 70.88, which is 8.7 percent off what a phone downloads on a cold cache. NO SCHEMA CHANGE, so no Neon SQL is owed and the deploy is the push. **THE FOUR REAL BUGS.** (1) The shared barrel put every pack's CONTENT CATALOGUE in the entry chunk: the whole Social Deduction title list with its factions and roles, the full Smash fighter roster, the Casino Run modifier table, none of which any entry-path screen reads. Three eager modules imported `@gamenight/shared` for one value and dragged the lot. Fixed with an `exports` map plus three deep imports, and VERIFIED BY BOOTING THE SERVER rather than by typechecking, because production runs `tsx src/index.ts` and an exports map that satisfies the compiler and not the runtime loader would have passed every gate and taken the app down on deploy. (2) and (3) `matches.format` was read back by two hand-maintained tables on opposite sides of the wire, and both had drifted: the label map was missing `casino_run` and `deduction`, which printed their raw database spelling on the crew leaderboard and the recap card, and the sort order was missing SIX of twelve keys, which `indexOf` returning -1 sorted ABOVE the first entry rather than below the last. Collapsed into one registry, `packages/shared/src/formats.ts`. (4) Beerio's champion, champion modal, runner-up chip and TV headline all rendered `Racer ${seed + 1}` for an already 1-based seed, so a racer with no typed name was called Racer 4 by the champion panel and Racer 3 by the bracket beside it. All eighteen hand-written copies of that rule now call one `racerLabel`. **THE STRUCTURAL HALF, which is where the session earns its keep.** `noUnusedLocals` and `noUnusedParameters` are on, at a cost of ten deletions across four packages; the four pre-registry packs read their identity from `SESSION_PACKS` instead of literals; the one-format pack predicate is asked of the catalogue instead of listed twice; and three new source-scanning tests make the classes non-recurring: no pack identifier outside the registry, no format the server writes without a label and a sort position, and no racer naming rule written by hand. `pack-screens.test.ts` derives its checklist from the registry rather than a hand-written array. The entry bundle now has a gzip budget with stated headroom AND a separate tripwire that greps the built chunk for pack catalogue strings, because a size budget alone would not have caught the bug that motivated it. **THE TWO TESTS THAT PAID FOR THEMSELVES ON DAY ONE:** the identifier scanner immediately found ELEVEN more hardcoded identifiers on the CLIENT that the audit never looked for, including ten copies of the crew leaderboard's join key in StatsPage; and a Mario Kart test written in phase 2 found that `isRacer` rejects Paratroopa, a racer the Double Dash picker offers. Deploy: none beyond the push.
- [x] Auth: 6-digit email login codes (primary) + magic links + password accounts, 30-day session cookies
- [x] 6-digit login codes (2026-07-20). Login now emails a 6-digit code with the magic link kept below it as a desktop fallback, because an installed iOS PWA runs in a browser context whose cookie jar is separate from Safari's: a link tapped in Mail logged the session into Safari and left the installed app logged out, while a typed code never leaves the app. Code TTL 10 minutes; 5 wrong attempts per code then it dies; 60 second resend throttle; one generic "Invalid or expired code" for every failure mode so accounts can't be probed. Schema: magic_link_tokens gained code + attempts columns and an (email, code) index; the token column stayed, so links already in flight kept working.
- [x] Crew: groups, invite links, join flow, roles, member removal, self-leave, crew deletion
- [x] Events + RSVPs with live updates, event deletion (cascades)
- [x] Bracket engine: single elim (derive-from-results), byes, undo cascade, scoring lock; double elim with losers bracket + grand-final reset (packages/shared/src/bracket.ts, tests via `pnpm test:bracket`)
- [x] Live sync: in-process WebSocket hub (apps/server/src/ws.ts), broadcast on every write, shared client hook useLiveUpdates

- [x] THE BRACKETED TVs GET A STILL-ALIVE BOARD AND A ROUND STRIP (2026-08-15). Two screens in this app render a bracket, the shell's `/tv/:id` and Beerio Kart's `/beerio/tv/:code`, and until now they answered the same questions with two separate piles of code and one of them answered one of them wrong. Three pieces, plus a harness finding that is the more important half.
  **THE BUG. BEERIO'S ON DECK LIST WAS IN INSERTION ORDER**, which is every winners round, then every losers round, then the grand final. `TvPage` had already solved this and said why in a comment (sort by DEPTH then side, so the room reads winners R1, losers R1, winners R2, losers R2, grand final last, and the losers path never reads as an afterthought); Beerio took `Object.values(M)`, filtered and sliced with no sort at all. AT THE START OF A NIGHT IT LOOKS CORRECT BY ACCIDENT, which is why nobody had caught it: it only goes wrong once a second round exists, and then it floats a winners R2 matchup above a losers R1 one that has been waiting longer.
  **THE COMPARATOR IS NOW SHARED** rather than written twice (see DECISION LOG): `packages/shared/src/bracketboard.ts` takes `{ side, depth }` and nothing else, and both TVs map into it, `TvPage` from the round metadata its payload already carries and Beerio from a parse of `def.grp`. Beerio's group keys are already `W{r}` / `L{lr}` / `GF` and its losers rounds are numbered the same way this package's are, so the depth lines up rather than only looking like it does. 17 `node:test` cases in `packages/shared/tests/bracketboard.test.ts`, every fixture built from real `computeBracket` output rather than by hand (DECISION LOG, 2026-08-10).
  **THE RESULTS COLUMN IS NOW A STILL-ALIVE BOARD.** Both TVs gave their right-hand column to what just finished; that is the one thing the room already knows, because they were standing there when it happened. It now answers who is still in it: UNBEATEN, ONE LOSS AND NEXT ONE IS OUT, and OUT in elimination order, with the header reading "6 of 8". **SINGLE ELIM IS A DIFFERENT BOARD AND SHIPPED AS ONE**, not a double one with an empty middle: one loss is out, so it reads "Still in" and "Out" and there is no one-loss group at all. A loss counts only on a match that is decided and NOT `auto`, which is what keeps a bye walkover from counting without special-casing the bye slot. Generic renders it in `--gn-*` tokens (teal edge unbeaten, gold one-loss, dashed and struck out); Beerio in its own crayon language using the racer colours `state.colors` already carries, indexed `seed - 1` because bracket seeds are 1-based and that array is 0-based.
  **THE ROUND STRIP** is a band between the header and the columns, one cell per round in the SAME comparator order, so the strip and the on-deck list can never disagree about what comes next. Round name, decided-of-total, and three states carried on the TOP BORDER rather than a background so the band stays quiet: done (dimmed), now (accent) and not yet. MORE THAN ONE CELL IS LIT AT ONCE IN DOUBLE ELIM and that is the normal case, not an edge one.
  **CHAMPION STILL WINS OVER THE BOARD** on both, and the reason is stronger than tidiness: once there is a winner the alive board is not redundant, it is WRONG. "Next one is out" is false after the last race, and in double elim the champion can be sitting in that very group, having come up through the losers bracket. The strip stays under a champion, because every round being done is still true.
  **THE FIT LADDER (2026-08-15, same day, own commit).** The boards shipped above did not fit a television and neither screen ever had: measured the first day anything measured them, the shell TV ran 1548px and Beerio's 1662px at sixteen entrants against 1080px of screen, and both were over at EVERY count because their columns were capped lists. Both now fit at 4, 8, 12 and 16 entrants, in all four states a night passes through, in both themes on the shell and one on Beerio, with nothing painted under the rail and NOTHING added to tv-fit's `KNOWN`. A density ladder per screen (`apps/web/src/pages/tv-band.ts`, `apps/web/src/beerio/band.ts`), the shape `band.ts` proved on the money board and deliberately not that file, spent as CSS variable overrides so a rung is a dozen-line diff. Every entrant's name is still on the screen at sixteen, and at twenty-four, with no truncation and nothing collapsed into a count. The type floor is 2.2vmin / 1.25vw, which is 24px and is the money board's, reused rather than re-argued. `scripts/tv-fit.mjs` grew from 4 bracketed cases to 34, learned to FAIL on a fit rather than only report it, and carries a negative control that re-injects the pre-ladder metrics and requires them to overflow. See BUGS (FIXED) and the DECISION LOG.
  **NO ENGINE CHANGE, NO SCHEMA, NO LEDGER.** `packages/shared/src/bracket.ts`'s derivations are untouched, `BracketPage`'s tree is untouched, Beerio's Grand Prix board is untouched (it is not a bracket), and `BeerioApp.tsx` / `beerio.ts` stay the vendored 1:1 port they are. Files: `packages/shared/src/bracketboard.ts` (new), its tests (new), `index.ts`, `apps/web/src/pages/TvPage.tsx`, `apps/web/src/beerio/BeerioTvPage.tsx`, `apps/web/src/index.css`, `scripts/tv-fit.mjs`, `scripts/theme-sweep.mjs`.

Profiles gained a "show-up record": show rate, current/best show streak (🔥 at 3+), and flake count, computed from rsvps + event_attendance ordered by event date. Flake definition (revised same day, James): a flake is "RSVP'd yes and never confirmed showing up" — silence after a yes counts as a flake once the night is 24h past its date, because real flakes don't open the app to tap no; an honest "didn't show" answer counts immediately; a yes on a dateless event never counts. Server: /api/friends, /api/friends/:userId/stats, /api/friends/:userId/rivalry; rivalry + profile aggregation refactored to run over a set of group ids. No schema change.
- [x] Event share + night recap (2026-07-20). Event page gained a Share control: navigator.share when available, clipboard copy with a "Link copied" toast otherwise. The shared link routes through the existing crew invite/join flow with an event id query param (/join/:code?event=:id), so a logged-out tap lands on the join page and, after join or login, redirects to the event. No schema change: the invite code carries the event via query param, and the event detail response now also returns groupName + inviteCode so the page builds the link in one request. New route /e/:id/recap: the night recap card, aggregating every completed game under the event across every pack (Beerio, Smash, Mario Kart, Mario Party, brackets) from the one materialized ledger (matches/match_participants), with per-player rollups (wins, games, avg placement), a games-played list with each winner, and an MVP callout. Reuses the canvas-to-JPG recap pipeline (same Arcade palette, fonts, share/download path); a friendly "nothing played yet" empty state when no games exist. Members only, since guests are never materialized (deferred: showing guests needs per-pack jsonb session reading).
- [x] Profiles + rivalry cards (2026-07-18). Tapping a member in the crew list opens /g/:id/member/:userId. Yourself = profile (games/wins/win rate/best/avg + by-game table; podiums was removed 2026-07-27). Anyone else = rivalry: side-by-side stats, head-to-head record banner, per-game H2H, shareable canvas-to-JPG rivalry card. Home gained a "Your stats" card aggregating across every crew including quick play. Server: three read-only endpoints in stats.ts. No schema change.
- [x] Past game nights collapse on the crew page (2026-07-23). The crew "Game nights" list now splits on time: nights more than 24h past their scheduled date (the same grace window flake tracking uses) drop into a single collapsible "Past game nights (n)" cabinet tile, built exactly like the Friends cabinet on Home (gn-cab button, tap to expand/hide). It sits below the upcoming/current nights and above "Schedule a new one". Dateless (TBD) nights are never treated as past. Same event tile inside both lists (one shared renderer), so delete and RSVP info are identical; no server or schema change (partition is client-side on the existing events list).
- [x] Link preview + favicons + head meta (2026-07-23). Pasting the app link in iMessage/Slack/Discord now shows a real card instead of a blank rectangle. Added apps/web/public/og-image.png (1200x630, opaque RGB, plum background, the existing coral GN badge floated with a soft shadow beside the "GameNight OS" wordmark set in Luckiest Guy with a teal underline echoing the mark), generated from icon-512.png with Pillow (scripts note: the real Luckiest Guy TTF was pulled from Google Fonts to match the app wordmark). Favicons derived from the same mark: favicon-32.png (downscaled with LANCZOS, GN still legible), favicon-16.png (simplified to the bare coral square + teal bar since "GN" smudges at 16px), and a multi-res favicon.ico (16+32) for older browsers and bookmark bars. index.html head gained a meta description matching the manifest, the full Open Graph set (type/site_name/title/description/url/image + width/height/alt) and Twitter summary_large_image tags, all with ABSOLUTE https URLs (relative paths fail silently in link unfurlers), plus rel=icon links for the ico/32/16 with icon-192 kept as a larger fallback. The existing manifest, apple-touch-icon, theme-color and apple-mobile-web-app-* tags were left untouched; the manifest needed no change. No schema, no new runtime dependency (Pillow is a build-time tool only), no server change: Vite copies public/ into dist/ and Express already serves dist/ statically, so the files resolve at the site root. Per-route dynamic previews stayed out of scope (logged under FEATURES).
- [x] Keep-warm ping (2026-07-25). GitHub Actions scheduled workflow (.github/workflows/keep-warm.yml) GETs {WARM_URL}/api/health every ~13 min during evening Central hours (21:00-06:59 UTC = ~4pm to 1am CT year-round), keeping the Render free instance warm so game night never opens on a 30-60s cold start. Reads WARM_URL from a repo Actions variable set to the *.onrender.com ORIGIN (not the Cloudflare-fronted custom domain, which could answer /api/health from cache without waking Render); no-ops if the variable is unset. Evening-only, never blind 24/7: ~310 instance-hours/month, far under the free 750/month cap (24/7 would be ~744h with no margin for redeploys). The 13-min cadence is under Render's 15-min idle timeout so the service stays warm across the window, then sleeps; single fail-soft curl step; workflow_dispatch button for a manual pre-game warm. No app or schema change. Deploy: none on Render; James sets the WARM_URL repo variable once (Settings -> Secrets and variables -> Actions -> Variables). Follow-up idea logged: an event-aware warmer that only pings in the hour before a scheduled event start (fewer hours, needs an endpoint listing upcoming event start times). WIDENED 2026-07-28 to noon-2am Central (cleanup phase 8b, James picked the window option): cron `*/13 17-23,0-8 * * *`, one continuous 16-hour UTC window, 21:00-06:59 UTC before. Because the UTC window is fixed and the Central offset is not, both ends are chosen to cover the target in BOTH offsets rather than one, so each end overshoots by an hour for half the year: 17:00 UTC is noon CDT exactly and 11am CST, 08:xx UTC is 2am CST exactly and 3am CDT. ~487 instance-hours/month against the free 750, up from ~310, leaving ~260 hours of headroom for redeploys. Workflow-only change: no app code, no schema, and nothing for James to set up, since WARM_URL is already configured.
- [x] Double-elim grand-final "needs 2" clarity + TV up-next ordering (2026-07-25). The grand final now signals that the winners-bracket finalist needs one win while the losers-bracket finalist (always slot B) must win the set AND the reset. BracketPage labels the first game "Set 1", shows a "must win twice" badge naming the losers finalist, and renders the reset (GF2) as a clearly conditional game ("only if <lb> wins Set 1"), with a "no reset needed" note when the winners finalist takes Set 1. TvPage adds "Grand Final / Set 1" and "Grand Final / Reset" labels, a "needs 2" chip on the losers finalist, and an on-deck explanatory note. Shipped alongside the related item: the TV on-deck list is now ordered by depth then bracket side (winners R1, losers R1, winners R2, losers R2, and so on) with the grand final last, so the losers path is not an afterthought. UI only (the engine already models GF/GF2); no schema, no engine change. New CSS gn-bkt-gf2 / gn-bkt-gfnote and gn-tvm__needs2 / gn-tvm__note in the existing gn-bkt-* / gn-tvm-* layers. Deploy: none.
- [x] Link a guest to a crew member (2026-07-26). Owner/admin one-time backfill that credits a past typed guest's results to the crew member they turned out to be. Guests are never in the ledger (match_participants.userId is NOT NULL), so this RE-MATERIALIZES the guest's recoverable history from each pack's stored jsonb under the member's userId, reusing the live materializers via an added optional linkMap (guest display name -> userId) parameter; when linkMap is absent the materializers behave exactly as before. Covers generic brackets, Smash, Mario Kart, Mario Party, Ping Pong; Beerio was excluded at first and was added forward-only on 2026-07-27 (see the GAME PACKS entry). Preview-first: a dry run shows exactly what would be credited (per pack, each game with its label, event, date, placement) and writes nothing; confirm then writes. Additive and idempotent: the guest's already-materialized match is REUSED (not recreated) and the previously-skipped participant is inserted ON CONFLICT (matchId,userId) DO NOTHING, so re-running is a no-op and existing rows are never disturbed; the preview skips units the member already has (memberCreditedKeys per event). Exact name matching from an admin-picked list; the target is validated as a crew member server-side. New: apps/server/src/guest-link.ts (router: GET /groups/:id/guest-names, POST /groups/:id/guest-link/preview, POST .../confirm; confirm broadcasts leaderboard_updated per touched event) and apps/server/src/guest-link-util.ts (shared GuestCreditItem/Result type + the memberCreditedKeys idempotency helper); linkMap threaded through all five materializers (brackets/smash/mariokart/marioparty/pingpong); web apps/web/src/pages/LinkGuestPage.tsx at /g/:id/link-guest, reached from an owner/admin button in the crew page members card. No schema change (so db:push:ci applies nothing new). No unlink/retract in v1 (a clean retract needs a column marking backfilled rows, deferred). Deploy: none.
- [x] Per-character stats on profiles + podium stat removed (2026-07-27). Every character pack already stored the fighter/racer/character on each result (match_participants.character) and no stats screen read it; the personal profile views now do. The shared Agg helpers in stats.ts gained a byCharacter tally (keyed by character NAME, so one character is one line across titles, per the standing rule) and finishCharacters rolls it into the list plus the main (most played) and the best (highest win rate among characters clearing MIN_CHAR_GAMES = 3, so one lucky win cannot outrank a real main). Returned under a nested `characters` object rather than spread flat, because the character summary has its own `best` and the response already carries one (best placement). Rendered by one shared component (apps/web/src/CharacterStats.tsx) on MemberPage (crew member profile and the friend route, both of which run through the same Profile view) and MyStatsPage; it hides itself when the list is empty, so Beerio / Ping Pong / bracket-only profiles look exactly as before. The crew leaderboard (StatsPage) deliberately got no character section in that pass; that was REVERSED the same day, see the crew leaderboard entry below. Same pass removed the podiums stat end to end: out of newAgg/feedAgg/finishAgg, out of the /groups/:id/stats leaderboard aggregation and its Row type, and off MemberPage (profile grid and the rivalry compare), MyStatsPage and StatsPage. The recap card's visual 1-2-3 podium and Beerio's vendored podium modal are a different thing and were left alone. Read-layer and UI only: no schema change, no change to how games are recorded, live sync unaffected (read-only endpoints). Deploy: none.
- [x] Time-based stats + a match timestamp (2026-07-27). Added matches.playedAt (timestamp NOT NULL DEFAULT now()), the one thing missing before streaks and recent form could exist at all. Capture is automatic: all eight matches insert sites across every pack (brackets, Beerio, Smash x2, Mario Kart x2, Mario Party, Ping Pong) create the row inline with status "completed" and nothing ever updates a matches row afterward, so the column default IS play time and no pack sets it by hand; the guest-link backfill reuses the existing row, so a link never restamps history. Verified in the clone before relying on it. Profiles (MemberPage + MyStatsPage, the same two Session A picked) gained a Form block: current win streak with a fire marker at 3+, longest win streak, the last five results as win/place pips most recent first, and nights played. Server: the shared Agg helpers gained a timeline and finishForm (one ascending pass computes both streaks, since the run ending at the newest result IS the current streak) plus a distinct-eventId count; feedAgg moved from five positional args to one ResultRow object, because the query rows already have exactly those field names and five like-typed positional args were a swap waiting to happen. NO BACKFILL by decision: the app has not been in real use, so pre-existing rows taking the migration timestamp is meaningless and harmless. First-place finishes deliberately NOT added as a separate stat: placement 1 equals isWinner in every pack, so the existing wins figure already is it. Nights played counts distinct events off the ledger rather than attendance check-ins (see decision log). Schema deploy: db:push:ci applies the column; fallback ALTER TABLE matches ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS played_at timestamp NOT NULL DEFAULT now().
- [x] Crew leaderboard gets the advanced stats too (2026-07-27). James asked for the per-character and time-based stats on the crew Stats page, reversing the scoping call that had held both to the personal profiles. The crew endpoint had its OWN duplicated aggregation loop, so rather than bolting the new tallies on twice it now uses the same shared Agg the profiles use: one newAgg/feedAgg/finishAgg per player, per game bucket too. That deleted the duplicate placement/win logic and means the crew page and a profile can no longer disagree about the same player. NUMBERS CHANGED IN ONE PLACE: crew avgPlacement used to divide by games played, while the profile divides by results that actually carried a placement, so the two disagreed whenever a pack recorded a result without ranking it. The crew page now uses the profile's definition, which is the more correct one, so an avg finish on the crew page may read slightly different from before and now matches the profile exactly. UI: every leaderboard row is expandable on EVERY tab now (it used to expand only on the overall tab), and the expanded area shows the last-five pips with current streak, best streak and nights played, plus main / best character and the top three characters. By-game stays on the overall tab only, since inside a game tab it would repeat that tab's own name. The pip visual is exported from FormStats so the crew page reuses it instead of redrawing it. Read layer and UI only, no schema change. Deploy: none.
- [x] Crew leaderboard display unified across packs + expand affordance (2026-07-27). The same player read differently depending on which tab you were on: the generic list (All, Mario Kart, Beerio, Tournament) showed wins with a played/win-rate/avg-finish subline and expanded to the advanced stats, while Smash, Mario Party and Ping Pong each drew their OWN player list with a different headline number (Smash and Mario Party wins/played, Ping Pong game wins in green), a different subline, their own ranking, and no expand at all. There is now ONE player row renderer (PlayerRows + ExpandedStats in StatsPage) used by every tab, fed by the shared per-game leaderboard, so rank, headline and expanded detail are identical everywhere. Each pack keeps the sections that are genuinely its own below the list (Smash fighters + head to head, Mario Party boards + bonus stars + characters). Per-player numbers that exist in only one pack were NOT dropped, they moved to a subline tail via an extras map keyed by userId: Mario Party total/avg stars, Ping Pong single-game wins, Smash fighter variety and the night-scoped hot streak (kept deliberately, since it measures a run within one night and the expanded block's best streak is lifetime). CONSEQUENCE: the Ping Pong tab's headline is now MATCH wins like every other pack, with game wins in the subline, and each pack tab's ranking is the shared wins/win-rate/played order rather than its old bespoke one. Also added the expand affordance James asked for: a tiny ▾ that flips to ▴ when open, on every leaderboard row and on the two cabinets that had none (Friends on Home, Past game nights on the crew page); the GamePicker and the install hint already had one, and the same ▾ is the app's existing token. Read layer and UI only, no schema change, no endpoint change. Deploy: none.
- [x] Deep personal stats + a richer head to head (2026-07-27). Three new groups of stats, all derived from data already stored, all added to the ONE shared Agg in stats.ts so the member profile, /me/stats, the friend profile and BOTH sides of a rivalry inherit them from a single implementation. Placement depth: distribution across 1st/2nd/3rd/4th-or-worse with shares, plus a last-place count. Field size is not stored anywhere, so last place is derived from one grouped count of participant rows per match and compared against the placement, one query for the whole history rather than one per match; a field of one is skipped, since its winner would otherwise qualify as last. Time and history: playing-since, a 12-month games-per-month series with empty months included so the shape is honest, games per night, best night (most wins, ties to the night that needed fewer games), and loss streaks mirrored off the existing win walk. Character depth: best and average placement per character plus a distinct-character count, still unified by name across titles. Rivalry gained the genuinely head-to-head items off the meeting map, which now carries playedAt: a signed current streak (positive is you, negative is them) so the client knows whose run it is, each side's longest run, last meeting with date/game/winner, last-five meeting pips, and each side's most-used character in SHARED matches only. Undated meetings are excluded from anything order-dependent rather than guessed at. Web: one collapsed "More stats" disclosure per profile (new Disclosure + DeepStats components), closed by default, so the always-visible tiles are untouched; the rivalry tab got the same disclosure holding a deeper side-by-side compare, and the character card gained the per-character placements and the distinct count. IMPORTANT SPLIT: finishAgg stayed sync and cheap because the crew leaderboard calls it once per player, and the two query-backed stats live in a separate finishAggDeep used only by the four personal endpoints, so the leaderboard never pays for them. Opponent/nemesis aggregates and meta-column pack extras stayed out by decision. Read layer and UI only, no schema change. Deploy: none.
- [x] Repo hygiene + a real test harness (2026-07-27, pre-pack cleanup phase 0). Deleted attached_assets/, 107 files and 44,555 lines of stale copies of App.tsx, BACKLOG.md, EventPage.tsx, BeerioApp.tsx and schema.ts, some a dozen versions deep and 2.2 MB, larger than the whole git history. The real cost was not disk: every grep and file search a session runs hit stale copies of the exact files it was about to edit, so a session could open a months-old EventPage and write confident nonsense against it. Added attached_assets/ to .gitignore so a future upload cannot silently re-commit it; the content stays in git history if it is ever wanted. Deleted GAMEPLAN.md, which had become a third source of truth that disagreed with this file (it still said "NOW: Smash Bros", listed eight standing rules against this file's ten, and duplicated rules this file owns); the two README pointers now point here. Everything in it was already covered except ONE rule, which was rescued into STANDING RULES rather than lost: SINGLE INSTANCE ONLY (numInstances: 1), because the WebSocket hub is in this process's memory and a second instance would split the room. It had been stated as a standing rule only in GAMEPLAN and a render.yaml comment. Phase 0b added a `pnpm test` script that globs every test file (packages/shared/tests/*.test.ts plus apps/server/tests/*.test.ts) instead of naming one, keeping test:bracket working, and created apps/server/tests/ with its first real test: six assertions covering async-safe.ts, chosen because its failure mode is the worst in the server (a rejected promise takes the process and the WebSocket hub down) and because it was verified by hand once on 2026-07-16 and never captured. Now 18 tests run, 12 bracket plus 6 async-safe. Still node:test via the server package's tsx, no new dependency, so the lockfile stays frozen for Render. Deploy: none.
- [x] What every request costs (2026-07-27, pre-pack cleanup phase 1). Four latency fixes, no payload change anywhere. (1) attachUser was mounted globally, so its sessions x users join ran on EVERY request a logged-in browser makes, including index.html, the JS bundle, the CSS, five favicons, the manifest, the apple-touch-icon and the OG image; a cold page load fired roughly ten needless Neon round trips before a pixel rendered. Now mounted on /api only, which is where every reader of req.user lives. (2) eventDetail ran seven queries in series when six of them only need the already-loaded event row; they now go out in one Promise.all, 7 serial round trips down to 2. This is on the hot path twice, the event page load and every RSVP, attendance and date mutation, which all return through it. The same shape in all four pack context endpoints went 5 down to 2. (3) Participant rows were inserted one at a time in a loop, so an eight-player Smash FFA was eight sequential inserts and a twelve-racer Beerio grand prix was twelve; all eight materialize sites (four packs plus generic brackets plus Beerio) now build the rows and write them in one insert through a shared insertParticipants helper. ON CONFLICT (matchId,userId) DO NOTHING behaves the same for a multi-row insert, so guest-link idempotency is unchanged. (4) sessionView gained an optional already-loaded argument, and the authed GET in each pack now reuses the row its role check just read instead of selecting it a second time. ONE behaviour change, deliberate: rows are deduped by userId before the insert, so if two guest slots typed with the same name both link to one member, `recorded` now reports 1 (the number of rows actually written) instead of 2. The old loop counted both even though the second insert wrote nothing. Deploy: none.
- [x] The missing index (2026-07-27, pre-pack cleanup phase 2). match_participants was indexed on (match_id, user_id) unique and on group_id, but nothing on user_id alone, and the unique index cannot serve a user_id-only filter because match_id leads it. Every personal stats read filters on exactly that: /api/me/stats, a member profile, a rivalry, and both friend endpoints. All of them were sequential-scanning the whole participants table. Added match_participants_user_idx on user_id, and matches_game_idx on game_id in the same pass since stats.ts joins matches.game_id to games.id on every leaderboard read. Both are additive index creations, no data change and no downtime. Done now precisely because the table is small: this is the cheap moment, not the painful one. Also logged countLastPlace's unbounded IN list as a BUGS watch rather than fixing it here (see the watch for the shape of the eventual fix). Schema deploy: db:push:ci applies both; confirm the drizzle-kit "Changes applied" line in the build log, because push --force can silently no-op in non-interactive CI. Fallback SQL: CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS match_participants_user_idx ON match_participants (user_id); and CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS matches_game_idx ON matches (game_id);
- [x] Stop shipping every pack to every screen (2026-07-28, pre-pack cleanup phase 4). App.tsx statically imported all 24 routes, so opening the login page downloaded and parsed the entire Beerio Kart app plus lz-string, all four pack pages, all five TV pages, the recap canvas with qrcode.react, and all five pack stylesheets. Route-level React.lazy + Suspense now splits everything you reach by choosing to go there, keeping only the entry path eager (Home, GroupPage, EventPage, JoinPage; there is no separate Login route, Home covers logged out). MEASURED: entry JS 490.67 kB down to 210.83 kB (gzip 136.22 down to 66.07), entry CSS 71.06 down to 49.62 kB, so first load roughly halved. Beerio is its own 99 kB chunk and carries lz-string with it; qrcode moved into a lazy shared chunk; each pack's CSS split into its own file automatically because the import lives inside the lazily loaded page. Two supporting pieces: beerio/BeerioRoute.tsx exists so the route component can be lazily loaded as one chunk (importing the named setBeerioNavigator from App would have pulled the vendored app back into the entry chunk and undone the split; BeerioApp itself is untouched per the 1:1 rule), and RouteBoundary.tsx catches a chunk that fails to arrive, which is a new failure mode that did not exist when everything shipped in one bundle. Its recovery is a reload rather than a retry, because a stale index.html keeps asking for a hashed chunk that a deploy removed and only a full load picks up the new one. VERIFIED BY ACTUALLY LOADING ALL 23 ROUTES headless: no test dependency was added, the pre-installed Chromium was driven over CDP from a throwaway script against vite preview, polling each route until React rendered. Every route mounted, none hit the boundary, no unexpected console errors. Pack selectors were confirmed present in their own CSS chunk (the *-root duplicates in the entry CSS are the shared safe-area inset rules index.css defines for every pack root, not a leak). Deploy: none.
- [x] Cache and compress the static build (2026-07-28, pre-pack cleanup phase 5). express.static ran with its defaults, so every asset revalidated on every load even though Vite content-hashes the filenames, which makes that revalidation buy nothing; and there was no compression anywhere in the server. Now /assets is served immutable with a one year max-age (the filename IS the version, so a change to contents changes the URL), the rest of the build gets one hour with index: false, and the SPA fallback sets Cache-Control: no-cache on index.html explicitly. That last one is the whole sharp edge: a cached index.html pins users to an old bundle because it never asks for the new hashed chunks, and index: false is what stops the second static mount serving it with the one hour max-age. Added `compression` as a dependency rather than relying on the edge, and the lockfile was regenerated in the same commit because Render's install is frozen. Justification for the dependency: it gzips API and static responses at the ORIGIN so the bundle is never served uncompressed whatever the CDN in front is doing, which matters because Cloudflare's compression is a dashboard toggle outside this repo, it does nothing if the DNS record is grey-clouded, and anything hitting the *.onrender.com origin directly bypasses the edge entirely (the keep-warm ping does exactly that, on purpose). VERIFIED against a running server rather than by reading the code: hashed asset returns public, max-age=31536000, immutable; / and a deep SPA route both return no-cache; manifest returns max-age=3600; a real GET of the bundle with gzip accepted transfers 66,065 bytes instead of 210,833, the same request without gzip accepted has no Content-Encoding, and a PNG is correctly left alone. Note that curl -I says nothing useful here, since HEAD has no body for compression to act on. Deploy: none.
- [x] Your own stats page stops disagreeing with your crew profile (2026-07-28, pre-pack cleanup phase 7). /api/me/stats returned no attendance while /groups/:id/members/:userId/stats did, so the crew profile showed your show-up record, streak and flake count and your own cross-crew page showed nothing: same person, two pages, different answer. It now returns attendance too. MyStatsPage also never rendered `best` (best placement) or `avgPlacement` even though the endpoint had been returning both since the deep-stats pass, so those are now two tiles matching the crew profile's. DECISION, as flagged: the cross-crew show-up record counts REAL crews only and excludes the hidden personal quick-play crews, because a personal crew is just you, there is nobody to have flaked on and no RSVP to break, and counting them would dilute the rate with nights that cannot be missed. Lifetime totals still include quick play, unchanged. NOT done, deliberately: the phase asked for /me/stats to be pointed at aggFor(). It already shares the aggregation (newAgg/feedAgg/finishAggDeep, since the time-stats pass), so the numbers already agree, and switching would have made things worse twice over: aggFor runs its own query, so byFormat and byCrew would need a second scan of the same rows, and aggFor filters by current memberships while the existing query filters by userId, so it would silently DROP the stats of any crew you have left (leaving deletes the membership row but keeps the participant rows). ShowUpRecord and its Stat tile moved out of MemberPage into apps/web/src/ShowUpRecord.tsx rather than being imported across pages: after the route splitting, importing it from MemberPage would have pulled that whole route's chunk into MyStatsPage's. One implementation, and MemberPage got smaller. Deploy: none.
- [x] One pack runtime on the server (2026-07-28, pre-pack cleanup phase 3). The four session packs were each adapted from the one before, so they carried the same plumbing four times: roleOf, isHostRole, loadState, saveState, ensureGame, ledgerKey, deleteMaterialized, the launch-context endpoint, the guest-name scan, the find-or-insert half of every materializer, and the sessionView/respondState pair. roleOf alone existed SIX times, counting guest-link.ts and brackets.ts. Adding a pack meant editing fourteen files and hand-copying roughly five hundred lines, with four more packs queued. New apps/server/src/pack-runtime.ts holds one parameterised set built from a per-pack config (pack, gameName, wsType, keyPrefix, table, extras); each pack keeps its own routes, request validation, state shape, materialize* and the fields its session payload adds, because the LEDGER UNIT genuinely differs (game-as-unit for Smash FFA and MK races, match-as-unit for a best-of series or a ping pong match, board-as-unit for Mario Party). MEASURED: smash 1091 -> 844, mariokart 908 -> 666, marioparty 779 -> 580, pingpong 731 -> 529, plus brackets and guest-link losing their roleOf copies; 1281 lines deleted against 371 added outside the new 552-line runtime. TESTS WERE WRITTEN FIRST and confirmed green against the ORIGINAL code before anything moved, by temporarily exporting the four private ledgerKey functions and asserting the exact strings they produced; those same strings are what the committed test now asserts against the runtime, so the test cannot have canonised the refactor. 18 tests became 55. Covered: every pack's ledgerKey (modern, legacy, idx 0 in both shapes, empty sessionKey, and two sessions on one event not colliding), all four configs field by field, the Smash/shared table split as its own assertion, distinctness of keyPrefix / wsType / gameName across packs, and the pure participant-row decision logic exhaustively (all members, all guests, a mix, a linked guest, a link map that does not name the guest, a link map that must not override a real member's userId, two slots resolving to one userId, a line naming a slot not in the roster, and which of character/score/meta ride through). No Drizzle stub anywhere: stubbing it would test the stub. THE DEFERRED HALF OF PHASE 1 CAME WITH IT, as planned: saveState now returns the view-ready payload, so all ~17 mutation handlers per pack answer from the state they already hold instead of re-SELECTing the row they just wrote, and startSession does the same for the four start routes. That was 68 careful hand-edits before this phase and was nearly free once the four disagreeing saveState signatures became one (Smash's took groupId, the others did not). wsType is now a literal union type rather than a string, so a typo is a compile error instead of silent live-sync death. VERIFIED: pnpm -r typecheck, pnpm build and pnpm test all clean, and the route table (52 routes across ten routers, method, path and handler count) was enumerated from the actual Express router stacks on both this tree and HEAD and diffed byte for byte: identical. The four sessionView payloads were compared field by field against their originals, same fields in the same order. Deploy: none, no schema change.

- [x] Make it feel fast (2026-07-28, pre-pack cleanup phase 8a). Opening the app as a returning user ran three sequential dead stages; this kills stages 2 and 3. (1) App.tsx BLOCKED THE ENTIRE APP on GET /api/auth/me behind `if (loading) return <RouteFallback />`, so every launch showed a full-screen "Loading..." before any route could mount. The Me object is now cached and read synchronously on the first render, the real UI paints immediately, and the request confirms in the background.
