THREE STATES, not two: `undefined` (nobody has told us yet) is distinct from `null` (known signed out), because collapsing them would flash the LOGIN SCREEN at every signed-in user on any cold cache, which after every deploy is everyone. So a warm launch paints instantly and a genuinely unknown answer waits exactly as it used to; nothing got slower. (2) New apps/web/src/cache.ts: useCachedApi<T>(key, path) -> { data, stale, loading, error, refetch, set }, memory then localStorage, returning the cached value SYNCHRONOUSLY during render rather than in an effect. A loading state now only appears when there is genuinely nothing to show. `set` is the write-through path, and EventPage is where it pays: /api/events/:id/rsvp, PATCH /api/events/:id and .../attendance all return the full updated event and were already fed to setEvent, so routing setEvent through the cache makes a return visit instant AND correct rather than instant and pre-RSVP. Applied to Home (crews, friends, your-stats tile), GroupPage (crew + nights), EventPage, StatsPage (leaderboard plus all three pack panels), MyStatsPage and MemberPage. NOT applied to the TV routes or anything under a pack's live session, deliberately. Storage is namespaced with a __BUILD_ID__ define, and foreign namespaces are swept on boot. ONE BUG CLASS FIXED WHILE WIRING IT UP: several pages checked `if (error)` BEFORE `if (!data)`, which with a cache would throw away perfectly good cached content the moment a revalidation failed; cached content now wins and the error screen is only for having nothing at all. (3) New prefetch.ts: pointerdown on a game tile, format row, crew stats button, recap link or TV link starts that route's chunk, turning the 80-150ms between finger-down and click into a head start. Same import() specifier as the route, so it is memoized, not duplicated. (4) New Skeleton.tsx plus a gn-skel CSS layer: layout-shaped placeholders on the crew list, night list, event page and stats screens, with a reduced-motion opt-out and an aria-live label since the shapes are aria-hidden. Only ever seen on a first visit. (5) The three fonts are self-hosted: six woff2 files (latin + latin-ext; Fredoka and Nunito are variable, so one file covers each whole weight range) in src/fonts, referenced from index.css so Vite hashes them into /assets and they inherit the immutable one-year caching phase 5 set up, which the Google URLs never could. That removes a render-blocking third-party stylesheet, a DNS lookup and a TLS handshake from the critical path, and makes the recap canvas font wait deterministic. COST: entry JS 210.84 -> 214.73 kB (gzip 66.07 -> 67.51). VERIFIED IN A REAL BROWSER, not by reading: Chromium driven over CDP against the real built bundle plus a stub API with a deliberate 250ms delay. Warm launch paints real data with no loading state at 120ms, well inside that delay; Home -> crew -> event -> back shows no loading state and still revalidates; a foreign build namespace is swept; every font request is same-origin; a pack chunk is absent until pointerdown and present after; a 401 lands on the login screen and clears the cache; and all 23 routes still mount without hitting the error boundary. Deploy: none, no schema change.

- [x] One pack registry, before more packs (2026-07-28). Every pack had TWO spellings and nothing reconciled them: the server's `games.pack` / `game_sessions.pack` key (smash / mario_kart / mario_party / pingpong) and the client's route segment and picker key (smash / mariokart / marioparty / pingpong). They agree for two packs and disagree for two, which is the worst possible arrangement, because every consumer only ever touched one side and so nothing ever failed loudly enough to reveal that two lists existed. The next pack was a coin flip: name it air_hockey on the server and airhockey on the client, following Mario Kart's own example, and everything compiles, the picker works, the session runs, and the recap card silently draws a blank emoji while the stats tab shows nothing. NOW: `packages/shared/src/packs.ts` holds SESSION_PACKS, one entry per pack carrying the ledger key, gameName, keyPrefix, route segment, wsType, table, display name, emoji and quick-play title; SessionPackKey, PackWsType, PACK_WS_TYPES and PACK_BY_LEDGER are all DERIVED from it, so a pack without an entry is a compile error rather than a blank space on a screen. Both sides import it. Now data-driven instead of hand-maintained: the WsEvent pack members, the four pack runtime configs (each pack file supplies only its `extras` now, via a new packConfig() helper), quickplay's four near-identical routes (one loop), the guest-link adapter table, the event TV resolver's ledger map, the event detail payload's, the recap card's emoji table, the picker catalogue's names and emoji, the event page's pack-name table, Home's hand-typed union, the eight wsType literals on the pack pages and their TV views, and the event TV's live-subscription list. TWO REAL BUGS FELL OUT, both of the exact class the registry prevents, both silent. (1) The event TV resolver and the event detail payload each hand-wrote a ledger->client table keyed "ping_pong" — a spelling that exists NOWHERE in the app, since Ping Pong's ledger key is "pingpong" — and both lookups gate on a hit, so a live Ping Pong session was invisible to the event TV (the screen sat on the lobby while a game was being played) and never showed "live now" on its tile. Shipped two commits earlier, in the TV session, which is the point: that session hit this problem on its first day and papered over it with two more one-off maps. (2) The recap card's emoji table was keyed "beerio" while Beerio writes `games.pack = "beerio_kart"`, so every Beerio night on every recap card has been drawing the fallback trophy instead of its beer since the pack shipped. Nobody noticed, because a wrong emoji does not throw. TWO THINGS DELIBERATELY LEFT MANUAL, and named in the pack checklist below rather than forced: the lazy route imports in App.tsx, because Vite needs static literals inside import() to split chunks, and the router mounts in server/index.ts, because mount ORDER is load-bearing (the public TV routers must come before the authed ones). prefetch.ts's chunk table keeps its hand-written importers for the same Vite reason, but is now typed `Record<PackKey | "beerio", Importer>`, so forgetting to register a new pack's chunk IS a compile error. TESTS: 11 new (75 -> 86). The registry's ledger keys, gameNames and keyPrefixes are pinned to the exact strings transcribed out of the pack files BEFORE the refactor — the same discipline the pack-runtime pass used, because all three fail silently when changed (a changed ledger key orphans every row already written; a changed gameName splits a pack's history across two leaderboard tabs) — plus uniqueness across every key-ish field, PACK_BY_LEDGER round-tripping, PACK_WS_TYPES covering every pack in order, the emoji lookup for all six `games.pack` values, and a named regression test for each of the two bugs above. Adding a pack goes from about fifteen files to about six, and the six are the ones that genuinely differ. No schema change, no endpoint change, no payload change. Deploy: none.
- [x] One TV button per night (2026-07-28). There was no TV entry point on the event page at all: every TV view was reached from inside the thing it shows, on an address naming a PACK rather than the night (/tv/:bracketId, /smash/tv/:eventId, /mariokart/tv/:eventId, /marioparty/tv/:eventId, /pingpong/tv/:eventId, /beerio/tv/:code). Putting a game on the big screen meant knowing which pack was being played, opening that pack on a phone, finding its TV button and getting that url onto the TV — again, by hand, every time the crew switched games. NOW: one public route `/e/:eventId/tv` (same shape as /e/:id/recap), stable for the whole night, and one `📺 TV` button on the event page, labelled with what is on when exactly one thing is on (`📺 TV · Smash Bros`; "live now" when two are running, because then the answer depends on which was touched last and this page cannot know that without asking the resolver). Never disabled: the lobby is a legitimate thing to put on the screen before the night starts, and that is when the TV actually goes on. AUTO-FOLLOW, no host picker and no stored "now showing" pointer: `GET /api/tv/event/:eventId` (new apps/server/src/tv.ts, on the public tv mount) reads the four session packs, the bracket and the Beerio room in one Promise.all, drops anything completed, and returns the most recently TOUCHED as `now`, which the client renders as that pack's OWN TV view inside itself (standing rule 3 untouched: this is a router, not a generic TV mode). RENDERS THE CHILD, NEVER NAVIGATES TO IT — a TV must not accumulate history entries, and swapping in place is what makes the Mario Kart -> Smash switch happen with nobody touching the screen. Three supporting pieces, each a real gap: (1) `brackets.updatedAt`, touched on score and undo, so a bracket being scored all night beats a Ping Pong session started later and abandoned; (2) Beerio gets the completion marker the other four packs already had (`events.beerioCompletedAt`, set at /beerio-complete, cleared when a new room opens), so a finished room ages out instead of staying "open" forever, and a crew running a SECOND tournament on the same code comes back on its own once beerio_sessions.updatedAt passes the stamp — no time window, no guessing at the vendored engine's opaque blob; (3) `event_session_changed` was broadcast from exactly ONE place in the whole server (beerio-gn's room-open route), which is why the event page never noticed a host starting Smash — it now fires from every pack's start and complete (one change in pack-runtime, so all four inherit it) and from bracket create, score and undo. `EventDetail` also gained the four session packs, so every pack tile shows "live now" like Beerio and the Tournament always could. The five TV components each gained an optional id prop falling back to the route param, so they work embedded and standalone; the six original routes stay forever for deep links, bookmarks and any QR already in the wild, and only the in-app buttons were repointed. TESTS: 13 new (55 -> 68) on the pure resolution rule, which is the half that breaks silently — nothing live, one live pack, two live (newest wins, array order irrelevant), a completed session never winning even when it is the most recent thing, Beerio stamped and not newer (including exactly-equal), Beerio newer than its stamp, a code with no room row, and identical timestamps resolving to the DECLARED tiebreak order rather than to row order, because an unstable answer makes the TV flicker between two packs and read as broken. No Drizzle stub. VERIFIED IN A REAL BROWSER against the built bundle plus a stub API, because the acceptance criteria are things nobody can check by reading: the lobby renders with names and a scannable QR when nothing is live; the Beerio board and the bracket board each render on the event TV route; flipping what is live swaps the child with the URL unchanged and NO new history entry; and the Beerio poll timer is genuinely gone after the swap (its request count frozen across 7s with a 3s poll, asserted only after first proving the poll was running, so the check means something). Schema deploy: db:push:ci applies both columns; fallback SQL in the decision log. Deploy: nothing else. FOLLOW-UP THE SAME DAY (James, after testing it on a real TV): the waiting screen now shows the NIGHT RECAP between games. It turned out to have two jobs, not one — it is on screen for the evening's first twenty minutes AND again between every game after that, and an empty "waiting for the host" is a wasted TV at exactly the moment a room looks up to see where they stand. So it splits on whether anything has been played: nothing yet keeps who's-in plus the join QR, and once there is a result it shows standings (leader crowned, wins, games, average finish) beside the latest results. It reads the SAME rollup the recap card uses — the recap route's inline aggregation was extracted to a pure rollupRecap(rows) plus an eventRecap(event, groupName) that both callers share — so the big screen and the card can never quote different numbers for the same night in the same room. The extra reads only run when nothing is live, which is exactly when the server is idle. 7 more tests (68 -> 75) on the rollup, since the ORDER of its players array is now load-bearing beyond the card: the TV reads index 0 as who is leading and puts a crown on it. Both lobby states were re-checked in the browser. No schema change, no new endpoint.
- [x] GIVE THE TABLE ROOM, AND GET THE CARDS OFF THE PLASTIC (2026-08-03, third pass of the day, against the approved render as a reference). Five items, and the first is a correctness one. **THE FIT CHECK WAS MEASURING LAYOUT WHILE A FIXED OVERLAY ATE THE RESULT.** The rail is `position: fixed` at z-index 3 over every route, television included, so it CANNOT move an element and every fit number was unchanged the day it shipped, which was true and beside the point: it paints on top of whatever the layout put at the edge. `scripts/tv-fit.mjs` is now in the repo rather than in a scratchpad and asks two separate questions, does the layout fit and is anything it placed still covered, reading the rail's width off the live document so any future overlay is caught by the same check. **THE SPECIFIC CLAIM DID NOT REPRODUCE and the numbers are worth keeping:** the money board's back button clears the timber by 43px at four seats, and every fitting case clears by 43 to 210. The one control the rail genuinely covers is Ping Pong's at seven players, which is already 3px off the bottom before any rail exists and is already logged; the rail deepens it to 17. That case is exempted by name in the harness rather than the check being softened. **CONTENT NOW STANDS BACK FROM THE FRAME.** `--gn-shell-inset` is the rail plus 10px, folded into the shell's safe-area rule and into the signed-out Home screen's inline padding (which overrides that rule and was exactly the miss a test now catches). Arcade's value is `0px`, so every expression collapses to what it computed before. This is also what stops the last line of a page being sliced by the stitch: content ends inside the frame instead of under it. **THE CARDS CAME OFF THE PLASTIC.** Opaque walnut boxes on green felt read as holes cut in the table; `--gn-surf` under Tabletop is now `rgba(0,0,0,.5)`, a darkening of whatever it is laid on, so the weave and the lamp carry through and a card near the crown is genuinely lighter than one at the foot of a long page. **THE ALPHA IS MEASURED, NOT CHOSEN:** at the render's `.24` five text pairs fall under AA once the lamp is under the card; `.5` lands the card on the crown at `#112e1f`, within a level of the render's own, and every pair holds. **THE LAMP IS 70% STRONGER**, a 22-level lift of the felt's green against 13, bought by taking the headroom in `--gn-dim` (`#c6b99f` to `#d4c9b1`) rather than in the light, since the crown's brightness was only ever capped by whichever ink sits on it. The render's own 48-level lift is not shippable here at any ink this palette can afford. **AND THE WEAVE READS AS CLOTH RATHER THAN STUCCO** now that the 512px tile is painted at 220px on small screens; the TV keeps 900px, which exists because the lattice is what an eye finds across a room. **VERIFIED:** Arcade IDENTICAL across 14284 values again; all nine TV cases unmoved; thirteen negative controls, including one that widens the rail until it eats the money board and confirms the new overlay gate fails. **THE ALPHA WAS THEN SET TO .24 BY JAMES** (raise .16), which is the render's own number, and it costs three ink lifts and a parity exemption. At .24 the card sits at `#1b412d` over the crown rather than `#112e1f`, far lighter than the walnut panel it replaced, so `--gn-place` (#a2957c to #baae99), `--gn-faint` (#8a7f68 to #998e79) and `--gn-p1` (#f07a58 to #fc9375, a shade warmer lacquer red) each came up by the least that clears its floor with the cloth on it. Every pair still clears AA. WHAT IT DOES COST IS PARITY, and that is now written out pair by pair in `theme-contrast.test.ts` rather than absorbed by a wider tolerance: eight accents on a card lose ground against Arcade's opaque panel (`--gn-ink` 14.45 to 10.06, `--gn-gold` 11.26 to 6.81, `--gn-p2` 9.97 to 5.77, `--gn-yes` 8.28 to 4.85, `--gn-act` 7.36 to 4.76). The exemption list is asserted to stay LIVE, so a line that stops drifting has to be deleted rather than left to hide a future regression, and a ninth pair cannot join it without somebody adding a line. Card content under Tabletop is flatter than under Arcade; that is the accepted trade for the material. **ONE HONEST COST, NAMED:** the rendered-pixel variance check reads 2.86 where it read 4.04, because a per-pixel deviation necessarily falls when a tile is painted smaller, and the script now says so in its own comment so nobody "improves" the number by making the grain coarse again.
- [x] THE TABLE LOOKS LIKE THE TABLE (2026-08-03, same day, follow-up to the felt and rail). The architecture from that session was right and is untouched; the SCREEN did not look like the render James approved, and closing that gap was a materials pass. **THE ONE FINDING THAT EXPLAINS MOST OF IT IS ARITHMETIC.** `overlay` against a base darker than 50% resolves to `2 x base x texture`. The tint pointed at `--gn-bg` (`#15110b`, channels .082/.067/.043), so the tile's entire 102-to-139 range collapsed to a swing of **6, 5 and 3 levels out of 255** on screen. The weave was not subtle, it was absent, and no better tile would have fixed it: the surface was too dark for a multiply to have anything to work with. **THREE CHANGES FELL OUT OF THAT.** (1) THE SHELL HAS ITS OWN FELT, `--gn-felt: #16402c`, a deep green rather than the page's walnut, which stays where it belongs as the rail and the card stock. (2) THERE IS A LAMP: the render's crown of light generalised into `--gn-lamp-geom` / `--gn-lamp-fade`, which Ping Pong's table now reads instead of its private copy. It does double duty, since it is both most of the depth and what lifts the middle of the screen enough for the weave to appear. (3) THE TILE'S AMPLITUDE WENT UP, standard deviation 4.6 to 8.1, because the old bound was blunt: it capped the tile so that no pair moved, INCLUDING pairs on cards and pack roots that the cloth never touches (`.gn-app::before` is z-index 0 and content sits at 1). The gate is now the AA floor on the two surfaces the cloth genuinely covers, and the binding case is `--gn-dim` on the lit crown at 5.16, which is also what caps how bright the lamp may be. **THE RAIL GOT ITS FOUR MATERIAL FIXES**, all from the render: a grain stripe over the timber (one smooth gradient is moulded plastic), a DASHED brass stitch set 4px in rather than a solid flush hairline (this detail does more work than the timber does), 16px corners with the cloth at 4px inside them, and a 44px well plus a 2px lip so the felt sits UNDER the rail rather than beside it. **THE CHECK THAT WOULD HAVE CAUGHT THIS NOW EXISTS**, and it is the point of the session as much as the pixels are: `scripts/felt-variance.mjs` screenshots the built app, samples a rectangle of empty surface and asserts the standard deviation of its pixels clears a floor. The sweep was green throughout the flat build because every computed value matched its specification exactly, and **a sweep cannot catch a correct implementation of a wrong spec**. Measured 0.42 before and 4.04 after. **ITS FLOOR WAS SET FROM THE NEGATIVE CONTROL, NOT GUESSED:** at 1.2 the deliberately-broken build still measured 1.36 and still passed, so the floor is 2.5, between the two. **VERIFIED:** Arcade IDENTICAL across 14284 values (the eight-layer rail face and the outline-based stitch are held in token LISTS precisely so Arcade computes the four `none` layers and the `currentcolor` outline it computed before); all nine TV cases unmoved in both themes; eleven negative controls on the new assertions; an Arcade session still fetches no image at all.
- [x] TABLETOP IS A TABLE: FELT TEXTURE AND A RAIL (2026-08-03). James on the shipped Tabletop: it still does not feel like felt, and the cross-hatch is not cutting it. **THE DIAGNOSIS WAS NOT THE WEAVE, IT WAS THE EDGE.** A poker table reads as a table because the felt STOPS somewhere; stage 3's two crossed gradients were the right idea at the wrong scale, a pattern rather than a material, and no amount of tuning a pattern produces cloth. Shown a rail and no rail side by side, James chose the rail definitively. **THE IMAGE-ASSET RULING FROM STAGE 3 IS OVERTURNED, by James:** felt is a grey texture plus a colour, so ONE greyscale tile serves every pack and every future theme that wants cloth (see DECISION LOG). The tile is generated by `scripts/generate-felt-tile.mjs` — 512px, three layers (slow unevenness, a directional nap, ~13k fibre strands each with an off-axis shadow), seamless BY CONSTRUCTION rather than by mirroring: every sinusoid has an integer period across the tile and every strand is plotted modulo the width, so there is no seam to hide and no mirrored motif to spot. 32.9KB, greyscale, tinted at the use site by `--gn-texture-tint` and folded into the page with `background-blend-mode: overlay`, which is why grey works at all: mid-grey is overlay's identity value, so the tile carries only the deviation and every surface keeps its own colour. **THE FAILURE MODE IS REPETITION, NOT SEAMS**, so the amplitude is asymmetric (0.018 up against 0.075 down) — dark fibre reads as cloth, bright fibre reads as a repeating motif. **THE GENERATOR REFUSES TO WRITE A TILE THAT COSTS TOO MUCH CONTRAST**: it re-decodes its own encoded WebP and measures seven guarded pairs through the actual blend, and fails if any pair loses more than 4% of its ratio or crosses its floor. Worst measured cost is 3.9% (`--pp-accent` on `--pp-felt`, 6.06 to 5.82). That guard earned its keep twice: an absolute contrast bound was the wrong metric entirely and had to become relative-plus-floors, and the guard originally measured the pre-encode canvas while lossy WebP was ringing the greys 136 to 147, hiding a real 6.6% cost. **THE RAIL** is `<div class="gn-rail">` in `index.html` beside `#root`, `position: fixed`, `pointer-events: none`, pinned inside `env(safe-area-inset-*)`: four timber gradients as one background, an inset stitch line and a contact shadow. Under Arcade every one of its seven tokens is inert (`--gn-rail-w: 0px`, timber `none`, colours `transparent`) because an arcade cabinet has no rail. **THE BIGGEST RISK WAS THE VERTICAL BUDGET AND IT MEASURED AT ZERO:** all nine TV cases (money board at 4/6/8/12 seats and 12+5 modifiers, Ping Pong at 6 and 7, Casino Run at 6 and 12) are byte-identical before and after in both themes, because a fixed frame costs no layout; the smallest back-button clearance at 1080p is 57px against 14px of timber, so no rail covers a control. **VERIFIED:** Arcade sweep 0 changed values (the sweep first gained `background-size`, `-repeat`, `-position`, `-blend-mode` and `mix-blend-mode`, and those were negative-controlled by mutating the tile size and blend and watching both the route and rule passes report it); an Arcade session fetches NO image asset at all and still exactly three faces, Tabletop fetches the felt and nothing else, measured over CDP; nine negative controls on the new tests, each confirmed to turn the suite red. **LOGGED, NOT FIXED: the felt does not reach the packs**, and that is the mechanism's real boundary rather than a bug in this work (see BUGS).
- [x] TABLETOP THEME, STAGE 4 OF 4, THE PILOT: PING PONG IS A TABLE (2026-08-02). Two commits. **FIRST, the display face across the packs:** stage 3 put the face behind `--gn-font-display` and left 26 hardcoded `"Luckiest Guy"` references in the pack stylesheets, so a Tabletop PACK screen still showed the arcade face on every pack. 25 are now `var(--gn-font-display)`; Beerio Kart keeps its literal because it is the vendored replica and must NOT follow the theme, commented in the file so it does not read as a miss. Not quite Arcade-identical, and the sweep is why that is known rather than assumed: the references carried TWO fallback stacks, so sixteen sites gained `system-ui` in their chain. Rendered face is unchanged; the chain only decides what happens if the webfont fails, where system-ui beats the browser's cursive default. **SECOND, the pilot.** Blackjack was rejected on purpose: 36 lines, already fully on `--cg-*`, so converting it would have proved nothing, and a mechanism that only works on the pack that needed no work is not a mechanism. Ping Pong was the honest shape (129 lines, 34 hex, 12 rgb, 15 token reads). Its Tabletop object is a real table tennis table: dark blue-green painted top, wooden rail, painted white centre line, and the brand orange surviving as the rubber. **THE FELT WAS TUNED, NOT PICKED:** the first candidate put the rubber at 4.72 against it, barely over AA where Arcade reads 5.90, so it was darkened until the pair came back to parity. **THE SWEEP NEEDED TWO FIXES BEFORE IT COULD JUDGE ANY OF THIS**, and both were the same shape as the trap stage 3 hit: it ran the rule pass only on `/`, where pack CSS is not loaded at all because it rides a lazy chunk, and its probe hung off `<html>`, where `--pp-*` declared on `.pp-root` does not exist. Together those meant "Arcade is identical" would have been true and almost entirely vacuous for the one file the session changed. The rule pass now visits one route per stylesheet and the probe carries every token block's declarations inline: 523 declarations across 11 stylesheets, up from 213 on index.css alone. Arcade IDENTICAL across 10624 values. **LOGGED, NOT FIXED:** Ping Pong's TV does not fit 1080p past six players (see BUGS).
- [x] TABLETOP THEME, STAGE 3 OF 4: THE SHELL'S MATERIAL (2026-08-02). James on the shipped Tabletop: it felt like Arcade with a different scheme. He was right, and it was not a defect in stage 2. Stage 2's brief said "this is a palette" and stage 2 delivered exactly a palette, correctly, including a note that removing the scanline was structural and therefore out of scope. **THE SCOPE WAS THE MISTAKE, NOT THE WORK**: a theme is not only its colours, and swapping sixteen hex values was never going to make a screen feel like a table. Going straight to the packs would have compounded it, because a pack in felt sitting inside a shell that still rasters and glows reads as a felt patch on an arcade cabinet. **WHAT WAS STILL ARCADE, all of it structure rather than colour:** a CRT raster on `.gn-app::before` and `.gn-tv::before` (stage 2 had warmed it to cream, so Tabletop was a CRT with cream scanlines), neon glow behind every heading, hard-offset zero-blur bevels that are moulded plastic, ten `border-radius:999px` lozenges, and Luckiest Guy carrying the wordmark on every screen. **THE MECHANISM IS THE POINT: structure became TOKENS, exactly as colour did in stage 1.** Twenty-one of them (listed in the DECISION LOG), each named for its JOB so that `--gn-glow-title: none` is a sensible thing to write. The alternative was a pile of `:root[data-theme="tabletop"] .gn-title { }` overrides, which is how the hardcoded-colour problem started: a rule a theme has to remember to override is a rule the next theme forgets. **TABLETOP'S MATERIAL:** felt as two crossed 45-degree gradients (deliberately not an SVG feTurbulence, which is a real GPU cost on a television left open all night, and not a tiling image, which is another request on a deliberately trimmed bundle), opacity dropped .05 to .03 because two crossing layers ink ~55% of the pixels where one inks 33%; a soft warm contact shadow plus a light top edge instead of a moulding lip; no glow anywhere; cards 16px to 5px and tiles 12px to 4px, with the pill kept where the thing IS a pill. **TYPE:** Bevan, a woodtype slab in the register of a pub sign, vendored as two latin subsets (39KB) beside the existing faces. Fredoka was the free option and was rejected as another rounded geometric in the same cartoon family as Luckiest Guy. It is named in exactly ONE place, the `--gn-font-display` token inside the Tabletop block, so an Arcade session never requests it; measured over CDP, each theme now fetches exactly three faces and swaps Luckiest Guy for Bevan, so neither theme pays for the other. **VERIFICATION:** the sweep was extended FIRST, with border-radius, font-family and opacity added and pseudo-element capture for the texture overlays, and the Arcade baseline was taken against `main` BEFORE any work; a sweep extended afterwards can only tell you the two trees you already changed agree with each other. 8390 computed values, Arcade IDENTICAL. It earned its keep immediately: the first cut of `--gn-bevel-btn` read a `--gn-btn-sh` that each accent button set locally, which is invalid because a custom property containing a var() resolves where it is DECLARED, and it silently painted every accent button's lip transparent.
- [x] TABLETOP THEME, STAGE 2 OF 3: the Tabletop palette (2026-08-02). Warm dark, James's call: felt, brass and low pub light. **The shell STAYS DARK and that is the load-bearing half of the direction**, not a preference: a light Tabletop would have forced a second treatment of all ten pack palettes and turned stage 3 from a design pass into a rebuild, whereas keeping the shell dark means every pack's identity hue goes on working exactly as it does today. The move is Arcade's cool deep plum to warm walnut: same depth, different temperature, which is a small enough shift that a bad token reads as WRONG rather than merely different. **ONE `:root[data-theme="tabletop"]` BLOCK, 44 of the 52 tokens.** `:root[...]` rather than a bare attribute selector because a bare one has the SAME specificity as `:root` and would win only by sitting lower in the file, so the next person to reorder would silently un-theme the app. **THE EIGHT IT DOES NOT DECLARE ARE THE POINT**: the cabinet INK tokens are inherited, which is the identity-versus-treatment split from the DECISION LOG written as code. Smash is red under every theme, so its ink is declared once; what Tabletop overrides is the treatment, the edge and the backdrop its gradient falls from, re-laid over walnut by holding each cabinet's perceptual lift over its own background constant, which kept every cabinet name within 0.3 of its Arcade contrast. **MEASURED, NOT EYEBALLED:** `apps/server/tests/theme-contrast.test.ts` asserts WCAG AA on 19 pairs for BOTH themes, asserts Tabletop stays within 2.0 of Arcade pair for pair, asserts no token is accidentally inherited, and asserts every theme has a pre-paint entry in index.html. All five of its failure modes were negative-controlled. **THE `var()` FALLBACK HUNT FOUND A LIVE ONE**, not just the latent casino ones: `MemberPage.tsx` read `var(--gn-teal, #2dd4bf)` and `var(--gn-coral, #ff5a5f)`, and there has never been a `--gn-teal` or `--gn-coral`, so the whole rivalry UI had been painting the literals since it shipped and would never have followed a theme. A fallback is the one place a hardcoded colour hides from a sweep that reads property values, because the property value IS a var(). **PROVED:** the sweep grew a `--same` mode, because stage 2's question is the inverse of stage 1's, and under Tabletop the only shell colours that did not move are the six cabinet inks that were designed not to. Arcade itself moved in exactly two ways, both intended and both verified against the pre-palette tree.
- [x] TABLETOP THEME, STAGE 1 OF 3: Arcade is actually swappable now (2026-08-02). Deliberately adds NO new colours: at the end of it the app looks exactly as it did and the switcher exists with one theme in it. Splitting "make it swappable" from "add the second palette" is the whole point, because the risk in a theming pass is not the new theme, it is silently wrecking the one already shipped, and a regression in the first half is only provable against a fixed target if nothing is meant to move. **WHAT WAS ACTUALLY WRONG:** the theming decision was made on 2026-07-15 and the token block was written, and then the file drifted away from it. `index.css` had 54 hex values and 50 rgba() literals OUTSIDE `:root`, several of them a token spelled out by hand (`#241a30` appeared eight times and is exactly `--gn-surf`; `rgba(255,207,63,.16)` is `--gn-gold` at 16%, written out in five places). None of those follow a theme swap, so setting a second theme would have half-changed the screen: backgrounds move, and the borders, pills and chips sitting on them do not. Same silent-registration shape as the `ping_pong` ledger typo and the missing `.bj-root` safe-area entry. **THE SWEEP:** every literal now resolves to a `var()`, or to `color-mix(in srgb, var(--gn-x) N%, transparent)` where it was a token at an alpha, or to a NEW token named for what it is FOR rather than what colour it is (`--gn-shadow-depth`, not `--gn-dark-purple`: a token named after its colour cannot survive a light theme). 35 new tokens, 26 of them the per-pack cabinet identity on Home. `--gn-yes`/`--gn-yes-ink` moved up out of a second `:root` block halfway down the file, because a theme has to be able to override the whole set by copying ONE block. **ENFORCED, NOT HOPED FOR:** `copy-rules.test.ts` now fails on any colour literal outside the token block, scoped to `index.css` (the pack stylesheets are stage 3 and would fail today). Negative-controlled by reintroducing one of each shape and confirming it fails with the right line numbers. **PROVED:** `scripts/theme-sweep.mjs` drives Chromium over CDP against the real built bundle and compares COMPUTED colours, not screenshots, because a screenshot diff on this app is noisy (webfonts, `.gn-pulse`, the skeleton shimmer) and would train whoever runs it to wave small differences through. 8255 resolved values across 33 routes, 137 classes and 251 rules: identical, with the only differences in the whole session being the four new elements of the switcher itself. **THE SWITCHER:** `useTheme.ts` plus a `.gn-tab` pill row on Home by the name and password fields, `data-theme` on `<html>`, persisted per device in localStorage under its OWN key (`gamenight.pref.theme`) and explicitly NOT through `cache.ts`, whose keys are namespaced by `__BUILD_ID__` and swept on boot: a preference that resets every deploy is worse than no preference. Applied before first paint by a fail-soft inline script in `index.html`, because correct state arriving after the first paint is still a flash, and a throw there is before React and takes the whole app down.
- [x] One implementation per idea on the client (2026-07-28, pre-pack cleanup phase 6, the LAST phase of the run). THREE live-sync implementations became one. BracketPage and TvPage each hand-rolled a connect/retry/visibility WebSocket instead of using the shared hook, and had already drifted: BracketPage skipped its own echo, TvPage did not. The same origin-skip filter was also written EIGHT times across the four pack pages and their four TV views, identical apart from one message-type string. useLiveUpdates.ts gained useLiveRefetch(types, field, id, refetch) plus two thin wrappers, usePackLive and useBracketLive, and eleven screens now share one implementation. The own-echo skip is now universal, which is a no-op on the read-only TV views rather than a behaviour change, because a screen that never writes never sees its own client id come back. useLiveUpdates itself stays for GroupPage and EventPage, which listen for several unrelated types and act differently on each (one navigates away when the event is deleted); Beerio's polling is correct for a vendored pack and was left alone. THE PACK-PAGE SHELL became one hook: new usePackSession<S> holds the ctx/session/loading/err/busy/reqSeq state, the refetch that Promise.alls context and session, call() with its optimistic update, snapshot rollback and newest-request-wins guard, and startSession() with the 409 confirm-and-replace dance. The four pages keep all of their own rendering, which is where packs must stay different. ONE MAP AND ONE CATALOGUE: FORMAT_LABEL existed three times (StatsPage, MyStatsPage, and recap.tsx as FORMAT_NAME) with the unit noun twice, now one src/formats.ts that all three read plus the three pack Start buttons; and the pack catalogue existed twice (quickGames in Home, eventGames() in EventPage), now one src/packs.ts with the destination, Beerio's live subline and the Tournament gating supplied by the caller. MEASURED: 599 lines deleted against 218 added across 18 files, with the four pack pages down 56-81 lines each. TWO REAL BUGS THE UNIFICATION EXPOSED, both in Mario Party, both fixed. (1) Mario Party was the only pack whose session start went through call() instead of a startSession(), so it never had the confirm-and-replace dance; and the SERVER matched, missing the `!req.body?.force` check the other three have. An in-progress Mario Party session could therefore never be replaced at all: the host got a raw 409 on the setup screen with no way forward, and the only escape was to finish or abandon the night. Both halves fixed, so standing rule 8 now behaves the same in all four packs. (2) One visible copy change, called out rather than slipped in: Smash's start button said "Start FFA" where every other surface, including the picker that launches it, says "Free-for-all". It now reads from the shared map like the rest. DECISION, contrary to this entry's own earlier note: usePackSession does NOT use the 8a cache. Phase 8a's rule that a pack's live session must never be served from cache wins, because a live pack screen is not just showing state, it is offering buttons that WRITE, and a cached roster means someone can tap a player who is no longer in the session. The pack pages are now the one place that still shows a loading state on a return visit, which is the correct trade. VERIFIED IN A BROWSER: the picker catalogue was read back out of the rendered DOM on both Home and the event page and compared tile by tile and format by format (identical, with Tournament correctly still gated on the event page), all 23 routes still mount, and the phase 8a cache checks still pass. Deploy: none, no schema change.

## SHIPPED — GAME PACKS
- [x] A CASINO IS THE DEFAULT BANK, ON EVERY CASH PACK (2026-08-22, James's call; written up 2026-08-23 by the reconcile that found it missing). **TWO PLACES DECIDED THIS AND BOTH HAD TO MOVE**, which is the part that was not obvious from the request. `CasinoSetup`'s `useState<CashBank>("player")` is the one a host sees, and `casino-runtime.ts` separately defaulted `req.body?.bank === "casino" ? "casino" : "player"`, so ANY body that did not say "casino" opened a player-banked table. The client sends `bank` on every start, so in practice the server's fallback only decided for an older installed PWA on a cached bundle — invisible until somebody opens a table from a stale app and finds a banker they did not pick. Both now default to a casino and the server's test is inverted: only the literal "player" opts in.
  **THE DIRECTION IS THE SAFER ONE, which is the argument even before anybody's preference comes into it.** A PLAYER-banked table DERIVES the banker's net as the exact inverse of everyone else's, so a host who never touched the control would have had one person's money worked out for them rather than counted. A casino-banked table derives nobody: every net stands on its own. Same argument `stakes` already makes for defaulting to real money.
  **ONE THING THE FLIP WOULD HAVE BROKEN, caught before shipping it.** `minPlayers` was `bank === "player" ? 2 : 1`, correct for the two banks that existed when it was written and WRONG for poker's `table`: nobody banks there and two people are still needed to have a game, so with the default flipped a poker table would have been openable with a SINGLE SEAT on the default path. It is now `effBank === "casino" ? 1 : 2`, which states the real rule: only a table against a real house can legitimately have one player.
  **AND POKER STOPS SHOWING A CONTROL THAT DOES NOTHING.** `poker.ts` pins `fixedBank: "table"` and ignores `bank` on the request entirely, but the shared setup screen rendered "Who is banking? / One of us / A casino" anyway and threw the answer away. `CasinoSetup` now takes an optional `fixedBank` and PokerPage passes `"table"`. Verified on the real built bundle over CDP: picker present and reading "A casino" on blackjack, roulette and craps; no banking card at all on poker. Five new tests, because this path had none.
- [x] A VOTER CAN CHANGE THEIR NICKNAME, ALL NIGHT (2026-08-19). The crowd's name box rendered only while a voter had NO name, so the first thing anybody typed was permanent for the night: a typo, a phone handed to someone else, or a name picked before the joke landed were all stuck. **THE IDENTITY WAS NEVER THE PROBLEM AND THAT IS THE FINDING.** Predictions are keyed by `sid`, a per-device id in localStorage, and the nickname is one field on the entry beside the picks. Nothing anywhere keys on the name: the leaderboard rows key on `id`, the "(you)" marker compares `r.id===sid`, and the server merges under `:sid` with its lock enforcement intact. So renaming has never been able to move a row, split a score or orphan a pick. What was missing was the affordance, not the safety.
  **WHAT LANDED:** "Predicting as <name>" with an Edit name button once you have joined, opening the same input pre-filled, with Save, Cancel, Enter to save and Escape to abandon. The two rules that keep an editable label from becoming an identity live in `specname.ts` with twelve tests: `cleanSpecName` refuses a blank or whitespace-only name (a voter holding backspace would show as "Mystery fan" and, worse, look to THEMSELVES like somebody else, which is the confusion the sid exists to prevent) and the Save button is DISABLED rather than silently ignoring the tap; `renameInPreds` changes the name and carries the picks across by reference, so the optimistic local echo cannot drop a vote while appearing to touch only a string.
  **TWO SMALLER THINGS FIXED ON THE WAY.** The name draft was `useState(myName)`, an initial value only, so a voter whose name arrived later (the predictions poll adopts the name this device already had on the room, which is what makes a returning voter keep their identity) would open the editor on an empty box and their edit would look like a fresh join. And the rename now echoes onto the local board immediately instead of waiting up to 4s for the next poll.
  **VERIFIED BY DRIVING THE REAL SCREEN**, because the invariant is behavioural rather than arithmetic: join, cast a pick, rename, and the server entry is under the SAME sid with `picks` byte-identical and non-empty, the board shows one row rather than two, the tick stays on the pick, and a blank name leaves Save disabled. No server change: the endpoint was already keyed correctly.
- [x] TWO HOST DEVICES ON ONE BEERIO ROOM (2026-08-19). Reported as "the main Beerio page doesn't update without a refresh" while TV mode updated fine. The setup was a laptop parked on the editable screen and a phone on the SAME ACCOUNT driving the night: adding players and picking winners on the phone never reached the laptop. **THE HOST PAGE WAS WRITE-ONLY.** It pushed its local state to the room on every change (debounced 600ms) and never read the room back, so the laptop was not merely stale, its own next push would overwrite the phone's work. TV mode was unaffected because a TV is a pure reader that already polls.
  **IT WAS A HALF-BUILT FEATURE, NOT A MISSING ONE, and that is the part worth remembering.** `BeerioApp.tsx` already adopts the room's state for a host, with the reason written on it: "Host rejoining an open room: adopt its state so the auto-sync effect can't overwrite the live night with this device's stale copy". That adopt is correct and it only ever ran ONCE, when the GameNight event context resolved on mount. **Refreshing the page was a person re-running it by hand**, which is exactly why the refresh worked and why the bug read as a rendering fault rather than a sync one. It now runs on the 3s poll the rest of the pack already uses, plus on `visibilitychange`, which is the half the TV has always had and this screen never did.
  **THE COMPARISON IS THE WHOLE DESIGN, and it lives in `roomsync.ts`, a module of ours beside `band.ts`, `racer.ts` and `crowd.ts`.** A device tracks the state it last AGREED with the room on, whether it pushed it or read it. It pushes only when its own state differs from that, and adopts only when the room's differs from that and no push of its own is in flight. Adopting sets local state EQUAL to remote, so the push side then finds nothing to say: **the exchange terminates instead of two devices echoing each other's writes forever**, which is the failure mode a naive "poll and apply" produces. The in-flight guard is what stops a half-typed name being wiped inside the 600ms debounce window.
  **TWO TRAPS THAT WOULD EACH HAVE CAUSED AN INFINITE WRITE LOOP.** (1) The room is a Postgres `jsonb` column and jsonb does not preserve key order, it NORMALISES, so the bytes a device PUTs are not the bytes it reads back and a plain `JSON.stringify` compare reports a change on every poll of a room nobody has touched. Hence `stableJson`, which sorts keys at every depth and deliberately does NOT sort arrays, since `names` and `colors` are keyed by seat. (2) `hofCode` is excluded from the comparison: it is a per-device localStorage value that the host's adopt has never applied, and a field one device sends while the other never adopts it can never be resolved.
  **VERIFIED BY SIMULATING BOTH DEVICES**, with the stub reordering jsonb keys on every write the way Postgres would: the laptop follows a rename AND a recorded winner with no reload, causes ZERO writes by adopting, and the room still holds the phone's state afterwards rather than the laptop's. Spectator and TV paths re-checked unchanged. Files: `apps/web/src/beerio/roomsync.ts` (new), `apps/server/tests/beerio-roomsync.test.ts` (new), `apps/web/src/beerio/BeerioApp.tsx` (wiring only). No server change: `GET /api/sessions/:code` already returned what was needed.
- [x] THE BEERIO CROWD BAR, READABLE FROM THE COUCH (2026-08-19). From a photo of the live TV: the percentage inside each prediction segment could not be read across a room, for three separate reasons, and only the first is the one that gets noticed. **SIZE** was `.beerio-tvpb__pct` at `calc(var(--bt-pb-bar) * .64)`, roughly 17px on a 1080p TV at the roomy band. **CONTRAST** is the one that decided the design and is written up in the DECISION LOG with its numbers. **MEANING** is the quiet one: at one vote every segment reads 100%, and the photographed screen had four bars all saying 100% on 1 vote each, which is the bar restated rather than anything new.
  **THE BAR CARRIES NO TEXT NOW, AND THAT IS FORCED RATHER THAN PREFERRED.** It is pure shape, which is the one job it was doing well from the far side of a room. The signal moved UP to the "Crowd says" label row, which sits on `--foam` at 5.83:1 and was already legible in the photo. Two states, because the old copy was right for one of them: a crowd that agrees still reads `{n} votes`, and only a genuine split gets `{label} {pct}% · {label} {pct}%` in descending share.
  **THE ROW CAN NEVER WRAP, and that is a fit rule wearing typography's clothes.** The Grand Prix call site passes one option per racer and seats twelve, with names long enough that `tv-fit.mjs` uses them as its worst case. A wrap makes the row taller and a taller row on a screen that fits 1080p exactly is a fit regression. Enforced twice on purpose: `crowdSplit` caps at three shares with `+{n} more`, and `.beerio-tvpb__say` carries `nowrap` plus an ellipsis, with `min-width: 0` doing the actual work, since a flex child will not shrink below its content without it.
  **PERCENTAGES ARE ROUNDED INDEPENDENTLY AND DELIBERATELY NOT RECONCILED TO 100**, with both directions pinned by tests: an even three-way split prints 33/33/33 and sums to 99, and 4/1/1 prints 67/17/17 and sums to 101. Largest-remainder would print 34/33/33 and show a room two identical vote counts as two different numbers. The sum stopped being an invariant the moment the cap existed anyway.
  **THE ROW'S SWATCHES ARE PART OF THE FEATURE, NOT TRIM, and they were missed on the first pass.** The row is sorted by SHARE and the bar is drawn in BOARD order, so a lopsided card lists the leader first and paints the trailer's colour on the left. While the percentage sat inside the segment that could not go wrong, because the number was on the fill it described; taking the text out of the bar cut the tie and left a name with no way to say which colour it meant. **FOUND BY RENDERING THE SCREEN, not by reading the code**, and not by the fit harness either: its one split card is 1-1, where a stable sort keeps board order and the two happen to agree. Each share now carries its racer's colour as a dot, sized in `em` so it follows `--bt-pb-l` at every band without a metric moving.
  **NO BAND METRIC MOVED.** `--bt-pb-l`, `--bt-pb-bar` and `--bt-pb-pad` are byte-identical at all four bands, and the diff was checked for it rather than the claim being asserted. `tv-fit.mjs` was RE-RUN and not extended, because it already covers this exactly: its two spectators disagree on `M:W1M0` only, so one card is a genuine 1-1 split and every other is unanimous 2-0, which puts both new label-row states on screen in a single run. PASS, exit 0, every Beerio case fits with the back button clear by ~1000px. `theme-sweep.mjs` compared IDENTICAL across 20842 resolved colour values, which confirms nothing OUTSIDE Beerio moved and is all it can confirm: line 322 skips every element inside `.beerio-root`.
- [x] POKER, CASH GAME (2026-08-17). The fourth pack on the shared cash engine, and it needed ONE genuinely new piece of engine work: a third `CashBank`. **"casino" and "player" are both wrong for poker.** A casino table checks nothing, because the money went to a building; a poker table is zero-sum and a night that does not add up is the most useful thing this app can say. A player-banked table DERIVES somebody, because the banker is the other side of every hand; on a poker table nobody is, so inverting one net would invent a number and hide the disagreement worth reporting. So `"table"`: no banker, nobody derived, checked once every seat has a net, delta the sum of them. The trigger is what differs from a banked table, not the arithmetic: a banked table checks the banker's own count once the banker has counted, a poker table checks the room against zero once EVERYBODY has, and a partially counted table is unfinished rather than unbalanced.
  **THE BALANCE CHECK IS THE FEATURE AND THE SCREENS ARE BUILT AROUND IT**, above the money board on the phone and in the TV's hero slot, because the point of this pack is ending the "we are forty dollars short" argument while everyone is still in the room, not the standings. The standings are free anyway: placement comes from net rank like every cash pack, so the crew leaderboard, rivalries, form and streaks all work with NO new aggregation and NO money column on `match_participants`. Money rides `meta`, which is what `meta` is for.
  **WHO PAYS WHOM, AND IT COST NO NEW INPUT.** `settleTransfers` derives the payment vector from the signed nets, which were already derived from the buy-in, the rebuys and the cash-out the pack has to collect anyway. It returns NULL unless the table balances, because a list built on a wrong number is the app inventing a debt between friends. Ties break on playerId on BOTH sides of the ledger, so two phones over live sync cannot show two different sets of debts for one table. At most n-1 payments, greedy, and the comment says outright it is not minimal (that is subset-sum) so nobody files the fourth payment as a bug.
  **THE GUEST TENSION, AND IT IS TESTED IN BOTH DIRECTIONS.** Every other pack drops guests at the ledger harmlessly. Here, dropping a guest's buy-in from the SETTLEMENT would make delta non-zero and fire the warning on a table that is actually correct. The settlement counts every seat and only the ledger WRITE skips guests; there is a test for the correct table and a negative control that removes the guest and watches it break, because the silent version of this bug reports every guest night as broken.
  **THE PROFIT SURFACE COST NOTHING, which is what putting placement in the ledger and money in `meta` was for.** Poker's lifetime panel is the same `CasinoPanel` the other three cash packs use, reading the same `/api/groups/:id/:pack-stats` endpoint, which is `aggregateCashNights` split by stakes and sorted by `compareCashLifetime`. No new aggregation, no money column on `match_participants`, and `stats.ts` was not opened. Its extras are the variant somebody plays most and how much of the dealing they have done, both out of meta bags the pack already writes.
  **VARIANTS ON THE TITLE-NIGHT PATTERN, NOT THE MODIFIER DECK.** Hold'em versus Omaha is not a house rule laid on top of a game, it is which game is being played, so it rides `nowPlaying` plus a log the way Board Game's titles do. Dealer's choice is the pack's repeatable interaction (standing rule 9) instead of a hand tracker: no hand-to-hand recording, buy-ins and final stacks only. **IDENTITY IS OXBLOOD**, the one card-room colour the casino group had not claimed (blackjack is near-black baize, roulette deep green, craps navy, Casino Run violet), and it carries its own cabinet ink rather than reusing --gn-gold so the two casino tiles are told apart by name colour as well as by edge. **NO SCHEMA CHANGE**; deploy is the push.
- [x] MARIO KART PAIRS: TWO PLAYERS, ONE KART, ACROSS ALL FOUR FORMATS (2026-08-16). Double Dash puts two people in one kart, so this pack is now side-shaped all the way through: a race is a tapped order of KARTS, a Best Of set is between two KARTS, and the King of the Hill throne is held by a KART that holds it or loses it together. A solo night is karts of one, which is not a special case in the code and is not one in the ledger either, because `sideIdFor` writes null whenever every side has one member. **NOT Team VS**: nothing here computes a placement from summed points, and the 2026-08-05 decision on that stands untouched.
  **THE FORK, AND `smash.ts` WAS NEVER OPENED.** `MkSessionState` keeps its `Omit` for everything genuinely shared and now drops two more fields beside Smashdown's three: `games`, whose lines gain a `side`, and `koth`, which is keyed on kart ids. Mario Kart declares its own. Putting sides into `SmashSessionState` would have made Smash 2v2 free and would have dragged Smashdown's burn board into a decision nobody has taken, and changing what `kothAdvance`'s ids MEAN fails silently: nothing errors when a throne is held by somebody who never played. Ping Pong answered the same question the same way on 2026-08-05. Smash 2v2, when it comes, now has TWO worked examples to copy rather than a shape nobody validated against it.
  **THE EXTRACTION THIS EARNED:** `packages/shared/src/sidelog.ts`, the side-arrangement log (`{ fromIdx, sides }`, `currentSides`, `sidesAtIdx`, `reshuffle` with its replace-rather-than-stack rule, `truncateSideLog` for undoing back past a reshuffle). Out of Ping Pong under the same rule the money board and the title-night layer came out under: one example is a pack, two is a layer. Ping Pong converted in the same commit and the conversion is PROVED behaviour-free, because the fixtures were captured off the unmodified engine FIRST and still pass unedited.
  **FIXTURES BEFORE PRODUCTION CODE, AND THEY EARNED IT AGAIN.** Commit 1 was fixtures only, captured by running the shipped engine across all four formats. The first hand-written draft of the Grand Prix cup had four wrong totals in it, which is the argument for the discipline in one line. The solo equivalence is pinned in three places: the engine fixtures, the ledger rows (`side` NULL on a solo night), and the KOTH ladder reproduced step for step against the sequence `kothAdvance` produced before any of this existed.
  **AUTO-APPLY: Double Dash plus exactly four players opens in pairs**, and nothing else does. Three guards, each with its own named test. **UNEVEN KARTS ARE SUPPORTED**, 2v1 and friends, because a three-player Double Dash night is a real thing a crew does: the solo racer gets a REAL kart id rather than null (another kart holds two, so there IS team structure), placements are 1,1,2 or 1,2,2, and the losing solo is second of three rows and never LAST. See DECISION LOG for both.
  **`normalizeMkState`** through the pack runtime's existing `normalize` hook, so a night live when this deploys keeps racing: one kart per racer, `side: null` on legacy race lines, the throne and every series mapped from player ids onto the karts holding those players.
  **STATS**, in `StatsPage.tsx`: this pack has had a leaderboard tab since it shipped and never a panel. It has one now, split solo from shared karts, DERIVED from `side IS NOT NULL` exactly as Ping Pong's doubles split is. No new format string, no new `matches.label` value, and both halves plus the total are printed so a reader can add them up.
  Files: `sidelog.ts` (new), `mariokart.ts` and `pingpong.ts` in shared, `mariokart.ts` on the server, `MarioKartPage.tsx`, `MarioKartTvPage.tsx`, `StatsPage.tsx`, `scripts/tv-fit.mjs`. 767 tests to 890, 0 fail and 0 todo. No schema change: `match_participants.side` shipped 2026-08-02, so the deploy was the push.
- [x] SOCIAL DEDUCTION, in two parts, and the pack SHIPS (part A and part B both 2026-08-10). The first pack in this app whose session state contains a SECRET, and the only one with a TV that has a secrecy constraint. **THE FINDING PART A EXISTED FOR:** every other pack's state is public by nature (fighters, scores, standings, money), so `createPackRuntime`'s `sessionView(eventId, preloaded)` takes NO VIEWER ARGUMENT and `viewOf` builds the payload by spreading the whole state. Standing rule 2 says members join the HOST'S live session, so on the plumbing as it stood, a role in session state reaches every player at the table the moment they open the page. Nothing errors. The game is simply over, and nothing anywhere reports it. **THE FIX IS A SEPARATE STORE, NOT A FILTER** (DECISION LOG): roles live in `apps/server/src/deduction-secret.ts`, a second `game_sessions` row under a pack value no registry entry claims, so the event TV resolver and the event detail payload both DROP it (they resolve through `PACK_BY_LEDGER`, and a miss is discarded). It is reached by exactly two routes: a per-viewer one returning only the caller's own role, and a host-gated one returning the whole deal. Both authed, both `no-store`, both gated SERVER-SIDE.
  **PART A** shipped `packages/shared/src/deduction.ts` (the faction model with third-party solo roles, catalogues for the six titles, the deal, the pure result path on `placementsFromRankedSides`), the pack router, the page on `usePackSession`, the registry entry and guest linking, and deliberately no tile and no TV.
  **PART B** shipped the LIVE MODERATOR BOARD (phase, day count, alive/dead, reveal on death), the TV, and every registration step. The board is OPT-IN AND OFF BY DEFAULT and flips mid-session in both directions: turning it on partway through opens at Night 1 with everybody alive rather than inventing a history the app did not watch, and turning it off discards nothing already recorded, because each game bakes its outcomes onto its own lines when it is recorded. `survived` and `votedOutFirst` stay ABSENT rather than zero for any game played with the board off, with deliberately no box to type them into.
  **THE TV IS THE HARD PART, twice over.** On SECRECY it is public, UUID-keyed and unauthenticated, so `sdTvView` is a PROJECTION rather than a filter: it rebuilds each player out of seven named fields and never touches the secret store, so a role planted on session state is dropped on the way out rather than filtered on the way in. That property was found by writing the negative control and watching it fail to leak, and it is now asserted. On FIT this pack seats TWENTY, the largest cap in the app, and shows every player at once. It was designed against 1080p from the first commit and MEASURED at the full twenty: 20 tiles in 5 columns, board ending at 767px, back button at 1048px, fits with 32px of clearance in Arcade and 18px in Tabletop, in both themes, nothing covered. Board Game's TV shipped 176px over at twelve and went five days unmeasured; that entry stays open in BUGS and is James's call for its own session.
  **ALSO CLOSED: part A's residual risk.** Its secrecy rested on a real but unpinned invariant, that no generic reader of `game_sessions` selects the `state` column. `events.ts` and `tv.ts` both read by eventId alone across every pack's row including the secret one, and a future session adding `state` to either select would hand the deal to the event detail payload with EVERY EXISTING TEST STILL GREEN. Now pinned by a check that walks every server source, finds each `.from(gameSessions)` and reads the projection that precedes it. 22 tests added (565 to 587; 533 to 587 across both parts). Three harnesses updated IN THE SAME COMMIT. NO SCHEMA CHANGE.
  **THE ROLE CATALOGUE PASS (2026-08-10), a third session on the same pack, opened by James spotting that Salem had no Witch.** The missing names were the small half. THE REAL PROBLEM WAS THAT TITLES WERE OPEN AND ROLES WERE CLOSED, AND BOTH REACH THE PERMANENT RECORD: a free-typed title always won and got the default shape, while the role catalogue was closed per title and the documented workaround was to deal the baseline and say the rest out loud. Every ledger line carries `meta.role`, so a Salem Witch was recorded as a Townsperson, permanently. That is not a missing feature, it is a FALSE RECORD WRITTEN ON PURPOSE, and any future win-rate-by-role surface reads it. Roles now get the treatment titles already had. **THE GAPS:** Salem gained the Witch and the Executioner, each on its own solo faction the way the Jester already was; Blood on the Clocktower gained the Traveller as TWO entries, because a Traveller can be good or evil and a role whose faction floats cannot be ranked; Werewolf gained the Minion and the Sorcerer, evil but not wolves. Secret Hitler, Avalon and Mafia were left alone on purpose. **FREE-TYPED ROLES** canonicalize to the id the curated role would have, so a Witch typed tonight and a Witch curated next month are ONE row in the ledger rather than two halves of one player's history, and a typed role must name the faction it wins with because every player's side is derived from their role. **SHARED WINS** are `tiedWithAbove` and score 1, 1, 3: two factions can win together and the faction that lost to them is THIRD, because there was no second place. 19 tests added (587 to 606). TV fit re-measured and unchanged: 20 players still clear by 32px in Arcade and 18px in Tabletop.
  **THE SALEM CORRECTION AND THE END OF THE WOLF DEFAULT (2026-08-14), opened by James playing a night and finding a Mafioso.** TWO DIFFERENT GAMES SHARE THE NAME SALEM and the catalogue implemented the one this app cannot be used for. TOWN OF SALEM is the online game (BlankMediaGames): Mafioso, Godfather, Sheriff, Investigator, Serial Killer, Jester, Executioner, Witch, played in its own client, where a crew has no use for a moderator board or a TV. SALEM 1692 is the physical card game (Facade Games, 2015), and it is the one the crew plays and the one that needs exactly this pack. Its tryal cards are Witch, Constable or Not A Witch, and its factions are the Townspeople and the Witches, so Salem is now four roles: Townsperson, Witch, and the Constable SPLIT IN TWO (town-aligned and witch-aligned) exactly as Blood on the Clocktower's Traveller is, because the Constable is independent of alignment and a role whose faction floats cannot be ranked. **THE LABEL STAYS "Salem"** and an alias folds "Salem 1692" onto it, because renaming would split that title's history across two names with nothing erroring. **THE WITCH KEEPS HER ID AND CHANGES ALIGNMENT**, solo to evil, which is the one thing here that changes meaning underneath a stable id. **A GAME NOBODY PICKED IS NOT WEREWOLF:** the default catalogue was Village/Wolves and was returned for every free-typed title, so typing "One Night Ultimate Werewolf" offered `villager` and `wolf` and a host who accepted them wrote a false permanent record on the DEFAULT path. An uncurated title now opens EMPTY and the host types the roles, which is what typed roles were built for, so the fix was a deletion rather than a feature. **CONSPIRACY WAS CHECKED RATHER THAN ASSUMED** and is already recordable: the record path takes `memberIds` from the caller rather than deriving them from the deal, and every player row on the record form has a tappable chip per faction, so a player who flips is moved before recording and the row says both what they were dealt and what they finished as. 6 tests added (606 to 612). TV fit re-measured and unchanged.
- [x] CARD TABLE, and the screens half of the extraction that carried it (2026-08-09). The pack that three commits of groundwork were for, and the receipt on the layer: `apps/server/src/cardtable.ts` is FIFTY LINES (a registry key, a format string and a config), `packages/shared/src/cardtable.ts` is a title list and a partnership table, and the stylesheet is a palette. **WHAT CAME OUT FIRST, because Card table could not be cheap until it did:** the shared title-night SCREENS (page, TV, stats panel and all ten server routes) moved into `apps/web/src/titlenight/` and `apps/server/src/titlenight-runtime.ts`, following the casino group exactly, and Board Game's server file went 570 lines to 50; the TEAM PICKER came out of `PingPongPage.tsx` into `apps/web/src/teams/TeamPicker.tsx` before it could be copied a third time. THE HEADER DELIBERATELY DID NOT MOVE, on both packs: the "two ways out" rule is checked per screen by `pack-screens.test.ts` and a header hidden behind a shared component would make that check pass for a pack that had lost its way back. TITLES: Euchre, Spades, Hearts, Cribbage, Rummy, Gin, Uno, President, with EUCHRE AND SPADES opening in partnerships and the rest free-for-all. Cribbage is free-for-all on purpose and has a test saying why (four-handed cribbage IS partnership and two-handed is the game most people mean, so a default of 2 would be wrong more often than right; and with two players the two shapes are the same arrangement anyway). Poker is absent: it is money, it settles, and it belongs to the casino group's engine. TWO THINGS THE PACK NEEDED THAT THE LAYER DID NOT HAVE: the record form now taps SIDES rather than players (on a free-for-all night a side of one IS a player, which is why Board Game's screens come back byte-identical, and on a Euchre night it is the only thing that works, because tapping both halves of a pair into an order is the same side twice), and the host can now OVERRIDE the shape from the live screen, which the decision log had promised since 2026-08-05 and nothing implemented. Only a pack that declares partnerships gets that control, so Board Game's screen is exactly as it shipped. **THE PIXELS ON THE SHIPPED PACK DID NOT MOVE AND THAT IS MEASURED RATHER THAN INTENDED:** the screens baseline compares TEXT and could not have seen a repaint, so `scripts/theme-sweep.mjs` captured every resolved colour on both Board Game screens before and after, and it caught two places where the first draft of the shared stylesheet was wrong. 533 tests, up from 506.
- [x] THE TITLE-NIGHT LAYER, extracted from Board Game (2026-08-05). Scoped as the Card table session; CARD TABLE ITSELF DID NOT SHIP, see NEXT UP slot 1, which now carries the corrected scope. **THE SESSION OPENED BY CORRECTING THIS FILE.** It said Card table was "the cheapest pack in this file, because it shares Board Game's whole implementation", and THAT SHARING DID NOT EXIST: Board Game's implementation was Board Game's, and Card table is cheap only on the far side of an extraction nobody had done. So the real work was the extraction, and Card table is the registry entry that proves it. Same shape as the roulette session: blackjack shipped alone, roulette arrived as the second example, and the money board and money routes were pulled out THEN rather than guessed at from one pack. One example is a pack; two is a layer. WHAT LANDED: `packages/shared/src/titlenight.ts`, the engine for any pack whose night is a sequence of named games with a tapped finish order, plus Board Game converted onto it and its server routes with it. A pack now owns only what a pack should: its curated titles, its partnership defaults, its cap, and the noun it calls one recorded unit. IT DOES NOT RE-DERIVE PLACEMENT: `teams.ts` gained a tie-aware `placementsFromRankedSides`, so free-for-all competition ranking (1,1,3) and side ranking (1,1,2,2) are ONE implementation with two entry points rather than two rules that can drift apart. The layer also carries SIDES and the SCORE GRAIN, because its types could not be split from them without writing the engine twice. **THE FIXTURES CAUGHT A REAL BUG AND THAT IS THE WHOLE ARGUMENT FOR WRITING THEM FIRST:** `tnPlacements` read per-player scores only out of `memberScores`, so on a free-for-all session (singleton sides, player grain) a score typed against the side was silently dropped, and every Board Game score would have stopped being recorded with nothing erroring. On a side of one the two grains are the same number, and the engine now says so. Fixed in the engine, not the fixture. TWO CHANGES TO BOARD GAME'S ROWS, both recorded in the fixture where they are visible: `side: null` is now written explicitly rather than being absent (THE DATABASE ROW IS IDENTICAL, since `participantRows` sets the column to NULL either way), and a row WITH a typed score now also records the GRAIN it was typed at, which is additive and is the thing worth storing because a score that does not say whether it belonged to a player or a side is a number nobody can interpret in a year. Rows with no score are byte-identical. One copy change: "Need at least 2 players in a game" became "in a board game", since the layer is shared and "a game" reads wrong for a pack whose unit is a hand. Board Game's `ledger`, `wsType`, `route` and `games.name` are untouched, which was the binding constraint: any of them moving splits a shipped pack's history into a tab of its own. TESTS: 506, up from 493, including thirteen fixtures captured by RUNNING the unmodified pack and a CDP baseline of the team picker's behaviour for the extraction that follows.
- [x] THE TEAM PRIMITIVE, and Ping Pong doubles as its proof (2026-08-05). `match_participants.side` shipped 2026-08-02 with Casino Run, so the LEDGER had understood teams since then and `buildRivalry` already read it. What did not exist was the SESSION half: picking sides, scoring a result as two or more sides, and writing a DIFFERENT `side` value per participant instead of Casino Run's one shared one. `packages/shared/src/teams.ts` is that half, and it is deliberately NOT in a pack, because Card table, Social deduction and Party games are all queued behind it and the thing that must not happen is four packs each deciding for themselves what a side result does to placement. **TWO PLACEMENT RULES NOW LIVE IN THIS APP AND BOTH ARE CORRECT**, which is written at the top of `teams.ts` with the sentence explaining why: a genuine TIE uses competition ranking (two tied at the top are 1,1 and the next is 3), and a TEAM result ranks SIDES 1..N, so a 2v2 is 1,1,2,2 and never 1,1,3,3. There were two sides, so there were two places to finish. They look contradictory and are not, and unifying them would silently rewrite what a placement means in every row already written by the other rule. **THE COLUMN GETS STABLE SHORT IDS** (`a`, `b`, `c`) and never a display name: names live in session state, which is jsonb and free to change, while a name in the column would make a rename a data migration and buy nothing, since `side` is only ever compared for equality. `sideIdFor` holds the NULL-WHEN-EVERY-SIDE-IS-ONE rule in one place, and it is load-bearing: a pack writing "a"/"b" for a 1v1 would make `meetingOutcome` classify two OPPONENTS as having played TOGETHER, and the rivalry would be wrong forever with nothing erroring. Uneven sides are a returned FACT rather than an error (five into two is a real thing a crew does), and the shuffle distributes the remainder rather than dropping anybody. **PING PONG IS THE FIRST CONSUMER AND THE PROOF.** Both of its core shapes were single-player by construction, so both had to become side-shaped: a match was `aId`/`bId` and KOTH was a `kingId` plus a queue of player ids. A match is now between two SIDES, it holds SNAPSHOTS of them rather than references into the current arrangement (a completed match must still know who was on it after a reshuffle), and all three formats are converted. Free Play, Best Of and KOTH all seat pairs; in KOTH the winning PAIR holds the table and the losing pair rotates to the back together. **THE SIDE SET IS A LOG, not a field**: each arrangement carries the match index it took effect from, because KOTH's throne and queue are REBUILT by replaying matches rather than maintained, so the replay has to know which arrangement each stretch was played under, and undoing back past a reshuffle has to restore the previous one or the rebuild replays old matches under new sides and hands the throne to a pair that never played. Same derive-from-a-log shape as craps' hands and Smashdown's burn board. **THE REGRESSION THAT MATTERED MOST WAS SINGLES, and it was proved rather than hoped**: 21 fixtures over the exact ledger rows, the night standings and the KOTH rebuild were captured by RUNNING THE UNMODIFIED ENGINE and confirmed green before a line of the conversion was written, the same discipline the pack-runtime refactor used. A singles night writes `side` null on every row and is byte-identical to what shipped before. Worth recording: the first draft of one fixture was wrong in three places because it was reasoned out by hand instead of observed, which is the argument for the whole approach. Legacy sessions load through `normalizePpState`, wired to a new optional `normalize` hook on the pack runtime that runs at the two points where jsonb becomes state, rather than at the pack's eight call sites plus the guest backfill. JAMES'S CALLS, all in the code where they bite: the KOTH reign record is BOTH things (it names the pair on screen and credits each member in their own `longestReign`); points go on EVERY member of the losing side, with the accepted double-count cost written down; and sides are fixed for the night with an explicit host reshuffle that applies from the next match on and says so, along with the fact that the ladder restarts. STATS: the panel splits singles from doubles, DERIVED from `side IS NOT NULL` rather than labelled, so no new `matches.label` value, no new format string, and the `bo{N}` labels do not move. A mislabelled row would be unrecoverable history; a derived split is a query anyone can change their mind about. The two halves sum to the unsplit totals by construction and the panel prints both so a reader can check. TESTS: 68 new (425 -> 493), including three that drive REAL doubles rows through `meetingOutcome` rather than assuming that path works, since Casino Run writes one shared side and it had never seen two different non-null sides. VERIFIED IN A BROWSER as well, 15 checks against the real built bundle with a stub API. **THE TV MEASUREMENTS CHANGED THE CODE**: the first version put "Up next" and the longest hold in blocks of their own, costing 66px at four players and 112px at eight against the BACK BUTTON, on a screen that is already over 1080 past six players and that this session was scoped not to worsen. Both were folded into lines that already exist, and the re-measured figures (885.7px at four, 1149.1px at eight, page bottom 1192.3px) are identical to the baseline with them removed. NO SCHEMA CHANGE: `side` already existed and was already nullable. Deploy: nothing beyond the push.
- [x] BOARD GAME, the tabletop pack, and the cheapest one built (2026-08-04). A crew plays board games on a night; each board game played is ONE recorded result. The host picks a title, taps the finish order, confirms. No per-turn input, nothing to maintain between games, and NO NEW ENGINE: it reuses the FFA placement path, `usePackSession`, `createPackRuntime` and the shared leaderboard row, and the whole pack is a registry entry, a ~430-line server file, a ~430-line page, a TV view and one pure module. LEDGER UNIT IS THE GAME, not the night: a night with four board games writes four `matches` rows sharing one `sessionKey`, keyed `bg:{eventId}:{sessionKey}:{idx}`. Title on `matches.label` (Mario Party's pattern for its board), `format = "boardgame"`, ONE `games` row for the pack and never one per title, because a row per title would split this into a leaderboard tab per board game, which is exactly what the registry's own `gameName` warning exists to prevent. **PLACEMENT IS TAPPED, NEVER DERIVED FROM A SCORE**, and that is the design rather than a shortcut: board games disagree about direction (Catan is high-wins, Hearts is low-wins, plenty have no score at all), so an engine deriving placement from a number would need each title's direction and a wrong direction silently INVERTS a leaderboard. TIES ARE CO-PLACEMENTS at competition ranking, the convention already used everywhere here: two tied at the top are both placement 1, both winners, and the next player is 3. The affordance is a "tied" chip on a row rather than a separate mode, so the order reads the way somebody would say it out loud. SCORE IS OPTIONAL AND IS A NOTE: it rides in `match_participants.meta` (deliberately not `match_participants.score`, which is a RANKING INPUT where packs use it, Mario Party's stars decide its winner), and a typed score that contradicts the tapped order is allowed to stand, asserted directly by a test that feeds a low-wins scoreline and checks nothing moved. **THE ONE REAL RISK WAS THE TITLE**, and it is the same failure class as `games.name` one level down: per-title stats read `matches.label`, so "Catan", "Settlers of Catan" and "catan" would be three titles silently splitting a crew's history. Three inputs in this order on screen: the CREW'S OWN RECENTS (most recent first, the path most nights take and what actually keeps spelling stable), a modest CURATED starter list in `packages/shared` so a first night is not a blank box, then FREE TEXT. Every submission is canonicalized SERVER-SIDE on entry (trim, collapse internal whitespace, case-fold against the crew's recents AND the curated list, existing spelling wins) so a stale client cannot write a third spelling, and the DISPLAY name is stored rather than an id, because `label` is a display string everywhere else and the leaderboard reads it directly. Codenames is deliberately NOT on the starter list: it belongs to the Party games pack. ROSTER SEATS 12, and the way that was done is the point: `validateFfa` caps at 8 and that 8 IS LOAD-BEARING FOR SMASH (Ultimate seats 8, and Smashdown's `floor(rosterSize / playerCount)` battle cap is arithmetic against it), so the global was NOT raised.
