The cap is now an argument with a default of 8, `validateFfaSize` is the shared size rule, every existing caller stays on the default, and tests assert Smash still validates at 8 and still rejects 9. WHAT IS ON THE TABLE is one tap when the box comes out and it is why the TV works: a board game night has long gaps between results, so the TV's PRIMARY state is between games (the lesson the event TV learned on 2026-07-28), and it carries the current title large, the night's standings across every game played, and the last game's result at all times. TESTS: 30 new (395 -> 425) in `packages/shared/tests/boardgame.test.ts`, all pure and with no Drizzle stub: placement from a tapped order including a two-way tie at the top, a tie in the middle and a three-way tie; canonicalization across exact match, case, leading and trailing whitespace, an internal double space and a genuine miss; the per-pack cap in both directions for both packs; the score-contradicts-the-order assertion in BOTH directions so it cannot pass on one; and the ledger key shape including two sessions on one event not colliding. PICKER GROUP is a new one, "BOARD AND CARD", and it is deliberately NOT called "Tabletop": that word already means the THEME in this repo, and one word with two meanings here is how "stake" and "floor" both went wrong. Card table joins it when it lands. `group` is display only, so the name is safe to change at any time. ONE SMALL SHARED CHANGE CAME WITH IT: `usePackSession` is now generic over its LAUNCH CONTEXT as well as its session, defaulting to the shared `PackCtx`, because this pack's context endpoint returns the crew's recent titles on top of the envelope and the setup screen needs them before the first game is recorded. Every other pack is untouched by the default. NO SCHEMA CHANGE: label, meta and the shared session table all already existed. Deploy: nothing beyond the push.
- [x] THE `side` PRIMITIVE, and the Casino Run review fixes (2026-08-02). Five findings from reviewing the Casino Run commits against a clean checkout; none was a deploy blocker and one was a real data bug. **THE REAL ONE: a co-op result registered as a TIE between every pair of players in it.** `buildRivalry` classified a shared match by comparing placements, which is correct for every format that had existed; Casino Run writes an IDENTICAL row for everyone on the run, so two people who cleared one together came out as a draw, and so did two who busted. The cheap patch (skip `format = "casino_run"`, the way `isSeriesSummary` is skipped) was rejected: the same two people are teammates in one match and opponents in the next, so team structure cannot be a property of the PACK. It is a property of the MATCH, so `match_participants` gained a nullable `side text`. NULL MEANS NO TEAM STRUCTURE and null rows classify exactly as they always did, which is asserted by brute force over every placement/isWinner pair rather than by a couple of examples. `buildRivalry` gained a FOURTH outcome, `together`, ahead of win/loss/tie (it has to be ahead, or the identical rows reach the comparison and tie), counted separately in the totals, in the h2h-by-game breakdown, on the share card and in the last-5 chips. THE STREAK SKIPS IT, and that was the question worth asking before shipping: counting a teammate game as a win reads a co-op night as an unbeaten run, and BREAKING the streak on it claims something happened between two people who were not playing each other, so three wins, a run played together and a fourth win is a streak of four. The classification and the streak walk were extracted as pure functions (`meetingOutcome`, `meetingStreaks`) for the same reason `resolveNow` was: a wrong answer here is never an error, it is a rivalry record that is quietly wrong forever. Casino Run is the ONLY writer this session; `feedAgg` and `finishAgg` were deliberately not touched, because a teammate game is still a game you played and belongs in both players' lifetime totals unchanged. THE OTHER FOUR: (1) an undo could DOUBLE-DRAW a stage-clear card, fixed by claiming a draw against the TRANSITION rather than the leg, which also makes a retried request idempotent; (2) 291 EM DASHES across 65 files swept out against the standing rule, rewritten to a colon, comma, full stop or parentheses as each sentence wanted and never mechanically to a hyphen, with the four genuine typographic placeholders moved to en dashes instead. The first pass reported clean and was not: five were written as `&mdash;` in JSX, which a grep for the character cannot see, and a screenshot caught one. Both that rule and the event-link rule are now TESTS rather than notes (copy-rules.test.ts checks four spellings; pack-screens.test.ts lists every scoring screen), each negative-controlled by reintroducing the fault and confirming the test fails; (3) all NINE retired modifier ids now have display names in `RETIRED_MODIFIER_NAMES` rather than the three the review spotted, with a test that fails if a retired card has none; (4) a link back to the event in ALL NINE scoring screens, not the four the review named, since `BackButton` is history-based and sends somebody who arrived from a shared link to home instead of the night (Beerio stays exempt as a vendored 1:1 replica). TESTS: 368, up from 343, including the four outcomes, the null-side regression sweep, the streak's treatment of a teammate game, and the draw idempotence end to end through undo and redo. DEPLOY: `ALTER TABLE match_participants ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS side text;` in the Neon console, because drizzle-kit push can no-op in CI.
- [x] CASINO RUN, round five: THE PLAYTEST, and what it found (2026-08-02). James asked for the deck to be played through "with all cards through big and small bets, real wins and losses" and for changes to be pitched where a card almost never matters. `packages/shared/tests/casinorun-playtest.test.ts` models what a table would DO with each card — a multiplier on wins or losses, a shift in the odds, a floor or cap on the bet, a refund — and measures the change in a run's clear rate over 8,000 runs at two betting styles. The app still never computes a card's effect; this is a DESIGN harness, exactly like the ladder simulation. WHAT IT FOUND, none of which was visible any other way: (1) **21 of 36 severities were wrong.** "Everyone antes" was a severity 1 that took the clear rate from 53% to ZERO; two severity 3s moved it by under a point. Severity is now MEASURED — 12+ points is a 3, 4-12 a 2, under 4 a 1 — and the buckets are asserted, so a new card cannot be rated by feel. (2) **Three "banes" were not banes.** Escalating minimum, Ante surge and No small bets only raised the BET FLOOR, and in a game where the bank must grow, forcing bigger bets HELPS. The first two now say the bank posts the ante dead once a leg, which is a cost a human can apply and the model can measure (-18 and -26 points). The third was relabelled a BOON and reworded, because raising the floor genuinely helps and a wrong sign on screen is worse than a weak card. A test now checks direction across BOTH betting styles — checking bold play alone is what let all three through. (3) **Three cards had literally zero effect** — Dealer's choice, No walking and Pushes pay — and were replaced by On the house (a refund a stage), Table maximum (the only card that punishes a bold table) and No pushes. (4) **Flat per-leg costs are lethal** against a target that must grow ~75%: anything costing a full ante a leg is a death sentence, which is why the rake cards are now shares of the pot. ALSO THIS ROUND, all James's calls: tokens repriced to hurt (8-30% of the bank, Mulligan dearest at 30% because a free re-do of a losing hand is the strongest thing in the shop); the default bank is P$200 rather than P$100 (play money, and bigger numbers read better — the playtest asserts the game is SCALE-FREE so this needed no retuning); the deck browser is grouped into "On at every game" and one section per table; Poker is gone from the leg games (a poker leg is a tournament or a long session, not a stretch you step away from with a number — type it under Other); and Vig, House rake and Tithe take a share of the POT or the WIN rather than a flat multiple of the ante, with House match matching the WIN. A win-based percentage deliberately does NOT render as money: the app never sees a hand, so it prints the percentage and the humans do the rest. TESTS: 341, including 8 new playtest assertions. BROWSER: 330 checks.
- [x] CASINO RUN, round four: FOUR CLARITY BUGS, all found by James reading the cards back (2026-08-02). (1) "STAKE" WAS DOING TWO JOBS — `stakes` already means real vs play money across the whole group, and the bonus percentages were quoting "the table stake", which is a different thing. Same trap "floor" fell into two rounds earlier. The unit is now NAMED on every card: the ANTE in a run, the BUY-IN at a cash table, and a card reads "P$2.00 (100% of the ante)" so nobody has to ask what the percentage is of. (2) BONUSES SAY "EXTRA". "Pays P$2.00" reads as a total; these are on top, so the text says "pays an EXTRA P$2.00". (3) MERCY CHIP OFFERED A REBUY IN A PACK WITH NO BUY-INS. That exposed a class of bug rather than one card: three "any" cards needed per-player money that Casino Run does not have (Mercy chip's rebuy, Leader tax and Underdog bonus, both of which need an individual position). `appliesTo` gained a third case, `"cash"`, meaning "every cash table, never a co-op run", and a test asserts no card in the run pool needs per-player money. (4) THE GAME-SPECIFIC CARDS COULD NEVER APPEAR IN A RUN, which is the mode that actually plays all three games. A run's pool is now the UNION of every table's cards; a blackjack card sits on the board all run and bites on blackjack legs. The leg form says which rules apply the moment a game is picked ("At Blackjack: 3 of 5 house rules apply — ... Sitting out until you play their table: ..."), the strip and the TV dim the dormant ones, and each card carries a game tag. The deck is 36 cards, 18 boons / 18 banes, and the run's own 32-card pool is exactly 16/16 — which matters more than the overall split, because a run's entire texture comes from its draws. TESTS: 332. BROWSER: 324 checks.
- [x] CASINO RUN, round three: TOKENS, a money-first deck, and percentages that compute (2026-08-02, all James's calls). THE DECK WAS REWRITTEN AROUND THE MONEY. Six cards were retired — Loser buys, Bust penalty, Silence, Phones down, Last to sit, High roller — because they policed the ROOM rather than the board: where your phone is and whether you are talking have nothing to do with the numbers this app tracks, and "pays the table a forfeit" never said what it cost. Six replacements are about totals and stakes: House rake, Ante surge, Losses double, Table minimum up, Tithe and House match. Retired ids are NOT reused, and a test says so — a run played under one still has ledger rows, and `modifierName` renders an unknown id as itself so that history stays readable. PERCENTAGES THAT COMPUTE: "pays a bonus" is not a rule, it is an argument waiting to happen, so a card that involves money now carries a `bonusPct` and `modifierRule()` renders it against the TABLE'S OWN STAKE — Casino Run passes its live minimum ante (so a card gets dearer as the ante climbs) and the cash packs pass their default buy-in. A card reads "pays P$2.00", not "pays a bonus" and not even "pays 100%". With no stake in hand it falls back to the percentage rather than a gap, which is what the cash setup screen needs before a buy-in is typed. THE PIPS HAVE A LEGEND: they shipped as one to three bare dots with nothing anywhere saying what they meant, which is decoration. They are now filled/empty out of three, carry `SEVERITY_LABEL` as a tooltip and as screen-reader text, and every screen that draws them prints the key once. ONE-SHOT CARDS ARE NOW "TOKENS" in all copy, and there are nine: Hedge was cut for being boring, and LET IT RIDE replaces it as the headline — play the leg, take the payout, THEN flip for it, which is a genuinely different decision from doubling the stake up front because you already know what you won. Added alongside: House bonus, Steady hand, Second chance (buys a whole extra attempt, the only token that buys you out of the loss condition, and the dearest for it) and Shave the target (a tenth off THIS stage's quota, and only this one — carrying it forward would let one purchase discount the rest of the run). ANTE SURGE is the first card that moves the tracked ante directly, which is the point of having a tracker: a rule that says "the ante is doubled" should show a doubled ante rather than a sentence to remember. TESTS: 329 total. BROWSER: 317 checks, up from 308. Also fixed: the longer rendered rule text pushed the TV modifier wall onto a second row and 43px off a 1080p screen — the wall's cards are now shrinkable so a pair always shares one row, and the fix is really that the TV wall was the one modifier surface never given a stake unit, so it was rendering the long "100% of the minimum" fallback.
- [x] CASINO RUN, round two: A REAL FAIL STATE, the ante, one-shot cards, play money only, and a 50/50 deck (2026-08-01, all James's calls after playing round one). **The important one: there was no way to lose.** A missed stage handed out a fresh set of legs forever, so the only fail state was the floor — which a bank that hovers can dodge indefinitely. Simulated, a grinding table took ~1,100 legs to resolve and 0.5% of runs never resolved at all inside a 4,000-leg ceiling. That is not a hard game, it is a game with no ending. FIXED with `attemptsPerStage`: a stage gives a fixed number of shots (4/3/3/2 by ladder) and running out ends the run with money still on the table. The two endings are recorded and reported separately (`floor` vs `attempts`) everywhere, because "we lost the bank" and "we ran out of shots" are different nights and a crew that mostly does the second is playing too cautiously rather than too riskily. THE CAP IS AIMED, and the simulation proves it: a grinder goes from ~1,100 legs to 15, while bold play moves by less than a point (52.6% either way), so it punishes the behaviour that was un-fun without becoming a difficulty knob. The four ladders needed NO retuning and stay in band. It also turned `legsPerStage` into a real lever for the first time — under the old rules it provably could not move the clear rate at all. MINIMUM ANTE TRACKER: a derived number, not a stored one. Base is set at setup (2% of the bank by default) and it RISES — once per missed attempt, plus every five legs when Escalating minimum is on — so grinding costs something even before it runs out of attempts. `Everyone antes` shows as a flag off the card. This is bookkeeping (a declared table stake), NOT the app computing a gambling outcome, and the distinction is written down where it can be checked. ONE-SHOT CARDS you buy out of the bank: Double or nothing, Hedge, Mulligan, One more shot, Ante relief, priced as a fraction of the STARTING bank so one is never trivial or unaffordable. Three are declarative like modifiers; two touch bookkeeping the app already owns, and `effect` says which is which. They are recorded in the SAME log as legs, which is what keeps undo honest — a purchase is just another entry that moved the bank, so popping it refunds and un-holds with nothing else to unwind — and a buy spends no leg budget but CAN bust the run, because spending your last chips on a hedge is a legitimate way to die. Tokens bought since the last leg are `held` and spent by it, all derived. PLAY MONEY ONLY: the stakes selector is gone and the server ignores a `stakes` body entirely, so a stale client cannot open a real-money run. The quotas climb to multiples of the bank and the hardest ladder is meant to be unwinnable; that is no place for real money. THE DECK IS NOW 32 CARDS, EXACTLY 16 BOONS AND 16 BANES, asserted overall AND within the "any" pool — which was 4 boons to 8 and is the only pool Casino Run draws from, so a co-op run was getting hurt by its own random draws twice as often as it was helped. Eight new cards: four "any" boons (Hot streak, Free round, Underdog bonus, Insurance) plus No outside bets, Come out bonus, No odds and No doubling. Every pack's own pool is now asserted to hold both kinds. TESTS: 51 in the engine suite and 10 in the simulation (320 total), including the grinder proof in both directions, that the cap does not touch bold play, both endings, undo of an attempts-bust, the ante clock and the miss-driven rise, ante relief flooring at the base, token pricing, buying one you cannot afford, and that a purchase is never counted as a leg. BROWSER: 308 checks, and the TV fit is now pinned at EVERY run state rather than one — adding the ante row pushed the footer 23px off a 1080p screen and only the live state was being measured, which is the money board's lesson relearned.
- [x] CASINO RUN, the co-op pack (2026-07-30). The crew shares ONE bank and plays a sequence of STAGES; each stage has a QUOTA the bank must reach and a budget of LEGS to reach it in, with a FLOOR underneath the whole run. Clear the last stage and everybody wins; drop through the floor and everybody loses. NAMING: the backlog used "floor" for the bank minimum, so the levels are STAGES and the floor is only ever the bust threshold — the two words never trade places, in the code or the UI. ITS OWN ENGINE, `packages/shared/src/casinorun.ts`, explicitly not `settleCash`: that engine is built on per-player nets summing to zero against a banker, and a co-op run inverts the assumption. It reuses what is genuinely shared (integer cents, `parseCents`/`formatCents`, the stakes-aware `money()`, `MoneyInput`, the modifier deck and draw, the `--cg-*` tokens) and owns its own settlement. EVERYTHING IS DERIVED FROM THE LEG LOG — bank, stage, attempt, status, clears, peak, comeback — the same call craps made for hands and Smashdown for its burn board, which is what makes undo a pop-and-re-derive rather than a pile of counters to unwind. THE HOST SETS A LUMP-SUM BANK; players do not buy in, and there is no per-player buy-in or cash-out field anywhere in the pack. QUOTAS ARE PRESET LADDERS, one choice at setup producing the whole curve, as `startingBank * (1 + escalation)^N` — the quota is the bank's TOTAL, not a delta. **THE LADDERS ARE SIMULATED, NOT FELT, and the first draft was badly wrong.** Proposed +15/+25/+40/+60% cleared 37/28/10/4% against the brief's own model, so the "casual" ladder would have lost the bank two nights in three. The simulation also proved LEGS PER STAGE CANNOT MOVE THE CLEAR RATE at all (a missed stage costs the attempt and nothing else, so it only changes how often the house adds a rule), and that the 70-80% casual band was unreachable under a fixed-fraction reference player at 48% — the fix was the MODEL, which now bets toward the quota (Dubins-Savage bold play, the optimal strategy for reaching a target in a subfair game, and simply what a crew chasing a quota actually does) rather than a blind fraction that ignores the quota entirely. SHIPPED LADDERS, all mid-band and strictly ordered: **Casual +6% x4 stages = 75.5%, Standard +15% x4 = 52.8%, High roller +30% x5 = 22.8%, Degenerate +50% x5 = 10.5%**, at 20,000 seeded runs each. A MISSED STAGE DOES NOT END A RUN: it costs the attempt and forces a bane, so only the floor and the final quota end anything, which is what makes the modifier pile-up the pressure rather than a timer. MODIFIERS: all five draw paths go through the existing `drawModifiers` with no new draw code — an opening draw, an escalating draw on clearing a stage (`crunEscalationWeight` interpolates from the deck's own 1/severity at the first stage to its inverse at the last), a forced bane on missing one (a `kind` filter), a host override, and a draft mode that deals three and is deliberately stateless between the deal and the pick. LEDGER: co-placement 1 and a win for EVERY player on a cleared run, an equal LAST for every player on a bust (roster size, floored at 2 so a solo bust is not recorded as a first place). Per-leg attribution rides along in meta as `myLegs`/`myDelta`. OFF-APP LEGS are free text, so a crew playing something the app has never heard of can still run one. TV: the bank at 19vmin and nothing competing with it, the quota and distance under it, the stage ladder, the house rules and whose leg was last at what. STATS: clear rate, deepest run, busts, biggest comeback as a named crew record, stages lost to the house, and clear rate per house rule behind "More stats" — crew-wide here, the mirror image of the cash packs' per-player rule, because a co-op result is the table's and identical for everyone on it. ACCEPTED CONSEQUENCE, in the pack's own copy and not just here: a co-op leg played at blackjack lands under Casino Run, not Blackjack. NO SCHEMA CHANGE. TESTS: 40 new (298 total) across the engine and the simulation — quota maths at every ladder and stage, integer-cent quotas, bank arithmetic including the float canary, bust on and below the floor, bust beating clear, stage advance (a WHILE not an IF: one big leg clears every quota it meets, and advancing only one would leave the bank sitting above its own stage's quota so the next leg cleared it regardless of what it did — a real bug the tests caught), missed attempts, undo including undoing the leg that busted the run, materialization both ways, the forced-bane filter, and the four clear-rate bands plus a strict-ordering check. VERIFIED IN A BROWSER against the real bundle (269 checks, up from 193): the ladder preview quoting real quotas, a live run's bank and distance, cleared and busted verdicts, the TV measured as both enormous and fitting a 1080p screen, the picker tile, and the stats panel behind its disclosure.
- [x] DECLARATIVE MODIFIERS across the casino group (2026-07-30). House rules a crew turns on for a night: the app DISPLAYS them and RECORDS which were live, and never computes their effect. That line is the whole design and it is written at the top of `packages/shared/src/modifiers.ts` with the reasoning, because it is the one thing a later session will be tempted to "fix": the moment the app enforces a card's payout maths ("blackjack pays double") it has to know what every hand paid, which means logging every hand, which means the minimal-input promise the entire casino group rests on is gone. One tap at setup and zero taps for the rest of the night, versus a tap per hand forever. THE DECK is 24 cards — 12 that apply to any pack plus 4 each for roulette, craps and blackjack — each with a name, a one-line rule, a kind (boon/bane) and a severity 1-3. `id` JOINS THE NEVER-CHANGE LIST alongside `ledger`, `gameName` and `keyPrefix`, for the same reason: it is written into `match_participants.meta`, so a rename does not error, it silently orphans that card's whole history. The 24 shipped ids are pinned in a test that spells the blast radius out. SELECTION is two paths, both host-driven: pick any number from the deck (filtered to the pack), or "Surprise me", a random draw weighted by `1 / severity` so the night-changing cards stay rarer than the flavour ones, plus "One more" which draws a single extra excluding what is already on. The draw helper takes A DECK, A COUNT, A FILTER AND A WEIGHTING rather than hard-coding "at setup", because Casino Run needs escalating draws, a forced bane and a draft hand, and those are this function with different arguments. Asking for more cards than exist returns the whole pool rather than looping; a weighting that zeroes the pool falls back to uniform. DISPLAY is a compact strip on the pack page (under the money board, shown to everyone, read-only once the table is open — a rule that could change mid-night would make the recorded ids a lie about what was played) and a LARGE wall above the board on the TV, name and rule together at 3.4/2.4vmin, colour-coded by kind off the existing up/down tokens. A night with no cards renders nothing at all, on either screen. RECORDING writes the active ids into EVERY participant's `match_participants.meta` under `modifiers` — per participant because the stat is per player, and because `matches` has only `label` (one display string, already Mario Party's board) and no generic meta column. Ids are sanitized against the deck AND the pack on the way in, so a junk id can never reach the ledger and render as garbage forever. A night with none omits the key entirely rather than storing `[]`, so a plain night's meta is byte-for-byte what it was before this shipped. THE STAT: win rate and net PER MODIFIER on each casino pack's stats panel, behind a "More stats" disclosure. Per player, not crew-wide, because a crew-wide net is zero on every player-banked night by construction. Computed by `aggregateByModifier`, which reuses `aggregateCashNights` on the filtered subset so there is no second definition of "win rate" to drift, and the panel says out loud that this is correlation and not cause. NO SCHEMA CHANGE. TESTS: 9 new in the shared suite (249 -> 258) plus 19 in `packages/shared/tests/modifiers.test.ts` — the pinned ids, deck mix, `appliesTo` filtering, 200-iteration draw fuzzing, over-draw, the weight override and forced-bane filter Casino Run will use, the recording grain, absent-not-empty, the stakes split and a pre-modifiers night counting towards no card. VERIFIED IN A BROWSER as well (193 checks, up from 122), because "the rule is readable from the sofa" is not something a unit test can see: the picker filtered per pack, a draw that never duplicates or escapes its pack, the strip on all three packs, the TV wall measured as larger than the board's own subline and positioned above it, a plain night rendering nothing, and the stat opening from behind the disclosure with the right numbers. THE TV WALL HAS A BUDGET, and finding out that it did not is the story of this entry's second commit: the first version was three-across boxes with the name over the rule, which cost 173px for a SINGLE card against a four-player layout that had 116px of slack — so one modifier pushed the footer off the bottom of a 1080p screen, and a television cannot be scrolled. The original checks asserted the wall was above the board and larger than its subline; both were true the whole time it was broken, because "it renders" and "it fits" are different questions. Fixed with a density ladder: one or two cards keep name and rule on ONE line at full size, three or more fall back to NAMES ONLY packed as chips. The cut at three is forced rather than chosen — three rules do not fit on one 1920px row (the longest cards run ~60 characters) and a second row costs ~135px, so the alternatives were covering the money board or ellipsing a rule mid-sentence, and a half-shown rule reads as complete. The full text stays one glance away on the strip on every phone. Now measured as fitting at every count from 0 to a pack's entire 16-card pool, pinned by six new checks, and the checks themselves were NEGATIVE-CONTROLLED by re-injecting the shipped geometry at runtime and confirming they fail on it.
- [x] STAKES: real money or play money, per session (2026-07-30, James's request; design decided 07-29). Every casino pack's setup screen now opens with a DROPDOWN — "What are the chips worth?" — and the choice is fixed for the night, exactly like who is banking, because a table cannot be half play money and changing it mid-session would retroactively re-denominate every buy-in already taken. It is a `<select>` rather than the segmented pills the other choices use, deliberately: this is the one setting a host should have to look at and decide, and two side-by-side pills read as a toggle you might fat-finger. It lives in the SHARED `CasinoSetup`, so all three packs (and poker) get it from one place. THE SPLIT RULE, and it is the whole feature: WINS AND PLACEMENTS UNIFY, ONLY MONEY SPLITS. A win is a win — you either finished the night up or you did not, and play money does not make that less true — so nights, win rate, streaks, rebuys, hours and nights banked are counted ONCE across both. Net, staked, averages, ROI, best/worst and net-per-hour are computed twice from the same rows and printed on one line each, because adding a $60 real net to an 80-play-chip one produces a number that means nothing. There is deliberately no combined net anywhere on the aggregate, so nothing can render one by accident. TWO SIGNALS mark play money, and both are deliberate: every amount carries a `P$` prefix (ASCII, so it survives a TV browser and a canvas recap card) and a violet "Play money" badge sits on the pack page and the TV header. The prefix is the precise signal; the badge is the one you catch from across a room without reading a number, which is the case the design was actually worried about. Up/down stay GREEN AND RED on a play table, because those colours mean direction rather than currency. NO SCHEMA CHANGE and no `games` row change: one row per pack, one leaderboard tab, `stats.ts` untouched. Stakes rides in `match_participants.meta` next to the money already there, and an ABSENT value means real — which is what every night recorded before this shipped carries, so a crew's whole history reads exactly as it did. The lifetime arithmetic moved into a pure `aggregateCashNights` in the shared engine, so the split is unit tested with no database anywhere near it, and `compareCashLifetime` orders the leaderboard REAL MONEY FIRST so a big play-money night can never outrank an actual one. 19 new tests (212 -> 231). The browser suite gained the invariant that matters most: on a play table, a `$` not preceded by a `P` is a call site somebody forgot to thread the stakes through, and that is now checked on the setup screen, the live table and the TV. Called "play money" in every piece of copy, never Monopoly — that is a trademark and this app is public. Deploy: nothing beyond the push.
- [x] Craps, and the shooter's hand (2026-07-30). Third cash pack, and the one that shows the shared layer paying off: the server file is ~140 lines, the page ~230, and neither re-implements a buy-in, a net, a placement or the balance check. THE ONE IDEA IT OWNS IS LONGEST ROLL — how many rolls a shooter survives before sevening out — which James called as a CREW-WIDE record rather than a personal stat, so it gets its own headline on the crew leaderboard naming the holder. Second detail stat is points made; biggest bet and biggest win are kept for consistency with the other two but are typed-only here, because this pack's tracker follows the DICE rather than the betting. THE TRACKER IS THREE TAPS: Roll, Point made (the hand continues), Seven out (closes the hand, records its length, passes the dice). A seven-out is NOT a roll, so "rolls survived before sevening out" means what it says and an instant seven-out is a hand of zero. HANDS ARE DERIVED FROM AN EVENT LOG, never maintained, which is the same call Smashdown's burn board made for the same reason: undo of a seven-out has to REOPEN the closed hand AND hand the dice back, and doing that by unwinding counters is right until it isn't. Here undo pops one event and re-derives, so it is correct by construction; the shooter is read straight off the popped event. Handing the dice on voluntarily is logged as a `pass` and closes the hand too, so twelve rolls count as twelve rolls whether or not they ended in a seven-out. Rotation is by roster order over players still at the table, so a cash-out mid-session is skipped and a shooter who cashes out still passes to the next SEAT rather than to the top of the list — and the host can hand the dice anywhere, because the rotation is a default and not a rule. THE LIVE READ EXCLUDES THE OPEN HAND so the TV's "to beat" number is a real target; completion includes it, because the night ending means the hand happened. TV: the shared `MoneyBoard` grew a `hero` slot (an extension, not a fork) and craps fills it with the shooter, their live roll count, and the night's longest hand beside it. 29 new tests (183 -> 212) on the hands, the undo and the rotation. THE DIE MOVED: 🎲 was Mario Party's and craps has the stronger claim, so Mario Party took ⭐ (which is its scoring unit anyway) — safe because emoji is derived from the registry and never stored. No schema change. Deploy: nothing beyond the push.
- [x] PACK PICKER GROUPS (2026-07-30). The picker is one list of ten tiles and was getting hard to scan, so it now carries a thin divider and a small caption per group: Nintendo, Casino, Bar and sports, Other. Grouping lives on `PACKS` in `apps/web/src/packs.ts` rather than in the shared `SESSION_PACKS` registry, and that is the load-bearing choice: the client list already holds things the registry does not — Tournament is not a registry pack, and Beerio Kart is a FORMAT under the Mario Kart tile — so the grouping has to be where all of them live together. `group` is DISPLAY ONLY, and the field says so in a comment: unlike ledger, gameName and keyPrefix it is never written to the database, never a join key and never in a URL, so it is safe to rename or reorder at any time. An EMPTY group renders nothing, so poker and pool appear in the right place by setting one field and touching nothing else.
- [x] Roulette, and the casino group's SHARED SCREENS (2026-07-29). The second cash pack, and the session that answered the question blackjack deliberately left open: with a real second example in hand, the money board, the setup screen, the live table and every money ROUTE were extracted, so roulette is a registry entry, a ~120-line server file, a ~220-line page and two detail stats. THE NUMBERS ARE THE ARGUMENT: `BlackjackPage` went from 17.6 kB to 5.0 kB and `RoulettePage` landed at 5.4 kB, because both are now copy, one cash-out field and a tracker on top of `apps/web/src/casino/` (CasinoSetup, CasinoTable, MoneyBoard, money.tsx, casino.css) and `apps/server/src/casino-runtime.ts`. Blackjack's own css dropped from ~200 lines to ~20: a casino pack's stylesheet is now a backdrop and a `--cg-*` token block, which is what lets two money boards read as different packs across a room (blackjack gold on oxblood, roulette teal on green felt) without forking any structure. THE ENGINE grew the generic half it was missing: `CashSummary<D>` / `summarizeCash` (per-player detail is the only thing that differs between packs), plus `CashPackState` and `newCashState`. TWO DETAIL STATS, deliberately different IN KIND, which is the group rule made concrete: FAVOURITE BET is reconstructable, so it is a typed field on the cash-out form AND derived from the tracker; MAX CONSECUTIVE WINNING SPINS is not — a streak is an ordering fact nobody remembers honestly at 1am — so it REQUIRES the tracker, is absent rather than zero when the tracker was off, and has deliberately NO box to type it into, because offering one would invite a guess into the ledger. A named test asserts that a `bestStreak` smuggled into a typed detail bag is ignored. Payouts are a property of the bet type (35:1 straight up down to 1:1 on the outside), so the tracker reports what a spin paid without the screen doing money arithmetic. STATS: one shared `CasinoPanel` on the crew leaderboard, since every free stat comes from buy-ins and cash-outs alone and is therefore identical across the group; each pack passes only its own detail line. 25 new tests (152 -> 177) in `packages/shared/tests/roulette.test.ts`, which deliberately does NOT retest the money — that split IS the point — plus the browser suite grown to 63 checks covering both packs. No schema change. Deploy: nothing beyond the push.
- [x] PER-PLAYER BUY-INS across the casino group (2026-07-29, James's request). The setup screen now carries an amount per SEAT, not just one table default, because the banker's float is nearly always different from everyone else's and a table where one person sits down with $100 and another with $20 is the normal case rather than an edge one. The behaviour that makes it feel right: a seat FOLLOWS the table default until you touch it, so a host who sets $20, adds four people and then changes their mind to $40 moves all four, and only the seats deliberately overridden stay put. Implemented with a React `key` carrying the default, so changing the default remounts an untouched input and re-seeds it while an overridden one is stable. The server half already existed (`buyIns`, keyed by roster index) and had shipped UNUSED with blackjack; this is the screen that finally reaches it. The setup screen also now states the total going on the table, which is the number a host actually sanity-checks before starting.
- [x] Blackjack, and the shared CASH-GAME ENGINE underneath it (2026-07-29). The first of the four casino packs, and the session was really about the engine: `packages/shared/src/cashgame.ts` owns buy-ins, rebuys, cash-outs, net, placement and the balance check for blackjack, roulette, craps and poker alike, so each pack file stays thin. See the CASINO GROUP section below for the design the other three inherit. MONEY IS INTEGER CENTS, everywhere, and that is not a style preference: the zero-sum check asks whether a table's nets sum to EXACTLY zero, and 3.33 + 3.33 + 3.34 is 10.000000000000002 in floating point, so a perfectly correct night would have reported itself broken. Cents in the state jsonb, cents in `match_participants.meta`, cents on the wire; dollars exist only at two edges (`parseCents` on the way in, `formatCents` on the way out) and the float canary test is written to fail if anyone reintroduces one. THE LEDGER UNIT IS THE SESSION: one completed night is ONE `matches` row plus one `match_participants` row per player, with placement DERIVED by ranking net descending (ties as co-placements at competition ranking, the Smashdown convention). That is what let this pack land with NO schema change and NO change to `stats.ts` at all — a cash night looks exactly like any other night to the leaderboard, the rivalry pages, the recap card and the profile aggregates. `character` stays null; money rides in meta. THE DERIVED BANKER is the piece worth understanding: on a player-banked table the banker's net is the exact inverse of everyone else's, computed and never typed, so the table sums to zero by construction. The BALANCE CHECK therefore is not checking the arithmetic, it is checking the banker's own count of their rack against what the players' numbers say it should be, and it reports the exact delta ("off by $17.25") with which direction is wrong. It is a WARNING, not a veto: the host is told and can still record it, because the app records what a home game did rather than refereeing it — what it must never do is materialize numbers it already knows disagree without saying so, hence the 409-then-force. The check is SKIPPED, not failed, until the banker has cashed out, because an absent count is not a count of zero, and treating it as one would report every table where the players finished up as broken. TWO INPUT PATHS: the cash-out screen (default, two interactions per player per night) and an opt-in live tracker (OFF by default, flippable mid-session) that logs per-hand bet and result and fills in biggest bet, biggest win and blackjacks automatically. The rule between them is that the tracker being off never loses a stat the form could capture, so all three details are also typed boxes on the cash-out form, prefilled from the tracker when it ran, and a typed value beats a derived one PER FIELD rather than per player. TV IS A LIVE MONEY BOARD: one line per player sorted by net, big green/red tokens, rebuy counts, the still-playing showing what they are in for rather than a fake net, and footer totals — built so roulette/craps/poker can reuse it by re-pointing four CSS tokens, deliberately NOT extracted into a shared component yet. FREE STATS from buy-in and cash-out alone, on the crew Stats tab: lifetime net, win rate as percent of nights finishing up, best and worst night, average buy-in, average net, rebuy rate, current and longest up-streak, ROI, and net per hour (session length rides in meta, because `matches.playedAt` is only the END of the night). Late arrivals can sit down mid-session, which a fixed roster would have forced into a second session for one person. 32 new tests (124 -> 156) on the pure engine in `packages/shared/tests/cashgame.test.ts`, plus 35 browser checks against the real built bundle with a stub API, because "the money board is legible" and "the warning is loud" are not things a unit test sees. NO SCHEMA CHANGE. Deploy: nothing beyond the push.
- [x] Beerio Kart joins guest linking, FORWARD-ONLY (2026-07-27). Beerio was the one pack the backfill could not reach: every other pack persists its full roster and results server-side, but Beerio reports a finished tournament fire-and-forget and the completion route kept only the names matching a crew member, discarding the guest names outright. Fix is at the source, not a recovery: completion now stores the FULL standings list, guests included, on a new nullable matches.rawResult jsonb ({ placements: [{ name, place }] }), and Beerio gets the same two adapters every other pack has (guestNamesBeerio / creditGuestBeerio in beerio-gn.ts, registered in guest-link.ts). SCOPE: only Beerio nights completed AFTER this deploy can ever be linked. Earlier nights are genuinely unrecoverable, and beerio_sessions.state was rejected as a source (the vendored engine's opaque shape, keyed by a reusable live-session code with no link to a finished match), so nothing is reconstructed and no credit is fabricated. The member-matching loop was extracted into one shared creditBeerioPlacements helper so the live path and the backfill can never drift; the live route calls it with no link map and behaves exactly as before. The backfill reopens the match that already exists (never creates one), passes an EMPTY member map so only the linked guest is credited (confirm writes exactly what preview showed, and a member who joined after that night is never quietly credited too), skips nights the member already has via memberCreditedKeys on the match's externalKey, and inserts ON CONFLICT (matchId,userId) DO NOTHING, so re-running credits nothing new. Preview label and date come from the completion key (b| = Bracket, g| = Grand Prix, with the embedded date, falling back to the event's scheduled date). Owner/admin gate is inherited from the shared guard; confirm's existing leaderboard_updated broadcast covers Beerio with no change. The vendored 1:1 beerio.ts was not touched. Schema deploy: db:push:ci applies the column; fallback ALTER TABLE matches ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS raw_result jsonb.
- [x] Beerio Kart: 1:1 replica of the standalone app, bound to lifetime stats. Double elim + Grand Prix, spectator predictions, its own TV mode, live room bound to the event.
- [x] Smash Bros, Session A: FFA Night (2-8) + King of the Hill + character system (self-select / random / host-assign, full Ultimate roster) + its own TV mode + lifetime character stats.
- [x] Smash Bros, Session B: double-elimination surfaced in the Smash section and the generic tournament tracker.
- [x] Smashdown, a fourth Smash format (2026-07-28). Ultimate's own mode: a fixed number of battles where every fighter used is struck from the roster for the rest of the series, most wins takes it. NOT A NEW PACK — no registry entry, no route segment, no server file, no ledger key: `SmashFormat` gained a fourth member and everything else rode the Smash pack that already existed. ONE CODE PATH for 1v1 and 2-8 player series, because a battle IS an FFA game the whole roster plays, which is also what makes the cap arithmetic true. THE CAP is the part that matters more than it sounds: `floor(rosterSize / playerCount)` for the SELECTED title, so four players get 21 battles in Ultimate (86 fighters) and THREE in Smash 64 (12), and the setup screen says so as a plain sentence up front rather than rejecting a number after the host has typed it. A cap of 0 (more players than a title can feed) is a real answer and blocks the start with a sentence naming the title. THE BURN BOARD is shared, server-side, and is only ever WRITTEN by `burnedFrom(games)` — one derivation, so undo cannot leave it disagreeing with the log. Undo drops the battle, re-derives the board, and hands the undone battle's fighters back TO THE PLAYERS WHO USED THEM, so a mis-tap is replayed rather than reassigned. All three assignment modes (self / random / host) exclude the burn board AND the fighters already taken in the current battle, enforced on the server as well as the client because every mode funnels through one route and a burned pick slipping through would silently un-strike it. After a battle the picks clear and a random-assignment session rolls the next set out of what is left — except on the last battle, where they stay so every screen can still show what the series finished on. MERCY RULE implemented as a host toggle, DEFAULT OFF, and settable mid-series: it ends the series once one player's total exceeds every other player's plus all remaining battles. Strictly exceeds, deliberately — when the best a chaser can do is draw level the series is still live, because a tie is a co-win here. A clinch is reported even with mercy off, so the screen can say the lead is unbeatable without ending anything. TIES are co-winners at competition ranking (two on 1, next on 3). NEW STAT, "unique fighters won with", derived from ledger data that already existed (distinct `character` on a winning line) with no new column: on the crew Stats page's Smash panel and in the night standings on the pack page. TV VIEW is its own screen rather than a panel on the FFA one, since the burn board is the format: fighters-left count at 7vmin, the full-width struck-through board, standings and the current fighters. STATS: each battle materializes exactly like an FFA game, `format = "smashdown"` on the matches row, fighter on `match_participants.character`. NO SERIES LEDGER ROW — see NEXT UP item 1 for the deferred half and why splitting it is safe. TESTS: 21 new (86 -> 107) on the pure rules in `packages/shared/tests/smashdown.test.ts`: burn accumulation and the exact undo, the cap across four title/player combinations plus the zero case, exclusion (500 random draws never returning a burned or taken fighter, and 200 four-player assignments out of a four-fighter pool), the mercy boundary in both directions, and winner resolution with a clean winner, a two-way tie and a three-way tie. VERIFIED IN A BROWSER as well, against the real built bundle plus a stub API, because "the cap reads as a sentence" and "the burn board is legible" are not things a unit test can see: 24 checks across the setup screen, a live series waiting on fighters, a series midway with a six-fighter board, a finished series with co-winners, and the TV view in both states. No schema change. Deploy: nothing beyond the push.
- [x] Smashdown SERIES rows: winning a series is now its own stat (2026-07-28, the deferred half of the entry above, shipped same day in its own cycle). A finished series writes ONE extra ledger row labeled `smashdown`, alongside the per-battle rows rather than instead of them, and it is surfaced as "series won" on the crew leaderboard's expanded row, both personal profiles, the rivalry compare and the Smash panel. THE WHOLE DANGER IS DOUBLE COUNTING: every other match-as-unit row in this app (a Best Of set, a Ping Pong match) is the only row its games produce, so counting it counts each game once; a Smashdown series row summarizes battles that are ALREADY in the ledger, so anything that counts games and forgets to skip it hands every player a phantom game per series and the winner a phantom win, silently and forever. So the exclusion lives in ONE predicate (`isSeriesSummary`, shared) and, for the aggregation, in ONE place: the top of `feedAgg`, which all five call sites already go through, so a sixth inherits it instead of forgetting it. The tallies that live OUTSIDE feedAgg were hunted down and are named in the code: the crew leaderboard's per-format buckets and its completed-match count, /me/stats' per-format and per-crew rollups, the rivalry MEETING map (a series would otherwise be an extra head-to-head encounter on top of every battle in it), the night-recap rollup (it shares its battles' sessionKey by design, so it would have landed in their unit and reported "won 4 of 6" for a five-battle series), and the Smash panel's fighter/streak/h2h loops. PLUMBING: `ResultRow` gained `label`, selected by all four queries (two through the shared `resultCols`); `Agg` gained `seriesWins`/`seriesPlayed`, returned nested as `series` so a crew with none can hide it. THE ROW: label `smashdown`, character null (a series is not played with one fighter), co-winners all on placement 1, per-player battle wins in meta, externalKey `smash:{eventId}:{sessionKey}:series` — a literal tail, so it can never collide with a battle whatever the battle count does, while still grouping with its battles on sessionKey. `materializeUnit` gained one optional `keyUnit` for that; position still comes from idx, which is NOT NULL. WRITTEN BY RECONCILING, NOT PATCHING: `syncSeriesRow` asks whether the ledger agrees with `smashdownStatus` right now, and both halves are idempotent, so it is called from every route where `over` can flip — the battle that ends the series, an undo that takes it back, the MERCY TOGGLE (which can end a series with nothing played, the case a "write it on the last battle" rule silently misses), and complete. An abandoned series writes nothing, deliberately: unlike a Best Of set, its battles are all already recorded, so there is nothing to rescue and no honest winner to name. Guest linking credits the series row too, additively. NO BACKFILL ROUTE, and that is a decision rather than an omission: see the decision log. 11 new tests (107 -> 118). Read layer plus one new row type; no schema change. Deploy: nothing beyond the push.
- [x] Smash Bros Best Of format (2026-07-21): explicit format picker (Free For All / King of the Hill / Best Of). Best Of is a 1v1 set: pick two players, tap the winner of each game, the SET materializes once when it is won (one matches row labeled bo{N}, winner placement 1 / loser 2, fighter on character, per-player game wins in meta) via the shared series primitive. FFA and KOTH unchanged (still game-as-unit).
- [x] Mario Kart general tracking pack, now four formats (2026-07-21): Free Play (single races), Grand Prix (a cup of N races on cumulative Mario Kart points, each race materialized game-as-unit with the cup id on matches.label, cups derived by chunking the games log, cup standings read-derived and never a ledger row), Best Of (1v1 race sets, match-as-unit), King of the Hill (ported rotation). Its own MkSessionState reuses the shared primitives (series engine, kothAdvance, FFA path, Smash session factory) but the two packs stay separate. Beerio Kart is untouched.
- [x] Mario Party pack: one recorded game = one board. Pick the board, enter each player's total stars, winner is the most stars (a top tie prompts the host to tap the winner, since coins aren't tracked); optional per-player bonus stars with one exclusive owner each. Own theme, own TV view, stats tab. Schema: matches.label = board, match_participants.meta = bonus stars.
- [x] Title-scoped character selection (cross-pack): a "Which game?" selector scopes both the picker and the random pool to that title's roster. Smash: 64, Melee, Brawl, Smash 4, Ultimate. Mario Kart: MK8 Deluxe, Mario Kart World, Wii, Double Dash, MK64. Stats stay unified by character name. No schema change (titleId lives in session jsonb).
- [x] Generic bracket tracker + its TV mode + recap card (the pack-agnostic path).
- [x] Ping Pong pack (2026-07-20). Two formats: King of the Hill (winner stays, challenger line rotates per match) and Singles (host picks the two players each match). A match is free play (each single game is its own recorded result) or best-of-3/5/7, chosen at start; recording a game is one tap on the winner, the loser's points are optional (typed then tapped, never a blocking field). Best-of matches end automatically at the required game wins; in Singles free play the same two players stay teed up so each game is one tap, with a "Change players" control to switch opponents. Its own green-felt/orange-ball theme and its own TV view. Night stats: matches, wins, win rate, individual game wins, current + best win streak, and KOTH longest king reign. Lifetime crew stats panel (StatsPage, Ping Pong tab): single-game total wins (counting every individual game, including the four inside a won best of seven and each free-play game) and match wins split by format (free play / best of 3 / 5 / 7). Match wins by format read from matches.label; per-player game wins/played ride match_participants.meta. No character selector (no characters in ping pong). Doubles is explicitly out (no team model). Storage: generic game_sessions keyed by (eventId, pack='pingpong'); match_participants.meta already exists (Mario Party uses it); no schema change.
- Ping Pong format picker reorg (2026-07-21): the setup screen is one Format choice (Free Play / Best Of / King of the Hill) instead of two separate mode + match-length controls. Free Play = singles single-game, Best Of = singles series (3/5/7), KOTH = single game per match, winner stays on (its old best-of length option was dropped). Same engine and ledger underneath (PpFormat expands to mode + bestOf); both entry points (Home quick-play and the crew event launcher) offer the three formats.
- Shared primitives available to any new pack: FFA/placement roster, King of the Hill rotation (kothAdvance), best-of 1v1 series engine (series.ts: newSeries/recordSeriesGame/finalizeSeries/seriesGameTally/summarizeSeriesLog), single + double elim bracket engine.

Blind levels are a wall-clock timer shared across devices, which is a live-sync problem rather than a money one; `poker:tourney` is reserved in formats.ts and joins the table on the commit that writes it. The original entry: **ANSWERS LOCKED 2026-08-17** (James): both formats eventually, cash game first; NO hand-to-hand tracking, buy-ins and final stacks only; lifetime stats carry both, with PROFIT as the headline; real stakes are the default and `play` is the per-session opt-out; the variant (Hold'em, Omaha, Stud, dealer's choice) rides `nowPlaying` on the title-night pattern rather than the Casino Run modifier system; identity is OXBLOOD, which is unclaimed (blackjack #1a120f, roulette #0d2a20, craps #0e1e38, Casino Run #1a1030 are all taken). **THE TOURNAMENT FORMAT IS ITS OWN SESSION**, because blind levels are a wall-clock timer shared across devices, which is a live-sync problem rather than a money one, and mixing it in is how the cash game ends up half-finished. The original entry: same engine and same screens, and LAST of the four CASH packs on purpose (Casino Run shipped before it and does not change that: it is a fifth casino pack but not a cash one, and shares no engine with them): it is the only one that carries a SECOND format on top of the net-based one. A `tournament` format is placement-based rather than net-based (one buy-in, a finishing position, a payout), which the cash engine does not model, so poker needs both paths in one pack. Pack-specific on the cash side: biggest pot and a bad-beat note.

Read those before starting: the answer is the same cloth at a different tint, one tile named once in `--gn-felt-tile`, and a pack composing its OWN layer list because a custom property containing a var() resolves where it is declared. The casino group's four tints (deep green, oxblood, royal, charcoal) are settled and need no further design work; what remains per pack is a tint decision measured against that pack's own accent on its lit crown, a token block, its pairs in `theme-contrast.test.ts`, its baize in `generate-felt-tile.mjs`'s guard list, and a line in `copy-rules.test.ts`. Ping Pong is converted and the mechanism is settled and written down in the DECISION LOG; the nine that follow apply it and should not re-derive it. **IT WAS EIGHT UNTIL 2026-08-04**, when Board Game shipped unconverted; that pack also carries the group's identity problem in its sharpest form, because it is walnut and brass under Arcade already and Tabletop's base is walnut and brass, so it is the one to settle the "identity from something other than the base material" question on if the casino group does not get there first. **NEXT: THE CASINO GROUP**, and it is nearly free: four packs, one `--cg-*` contract, roughly twenty lines each. **ONE DESIGN PROBLEM TO SETTLE BEFORE STARTING IT:** Tabletop's base is already felt and brass, so a casino pack re-treated in felt and brass dissolves into the shell and the four stop reading as four. Every pack's surface has to carry the cloth itself, and the 2026-08-17 correction to this sentence is that it must NOT read `--gn-texture`: that token resolves at `:root` and carries the shell's own `--gn-felt-lit`, so a pack reading it lights its table in the shell's green. It composes its own list out of `--gn-felt-tile`, `--gn-lamp-geom` and `--gn-lamp-fade` plus its own colours, which is roughly ten lines each and a decision about tint per pack — settle it once, on the pack the casino group converts first, rather than nine times. **PER-PACK CHECKLIST, from the pilot:** add the pack to `TOKENISED_CSS` in `copy-rules.test.ts` on the same commit that converts it, add its contrast pairs and its identity assertion to `theme-contrast.test.ts`, put the cloth on the pack's own surface, and run `node scripts/theme-sweep.mjs` under both themes (its rule pass already visits one route per pack). **TWO FOLLOW-UPS THE PILOT DELIBERATELY DID NOT DO:** the nine `--gn-cab-*` cabinet tokens still live in `index.css` and their own comment says stage 4 moves them onto the packs, but doing it inside a pack conversion mixes a shell refactor into a pack change and makes the identical-under-Arcade result ambiguous, so it wants its own commit (note that Ping Pong's `--pp-edge`/`--pp-card` are the SAME VALUES as `--gn-cab-pp-edge`/`--gn-cab-pp-top`, so the identity is currently written down twice); and the canvas share cards (`RECAP` in `recap.tsx`, `C` in `MemberPage.tsx`) follow no theme at all, which is a different rendering path and its own session, logged as a Watch trap in BUGS.

## FEATURES TO ADD
Wanted, not yet scheduled into a session.
- [x] CLOSED AS A FINDING, NOT A GAP: **SALEM 1692'S CONSPIRACY IS ALREADY RECORDABLE** (checked 2026-08-14). Conspiracy shifts loyalties mid-game: a townsperson who gains a Witch card joins the Witches and stays a witch. The deal happens at the start, so a player who flips is dealt one faction and finishes in another, and the obvious worry is that the deal defines the result. IT DOES NOT: the record route takes `memberIds` FROM THE REQUEST BODY rather than deriving them server-side, and the record form gives every player a tappable chip per faction, so the deal only PREFILLS. The host moves the flipped player and the ledger comes out right, with `meta.role` keeping the role they were DEALT and `meta.faction` carrying the side they FINISHED on, which is a truthful record of a flip rather than a contradiction. Pinned by a test and said out loud in the form's copy. **THIS IS THE SAME FAMILY AS LOVERS BELOW AND THE OPPOSITE ANSWER**, which is why both are written down: Conspiracy changes WHICH FACTION a player is on, and the model already stores that per game; Lovers would give a player a SECOND ROLE, and the model stores one.
- [ ] **LOVERS (CUPID), AND THE REASON IT IS NOT A BIGGER VERSION OF TYPED ROLES** (scoped out 2026-08-10). Cupid's lovers KEEP THEIR ORIGINAL ROLES and additionally win as a new pair, so a player would hold TWO roles and a faction would be formed AT DEAL TIME. Today a player has exactly one role and their faction is DERIVED from it (`factionsFromRoles`), which is the assumption the whole result path rests on: the record form ranks factions, `sdPlacements` writes one `side` per player, and `meta.role` is a single value. Lovers breaks all three at once, so it is a different feature rather than a larger one, and it should be scoped on its own merits. **THE WITCH IS NOT THIS**, which is why she shipped: she is a role on a solo faction whose win happens to coincide with another faction's, and `tiedWithAbove` expresses that with no model change at all.
- [x] CLOSED AS A FINDING, NOT A GAP: **CARD TABLE AND BOARD GAME NEED NOTHING FROM THE ROLE-CATALOGUE PASS** (checked 2026-08-10 rather than assumed). The asymmetry that pass existed to fix (an OPEN title space against a CLOSED per-title list that reaches the ledger) does not exist in the title-night packs. Their only per-title data is Card Table's PARTNERSHIP DEFAULTS, which are Euchre and Spades opening at 2 sides, and that is a DEFAULT the host overrides in one tap, with a free-typed title simply getting none. Board Game has no per-title data at all, closed as its own finding on 2026-08-09. Neither has a closed list that reaches `matches.label` or `meta`, so neither can write a false record the way a missing role could. **PER-TITLE EXTRAS STAY OUT** (Hearts' moon shot, Euchre loners, a cribbage skunk). If they are ever wanted, the pattern is the partnership default: a hint, never required, and never a closed list that reaches the record.
- [x] SHIPPED 2026-08-17: **TOURNAMENT ENTRANTS ARE NO LONGER LOCKED TO YES-RSVP.** The gap was never the engine: `BracketEntrant` already held members and guests and `guest-link.ts` already credited bracket guests. It was that the tournament had NO ROSTER SCREEN AT ALL, so `startBracket` POSTed `{ format }` and the server built the entrants out of the yes list. **WHAT LANDED:** a new route `/tournament?event=<id>&format=<f>`, matching every pack's address shape, holding the roster block Board Game and Card Table have had since they shipped: prefill from the yes RSVPs in answer order, add any crew member, a guest box, remove, and a shuffle on the seeding. The crew list is `rsvps` plus `noResponse` off the EXISTING event payload, whose union is the membership, so no tournament-context endpoint was needed and none was added. The picker tile navigates there and the screen creates the bracket, then REPLACES into `/b/:id`. **THE SILENT FAILURE THE ENTRY NAMED IS CLOSED AT THE SERVER, not just avoided in the UI:** `normalizeEntrants` verifies every member id against the crew and REJECTS an unknown one rather than downgrading it to a guest, because a downgrade means somebody plays a whole tournament, places, and their record never hears about it, with nothing to error about. It also rejects a duplicate member instead of deduping (the screen cannot produce one, so a repeat means the body did not come from the screen), trims guest names to 24, and caps a bracket at 2 to 32 entrants, which the endpoint had no cap for at all before. `GET /api/events/:id` now orders its rsvps by `respondedAt`, which "first in, top seed" had been quietly assuming and Postgres was under no obligation to provide. The `< 2 yes RSVPs` 400 is now `< 2 entrants`, and the format tiles no longer print a yes-count. A request with NO `entrants` key still falls back to the yes list: not a supported path, insurance for an installed PWA running a cached bundle on a game night.
- [x] CLOSED AS A FINDING, NOT A GAP: **BOARD GAME GETS NO PARTNERSHIP DEFAULTS AT ALL** (James, 2026-08-09). This was open as "title-driven defaults on Board Game", with the work described as "the judgement about which of its thirteen curated titles have a partnership default, which may honestly be none of them". The judgement was made and the answer is NONE OF THEM: not one of Catan, Ticket to Ride, Wingspan, Carcassonne, Azul, Monopoly, Risk, Scrabble, Clue, Pandemic, 7 Wonders, Splendor or Dominion is a partnership game by default. **THAT IS A RESULT RATHER THAN AN OMISSION, and it is written here so nobody reopens it as unfinished work.** The capability is present and costs the pack nothing: `TitleNightConfig.partnerships` is optional, Board Game declares no table, every title resolves to free-for-all, and the auto-apply guard makes every call a no-op including the sideSets log. There is a test named for exactly this (`Board Game declares no partnerships, so nothing about it moves`), so the day somebody adds a partnership title to that list they will find out whether they meant to. The moment to revisit is a NEW title, not a rethink of the thirteen. (Pandemic is the one title in the list with a shape the app models wrongly, and that is the co-op item below, which is a different question: it is one side, not two.)
- [ ] SAME PLAYERS AS THE LAST GAME. On a night that runs three packs the host builds the same roster three times, by hand, from the same list. The event already knows which sessions ran on it, so the setup screen can offer the PREVIOUS session's roster on this event, guests included, as one tap: "same players as Ping Pong" beside the prefill that is already there. Cheap, because every pack's roster block already takes a prefill and every session already carries its roster; what is new is reading the last one off the event rather than off the RSVPs. **AND ONE ADJACENT IDEA, FOUND WHILE SCOPING THIS AND WORTH MORE THAN THE FEATURE ITSELF:** rosters prefill from the yes-RSVP list, while `event_attendance` separately records who actually SHOWED. So once a night has started the app knows better than the RSVPs do, and the prefill could fall back down that chain instead: last session's roster, then who showed, then who said yes. The RSVP list is the weakest of the three and it is the only one used today.
- [ ] GUEST NAME MEMORY PER CREW. Typed guests are per session and nothing remembers them, so "Mike" and "mike" and "Mike D" are three people to the app and one person to the crew, and the guest-link backfill becomes a hunt through spellings that should have been one chip. Suggest previously typed guest names ON THIS CREW as tappable chips beside the guest box, newest first. It needs no schema change (the names are already in the ledger on past matches) and no new concept: it is the recent-titles pattern Board Game and Card Table already use for game names, pointed at guests instead. **IT MAKES AN EXISTING FEATURE MATERIALLY BETTER RATHER THAN ADDING ONE**, which is the argument for it: guest linking already works, and this is what stops it having to.
- [ ] CONNECTION STATE PILL: "reconnecting" on the scoring screens. **VERIFIED BY READING THE FILE RATHER THAN ASSUMED:** `apps/web/src/useLiveUpdates.ts` reconnects on close with `retry = setTimeout(connect, 3000)` and the hook RETURNS NOTHING. There is no status to render, so no screen in this app is able to say whether it is connected, which means a host on bad wifi taps confirm and has no way to tell whether it landed. The fix is small in the hook (return a status alongside what it already does) and shared in the UI (one pill, on the scoring screens). **SCOPED SMALL BUT NOT A RIDE-ALONG:** it touches every pack page's shell, which is the same shape as every other "one line in nine places" change in this file, so it wants its own session rather than being bolted onto a pack change.
- [ ] REPEAT OR DUPLICATE AN EVENT. `events` has no recurrence column and there is no duplicate action, so a weekly game night is retyped every week. **THE CHEAPEST USEFUL VERSION IS NOT A RECURRENCE ENGINE**, and that is the whole point of writing it down this way: "duplicate this event, next week, same time" is one button, one POST and no schema change, and it covers the weekly-night case that motivated it. A real recurrence model (rules, exceptions, "every other Thursday", editing one instance of a series) is a much larger feature that should have to justify itself separately, against a crew that has actually outgrown the button.
- [ ] LOCATION AND NOTES ON AN EVENT. **NEITHER COLUMN EXISTS TODAY, verified against `packages/db/src/schema.ts`:** `events` carries id, groupId, title, scheduledFor, status, createdBy and the two Beerio room fields, and nothing about where the night is or what to bring. Two nullable text columns plus two fields on the event form and two lines on the event page. **THIS IS A SCHEMA CHANGE, so it ships with explicit idempotent Neon SQL** (`ALTER TABLE events ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS location text;` and the same for `notes`) called out in that session's closeout and run against the database, rather than left to `drizzle-kit push --force`, which silently no-ops in non-interactive CI and is already a Watch trap in BUGS. Gates the geotagged-arrival idea under IDEAS, which needs somewhere to put a venue.
- [ ] RSVP NUDGE: "4 haven't answered" plus a button. The event payload ALREADY carries `noResponse` (`apps/server/src/events.ts` builds it, and `EventPage` already renders it as a "No answer yet" list), and the event page ALREADY has a share control with the copy-link fallback. So this is a count, a sentence and a button wired to what is there: no new endpoint, no new query, no schema change. Written down as its own line because it is the smallest item in this section and keeps being mistaken for part of a bigger notifications feature, which it is not: it sends nothing, it just makes the host's nudge one tap instead of five.
- [ ] **FIX OR DELETE A COMPLETED RESULT. WRITTEN AS A QUESTION TO ANSWER FIRST, NOT AS A FEATURE TO BUILD**, and the session that picks it up answers it before designing a single screen. Undo works INSIDE a live session and retracts from the ledger correctly, which is established and tested. What is NOT established is whether a junk result that has already materialized can be removed at all without opening the Neon console: a host who records a game against the wrong player, or records the same game twice, and only notices the next morning. **WHAT SCOPING FOUND SO FAR, offered as a starting point rather than as the answer:** `deleteMaterialized` exists in `apps/server/src/pack-runtime.ts`, keyed by (eventId, sessionKey, idx), so the PRIMITIVE is there; what is unconfirmed is whether any ROUTE reaches it once a session is no longer live, and whether the pack-side session state a correction would also have to fix is still addressable at that point. **IF NOTHING CAN REACH A MATERIALIZED MATCH, THAT IS THE FINDING AND IT RAISES THE PRIORITY**, because it means the ledger this whole app is built on is append-only by accident rather than by decision, and every crew's lifetime stats carry their mistakes permanently.
- [ ] CO-OPERATIVE TITLES. PANDEMIC IS ALREADY IN BOARD GAME'S CURATED LIST and it is fully co-op: one side, everybody on it, win or lose together. `validateSides` requires at least two sides, so the primitive as written does not model it, and the pack currently opens Pandemic free-for-all and records a tapped finish order for a game where everybody wins or loses together. Wrong, known, and deliberately not bodged. Casino Run already records this exact shape (one shared side for everyone, and `meetingOutcome` already classifies those players as having played `together` rather than tied), so the precedent exists and THE LEDGER NEEDS NOTHING NEW. What needs deciding is whether `teams.ts` relaxes the two-side minimum for a co-op result, or a co-op night models THE GAME as the opposing side. Found while scoping Card table on 2026-08-05; the recommendation then was log-do-not-build, because that session was already carrying an extraction and a conversion.
- [ ] SMASH 2v2 TEAM BATTLES. A real Ultimate mode and the obvious first team retrofit: the pack already seats up to eight, already assigns characters, and `teams.ts` already owns the placement rule. Applies to the FFA format first. **SCOPE IT PROPERLY BEFORE STARTING**, because Smash carries four formats and they do not all take sides the same way: Best Of is already head-to-head, KOTH has Ping Pong's worked example to copy, and SMASHDOWN IS THE INTERESTING ONE, because its burn board strikes a fighter per player per battle and whether a PAIR burns two fighters or a shared one changes the cap arithmetic (`floor(rosterSize / playerCount)`) that the setup screen prints as a sentence. Do not assume; ask.
- [x] SHIPPED 2026-08-17: **TEAM ENTRANTS ON THE GENERIC BRACKET (doubles tournaments).** Priced as the structurally expensive one and it was not, because of the one decision that made it small: **A TEAM ENTRANT IS ONE SLOT.** `entrants.length` stays the number of SLOTS, so `buildStructure`, `seedOrder`, `computeBracket` and `placements` were not touched at all; a bracket of eight pairs is the same eight-slot bracket the engine has always drawn. What the third kind cost was everything that READS an entrant, which is now uniform: `entrantMembers` flattens all three kinds (a solo entrant is a list of one), `entrantLabel` gives the display string (an unnamed team reads as its members joined with " + "), and `hasTeamEntrants` answers the side question. **PLACEMENT NEEDED NO NEW RULE.** `placements()` ranks entrants 1..N, which over team entrants IS rule 2 in `teams.ts`: a pair out in the semis is placement 3 for both. Competition ranking was deliberately not reached for and the two rules stay separate. **WHAT LANDED:** the type and `parseEntrants` (still upgrading legacy bare-userId rows); `normalizeEntrants` enforcing 2 to 8 members a team, refusing a team of ONE (a side of one is a plain solo entrant) and refusing one person on two sides; `deriveView` emitting `members` on EVERY slot, so a solo slot's is a list of one with its own userId and displayName and every existing consumer kept working untouched; a pure `bracketLedgerRows` that materialize now just adds ids to; guest linking walking into team members (without it a guest inside a pair was unlinkable forever and nothing errored); and a doubles toggle on the setup screen reusing `TeamPicker`. **NO SCHEMA CHANGE:** `brackets.entrants` is jsonb and `match_participants.side` has existed since 2026-08-02. Bracket teammates now classify as `together` in `meetingOutcome` for free, which is correct and wanted; surfacing it is PARTNER STATS SURFACE and still its own session. **ONE FIT FINDING, MEASURED:** the bracketed TV at SIXTEEN pairs runs past 1080p. Logged under BUGS; eight pairs fits in all four states.
- [x] SHIPPED 2026-08-22: **PARTNER STATS SURFACE.** `partnersFor` in `apps/server/src/stats.ts` is one SQL self-join beside `finishAggDeep`, wired into `/me/stats`, the crew member profile and the friend equivalent, and deliberately NOT into the crew leaderboard (`finishAgg` is sync and query-free because that page calls it per player). "Who you win with" is a top-level section on the member profile and on `/me/stats`. `PARTNER_MIN_GAMES = 3` on best and worst, none on most-played-with. **THE ENTRY ABOVE SAID TO PUT IT "beside the existing nemesis/victim pattern" AND THAT PATTERN DOES NOT EXIST** — grep finds no `nemesis` or `victim` anywhere in the app; they are a candidate on the Home block entry below, never built, and they stay one. **WHAT SHIPPED WRONG AND WAS CORRECTED THE SAME DAY:** the first version counted every matching non-null `side`, which meant Casino Run co-op runs and Social Deduction factions were counted as partnerships. See the DECISION LOG: `side` means three different things and only one of them is this feature. Headline figures now count COMPETITIVE TEAM sides only. **STILL NOT DONE, deliberately:** a crew-wide partner table on the Stats page (member profile first, same call that was made for Mario Kart racers), and any co-op or faction line, which would be a SEPARATE labelled line rather than folded into this average.
- [ ] REVISIT THE MODIFIER WALL'S CUT AT THREE, now that the room exists. `ModifierWall` drops rule text at three cards and shows names only, and its own comment says the fix, if the rules should survive to a higher count, "is not here: it is the money board scaling with player count, which would buy the room". That shipped 2026-08-02 (see BUGS), and the comment was right: measured on the real bundle at 1920x1080, a wall-bearing board now has 95px of slack at 3 seats with 2 cards, 105px at 8 seats, 81px at 12 seats with 5 cards, against a second wall row costing roughly 40px at names density and 68px at roomy. So a three-card wall with its rules intact would fit at most counts. DELIBERATELY NOT DONE IN THE FIT SESSION: it is a SECOND decision about what belongs on a television, not a consequence of the first, and it is not free either: a two-row wall costs more than the one board line the money board's `rules` weight currently charges for it, so raising the cut means re-measuring the ladder with the wall's new height and probably splitting that weight by card count. Scope it on its own merits: is a rule everyone can read from the sofa worth a band of density on the board it sits above?
- [ ] SINGLE ACTIVE PACK + bounce-everyone-to-the-hub (the half of the 2026-07-25 unified-TV request that was deliberately NOT built on 07-28; the TV half shipped, see SHIPPED — FOUNDATION). Still open if wanted: (1) starting a pack "fully" makes it THE active pack for the event, and starting another while one is live is confirm-and-replace across packs, never two live at once; (2) on start or switch, every client currently in the event, on any screen, is bounced back (replace nav) to the event page as the hub. Both were dropped from the TV session because the TV no longer needs them: auto-follow reads what is actually happening instead of a pointer someone has to maintain, and nothing has to be enforced for the screen to be right. They are now a separate question about how PHONES should behave during a pack switch, which is worth deciding on its own merits rather than as TV plumbing. The event-page half of the original ask (item 3, a live indicator on the active pack tile) DID ship: every pack tile now says "live now" and the TV button names what is on.
- [x] SHIPPED 2026-08-22: **DETAILED PERSONAL STATS BLOCK ON HOME** (requested 2026-07-21). The account row now stacks on a phone and holds the panel in the right third above `sm`. It renders lifetime wins / win rate / played, `best` and `avgPlacement` (which the endpoint has returned since 2026-07-27 and Home had never printed), and `FormStatsCard` for streak, best streak, nights and the last five. No new request and no server change: same `/api/me/stats`, same `useCachedApi`, same `me:stats` key `MyStatsPage` uses. **IT IMPORTS THE STATS PAGE'S OWN COMPONENTS RATHER THAN RE-DERIVING THEIR FIELDS**, which is a DECISION LOG entry of its own after the first version hand-rolled them. **AN EARLIER DRAFT OF THIS ENTRY CLAIMED `best` AND `avgPlacement` WERE RENDERED NOWHERE. FALSE:** `MyStatsPage` has rendered both as tiles, plus `FormStatsCard`, `ShowUpRecord` and `CharacterStatsCard`, the whole time. The true and narrower claim was always that HOME rendered wins and win rate only. **STILL NOT DONE, and both are LENGTH calls rather than value ones:** attendance (`ShowUpRecord`, 117px on a phone and 184px in the 223px desktop column) and the main character (`CharacterStatsCard` renders a full table, which is a page section rather than a panel row). Both are unchanged and in full on `/me/stats`. NEMESIS AND VICTIM are still candidates on this line and still do not exist.
- [ ] Mario Kart CREW-WIDE racer table on the Stats page: wins by racer across everyone (not per player) plus racer head-to-head, the two pieces of Smash's bespoke SmashPanel that the shared leaderboard does not cover. The per-player half of this request shipped 2026-07-27: the crew leaderboard already carries each member's main, best racer and top three racers, on the overall tab and inside each game tab.
