Build the crew-wide table only if the per-player view turns out not to be enough. (Was bundled with a Smash+Mario Kart unify idea, dropped: the two packs stay separate on purpose and only share primitives, per James 2026-07-21.)
- [ ] Mario Party: minigame head-to-heads as a second format in the pack, alongside Board night (requested 2026-07-18). Track who beats who in individual minigames; the game picker already supports multi-format packs, so this slots in as a second format entry.
- [ ] Per-route dynamic link previews (deferred 2026-07-23). Today the whole app shares one static og-image (the brand card). A shared crew or event link could unfurl with its own preview (crew name, event date, "who's in"), but that needs server-rendered per-route OG tags or an image endpoint, which means touching the SPA fallback in apps/server/src/index.ts. Left out of the link-preview session on purpose; the static brand card covers the common case.
- [ ] Smack talk feed (on the TV view and/or in-app)
- [x] SHIPPED 2026-08-22: **STATS ON THE TV VIEW (leaderboard between matches).** Amended in the same commit that shipped it rather than left to read 0% done, which is the point of the DECISION LOG entry beside it: THIS LINE WAS ALREADY PARTLY TRUE WHEN IT WAS WRITTEN and never said so. `EventTvPage`'s `NightSoFar` had shown tonight's standings and the latest results between every game since the one-TV-button session. What actually landed on 08-22: a density ladder replacing two hardcoded slices that were dropping players off the screen silently, LIFETIME crew standings alternating with tonight's on a 12s timer (which genuinely appeared on no TV anywhere), and the first fit-harness case this screen has ever had. **STILL NOT DONE and deliberately separate:** the spectator predictions ticker below, and a smack-talk feed. Neither is a leaderboard.
- [ ] Spectator predictions ticker on the generic Broadcast (port from Beerio Kart)
- [ ] Seasons: 8-12 week arcs with standings and an offseason
- [ ] CROSS-PACK NIGHT NET (deferred out of the blackjack session, 2026-07-29, deliberately not built). A night where the crew plays blackjack and then poker has a combined net that NO SINGLE PACK KNOWS ABOUT, because each pack's ledger rows describe its own table only. That is the correct place for the four casino packs to stop: a pack that reached across to another pack's rows would be the first thing in this app to do so, and the ledger key namespacing exists precisely to keep them apart. It is an EVENT-LAYER feature — the event already owns the night and the recap rollup already reads every pack's rows for it, so the natural home is the recap/event payload summing `match_participants.meta.net` across every casino-pack row on one eventId. Needs a way to say which packs are "money" packs (the registry can carry a flag) and a decision about whether a casino-banked night and a player-banked one should combine at all.
- [ ] Round robin format
- [ ] Availability polling and auto-pick-the-night

## BUGS
Open first, then environment traps worth remembering, then fixed (fixed items age out after a couple of map redraws).
- FIXED 2026-08-22 (the TV session): BEERIO'S GRAND PRIX TV DID NOT FIT A 1080p SCREEN AT ANY COUNT. Logged 08-19 at twelve racers only; measuring 4 and 8 for the first time on 08-22 found it had NEVER fitted. **BEFORE:** 1148 / 1717 / 2286px at 4 / 8 / 12 racers, over by 68 / 637 / 1206. **AFTER:** fits at 4, 6, 8, 10, 12, 14 and 16, with the prediction bar up and down. The largest gap of the five and the only one with nothing to tighten: `GpBoard` rendered Shell and Header at `band="roomy"` HARDCODED while the bracket board in the same file computed one per payload. Now `beerioGpBand` in `apps/web/src/beerio/band.ts` with its own `--bt-gp-*` rungs. **THE LAST TWO RUNGS GO TWO COLUMNS**, which is a layout decision the ladder makes rather than a shrink: a GP row carries a rank, a dot, a NAME, a record and a points total, so it cannot go under this pack's 1.25vw name floor, and sixteen single-column rows at that floor do not fit however tight the padding gets (measured: 10 rows of budget against 16 needed). `columns` flows column-major, which is the reading order a standings board wants. The original entry follows.
  **PRE-EXISTING AND VERIFIED SO, rather than assumed.** The same payload run against commit 8ef9431, before the crowd bar session, reports the identical 1206. The Grand Prix board also has NO density ladder: `GPBoard` renders `Shell` and `Header` at `band="roomy"` hardcoded, while the bracket board computes a band per payload, so there is nothing to tighten even if there were a case.
  **NOT FIXED HERE.** This is a density ladder plus a harness case, and a fit ladder has been its own session every time (the money board's is the worked example). Logged beside Ping Pong's, Board Game's, Mario Kart's and the bracket's.
- FIXED 2026-08-22, the day it was found: THE BRACKETED TV RAN 48px OVER AT FOUR ENTRANTS, FRESH, and this one is a REGRESSION rather than an ancient overflow. Introduced by the 2026-08-21 on-deck placeholder work, whose closeout states "tv-fit: PASS, every case fits". **VERIFIED AGAINST A CLEAN CHECKOUT of that commit rather than assumed.** 1128px, both themes, `fresh` state only, at four entrants only.
  **THE BAND'S INPUT WAS FINE; THE ANSWER WAS NOT.** `TvPage` already passed `ready: deckAll.length` with a comment saying in as many words that a pending card costs what a ready one does, so the ladder was being asked the right question. `DECK_CEILINGS` put four on-deck cards at `roomy`, and four cards do not fit at roomy metrics. That ceiling had NEVER BEEN EXERCISED: until 08-21 a four-entrant fresh bracket had two ready matches and the column never held four cards at the one entrant count whose board ceiling is roomy. The deck rule made a four-card deck reachable there and the ladder had no measurement for it. Eight and up were never affected, because their entrant count drags the band down through the board sub-ladder.
  **THE LESSON IS ABOUT CEILINGS THAT ARE NEVER HIT.** A ladder rung that no reachable payload exercises is untested by construction, and it stays untested until something changes what is reachable. That is the same shape as a screen with no harness case, one level down.
- OPEN: A LATE WRITE TO AN ABANDONED SESSION STEALS THE TV BACK OFF THE GAME ACTUALLY BEING PLAYED. Found 2026-08-19 by the second-tournament session, which needed to know whether touch-recency still held up once a night could run two tournaments. It does not hold up, and it did not before either: this is a pre-existing property of the ownership rule rather than anything that session changed, which is why it is logged rather than fixed.
  **REPRODUCED AGAINST THE REAL `resolveNow`, not reasoned about.** Mario Kart live and abandoned since 20:10, Smash started 20:30: the TV resolves to Smash, correctly. One stray Mario Kart write at 20:35, from a phone somebody left on the sofa with the scoring screen open, and it resolves back to Mario Kart. The bracket variant is the same shape and worse to watch: a tournament being actively scored at 20:30 loses the screen to a Mario Kart session nobody has touched since 20:10, on one tap.
  **NOT FIXED, AND THE REASON IS A SCHEMA CHANGE PLUS A WORSE FAILURE MODE.** See DECISION LOG: touch-recency was reaffirmed by James on 2026-08-19 over start-stickiness. Start-stickiness needs a real `started_at` column, because `createdAt` is wrong under upsert, and it trades this failure for one that is arguably worse: a session somebody opened and walked away from would hold the screen over a bracket being actively scored, which is the exact case touch-recency gets right. Neither rule is correct on its own, so this needs a session, not a patch.
- FIXED 2026-08-22 (the TV session): THE BRACKETED TV DID NOT FIT A 1080p SCREEN AT SIXTEEN PAIRS. **BEFORE:** 1309px fresh (over by 229), 1377 mid and late (over by 297). **AFTER:** all four states fit, and SIXTEEN SOLO and EIGHT PAIRS are byte-identical to before with zero chips truncated. **IT WAS A WIDTH PROBLEM, not a slot count one**, which is why the existing count-based ladder could not see it: `.gn-tva` chips are auto-width and the row wraps, so a doubled team label made every chip wider until sixteen wrapped onto ~300px of extra rows. The fix is a THIRD answer, `bracketChipBand`, capping `--gn-tv-chip-max` on its own `[data-chip]` attribute. **TWO DRAFTS OF IT WERE WRONG AND BOTH WERE CAUGHT BY MEASURING**, both the same mistake: spending a cap on a board that did not need one. Folding it into `bracketTvBand` truncated 24-character SOLO names (sixteen solo already sits at `tight` on entrant count); keying it on label length alone truncated EIGHT pairs, which had been measured fitting. It now needs a long label AND more than eight entrants, and both mistakes are pinned by unit tests. The original entry follows.
  **IT IS THE ALIVE BOARD, NOT THE MATCH CARDS, AND THAT WAS MEASURED RATHER THAN ASSUMED.** The harness now reports the lowest INK as well as the lowest box (the lowest box on any page is almost always `#root`, which names nothing), and in every failing state the lowest ink is `.gn-tva` inside `.gn-tv-alive__row`. The board's chips are auto-width and wrap: a doubled-length label makes each chip wider, sixteen of them wrap onto more rows, and the board grows about 300px. The bracket cards did NOT move, because a team slot deliberately keeps ONE line on the TV.
  **A STACK WAS TRIED FIRST AND IS WHY THE ROWS ARE ONE LINE.** Stacking a pair's names inside the row put the board 288px over at eight pairs and 297px at sixteen, in every state. A television cannot be scrolled, so past 1080 is gone rather than below the fold, which is strictly worse than the ellipsis a long name has always taken there. An unnamed pair's label already IS its people joined, so one line loses nothing.
  **NOT FIXED THERE.** This is a density ladder, and a fit ladder has been its own session every time (the money board's is the worked example, the bracketed TVs' is the recent one). Chip width against label length is exactly the decision that wants measuring rather than taste. Named in `KNOWN` in `scripts/tv-fit.mjs` with the numbers above, so the run stays a usable gate and the finding cannot be forgotten.
- FIXED 2026-08-22 (the TV session): MARIO KART'S TV DID NOT FIT A 1080p SCREEN PAST EIGHT RACERS. **BEFORE:** 1179px at twelve (over by 99), 1447 at sixteen (over by 367, back button 334px into the rail). **AFTER:** 8 / 10 / 12 / 14 / 16 all fit in both themes, back button clear by at least 180px. `mariokart-tv-band.ts` and `[data-mkband]`. **IT WAS NEVER THE KARTS AND THIS DID NOT HAVE TO RE-ESTABLISH THAT**: the 08-16 pairs session ran the same payloads through the PREVIOUS commit's component and got 1447 to the digit. The Players panel is per racer either way and the Karts panel REPLACES the Racers panel, so 16 solo, 16 karts and 16 koth land on one number before and after. The original entry follows.
  **IT IS NOT THE KARTS, AND THAT WAS MEASURED RATHER THAN ASSUMED.** The pairs session checked out the PREVIOUS commit's TV component and ran the same three payloads through it: 1447px, over by 367, identical to the digit in every case. The Players panel is per RACER whether or not karts are shared, so it is the tall column either way, and the Karts panel REPLACES the Racers panel rather than sitting beside it (karts are never more numerous than racers). The kart work added zero pixels.
  **NOT FIXED THERE, and the reason is the shape of the fix rather than the clock.** This is a density ladder, and a fit ladder has been its own session every time it has been done (the money board's is the worked example, the bracketed TVs' is the recent one). Named in `KNOWN` in `tv-fit.mjs` with that evidence beside it, so the run still exits 0 and a NEW overflow anywhere still fails.
- FIXED 2026-08-17 FOR THE MECHANISM, OPEN FOR EIGHT PACKS: THE FELT DID NOT REACH THE PACKS, AND THE PACKS ARE WHERE THE TABLE IS. **WHAT WAS FIXED:** the shell now holds the tile in a token of its own, `--gn-felt-tile`, and a pack composes its own layer list out of that plus `--gn-lamp-geom` / `--gn-lamp-fade` plus its own colours. Board Game is converted and is the worked example. The diagnosis below was right and the fix is the one it predicted, with one correction it did not see: a pack must NOT read `--gn-texture`, because a custom property containing a var() resolves where it is DECLARED, so the shell's carries the shell's green into any pack that reads it. **WHAT IS STILL OPEN:** eight packs (Ping Pong, Smash, Mario Kart, Mario Party, Card Table, Social Deduction and the casino four's shared surfaces) have not been converted. Each is now a tint decision plus a token block, with a template to copy, and the casino group's four tints were settled in the same pass. The original finding, kept because it is what made the fix structural rather than a line:  Found 2026-08-03 by checking a claim in the felt session's own brief rather than assuming it: Ping Pong is already on `--pp-felt`, so it "should pick the texture up for free". IT DOES NOT, and the reason is structural rather than a missing line. The texture is a `::before` on `.gn-app` and `.gn-tv`, and NO PACK PAGE MOUNTS EITHER CLASS — verified over CDP (`/pingpong/tv/x` renders `<div class="pp-tv">` as `#root`'s only child, with no `.gn-app`/`.gn-tv` anywhere on the route) and by grep across every `.tsx` outside `pages/`. Every pack owns its own root and paints its own opaque background gradient on it, which is the pattern the pack model deliberately chose. So even hoisting the texture to `<body>` would not fix it: a z-index-0 fixed layer sits BEHIND an opaque pack surface by construction. The honest fix is that a pack's own surface carries the cloth, which means one line in each pack's stylesheet reading the shell's `--gn-texture` / `--gn-texture-tint` / `--gn-texture-size` tokens — pack work, and the felt session was explicitly scoped not to convert a pack. **WHY THIS MATTERS MORE THAN IT SOUNDS:** the shell is the home screen, the crew page and the event page; the PACKS are the blackjack table, the casino money board and the ping pong table, which are the screens the whole "it should feel like a table" direction is about. Tabletop's cloth currently stops exactly where its argument starts. This belongs in stage 4 as a first-class item, not as a footnote to a pack conversion, and stage 4's per-pack checklist should gain it. NOTE 2026-08-04: stage 4 moved from NEXT UP slot 3 to LAST, so this is now open for a while rather than about to be fixed. It is not worth pulling out of stage 4 on its own: the fix is one line per pack plus a tint decision per pack, which is the conversion, so doing it separately would mean visiting all nine packs twice.
- FIXED 2026-08-22 (the TV session): BOARD GAME'S AND CARD TABLE'S TV DID NOT FIT A 1080p SCREEN AT TWELVE PLAYERS. **BEFORE:** Board Game 1256 Arcade / 1236 Tabletop (over by 176 / 156); Card Table 1256 in BOTH (over by 176 / 176). **AFTER:** both fit at 4, 8, 10, 12, 14 and 16 in both themes. One ladder for two packs (`titlenight-tv-band.ts`, `[data-tnband]`), which is why this was deferred rather than done twice. **MEASURING CARD TABLE FOR THE FIRST TIME BROKE THIS ENTRY'S OWN ASSUMPTION**: it had said since 08-09 that this was one bug on two packs drawing one component, and under Tabletop the two are 20px apart because they set different tokens on the same sheet. The ladder is tuned against the TALLER of the two, which is Card Table's; tuning against Board Game would have landed one pack and left the other 20px over. The original entry follows.
- FIXED 2026-08-22 (the TV session): PING PONG'S TV DID NOT FIT A 1080p SCREEN PAST SIX PLAYERS. **BEFORE:** 1126px at seven (over by 46), back button 3px into the rail under Arcade and 17px under Tabletop. **AFTER:** 864px at seven, and 6 / 7 / 9 / 12 / 16 all fit with the button clear by at least 73px. `pingpong-tv-band.ts` and `[data-ppband]`, in the pack's own metrics rather than in `band.ts`. **THE BACK BUTTON WAS THE REAL MEASURE HERE**: the page ran 46px over and the control left the screen at the same seat count, so a fit that landed the content and lost the button would not have fixed anything. The original entry follows.
- OPEN: CASINO RUN'S TV HAS THE SAME BACK-BUTTON BLIND SPOT the money board just had, found 2026-08-02 while confirming this session had not moved it. Measured on the real built bundle at 1920x1080, driving the shared engine's own summaries: mid-run it puts the back button at 1114px and with a full table and five cards up at 1093px, so the way back is 34px and 13px off the bottom of a television. The FOOTER fits in both (999px and 977px), which is exactly why nobody caught it: that pack's own fit note says "attempts and the ante share ONE line, they had a row each and that pushed the footer 23px off a 1080p screen", so it was measured against the footer, and the back button is another 116px below that. Standing rule 4 wants a way back on every screen and this one is off the screen in the two states a run spends most of its time in. NOT FIXED IN THE MONEY BOARD SESSION ON PURPOSE, twice over: that session's scope said explicitly not to touch this TV, and the fix is not the money board's ladder anyway. Casino Run draws its own board (`crun-tv__bank`, the stage ladder, the now-line) and would want its own, sized against the stage count rather than a roster. Cheap first move if it is wanted before a full pass: the bank number is 19vmin and the stage ladder is a row of boxes, so there is room to find without touching what it says. The measurement harness for it already exists in the money board session's notes: real `summarizeCrun` fixtures over five run states, driven over CDP.
- FIXED 2026-08-17, the day the pack shipped: POKER'S TV WAS 73px OVER 1080p AT EIGHT SEATS, and the shape of the finding was more interesting than the number. Found by the harness on the day, which is the checklist working: the pack went into `scripts/tv-fit.mjs` in its own commit rather than five days later. Measured at 1920x1080 with a settled table: **four seats 1080 (fits), eight 1153 (over by 73), twelve 1080 (fits)**. Two rounds fixed it. First the settlement band was cut from six payment rows to four and the variants moved off it into the header, which took four seats from 35px over to fitting. **EIGHT WAS WORSE THAN TWELVE, AND THAT WAS THE ROOT CAUSE**: `moneyBoardBand` costs a hero a flat `HERO_LINES = 2`, which is exactly right for craps' shooter panel (a fixed two lines whatever the night is doing) and understated poker's by three, because poker's hero GROWS with the table. Twelve was landing on `full` and fitting by accident while eight sat on `tight`, which does not fit that board. So `BoardLoad` gained `heroLines`: a pack whose hero grows says how much it costs, and `HERO_LINES` stays the default for every pack that does not. Poker counts its payment rows at TWO TO A BOARD LINE, because they are 2.1vmin against a much taller board line, and that halving is measured rather than assumed: counting them one for one put a four-seat table on `tight` when it demonstrably fits on `close`, which would have shrunk a board that had room. **AFTER: four 1080 (clear by 134), eight 1080 (clear by 141), twelve 1080 (clear by 66), and the five blackjack money-board cases are byte-identical**, so the shared ladder moved for nobody else. Nothing was added to `KNOWN`.
- Watch: THE COMPUTED-STYLE SWEEP DOES NOT TRACK BOX-MODEL LENGTHS, so two of the card tokens added on 2026-08-17 are outside what it can see. `TRACKED_PROPS` in `scripts/theme-sweep.mjs` covers colour, the four radii, font-family, opacity and the background geometry; it does NOT cover padding or border-WIDTH (border-*-COLOR is there, which is why this looks covered and is not). `--gn-card-pad` and `--gn-card-border` therefore move without the sweep reporting anything, and the card's 14/16 to 16/18 padding change in that session is unmeasured by it. Found by trying to negative-control the new tokens and getting nothing back from two of the five. **DELIBERATELY NOT FIXED IN THE SESSION THAT FOUND IT**, and the reason is that file's own rule: the radius, font and opacity entries were added BEFORE the work they measured, because "a sweep extended afterwards can only tell you that the two trees you already changed agree with each other". Adding padding now would also invalidate every existing baseline, since a captured tree without it reports four additions on every element. The gap is covered in the meantime by `theme-contrast.test.ts`, which asserts Arcade's `--gn-card-border` is `2px` and its `--gn-card-pad` is `14px 16px` outright. Add the four padding longhands and the four border-width longhands at the START of the next session that expects to move one, not at the end.
- Watch: A TILING TEXTURE HAS TO BE JUDGED AT THE SIZE THE STYLESHEET PAINTS IT, never at 1:1, and nothing in the harness set enforces that. Found 2026-08-17 by screenshotting the built bundle: the felt tile looked like felt when inspected on its own and drew a regular diagonal lattice every 75px once painted at 150px, because a 512px tile downscaled 3.4x averages the fine fibre away and leaves the coarse terms untouched. **THE THREE CHECKS ALL PASSED WHILE IT WAS WRONG.** `generate-felt-tile.mjs` measures the tile's own decoded pixels, `felt-variance.mjs` measures a standard deviation on screen (and a lattice is variance, so it scored 4.85, better than the fixed version's 4.48), and `theme-sweep.mjs` compares computed values. A number that goes UP when the surface gets worse is the shape to watch for here. No fix proposed: the honest check is a person looking at a screenshot at the painted size, and the cheap half of that is already possible since the environment has Chromium. Worth remembering when the casino group's four tints land, because every one of them repeats the same tile.
- Watch: BEVAN OVERFLOWS THE STATS TILE ON `.gn-h2` AT 390px, so the shell's section headings stay on Fredoka under Tabletop. Tried and backed out on 2026-08-17. Bevan sets far wider than Fredoka at the same size, and `.gn-h2` shares a row with a value, so a display face there needs its own width budget: that is a layout change rather than a token, and it wants measuring the way the TV ladders were measured. Left as a finding rather than deleted because the move is right and only the sizing is missing. **IT DID LAND WHERE THERE WAS ROOM**: Board Game's `.tn-tv__title` takes `var(--gn-font-display)` at 7.4vmin, which is the single cheapest thing that stops that TV reading as a phone app across a room, and it costs nothing because Bevan is already loaded under this theme.
- Watch: delivery to brand-new recipients can be cold at first while mail.gamenightos.app builds sending reputation, and early emails may land in spam. The code and link are still logged to the server console in every environment as the fallback path when delivery is slow or misrouted.
- Watch: countLastPlace in stats.ts builds an inArray of EVERY match id a player has ever played, then compares placements against a grouped count of participants per match. The IN list grows without bound, so the query gets larger every night someone plays. It is fine at current volume and the derivation is correct; the fix when it matters is a join against a grouped subquery instead of a client-built id list, so Postgres never receives the ids at all. Logged 2026-07-27 by the pre-pack cleanup audit, deliberately not fixed in it (the index added the same day is the cheaper half of the same problem).
- Watch: the WebSocket hub broadcasts every message to every connected client. There are no rooms and no topics, so every phone in the app receives every event from every crew and filters it client-side. The filtering is correct and this is fine at friend-group scale, but it is the first thing that breaks if the app ever grows. The fix when it matters is per-event or per-crew subscriptions in ws.ts. Logged 2026-07-27 by the pre-pack cleanup audit, deliberately not fixed in it.
- Watch: `drizzle-kit push --force` silently no-ops in non-interactive CI (exits 0 without applying). Confirm the drizzle-kit success line in the build log on any schema-changing deploy; otherwise run idempotent SQL in the Neon console (ALTER TABLE ... ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS).
- Watch: THE CANVAS SHARE CARDS FOLLOW NO THEME. `RECAP` in `apps/web/src/recap.tsx` (7 colours) and `C` in `apps/web/src/pages/MemberPage.tsx` (9 colours) are hardcoded JS palettes used to draw the night recap and rivalry cards onto a <canvas>. Canvas needs concrete colours, so they cannot be CSS tokens as written, which means NOTHING sees them: not the colour-literal lint (it reads .css), not the computed-style sweep (they never become computed styles). Under Tabletop the app is walnut and brass and the card it exports is still Arcade plum. Found 2026-08-02 during the stage 2 var()-fallback hunt, left alone deliberately: making them follow the theme means reading the computed tokens off the document at draw time, which is a real change to the share pipeline and does not belong in a palette session. The same hunt also found that `MemberPage`'s card palette disagreed with the recap card's on the teal (#2dd4bf against #35e0c4) even though a comment claimed they matched, so these two constants have already drifted from each other once.
- Watch: `color-mix()` IS NOW A HARD REQUIREMENT OF THE SHELL, and the fallback the build generates for it is wrong. Stage 1 of the theme work (2026-08-02) replaced 50 rgba() literals in `index.css` with `color-mix(in srgb, var(--gn-x) N%, transparent)`. Tailwind v4 runs Lightning CSS over that file and splits each one into an opaque `var()` fallback plus the real value inside `@supports (color:color-mix(in lab,red,red))`, so on a browser without color-mix 39 of the 50 paint the token at FULL OPACITY: chips become solid coral blocks, bevel shadows solid black slabs. That is worse than no fallback, which is what the pack stylesheets already get (they are not processed by Lightning, and `casino.css` has used color-mix unguarded since it shipped). NOT A BUG ON ANY BROWSER THE APP SUPPORTS: the affected set is Safari < 16.2, Chrome < 111 and Firefox < 113, all pre-2023, all of which already cannot render the casino packs. It is here rather than in the DECISION LOG alone because this is where someone looks for traps. The fix, if James wants it, is a `browserslist` for the build, which changes Lightning's output well beyond colour and therefore wants its own session and its own sweep.
- FIXED 2026-08-15, same day it was found: NEITHER BRACKETED TV FITTED A 1080p SCREEN, AND NEITHER EVER HAD. **THE HARNESS GAP WAS THE FINDING, not the overflow**: `/tv/:id` shipped with the bracket engine and `/beerio/tv/:code` came in with the vendored pack, both long before `scripts/tv-fit.mjs` existed, so this was not a pack that forgot a step (Board Game's 2026-08-09 miss) but the two OLDEST TV views in the app having a fit that was nobody's job for as long as there has been a way to check. A checklist item catches the next pack; it does not catch the screens that predate the checklist, and nothing did.
  **BEFORE**, at 1920x1080 against the lowest painted pixel with real engine payloads: the shell TV ran 1676px before the alive board and 1548 after, at eight entrants AND at sixteen (over by 468); Beerio ran 1561 then 1662 (over by 582). CONSTANT IN THE ROSTER, because both columns were capped lists, so the overflow was structural and had nothing to do with how many people were playing.
  **AFTER: every case fits.** Both screens, at 4, 8, 12 and 16 entrants, in all four states a night passes through (nothing played, mid-bracket with all three alive groups up, the last match outstanding, champion), the shell in both themes and Beerio in one (it is permanently exempt from theming). Nothing is painted under the rail. `node scripts/tv-fit.mjs` exits 0 with **NOTHING ADDED TO `KNOWN`**, which was the point: the two pre-existing pack overflows are still the only names in that set.
  **THE FIX IS A DENSITY LADDER PER SCREEN**, the shape `band.ts` proved on the money board: `apps/web/src/pages/tv-band.ts` and `apps/web/src/beerio/band.ts`, each computing a band from that screen's load and spending it as CSS variable overrides. Not `band.ts` itself, which is the casino group's and stays that way. See DECISION LOG for what the load inputs turned out to be and where the type floor landed.
  **THE HARNESS ONLY ASKED THE FIT QUESTION, IT NEVER ENFORCED IT.** A case could print "OVER by 468" and the script still exited 0, because only the rail-coverage question could fail the run. That is how four bracketed cases were added in one session, all four over, and the exit code said nothing. Fixed here: a case past 1080px now fails unless it is named in `KNOWN`. **AND THE RUN NOW CARRIES A NEGATIVE CONTROL**: before reporting that the ladder works it re-injects the pre-ladder metrics and requires the binding cases to go OVER (they do, by 229 and 564). That control caught itself on its first run, when its stylesheet lost on specificity to a pack sheet arriving on a lazy chunk and Beerio's control silently did nothing.
  **OUT OF CONTRACT AND REPORTED ANYWAY:** the shell's bracket takes entrants off the yes-RSVP list with NO CAP, and at twenty-four it also fits, at the tightest band, with every name visible. That is not a promise: nothing past sixteen was tuned, and the requirement on it is only that it degrades at the tightest rung rather than silently painting off the bottom. It is measured on every run so the day it stops fitting is the day somebody hears about it.
  **A SECOND GAP IN THE SAME FAMILY, also fixed:** Beerio's back button reported "no button" the first time it was ever measured. The harness hook is `.gn-textbtn, .cg-tv__back` with a comment saying every BackButton emits `.gn-textbtn` so it cannot go stale, and standing rule 4 says in as many words that Beerio has its own styled one instead of the shared component. It now carries `.beerio-tv-back` and is measured like everything else; both bracketed TVs put their back button at the TOP, so unlike Ping Pong's it was never the thing escaping the screen.
(Fixed items age out after a couple of redraws, as the rule above says. THE 2026-08-04 RAIL SAFE-AREA FIX AGED OUT ON 2026-08-15, having been drawn on the 08-09 and 08-10 passes; its permanent record is the DECISION LOG's `:where(#root) > *` entry, and its lesson (a change that looks identical in the environment where it is made is the exact shape that needs a test, and it had one) is the reason `theme-contrast.test.ts` still asserts it. Earlier: the 2026-07-29 safe-area fix aged out on the 2026-08-02 pass, having been rendered on 07-30. Its root cause lives in the DECISION LOG, which is the permanent record. FOUR MORE AGED OUT ON 2026-08-04, all fixed 2026-08-02 and drawn on the 08-02 and 08-03 redraws: the casino TV money board's fit, the co-op result registering as a TIE, Casino Run's double-drawn stage-clear card, and the three cosmetic ones from the same review. Every one of them has its permanent record in the DECISION LOG, which is exactly the arrangement that makes ageing them out safe: three entries cover the money board's lessons, one covers `side` and the co-op outcome, and the em dash and event-link rules became TESTS rather than notes, so they are enforced rather than remembered.)

## IDEAS (not solidified)
Not committed, no design decided.
- [ ] Draft night mode (snake drafts for characters/teams, TV draft board)
- [ ] Wager ledger (side bets between two people, confirmations, outstanding debts). The "bragging rights only, never money" restriction on this entry was SUPERSEDED on 2026-07-29 (see DECISION LOG): the casino packs record real currency, so a wager ledger denominated in money is no longer off the table on principle. It is still unbuilt and undesigned, and it is a genuinely different thing from the cash packs: those record a table that already happened, while this one tracks an obligation between two people over time, which is the part that would need thought.
- [ ] Achievements/badges, including group-created custom badges
- [ ] Beer pong pack. Would force the one missing primitive, a team model (2v2), which then unlocks cornhole, foosball and doubles ping pong cheaply. HALF OF THAT PRIMITIVE NOW EXISTS: `match_participants.side` shipped 2026-08-02 with Casino Run (see DECISION LOG), so the LEDGER already knows who was on whose team and the rivalry pages already read it. What is still missing is the session-side half: picking teams at setup, scoring a match as two sides, and writing two `side` values per match instead of Casino Run's one. THE MISSING HALF SHIPPED 2026-08-05 (see SHIPPED — GAME PACKS), so this pack no longer forces anything: `teams.ts` owns sides, validation, the deal and the placement rule, and Ping Pong doubles is the worked example to copy. What is left here is a beer pong pack, not a primitive.
- [ ] Pool pack. Rides the same KOTH/singles match engine ping pong now uses.
- [ ] Cornhole and darts. (Poker moved OUT of this line on 2026-07-29: it is committed at NEXT UP slot 3, and the "new session ledger engine (buy-ins, net results)" it was waiting on now exists and is tested — `packages/shared/src/cashgame.ts`, shipped with blackjack.)
- [ ] Capacitor native wrapper: packages this web app as a real iOS/Android app (same code, native shell, push notifications). Not needed while the PWA works.
  - GEOTAGGED ARRIVAL CONFIRMATION, and it hangs off THIS entry rather than standing on its own line, deliberately. With a location on the event (see FEATURES), a geofence could confirm attendance automatically when somebody actually reaches the venue, feeding `event_attendance` and therefore flake tracking and show streaks with nobody tapping anything. It is the most accurate version of a thing this app already models and currently learns only by asking. **IT IS NATIVE-ONLY, WHICH IS WHY IT IS INDENTED UNDER THE WRAPPER INSTEAD OF ABOVE IT:** geofenced background notifications are not reachable from a PWA on iOS, so this is not something to scope as buildable today and then discover halfway through. It is gated on the wrapper existing, and it is written down here so that when the wrapper is costed, this is on the list of what the wrapper would BUY rather than being remembered as a separate idea that was never possible.
- [ ] Offline score entry sync (PWA background sync)
- [ ] Event-aware warm ping: wake Render only in the hour before a scheduled event start, instead of on a fixed clock window. Updated 2026-07-28 now that phase 8b widened the fixed window to noon-2am Central (~487 instance-hours/month of the free 750): this idea no longer buys latency, it buys HOURS BACK, and it is the only option on the table that would let the window get wider still (or reach a 3am night) without approaching the cap. Needs an endpoint listing upcoming event start times for the workflow to read. Composes with the fixed window rather than replacing it: keep a modest always-on window for spontaneous use, and let the event-aware pings cover nights that fall outside it.
## DEFERRED
- CONCURRENT BRACKETS ON ONE EVENT (deferred 2026-08-19, and it is a DECISION rather than a queue item, see DECISION LOG). Two non-completed brackets at once is refused by `canStartBracket`, deliberately: at most one showable bracket is what keeps the TV resolver's answer unambiguous with no new tiebreak between two live tournaments, and what keeps the event page tile able to name one bracket. Building it is not "remove a check", it is answering which of two live tournaments a single screen and a single tile mean.
- BRACKET DELETION (deferred 2026-08-19, not needed rather than not wanted). The second-tournament work removed the reason it looked necessary: the only thing a delete route was ever going to be for was clearing a finished bracket out of the way so the night could run another, and the night can now do that with the finished one still reachable. Event delete already cascades (`events.ts` deletes brackets with the event), so nothing is stranded. (The "already cascades" half of that was true of BRACKETS specifically and false of the delete as a whole until 2026-08-20: on any event that had ever started a session pack it raised, after deleting the brackets. Fixed, see the cascade integrity entry in SHIPPED.)
- ERASING ONE PROFILE'S QUICK PLAY DATA (deferred 2026-08-20 by the cascade integrity session, and it is a PRODUCT decision rather than a missing route). Quick play lives in a hidden personal crew (`groups.is_personal = true`), and `GET /api/groups` filters `is_personal = false`, so that crew appears in no list and has no delete button. The crew delete route now works correctly on a personal crew and NOTHING CAN REACH IT, which is the whole of the gap: the fix above made the route honest, not reachable. The decision is not "add a screen", it is which affordance is right: a delete button on a crew that is deliberately invisible, an account-level "clear my quick play history" that does not pretend the personal crew is a crew, or nothing. It sits directly next to the guest-linking entry below, which is the same shape of gap on the same hidden crew and wants answering at the same time. A one-off Neon script covers the immediate need.
- GUEST LINKING FOR PERSONAL CREWS (deferred 2026-08-18 by the quick play parity session). A quick play guest cannot be linked to a member, because `groups.ts` filters personal crews out of the crew list, so the link-guest screen never offers one. This is true for all thirteen tiles today and the parity work did not make it worse: it is exactly as true for a quick play Smash night as it now is for a quick play tournament. It wants its own decision first, and the decision is not "add a screen": it is whether a PERSONAL crew should surface a link-guest flow at all, given the person doing the linking is the only member and the guests are people who were in the room once.
- CONVERT `titlenight.ts` ONTO `sidelog.ts` (deferred 2026-08-16, by the Mario Kart pairs session that created the module). It is the THIRD consumer of the side log and it carries its own copy under its own names: `TnSideSet`, `currentTnSides`, `isPartnership`, and a `reshuffleTnSides` with the same replace-rather-than-stack rule, keyed on the GAMES count rather than a matches count. The module's unit count is a plain number precisely so it fits, so this is a mechanical conversion. NOT DONE IN THAT SESSION because it moves two SHIPPED packs, Board Game and Card Table, and their fixtures, and folding that into a session scoped as "pairs in Mario Kart" is how one session becomes three. It wants the same discipline the Ping Pong conversion got: capture the screens and the title-shape fixtures off the unmodified engine first, then move a line. Small, and worth doing before a fourth consumer arrives.
- FROM THE 2026-08-15 CLEANUP AUDIT, deferred by James rather than missed. Each is written up in full in `AUDIT-2026-08.md` under the heading named, with its evidence, its cost and its confidence, so none of them has to be rediscovered.
  - SHOULD FIX 4, `guest-link.ts`'s thirteen sequential adapters and three missing indexes (`memberships.user_id`, `game_sessions.group_id`, `smash_sessions.group_id`). NOT ACTIONED, so NO SCHEMA CHANGE SHIPPED and no Neon SQL is owed. The draft `CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS` statements are in the audit's DEPLOY NOTES for whenever it is picked up.
  - SHOULD FIX 5's SECOND HALF ONLY. The event-delete half SHIPPED 2026-08-20 (see SHIPPED - FOUNDATION): the one-DELETE-per-match loop is now a single subselect, and the handler runs in a transaction, both carried by the cascade fix rather than picked up on their own. What is still deferred is the OTHER loop the audit named, two queries per completed bracket in the guest backfill (`brackets.ts:408`). It has no data loss behind it and no transaction argument forcing it.
  - SHOULD FIX 6, seven near-identical roster parsers carrying eight of the `any`s. No bug behind it and eight call sites in front of it.
  - SHOULD FIX 3's second half, `guest-link.ts`'s two hand-maintained adapter lists. The test half shipped (pack-screens now derives its list); the adapters still need a new pack added by hand in two places.
  - All eight original NOTED items, including both dead schema columns (`matches.advances_to_match_id`, which appears nowhere but the schema, and the write-only `match_participants.seed`). They stay: dropping a column is destructive and buys nothing.
  - NOTED 9, NEW and found by a test rather than by reading: `isRacer` rejects `Paratroopa`, which the Double Dash picker offers, so it would reach the ledger as null. LATENT rather than live, verified as such: both server gates currently have a fallback that rescues it. Left failing under a todo marker in `packages/shared/tests/mariokart.test.ts` so it cannot be forgotten.
- Smash character portraits: fighters are text-only for now. Portraits/thumbnails come with the UI pass (asset sourcing + licensing to think about). The roster and picker are built to swap in art without a data change.
- Smash stage tracking: which stage each game was on. Cut for Session A. Too much input per game for the payoff; the fun stat is the fighter, not the stage.
- Smash stock tracking: stocks remaining / KO counts per game. Cut for Session A for the same reason (per-life data entry). Winner + optional full placement is the low-barrier target (standing rule 9).
- House rules per group per game (versioned rule sets): deferred by James.
- Handicap engine: cut by James (not necessary).
- Venue/brewery league mode: different customer, different product. Revisit only if a venue asks.
- ~~Money wagers: regulatory mess. Bragging rights only.~~ SUPERSEDED 2026-07-29 by James's call that the unit is currency (see DECISION LOG). Kept struck through rather than deleted, because the reasoning it rested on is still the boundary: the app RECORDS what a home game already did with its own money, and does not facilitate, hold, transfer or settle anything. Anything that would move money — payments, escrow, settle-up transfers, a payout button — stays deferred on exactly the original grounds.

## STANDING RULES
- Read MAP STATUS at the top of this file at the start of EVERY session and increment its counter as part of any session that ships. When the counter hits 3, reconcile this file and redraw the Excalidraw project map from it (MAP PROTOCOL) BEFORE starting that session's feature work, then reset the counter. This file is the source of truth; the map is a rendering. Reconcile first, draw second, never draw from memory of the last map. A lost drawing costs one redraw; a stale backlog costs the record itself.
- Owners/admins run a mode: only they start it and edit results. Members watch. A per-mode toggle may open scoring to members, but it defaults OFF.
- Members join the HOST'S live session, never a local copy. Session state lives server-side, keyed to the event.
- Every game mode/tracker ships with a TV/spectator view, styled in that mode's OWN design language, not a generic one. Generic bracket has /tv/:id; Beerio Kart has its original live spectator QR flow.
- Every game mode/tracker screen has a BACK button (history-based, falls back to home), and a way back to the event it belongs to. Never a raw <a href> for internal navigation (full page load; iOS standalone turns it into a new Safari tab). Shared component: apps/web/src/BackButton.tsx. Beerio has its own styled one in its header.
- Completed sessions materialize into matches/match_participants. Guests are skipped until linked to a member.
- Live sync on every write (WebSocket hub, or the mode's own polling). Nobody should ever need to refresh.
- Standalone playable without a crew or event where it makes sense: typed names, no stats.
- Prefill rosters from the event's yes-RSVP list, and never clobber a session already in progress.
- SINGLE INSTANCE ONLY on any deployment (numInstances: 1 in render.yaml). The WebSocket hub lives in this process's memory, so a second instance would split the room: phones on instance A would never see updates from instance B, which breaks the live-sync rule above. Keep it at 1 until the hub moves to a shared backend (Redis pub/sub or similar). Rescued here 2026-07-27 when GAMEPLAN.md was deleted; it had been stated as a standing rule only there and in a render.yaml comment.
- Any pack with character selection has a "Which game?" title selector on its front page. The chosen title scopes the character picker AND the random pool to that title's roster (never assign a character that isn't in the game being played). Titles are subsets/variants of the series; stats stay unified across titles by character name. New character packs follow this: define the series' titles as GameTitle[] in the pack's shared module, thread titleId through the session state, and scope the picker + random with rosterForTitle().

## ADDING A PACK (the short checklist)
Most of what a pack needs is now derived from ONE registry entry. This lists what is not.

1. **Add the entry** to `SESSION_PACKS` in `packages/shared/src/packs.ts` (ledger key, gameName,
   keyPrefix, route, wsType, table, name, emoji, quickTitle). APPEND it; never insert above a pack
   that is already live, because `SESSION_PACK_KEYS` feeds the event TV's tiebreak and inserting
   would silently re-rank the live one. Everything data-driven follows from the entry, and the
   compiler finds the rest. Never change these five for an EXISTING pack: ledger, gameName and
   keyPrefix are written into the database and change nothing loudly — they just orphan history.
2. **Server:** the pack's own router, its state shape, its `materialize*` (the ledger unit is
   genuinely per-pack), and its `createPackRuntime({ ...packConfig("<key>"), extras })`.
3. **Mount it** in `apps/server/src/index.ts`. MANUAL ON PURPOSE: mount ORDER is load-bearing, so
   the public TV router goes with the other `/api/tv` mounts, above the authed routers.
4. **Client:** the pack page, its TV view, its CSS, and the format list in `apps/web/src/packs.ts`.
   A pack whose `*-context` endpoint returns MORE than the shared envelope (Board Game adds the
   crew's recent titles) passes its own context type as `usePackSession`'s second type argument;
   it defaults to `PackCtx`, so a pack that adds nothing writes nothing.
5. **Lazy routes** in `App.tsx`. MANUAL ON PURPOSE: Vite needs a static literal inside `import()`
   to split the chunk, so this cannot be looped. Add the chunk to `prefetch.ts` too — that one IS
   type-checked, so forgetting it fails the build rather than silently slowing a tap.
6. **Guest linking:** export `guestNames*` / `creditGuest*` and register them in `guest-link.ts`.
7. **Safe area:** nothing to do for the pack PAGE (index.css insets every shell structurally), but
   a TV screen that sets its own `padding` must fold `env(safe-area-inset-*)` into its own
   `calc()`, because a class rule beats the zero-specificity shell inset. Casino packs get this
   free from `casino.css`.
8. **Add it to the browser harnesses**, in the same commit. `scripts/tv-fit.mjs` needs a payload
   and its cases if the pack has a TV; `scripts/theme-sweep.mjs` needs the page and TV routes in
   both `ROUTES` and `RULE_ROUTES`; `scripts/screens-baseline.mjs` needs a stub if the pack has a
   screen worth pinning. THIS IS A STEP BECAUSE IT WAS MISSED: Board Game shipped a TV on
   2026-08-04 and was in neither harness until 2026-08-09, and when it was finally measured it
   was 176px over a 1080p screen at its full roster (see BUGS). A pack nobody adds is a pack whose
   fit and whose theme are nobody's job.
   **THE RULE IS ABOUT THE SCREEN, NOT ABOUT THE PACK.** Any TV view joins both harnesses on the
   commit that ships it, whether it belongs to a pack, to the shell or to a vendored app, and a
   screen that predates this checklist is not exempt, it is overdue. Added 2026-08-15, when the
   two OLDEST TV views in the app (`/tv/:id` and `/beerio/tv/:code`) turned out to be in neither
   harness and to have been ~600px and ~480px over a 1080p screen the whole time (see BUGS). The
   pack-shaped wording above could never have caught either of them. **AND THE BACK BUTTON NEEDS A
   HOOK**: `tv-fit.mjs` measures to `.gn-textbtn, .cg-tv__back, .beerio-tv-back`, so a screen with
   its own styled back button rather than the shared `BackButton` must add its class there, or that
   screen silently reports "no button" and its standing-rule-4 check is skipped rather than failed.
9. **A NEW TABLE CARRYING `group_id` OR `event_id` JOINS BOTH CASCADES IN
   `apps/server/src/cascade.ts`, IN THE SAME COMMIT THAT ADDS IT.**
   `cascade-integrity.test.ts` derives the required set from the schema, so a missing table is
   a red gate rather than a foreign key violation that destroys a crew's history mid-delete.
   THIS IS A STEP BECAUSE IT WAS MISSED: `game_sessions` shipped 2026-07-16 as an additive
   table and neither cascade learned about it for four months, and `smash_sessions` had never
   been in either one. **THE RULE IS ABOUT THE TABLE, NOT ABOUT THE PACK.** Any new table with
   one of those two columns joins both cascades whether it belongs to a pack, to the shell or
   to the event layer, and a table that predates this checklist is not exempt, it is overdue.
   A table with NEITHER column stays out: `beerio_sessions` and `beerio_hof` are keyed by a
   text room code, cannot block a delete, and are correctly absent (the test derives that too,
   and fails a cascade that deletes them).
10. **A CASINO pack is much less than the above.** See CASINO GROUP: the engine, the money routes
   and every money screen are shared, so steps 2 and 4 shrink to a handful of callbacks and some
   copy. **A TITLE-NIGHT pack is less still.** Board Game and Card Table share an engine
   (`titlenight.ts`), every screen (`apps/web/src/titlenight/`) and every route
   (`titlenight-runtime.ts`), so steps 2 and 4 shrink to a config object, a copy file and a
   palette; see `packages/shared/src/cardtable.ts` and `apps/server/src/cardtable.ts` for the
   whole of a pack.

## CASINO GROUP (design, shared by the four CASH packs)
Written down on 2026-07-29 with the blackjack session so roulette, craps and poker do not have
to rediscover any of it. Blackjack is SHIPPED and is the worked example — read its files
alongside this. Everything below is already built and tested unless it says otherwise.

**Four packs, not one.** Blackjack, roulette, craps, poker, each with its own registry entry and
its own `ledger` key, so each shows as its own leaderboard tab. That is the point: a blackjack
net and a poker net are different skills, and collapsing them would be exactly what the pack
registry's own warning about `gameName` says never to do. They share ONE engine, so each pack
file stays thin. Build order is blackjack -> roulette -> craps -> poker, with poker last because
it carries a second, placement-based `tournament` format on top of the net-based one.

**The engine: `packages/shared/src/cashgame.ts`.** Pure, dependency-free, fully unit tested
(`packages/shared/tests/cashgame.test.ts`). It owns buy-in, rebuys, cash-out and net per player;
net as `cashOut - (buyIn + sum(rebuys))`; placement derived by ranking net descending with ties
as co-placements at competition ranking (two at 1, next at 3, the Smashdown convention); the
zero-sum balance check; and `summarizeCash` / `CashSummary<D>`, where D is the pack's own
per-player detail and is the ONLY thing that differs between packs.

**Three shared layers, added in this order for a reason.** The ENGINE landed with blackjack. The
SCREENS (`apps/web/src/casino/`: CasinoSetup, CasinoTable, MoneyBoard, money.tsx, casino.css) and
the SERVER ROUTES (`apps/server/src/casino-runtime.ts`) landed with roulette, once there were two
real examples to generalise from rather than one to guess from. What that leaves a new pack is:
a registry entry, a server file supplying four callbacks (`newState`, `summarize`, `ledgerMeta`,
`applyDetail`) plus its own tracker routes, a page supplying copy and its cash-out fields, and a
TV handing `MoneyBoard` a brand and a per-player subline. `packages/shared/src/roulette.ts` and
`apps/server/src/roulette.ts` are the template — read them, not blackjack's, since blackjack
carries a little extra history.

**MONEY IS INTEGER CENTS. EVERYWHERE.** Never a float, never a JS number holding dollars. This is
not tidiness. Floats drift by fractions of a cent, so the balance check below would fail on
sessions that are perfectly correct, which is a miserable bug to chase months later. Store cents
in the session jsonb, in `match_participants.meta` and on the wire; format at the EDGE only, with
`formatCents` / `formatCentsSigned` out and `parseCents` in. The server's `cents()` helper rejects
a non-integer outright rather than rounding it, because rounding would hide a client that had
started doing dollar arithmetic. There is a named float-canary test; do not delete it.

**Placement is derived, so the ledger does not change.** These packs feed the existing
`matches` / `match_participants` tables with NO schema change and NO change to `stats.ts` at all.
That is why this shape was chosen. **Do not add columns to the ledger for money.**

**Who banks, per session.** Session state carries `bank: "player" | "casino"`, chosen by the host
at start.
- `bank: "player"` — a crew member is the house. Their net is DERIVED as the inverse of every
  other player's and is NEVER typed in, so the table sums to zero by construction. The banker's
  own buy-in (their float) and cash-out (what came back) ARE recorded, and the balance check
  compares their count against what the players' numbers say it should be. If it disagrees, show
  "the table does not balance, off by X" rather than silently materializing bad numbers. It is a
  warning, not a veto — the host confirms and it records. The check is SKIPPED, not failed, until
  the banker has cashed out. No other pack in the app can check its own correctness like this.
- `bank: "casino"` — a real casino is the house. Nets are independent, no derived player, no
  balance check, and the table correctly does NOT sum to zero: the missing money went to a
  building.

**Materialization unit is the SESSION, not the hand.** One `matches` row per completed session,
one `match_participants` row per player, placement from net rank. Buy-in, rebuy count, cash-out,
net, session length and the pack's detail stats ride in `match_participants.meta`. `character`
stays null for every casino pack. `matches.format` is `"cash"` for all four. Standing rule 5
applies unchanged: guests are skipped until linked.

**Live tracker: built for every pack, defaults OFF.** Each pack ships two input paths. (1) The
DEFAULT is the cash-out screen: two interactions per player per night, a buy-in at the start and
a short cash-out form at the end. That is the minimal-input path and the one most nights use.
(2) The OPT-IN is a live tracker the host can flip on mid-session, feeding the per-pack detail
stats. THE RULE: the tracker being off must never lose a stat the cash-out form could capture.
Anything the tracker produces is either ALSO a typed box on the cash-out form (prefilled from the
tracker when it ran, and typed beats derived per FIELD, not per player) or is genuinely
un-reconstructable — a roulette streak, a craps roll length — and simply absent when it was off.

**Free stats, from buy-in and cash-out alone. Do not add input to get these.** Lifetime net per
pack and overall, win rate as percent of sessions finishing up, biggest night up and down,
average buy-in, average net, rebuy rate, current and longest winning-session streak, ROI as net
over total buy-in, net per hour from session timestamps, best and worst night. Blackjack's
`/api/groups/:id/blackjack-stats` computes all of them; copy it.

**Per-pack detail stats** (recorded here so nobody redesigns them):
- Blackjack (SHIPPED): biggest bet, biggest win, blackjacks hit.
- Roulette (SHIPPED): max consecutive winning spins (REQUIRES the tracker, no box to type it
  into), favourite bet type.
- Craps (SHIPPED): longest roll (rolls survived before sevening out) and points made, both
  typed-or-derived; biggest bet and biggest win typed-only, since its tracker follows the dice.
  Longest roll is also a CREW-WIDE record on the stats panel, not just a personal stat.
- Poker: biggest pot, bad-beat note, plus a second `tournament` format that is placement-based
  rather than net-based.

**A pack's own headline on the TV goes in `MoneyBoard`'s `hero` slot**, not in a forked board.
Craps put the shooter there (name, live roll count, the night's longest hand to beat) and the
slot is generic: poker's pot and the co-op quota are the next two things that want it.

**STAKES (SHIPPED).** Session state carries `stakes: "real" | "play"`, chosen on a dropdown at
start and fixed for the night. Absent means real, which is every night recorded before it
shipped. WINS AND PLACEMENTS UNIFY across stakes; ONLY MONEY SPLITS — nights, win rate, streaks,
rebuys and hours count once, while net/staked/averages/ROI/best/worst/net-per-hour are computed
per stakes and printed on their own line. There is no combined net on the aggregate at all, so
nothing can render one. Format through `money(stakes)` from cashgame.ts, never `formatCents`
bare, inside any casino screen: the bound helper is what stops a missed call site showing
dollars on a play table, and the browser suite checks that invariant directly. Two signals mark
play money — the `P$` prefix and the violet badge — and up/down stay green/red, because those
mean direction rather than currency. Copy says "play money", never Monopoly.

**CASINO RUN is in this group but NOT on this engine** (2026-07-30). It shares the money
primitives, the modifier deck and the `--cg-*` tokens, and nothing else: one shared bank
against a target instead of per-player nets, so it has its own state module, its own routes
and its own screens. Everything below describes the four CASH packs. See SHIPPED.

**MODIFIERS are shipped and shared** (2026-07-30). `packages/shared/src/modifiers.ts` owns the
24-card deck and the draw; `casino/modifiers.tsx` owns all three renderings (the setup picker, the
strip on the pack page, the wall on the TV). A pack gets every one of them by passing its LEDGER
key to `CasinoSetup` — there is nothing per-pack to write, and a new casino pack that adds no
cards of its own still has the twelve "any" cards in its pool on day one. The rule that does not
move: the app displays and records, it never computes. See DECISION LOG.

**TV is a live money board**, and it IS a shared component now (`casino/MoneyBoard.tsx`, extracted
with roulette). Who is up, who is down, rebuy counts, running totals, sorted by net, updating over
the WebSocket on every buy-in, rebuy and cash-out. A pack passes a brand, an optional header
extra, and a per-player subline tail; everything else is the same board, so two packs can never
disagree about who is up.

**A casino pack's stylesheet is a token block, not a stylesheet.** All structure lives in
`casino/casino.css` and reads `--cg-*` tokens with `--gn-*` fallbacks. A pack sets the backdrop,
the brand glow and about eight token values on its `.xx-root` / `.xx-tv` — roughly twenty lines.
DO give each pack a distinct accent: two money boards that look alike are genuinely confusing
across a room, so blackjack is gold on oxblood and roulette is teal on green felt.

**Per-player buy-ins.** The setup screen carries an amount per seat as well as a table default,
because the banker's float is nearly always different from everyone else's. A seat FOLLOWS the
default until it is touched, so changing the default still moves everyone who has not been given
their own number. Only overridden seats are sent (`buyIns`, keyed by roster index).

**Deferred, do not build:** cross-pack night net. See FEATURES TO ADD.

## DECISION LOG

**`side` MEANS THREE DIFFERENT THINGS IN THE LEDGER, AND ONLY ONE OF THEM IS "WHO YOU WIN
WITH"** (2026-08-22, correcting partner stats shipped the same day). This is the most
important thing anybody adding a cross-participant stat to this app needs to know, and the
first version of partner stats got it wrong. Traced through `sideIdFor` rather than guessed:

| Writer | What a shared side means there | Sides per match |
|---|---|---|
| Ping Pong doubles | a competitive TEAM | 2 |
| Mario Kart Double Dash | a competitive TEAM (a kart) | 2 to 8 |
| Bracket team entrants | a competitive TEAM | 2 per match |
| Casino Run | ONE co-op team holding the whole table | 1 |
| Social Deduction | a FACTION, mostly dealt at random | 2 or more |

**SMASH WRITES NONE**, because 2v2 is backlog rather than shipped, and Board Game and Card
Table write null through `placementsFromRankedSides` with singleton sides. `brackets.ts` also
has a `side` that is the "W" or "L" half of a double elim and has nothing to do with this
column; do not follow it.

**THE BOTTOM TWO ROWS BREAK ANY "PARTNER" FEATURE THAT COUNTS THEM.** Casino Run writes the
single constant `CRUN_SIDE` to every player, which is CORRECT for the column, and one
eight-person run then yields TWENTY-EIGHT pairings with identical outcomes where a doubles
ping pong match yields one. Social Deduction's sides ARE factions, so a twelve-player Werewolf
with nine villagers yields THIRTY-SIX villager pairings per game, every game, dealt rather than
chosen. Counted naively, "who you win with" silently becomes "who else turns up to Werewolf and
Casino Run", doubles ping pong disappears into the noise, and NOTHING ERRORS.

**THE DEFAULT IS COMPETITIVE TEAM SIDES ONLY, AND THE TWO FILTERS ARE DIFFERENT IN KIND ON
PURPOSE.** Co-op is excluded BY SHAPE (keep a match only if somebody on it was NOT on my side),
which kills Casino Run for what it is rather than what it is called and will keep killing the
next co-op pack nobody has written yet, Pandemic included. Factions are excluded BY PACK,
because their sides are genuinely multi-side and no shape test separates them; the ledger key
comes off `SESSION_PACKS` rather than a typed string. Both are named constants defaulting to
false, so counting them is a one-line reviewable edit, and a test asserts the defaults so it
cannot be flipped silently. **IT IS A DEFAULT, NOT A LAW**: if a co-op or faction line is worth
showing it is a SEPARATE line with its own label, never folded into the same average. The
surface says TEAM GAMES out loud for the same reason.

**AND IT MAKES THE EMPTY STATE THE COMMON CASE**, which is the honest consequence rather than a
regression: once co-op and factions are out, only doubles ping pong, Double Dash karts and team
brackets remain, and a crew that has played none of those three correctly sees "no team games
recorded yet".

**IMPORT THE STATS PAGE'S COMPONENTS RATHER THAN RE-DERIVING THEIR FIELDS** (2026-08-22, from
the Home block, also correcting the same day). `FormStatsCard`, `ShowUpRecord`,
`CharacterStatsCard` and `Stat` are standalone modules precisely because two screens already
use each of them; a third screen that reads `form.currentStreak`, `attendance.showRate` and
`characters.mostPlayed` out of the raw payload and lays them out itself is a SECOND SPELLING,
and four chances for Home to disagree with the stats page about one person. **A COMPONENT THAT
DOES NOT FIT IS DROPPED, NEVER FORKED AND NEVER RESTYLED FOR ITS NEW HOME.** Measured rather
than judged: `CharacterStatsCard` renders a full table (a page section, not a panel row) and
`ShowUpRecord` costs 117px on a phone and 184px in a 223px column, which put the Home panel at
430px on a 390px phone. Both are gone from Home and unchanged on `/me/stats`, one tap away.


**"STATS ON THE TV" WAS A PARTLY-SHIPPED BACKLOG LINE, AND THE LINE DID NOT SAY SO**
(2026-08-22). The entry read "Stats on the TV view (leaderboard between matches)" and had sat
in FEATURES since 2026-07-18. Read cold it describes a feature that does not exist. It half
existed: `EventTvPage`'s `NightSoFar` has rendered tonight's standings and the latest results
between every game since the one-TV-button session, and the entry was never updated when that
shipped. A session that trusted the line would have built a second standings panel beside the
one already there. **THE REMAINING GAP WAS THREE DIFFERENT THINGS**, none of them "build a
panel": a ladder behind two hardcoded slices that were silently dropping players, LIFETIME
standings (which genuinely appeared on no TV anywhere), and a harness case for a screen nobody
had measured. **THE RULE THIS SUGGESTS**: when a session ships something a FEATURES line partly
covers, amend the line in the same commit or move it to SHIPPED with what is still missing. A
backlog entry that is 60% done and reads 0% done is worse than one that is 0% done, because it
prices the work wrongly in the one direction nobody checks.

**THE PUBLIC EVENT TV CANNOT READ AN AUTHED ENDPOINT, and that is a property of the device
rather than an oversight** (2026-08-22). The lifetime board was planned as a read of
`/api/groups/:id/stats`. That router carries `requireAuth` and its handler then verifies crew
membership, while every `/api/tv` route is deliberately public because, as `tv.ts`'s own header
puts it, typing a password on a television is misery and the event's unguessable UUID is the
access key. A TV is the one screen in this app that is reliably signed out. So a TV feature that
needs crew data serves it from the TV's OWN public endpoint, computed through the same shared
aggregation, rather than by pointing the big screen at an authed route and discovering on a game
night that it renders nothing. **WHAT THAT WIDENS, said plainly:** the event TV link now exposes
crew member names with lifetime wins, games and average finish. The same link already exposed
the yes-RSVP list and tonight's standings with the same names and win counts, so it is the same
class of data over a longer window, but it IS a widening and it belongs in a decision log rather
than in a diff.

**A SCREEN WITH NO HARNESS CASE HAS NO OWNER, and this is the FIFTH instance of one root cause**
(2026-08-22, corrected from "sixth" the same day). BUGS keeps this count itself and a session
continues it rather than restarting it: Board Game (2026-08-09) was the first, both bracketed
TVs (08-15) the second, Mario Kart (08-16) says "the third time", Beerio's Grand Prix (08-19)
says "the fourth time", and the event TV (08-22) is the fifth. THE TWO BRACKETED TVs ARE ONE
INSTANCE, not two: one session, one finding, one BUGS entry, and counting them separately is
exactly how the first draft of this entry got six. Every one of them: a screen shipped, nothing added
it to `scripts/tv-fit.mjs`, and it ran hundreds of pixels off a television for weeks or months
with nothing erroring. Two of them had never fitted at ANY count.
  **THE ADDING A PACK CHECKLIST CANNOT CATCH THIS ANY MORE, and that is the new part.** Four of
  the six were packs, so a pack checklist was the right instrument and the failure was that
  people did not run it. THE EVENT TV IS NOT A PACK. `/e/:id/tv` is a shell screen, it went
  through no pack checklist because there was none to go through, and it was in
  `theme-sweep.mjs`'s ROUTES the whole time, so it was being colour-swept while nobody had ever
  asked whether it fit. **A SHELL SCREEN NEEDS THE SAME OBLIGATION A PACK HAS.** The cheapest
  version is not a second checklist: it is a test that reads the route table and fails when a
  route matching `/tv` has no case in `tv-fit.mjs`, so the harness gap becomes a red test
  instead of a discovery. Written down rather than built, because it is a harness change and
  this session was already carrying five ladders.

**A LADDER RUNG THAT NO REACHABLE PAYLOAD EXERCISES IS UNTESTED BY CONSTRUCTION** (2026-08-22).
The bracketed TV's `DECK_CEILINGS` put four on-deck cards at `roomy`, and four cards do not fit
at roomy metrics. It was wrong from the day it was written and nothing noticed for a week,
because until the 2026-08-21 on-deck placeholder work a four-entrant fresh bracket had TWO ready
matches: the rung existed, was reachable in the type system, and was unreachable in practice.
The deck rule changed what was reachable and the wrong answer appeared immediately. This is the
harness-gap lesson one level down: a case nothing exercises is a case nothing verifies, whether
it is a screen with no fit case or a ceiling no payload reaches.

**A DENSITY CAP IS A LOSS OF INFORMATION AND IS ONLY SPENT WHERE THE ALTERNATIVE IS WORSE**
(2026-08-22, from the sixteen-pairs fix). Capping `--gn-tv-chip-max` fixes a doubles board whose
chips wrap off the screen. Two drafts of it were wrong in the same way: the first folded the cap
into the main band and truncated SIXTEEN SOLO, which already sat at `tight` on entrant count and
was never broken; the second keyed the cap on label length alone and truncated EIGHT PAIRS,
which had been measured fitting in all four states. Both were caught by measuring rather than by
reasoning. The rule that came out of it: an ellipsis is better than a row that falls off a
television (standing rule), but it is WORSE than a row that fits, so a cap needs positive
evidence that the board would otherwise overflow, not merely that its labels are long.


**PARTNER STATS DID NOT TOUCH THE AGGREGATION, AND THE CREW LEADERBOARD WAS EXCLUDED ON
PURPOSE** (2026-08-22). `feedAgg`, `finishAgg`, `ResultRow` and `resultCols` are byte-identical
after the partner-stats session, and `/groups/:id/stats` was deliberately NOT given partner
data. The reason is a property of `finishAgg` that is easy to lose: it is sync and query-free
BECAUSE the crew leaderboard calls it once per player, so anything costing a query in there
costs one round trip per member of the crew, every render. Partner stats cost a query, so they
live beside `finishAggDeep` and are wired only into the three profile-side endpoints
(`/me/stats`, the crew member profile, the friend equivalent), each inside the `Promise.all`
that was already there. THE EXCLUSION IS NOT CAUTION, IT IS THE SHAPE OF THE DATA: `resultCols`
selects no `side` and no `userId`, and `aggFor` filters to one user, so every row that
aggregation sees is the requesting person's own. Partner stats need the OTHER participants on
those matches, which is a different query however it is arranged. The characterization test
(`partner-stats-baseline.test.ts`) pins `finishAgg` byte-for-byte and was written BEFORE the
change, which is what makes "the leaderboard did not move" evidence rather than an assertion.

**A CROSS-PARTICIPANT STAT IS A SELF-JOIN, NEVER A CLIENT-BUILT ID LIST** (2026-08-22). This is
the one to read before adding the next one. `countLastPlace` in `stats.ts` collects every match
id a player has ever played and sends them to Postgres as an `inArray`; it works, and it is
logged as a Watch trap in BUGS because that list grows without bound, so the query gets more
expensive every night the crew plays and there is no threshold at which anything breaks loudly.
`partnersFor` does NOT repeat that pattern. It is one join of `match_participants` to itself on
`(match_id, side)` with `user_id <> user_id`, so Postgres receives five bound parameters and does
the matching internally. TWO ROWS ON ONE MATCH WITH THE SAME NON-NULL `side` WERE TEAMMATES, which
is the same rule `meetingOutcome` applies in JS, and the null case comes free from SQL semantics
rather than from a test: `null = null` is null, not true, so free-for-all rows never match each
other. The series-summary exclusion (`m.label <> 'smashdown'`, via the imported `SERIES_LABEL`)
has to be repeated in the SQL for the same reason `feedAgg` has it: a Smashdown series row
summarizes battles that are already in the same result set, and without the filter a series
counts on top of every battle inside it and inflates the partner record.

**`PARTNER_MIN_GAMES = 3`, AND WHY A FLOOR EXISTS AT ALL** (2026-08-22). Best partner and worst
partner are held to three games together; most-played-with has no floor. Without a floor the top
of the list is whoever you played with once and won, at a triumphant 100%, and the bottom is
whoever you played with once and lost: noise presented as a verdict, on a screen people
screenshot. Same reasoning and same number as `MIN_CHAR_GAMES`, and a named constant rather than
a literal so it can move without a hunt. The count view needs no floor because a count of one is
honestly a count of one. TWO SMALLER CALLS MADE WITH IT: every rendered line prints its sample
(`12 together · 9W · 75%`) so a rate is never printed naked; and a single eligible partner
returns a best and a NULL worst, because printing one person as both your best and your worst
reads as a bug rather than as a small sample.
- THE ON-DECK LIST IS ONE MERGED LIST IN TRUE PLAY ORDER, NOT READY-FIRST (2026-08-21). A pending card can sit above a ready one and push a fourth ready card off the bottom of a sliced column. **That is the correct outcome, not a regression to fix.** It is the order the night is actually played in, and a board that reorders itself to protect a ready card is lying about what comes next. Nothing is concealed by it: the "N ready" heading keeps counting READY matches only, and now computes that count off the deck entries themselves rather than off a second filter, so the number and the cards can never describe two different lists.
- PLACEHOLDERS SPEND FROM THE EXISTING DECK BUDGET, AND THE LADDER WAS NOT TOUCHED (2026-08-21). `TV_DECK_SLICE` and `BEERIO_DECK_SLICE` keep every number, and so do `tv-band.ts` and `beerio/band.ts`. A pending card costs a column exactly what a ready card costs, because it is the same height by construction: the feeder label rides in the existing one-line name class rather than in a new row. The band call now passes CARDS DRAWN rather than matches ready, with the reason at the call site, and that is not circular because both band functions already clamp to their own roomiest slice. **A fit ladder change has been its own session every time it has been done**, and the escape lever if a pending card ever did overflow was named in advance: drop the blank-vs-blank class at the tight and packed bands, never edit the ladder.
- BLANK VS BLANK REQUIRES LIVE FEEDERS, AND THIS IS THE ONE A LATER SESSION WILL WANT TO REOPEN (2026-08-21). A match with neither seat known reaches the deck only when every match it waits on is playable RIGHT NOW. It is tempting to follow the chain further so the board can show more of what is coming, and that is the trap: two hops out, every match in a fresh bracket qualifies, and the on-deck column becomes the whole tournament in round order. The condition is local on purpose. It is also why the rule takes `feedersLive` as a BOOLEAN a caller computes rather than a graph the shared module walks: the module cannot see either engine's matches, so neither board can quietly qualify a card on something the other cannot check.
- DATABASE-LEVEL `ON DELETE CASCADE` IS REJECTED, AND THE CASCADE STAYS HAND-WRITTEN (2026-08-20). Written down at length because it WILL be re-proposed: it is the obvious fix to the delete bug above, it is what most schemas do, and the reasons against it are not visible from the schema. Three of them, and any one is enough.
  - **IT IS A DDL CHANGE ACROSS ROUGHLY TWENTY CONSTRAINTS ON A LIVE NEON DATABASE**, each needing a DROP and a re-ADD. `drizzle-kit push --force` is already documented in this file and in PROJECT-INSTRUCTIONS as silently no-opping in non-interactive CI precisely on constraint resolution, so it would owe a long hand-written Neon script. The bug it would fix owes NO Neon SQL at all as it was actually fixed. Paying a migration to fix something a code change already fixed is the wrong trade.
  - **IT DELETES THE APP'S ABILITY TO KNOW WHAT IT REMOVED.** A hand-written cascade names every table it touches, which is what makes a delete auditable, countable and reportable. A database cascade removes rows the app never mentions and can never count.
  - **IT REMOVES A REAL SAFETY PROPERTY.** Today a stray `DELETE FROM groups` from a console or a mistaken query ERRORS, because the children are in the way. With database cascades that same stray statement silently takes the whole ledger with it and reports success. The foreign keys are currently acting as a guard rail, and this bug is not a reason to remove one.
  **WHAT CHANGED INSTEAD IS THAT THE LIST STOPPED BEING HAND-CHECKED.** The objection to a hand-written cascade was always that somebody has to remember it, and that objection was correct: nobody did, for four months. So the remembering moved into `cascade-integrity.test.ts`, which derives the required set and order from the schema itself. Hand-written, machine-checked. If this is ever re-proposed, the question to answer first is what the test does not already cover.
- THE BEERIO PORT BOUNDARY OPENS A SECOND TIME, FOR THE TWO-HOST SYNC (James, 2026-08-19). This is the same per-defect opening the 2026-08-15 racer-label ruling established, applied again and on the same terms: the boundary opens for THIS defect, not as a licence to refactor 2,500 lines of proven code. **WHY IT QUALIFIES.** The rule's actual reason is that rewriting proven engine code risks behaviour drift with no test to catch it. Nothing here rewrites the engine: `BeerioApp.tsx` gets one guard added to the existing push effect and one new effect that re-runs an adopt the file ALREADY performs, with the author's own comment explaining why that adopt is right. **THE REASONING LIVES OUTSIDE THE VENDORED FILE**, in `roomsync.ts`, with eleven tests, so the part that can actually be got wrong is the part that has drift protection. The file's exemptions from `copy-rules.test.ts` and `pack-screens.test.ts` are untouched.
- TEXT IS NOT ALLOWED INSIDE THE BEERIO PREDICTION BAR, AND HERE ARE THE NUMBERS SO NOBODY RE-PROPOSES WHITE-ON-RED IN SIX MONTHS (2026-08-19). The percentage used to be `--ink` (#16233B) painted on the racer's own fill. **MEASURED AGAINST THE PALETTE THAT SHIPS**, which is `PALETTE` at `BeerioApp.tsx:156`, 32 colours, of which the first 16 are the ones the app AUTO-ASSIGNS:
  - `--ink` on the fill, which is what shipped, clears 4.5:1 on **17 of 32**. Fifteen fail, and **nine of the sixteen auto-assigned colours fail**, including `#E10600` at 3.16, `#2962FF` at 3.20, `#7C4DFF` at 3.26 and `#0D47A1` at 1.82. That is not a number that can be tuned.
  - The obvious escape hatch, picking whichever of white or ink scores higher PER SEGMENT, is better and still not enough: it leaves `#F50057` at 4.18 and `#536DFE` at 4.21. **A per-segment switch has a ceiling below the floor**, so it cannot be a complete fix and is not worth building.
  - The app's own `textOn` helper (a luminance-over-140 rule, not a contrast ratio) would fail on 8 of 32, and `PredictionBar` never used it anyway: it hardcoded ink.
  - Where the text went instead is measured too. `--ink` on `--foam` at `opacity-70` is **5.83:1**, which is what made the label row the right home for the signal rather than an assumption that a light card is fine.
