  **TWO REASONS SURVIVE EVEN IF THE PALETTE IS EVER RE-PICKED**, and they are the reason this is a decision rather than a contrast note: a segment narrower than its own text overflows into its neighbour, and at one vote every segment reads 100%, which says nothing the bar has not said. **A NOTE ON WHERE NOT TO MEASURE:** the eight-colour array in `scripts/tv-fit.mjs` is a harness FIXTURE and is not the app's palette. A contrast table taken from it looks plausible and describes colours no crew has ever been assigned.
- RE-PICKING THE RACER PALETTE SO EVERY COLOUR CLEARS 4.5:1 IS DEFERRED, AND SO IS GROWING THE BAR (2026-08-19). The palette is vendored and shared with the 1:1 replica, so changing it is its own session, and it is not worth doing for a percentage that no longer sits on top of it. Growing the bar would read better and is off the table by the standing rule: band fit metrics are never modified for aesthetic reasons. **THIS SESSION IS NOT A THEMING PRECEDENT.** `BeerioTvPage.tsx` is GameNight's own TV view for the pack rather than part of the vendored replica, which is why the permanent Beerio theming exemption did not apply and why the harnesses were run rather than skipped. The exemption itself is untouched.
- A NEW BRACKET IS ALLOWED ONLY WHEN EVERY EXISTING BRACKET ON THE EVENT IS COMPLETED (2026-08-19). The first half is the feature: a night can run a second tournament. **THE SECOND HALF IS THE ONE DOING THE WORK, and it is a constraint rather than a limitation**: never two non-completed brackets at once. That is what keeps at most one bracket SHOWABLE, which is what lets the TV resolver keep one unambiguous answer with no new tiebreak invented between two live tournaments, and what lets the event page tile go on naming one bracket. The alternative was not "allow two", it was "allow two and then decide, in two places, which of them a single screen means". Relaxing this later is a bigger change than deleting the check.
- THE TV OWNERSHIP RULE DOES NOT CHANGE: TOUCH-RECENCY STAYS (James, 2026-08-19, chosen over start-stickiness). The most recently TOUCHED non-completed session wins, exactly as it has since the event TV shipped. **No `started_at` column, no stored "now showing" pointer, no recency window.** The known cost is written up as an OPEN bug with its reproduction: a late write to an abandoned session steals the screen back off the game actually being played. It was logged rather than fixed because the fix trades that failure for one that is arguably worse, a session somebody opened and walked away from holding the screen over a bracket being actively scored, and because `createdAt` cannot stand in for a start time under upsert. Both rules are wrong in one direction each; picking between them is a session's work and neither is obviously right.
- QUICK PLAY IS A ROUTE THAT MINTS CONTEXT AND STOPS, AND THE SECOND IMPLEMENTATION LOST (2026-08-18). This app had TWO quick play mechanisms and only one of them could stay correct. **The one that works:** `/quickplay/<route>` creates the hidden personal crew plus a live event, returns the event id, and the client then opens the pack's OWN screen at `?event=<id>`. Quick play therefore runs the identical setup, scoring and materializer a crew night runs, so a feature added to a pack screen is in quick play the moment it ships and nobody has to remember to port it. All twelve session packs work this way, which is why none of them has ever had a parity gap. **The one that did not:** the tournament had a second entrant screen (`/quick`, four typed name boxes) POSTing its own bracket-creating endpoint. It never touched the shared setup screen, so when that screen shipped on 08-17 with crew-member entrants, the member versus guest distinction, a seeding shuffle, team entrants, `normalizeEntrants` and an entrant cap, quick play got NONE of it and nothing anywhere failed. The second path did not break; it just quietly stayed behind, which is the failure mode a duplicated path always has. **So the fix was structural rather than a feature port:** give the tournament the same route shape, send the tile through the shared screen, and DELETE the second page. Keeping it as "the simple version" is exactly how the two drift apart again. `apps/server/tests/quickplay-parity.test.ts` now asserts the rule directly (exactly one client file may create a bracket) so the next person who wants a simple version gets a red run instead of a silent gap.
- A QUICK PLAY TOURNAMENT CREDITS THE HOST, WHICH IS NOT A NEW RULE BUT AN OLD ONE FINALLY APPLIED (2026-08-18). In the personal crew the host is a MEMBER, so adding themselves through "Add from crew" writes a `match_participants` row exactly as a quick play Smash night already did. The old bracket path credited nobody and its own copy promised as much ("Players are guests, so nothing counts toward lifetime stats"), and that promise had already been false for the other twelve packs for months. The rows land in the crew `/api/me/stats` labels "Quick play". Two guards were checked rather than assumed: personal crews are excluded from the show-up record everywhere `attendanceFor` is called, so the new auto-yes RSVP cannot inflate a flake rate, and `games.name` stays `"Tournament"` so a host cannot split their own lifetime record across a bucket per typed phrase. **If the old "nothing counts" behaviour is ever wanted back, it is one line on the setup screen (prefill nothing, let the host add themselves as a guest) and it is a decision, not a default.**
- THE HOST IS AUTO-RSVP'D YES ON A QUICK PLAY EVENT (2026-08-18). Prefill everywhere in this app is the yes list, so without it every setup screen opened with an empty roster on a night whose one known player was the person looking at it. One insert in the shared `quickEvent`, so it fixed that cold start for all thirteen tiles in the same line rather than for the tournament alone.
- `side` IS WRITTEN ONLY WHEN A BRACKET HAS AT LEAST ONE TEAM ENTRANT, AND THEN ON EVERY ENTRANT INCLUDING THE SOLO ONES (2026-08-17). This is `sideIdFor`'s rule from `teams.ts` restated for a shape it does not cover: an all-solo bracket keeps writing `side: null`, because null is what "no team structure" means, and putting `"a"` and `"b"` on a 1v1 makes `meetingOutcome` classify two OPPONENTS as having played together. Nothing errors, no insert fails, the rivalry is simply wrong forever, which is the failure mode that makes this worth writing down rather than leaving to whoever reads the column next. The other half is the one that is easy to get backwards: in a bracket that DOES have teams, an entrant of one still gets a side id, because a solo entrant in a doubles bracket is a side of one, and leaving them null would say "this match had no teams" about a match that plainly did. The ids come from the SEED INDEX via `sideIdAt`, never from the picker, which mints its own against roster indices for its own use. `validateSides` is deliberately NOT called on bracket entrants: its `MAX_SIDES` of 8 is a rule about how many sides ONE MATCH can have, a sixteen-team bracket is legitimate, and `sideIdAt` already falls back to `s8`, `s9` past the eighth. Equality is the only thing anything ever does with the column.
- BRACKET PLACEMENT OVER TEAM ENTRANTS IS ALREADY THE TEAM RULE, SO THERE IS NOTHING TO ADD (2026-08-17). `placements()` ranks ENTRANTS 1..N, and when an entrant is a side that is exactly rule 2 in `teams.ts`: rank the sides, write the side's rank onto each member. A pair that goes out in the semis is placement 3 for both players. The trap is reaching for competition ranking, which would make a four-pair bracket read 1,1,3,3,5,5,7,7 and claim a third place that never existed: there were four SIDES and they came first, second, third and fourth. The two placement rules at the top of `teams.ts` stay separate and neither was unified into the other; this session only found a third shape that the existing rule already fits.
- THE TOURNAMENT CREATE ENDPOINT KEEPS ITS YES-RSVP FALLBACK, AND IT IS INSURANCE RATHER THAN A SUPPORTED PATH (2026-08-17). A POST with no `entrants` key still builds the list from the yes RSVPs, exactly as it did before the setup screen existed. The reason is the deployment shape rather than tidiness: this is an installed PWA, a phone can be running a cached bundle on a game night, and the failure mode of deleting the fallback is a host who cannot start a tournament AT ALL in front of the room. The new client always sends entrants, so the fallback costs a branch and is never taken in practice. It is documented at the route as a fallback so nobody later reads it as a second supported way in and builds on it.
- A PACK GETS THE SAME CLOTH AT A DIFFERENT TINT, NEVER A DIFFERENT MATERIAL (James's direction, settled 2026-08-17). Board Game forced this because its base IS Tabletop's: walnut and brass under a lamp, which is what a board game night looks like and is exactly why the pack dissolved into the shell. Two objects made of the same material at the same tint are one object. The rejected alternative was a treatment per pack, and it fails on three counts: nine textures to maintain, nine stylesheets each naming their own asset, and a room that reads as nine pieces of furniture rather than one table with different cloths on it. One greyscale tile tinted at the use site is why the tile is greyscale in the first place, so this is the original decision being spent rather than a new one. **OLIVE FOR BOARD GAME**, chosen off three rendered candidates: tobacco still dissolves (rail and cloth read as one brown field), slate works and drifts into Deduction's cold territory, olive holds the brass accent and stays clear of both the shell's emerald and Ping Pong's blue-green. **AND IT UNBLOCKS THE CASINO GROUP WITH NO FURTHER DESIGN WORK**: deep green, oxblood, royal and charcoal are the same tile at four tints.
- THE PACK'S OWN ACCENT SETS ITS FELT'S CEILING, WHICH IS THE SHELL'S RULE APPLIED ONE LEVEL DOWN (2026-08-17). The rendered olive was `#2b3020` lit to `#4a5236`, and it was darkened before shipping because that crown puts `--tn-accent` (the now-playing title on the TV) at 3.78 against it and `--tn-tv-muted` at 3.72, where the same two pairs read 5.30 and 5.21 on Arcade's lit walnut. Every text colour in this theme is light, so a brighter surface is a smaller gap and the lamp's PEAK is the binding number, exactly as `--gn-dim` on the crown is the shell's. Same hue vector, lower value: `#232819` lit to `#3a4129`, which clears the brass at 4.90 and keeps the lift between felt and crown at +23/25/16 against the shell's +16/26/18. The pool is as strong as the shell's, it just starts from a darker table. **THE ACCENT ITSELF WAS NEVER A CANDIDATE FOR MOVING**: it is identity, and identity does not change with the theme.
- THE TILE GENERATOR'S GUARD LIST GROWS ONE PACK AT A TIME, AND BOARD GAME IS THE FIRST (2026-08-17). `generate-felt-tile.mjs` has always said its bound applies to the surface the cloth actually covers, and until now that was the shell alone, because a pack root is not inside `.gn-app::before`. Board Game composes the tile into its own backdrop, so its baize is a textured surface and its pairs are gated there now. **ITS TWO TELEVISION PAIRS ARE GATED AT 3.0, NOT 4.5**, and that is a property of the selectors rather than a concession: `--tn-accent` on the backdrop is only ever `.tn-tv__title` at 7.4vmin and `--tn-tv-muted` is `.tn-tv__label` at 2.6vmin, about 80px and 28px on a 1080 screen, both well past WCAG's large-text threshold. Holding an 80px title to the body-copy floor would set a whole pack's palette from a constraint that does not apply to it. Gated rather than merely reported, so a future weave still cannot eat them.
- THE CROWN IS THE FULL LIT COLOUR NOW, BECAUSE THE LAMP MOVED (2026-08-17). Both guards composited `--gn-felt-lit` at 85% over `--gn-felt`, which matched a lamp whose brightest stop was 88% and whose centre sat ON the top edge of the screen, so the full value was never painted anywhere a person could read. The revised lamp is a pool: centre at `50% 5%`, inside the viewport, first stop at full strength. The brightest surface text lands on is therefore exactly `--gn-felt-lit`, and keeping 0.85 would have measured a screen that no longer exists, 0.3 of a ratio point too generously. **IT COST REAL WEAVE AND THAT IS THE RIGHT TRADE**: `--gn-dim` on the honest crown is 5.40 where the old constant read 5.72, which is what forced TARGET_SD down 6.4 to 5.4. A guard measuring a friendlier surface than the one that ships is not a guard.
- A CARD READS AS AN OBJECT BECAUSE IT CASTS, NOT BECAUSE IT IS TRANSPARENT (2026-08-17). This supersedes the whole `.24` versus `.5` alpha argument, which was tuning the wrong variable: the complaint was that the card read as a hole cut in the cloth, and the fix for that is a drop, a contact edge and a lit top lip, not more transparency. Once it casts it reads as card stock at any alpha, which frees the alpha to be chosen for CONTRAST. At `.62` seven of the eight parity exemptions closed and were deleted, per the standing requirement that the exemption list stays live. **AND THE BORDER GOES**: a 2px warm-brown line on green cloth belongs to neither the card nor the table and flattens every panel into a sticker. `--gn-card-border`, `--gn-card-shadow` and `--gn-card-pad` are tokens carrying Arcade's shipped values, because a rule a theme has to remember to override is a rule the next theme forgets.
- THE MITRE IS GEOMETRY, AND IT COSTS FOUR EMPTY ELEMENTS IN index.html (2026-08-17). Four sides drawn as four background layers on one box can only ever butt: each layer is sized to 100% of its own axis, so the top layer covers both corner squares, the left layer covers the full height, and whichever is listed first wins. Background layers cannot be masked individually, so the only way to a real mitre is four boxes, each `clip-path`ped from its outer corner to the inner one. **THE MARKUP IS UNCONDITIONAL** so no theme has to remember it, which is the same call `.gn-rail` itself made: under Arcade every plank token is `none` and `--gn-rail-w` is 0px, so all four clip to zero area and paint nothing. **AND `--gn-radius-rail` GOES TO 0px**, which reverses "a table has rounded corners": a mitre's outer end has nowhere to land on a radius, the two are mutually exclusive, and the joint is worth more than the rounding. The old single-box frame's tokens stay declared and stop painting under Tabletop, because `:root` still needs them for the four `none` layers Arcade has always computed.
- AN ARCADE SWEEP OF ZERO IS THE WRONG TEST ONCE MARKUP IS ADDED, AND ZERO CHANGED VALUES IS THE RIGHT ONE (2026-08-17). Adding four elements to `index.html` necessarily adds keys to the sweep: the route pass keys on a structural path and records every element that resolves any tracked property, so four inert planks arrive as 3432 new entries and four new rules add the rest. The claim that means anything is the one that was actually verified: of 3473 differences, **every one is an addition and not one is a change or a removal**, and every addition belongs to the four planks. The sweep's own keying is what makes that checkable, because the new children sit inside `.gn-rail` and shift no existing sibling index.
- MARIO KART FORKS RATHER THAN SHARING, AND `smash.ts` IS NOT OPENED (James, 2026-08-16). Mario Kart and Smash share a session shape (`MkSessionState` is an `Omit` of `SmashSessionState`) and `kothAdvance` lives in `smash.ts` keyed on `winnerId`/`loserId`, so the tempting move when karts arrived was to put sides into the shared shape and get Smash 2v2 free. Rejected on three grounds. **PING PONG ALREADY ANSWERED IT**: it imports from `teams.js` and nothing else, wrote its own `PpKothState` with a `kingSideId` and a queue of SIDE ids rather than reusing `kothAdvance`, and never opened Smash. Share the primitive, fork the rotation. **SMASHDOWN**: sides in `SmashSessionState` drag the burn board into a session scoped as "pairs in Mario Kart", and whether a pair burns two fighters or one changes the `floor(rosterSize / playerCount)` arithmetic the setup screen prints as a sentence. That is a question to ask, not to assume. **AND `kothAdvance` IS SHIPPED AND STABLE**: changing what its ids mean fails silently, because nothing errors when a throne is held by somebody who never played, which is the exact class this project guards against. THE COST IS ACCEPTED AND WORTH RECORDING: the two packs diverge further, they were already typed apart on purpose, and Smash 2v2 now has TWO worked examples to copy from rather than a shape nobody validated against it.
- THE SIDE LOG COMES OUT AT THE SECOND CONSUMER, AND THE THIRD IS DEFERRED ON PURPOSE (2026-08-16). `sidelog.ts` is the money board's and the title-night layer's rule applied again: one example is a pack, two is a layer. It is a NEW FILE rather than an addition to `teams.ts`, because `teams.ts` is a pure rule about placement with no notion of a session timeline, and folding a timeline into it makes the rule harder to reason about. **THERE IS A THIRD CONSUMER AND IT IS NOT CONVERTED.** `titlenight.ts` carries the same log under its own names (`TnSideSet`, `currentTnSides`, the same replace-rather-than-stack reshuffle) keyed on the GAMES count. It fits the module exactly. Converting it moves two shipped packs, Board Game and Card Table, and their fixtures, and folding that into a Mario Kart session is how a session scoped as one thing ends up as three. It is in DEFERRED with the reason attached rather than left as a duplicate nobody wrote down.
- DOUBLE DASH PLUS EXACTLY FOUR PLAYERS OPENS IN PAIRS, AND THAT IS A LINE RATHER THAN A STARTING POINT (James, 2026-08-16). Grounded in the actual game: the GameCube has four controller ports, so four players in two karts is what a Double Dash co-op night IS. Follows the Euchre precedent and for the same reason, that a default which has to be found in a menu is a default that does not get used, and the alternative is a crew recording four races as a free-for-all and noticing in the stats a month later. **EVERY OTHER ARRANGEMENT IS OPT-IN.** Three players is not two even karts, and auto-dealing somebody into a solo kart is the app making a judgement about a night it was not at. Two is a 1v1. Six and eight are not reachable on one console, and a host running two consoles can set karts by hand. Do not widen this to "any even roster" because it looks tidier. Three guards, each with its own named test because all three fail silently: it fires only when the KART COUNT differs (free-for-all counts as one kart per racer, so its count is the roster size); reverting is deterministic; and it is SETUP ONLY, because once races exist the arrangement changes through a reshuffle with a `fromIdx` and that is a deliberate host action.
- THE AUTO-APPLY'S PAIRING IS ROSTER ORDER, NOT A SHUFFLE, WHICH IS WHERE IT LEAVES THE EUCHRE PRECEDENT (2026-08-16). `applyTitleShape` deals partnerships with `shuffleIntoSides`. Mario Kart's deals `[[0,1],[2,3]]`. Two reasons. **IT MAKES GUARD 2 HOLD IN BOTH DIRECTIONS**: Double Dash, then MK8 Deluxe, then Double Dash hands the host the same screen back rather than a fresh random deal, which is what "reverting is deterministic" is actually for. **AND IT IS THE LEAST OPINIONATED ANSWER AVAILABLE**: roster order is the order the host typed people in, which on a four-port console is the order they are sitting in, and a host who wants a random deal has the Shuffle button in the picker. Two more rulings fell out of building it and are worth the line each: **Double Dash NEVER dissolves a kart** at any roster size, because reverting is justified by "no other title in the roster has a shared kart" and Double Dash is the one that does; and **a ROSTER change only ever puts karts together, never takes them apart**, or a host who hand-built karts for a five-player MK8 Deluxe night and then added a sixth would watch them dissolve, which is the feature undoing the host's work.
- UNEVEN KARTS ARE RECORDED, NOT REFEREED, AND THE SOLO RACER GETS A REAL KART ID (2026-08-16). A three-player Double Dash night where two share and one drives alone is a real thing a crew does, so the host can set it and the app records it. `validateSides` returns `even` as a FACT for the screen to warn with, and only genuinely broken arrangements are refused. Four consequences, each with its own test because none of them errors. **THE SOLO RACER'S `side` IS NOT NULL**: `isTeamPlay` is true because ANOTHER kart holds two, so `sideIdFor` does not take its null-when-every-side-is-one branch, and a null there would make `meetingOutcome` read the pair and the solo as having raced TOGETHER, wrong forever with nothing erroring. **PLACEMENTS ARE 1,1,2 or 1,2,2**, because karts are ranked 1..N over N karts and a 2v1 has two places to finish and three participant rows; competition ranking is for genuine ties between individuals and is not reached for. **LAST PLACE IS UNAFFECTED**: `countLastPlace` compares a placement against the participant count, so the losing solo is second of three and never qualifies, which is the 2026-08-05 decision applied rather than a new one. **AND GRAND PRIX NEEDS NO CHANGE.**
- THE SIDE ON A RACE IS DECIDED OVER THE KARTS THAT RACED, NOT OVER THE ARRANGEMENT (2026-08-16). In a night with one pair and two solo karts, a race between the two SOLO karts writes `side: null` on both rows. That looks inconsistent with the night and is the only reading under which "null means no team structure" stays true on the row `buildRivalry` is actually looking at: that race had no team structure in it, whatever else was going on that evening. The alternative, keying the column off whether the SESSION has a pair anywhere, would put a side on two people who raced each other one-on-one.
- THE GRAND PRIX DOUBLE COUNT IS ACCEPTED, AND IT IS THE REIGN-CREDIT RULING AGAIN (2026-08-16). `cupStandings` accumulates per PLAYER, so a four-race cup between two karts of two puts `(15 + 12) * 4 * 2` points into the table, because four people scored. Per player that is correct and the standings table is per player: two people on a kart that finished first both genuinely finished first, which is the same call Ping Pong's reign credit took on 2026-08-05. `cupStandings` and `mkPoints` were NOT MODIFIED, and the strongest form of that promise is in the tests: two race logs differing only in the `side` column produce identical standings, so a future pass that teaches the cup about karts fails there. A per-KART cup view is a separate decision about what a cup means and was not taken.
- TRUNCATE THE SIDE LOG BEFORE REBUILDING THE THRONE, IN THAT ORDER (2026-08-16). Undo is two steps and the order is the whole function, which is why `undoMkRace` is in shared rather than inline in a route. The rebuild replays the races run under the arrangement IN FORCE, so rebuilding first replays them against an arrangement nothing is raced under any more and hands the table to a kart that never won it. Nothing errors either way. The test for it runs the two steps BOTH ways round on an identical night and asserts they disagree, so a future reader who swaps the lines fails in the function and lands on a test that names the mistake.
- THE BEERIO PORT BOUNDARY IS OPEN, AND THAT IS A RULING ABOUT ONE DEFECT (James, 2026-08-15). `BeerioApp.tsx` and `beerio.ts` have been "a vendored 1:1 replica, never edited" since they landed, and that rule is why four wrong racer labels sat in the file for months with an audit declining to rule on them. The ruling is: the boundary opens for the naming defect, not as a general licence to refactor 2,500 lines of proven code. What shipped is the diff that rule allows and no more, and the file's exemptions from `copy-rules.test.ts` and `pack-screens.test.ts` are untouched. **THE STRONGER FORM OF THE OLD RULE SURVIVES**: the reason not to touch that file was never sentiment, it was that rewriting proven code risks behaviour drift with no test to catch it, and that reason is still exactly right for its engine. It was NOT right for a display string written out eighteen times.
- ONE SEED FIELD, TWO BASES, AND THE FIX IS A SIGNATURE RATHER THAN A RENAME (2026-08-15). `Player.seed` is 1-based (built as `i + 1`) and `GPStanding.seed` is 0-based (an array index), both are correct for what they are, and both are called `seed` in the same file. Renaming either would have been the obvious move and is wrong: they are two genuinely different coordinates, and a name change makes them look different without making the conversion explicit anywhere. `racerLabel(seed, name?)` takes the 1-BASED number, so a caller holding a Player passes it straight through and a caller holding an index passes `index + 1`, which puts the conversion in the open next to its array lookup instead of inside a template. Eighteen sites, four of which had derived it wrong.
- THE PACK KEY IS SAFE AND THE PACK VALUES ARE NOT, WHICH IS WHY THE GUARD SCANS ONLY ONE OF THEM (2026-08-15). `SESSION_PACKS.smsah.wsType` does not compile, so the pack KEY is already guarded by the compiler and there is no way to name a pack without naming it. `ledger`, `gameName`, `keyPrefix` and `wsType` are plain strings nothing checks, which is exactly why retyping one is invisible, and they are what `pack-identifiers.test.ts` scans. A value is skipped when it is also a pack key, also a registered ledger format, or under four characters, which leaves 22 values including every gameName. That scan found eleven violations on the CLIENT the first time it ran, after the server had been swept by hand, which is the argument for writing checks rather than trusting sweeps.
- A SIZE BUDGET WOULD NOT HAVE CAUGHT THE BUG THAT MOTIVATED IT (2026-08-15). The entry chunk drifted 214.73 to 244.57 kB over four months because nothing asserted it, so a budget was obviously worth adding. But a pack catalogue can come back and fit inside the budget, and then the budget passes while the defect is back. So `bundle-budget.test.ts` also greps the built entry chunk for three strings that belong to exactly one pack module each and to no entry-path screen. The budget is on GZIP because that is what crosses the network, it carries deliberate headroom, and a third test asserts the headroom still exists so a later edit cannot quietly tighten it onto the current measurement and train people to raise it without reading it.
- ESLINT STAYS OUT, ON EVIDENCE RATHER THAN PREFERENCE (2026-08-15). The question was whether a linter was worth a new devDependency and the regenerated lockfile that comes with it, against Render's frozen-lockfile install. `noUnusedLocals` and `noUnusedParameters` produced ELEVEN errors across a 55,000 line tree, which was the entire dead-symbol population, and they cost nothing: no dependency, no lockfile, no runtime. The repo-specific rules that a stock config could never know (no hardcoded pack identifier, no format without a label, no racer rule written by hand) are source-scanning tests in the style `copy-rules.test.ts` already established. Revisit only if a category turns up that neither the compiler nor a test can express.
- THE LOAD IS TWO NUMBERS BECAUSE THE COLUMNS DO NOT ADD UP (2026-08-15). `band.ts`'s lesson was that a ladder keyed on the seat count alone is wrong because other blocks share the screen, and the bracketed TVs turned out to need the next step: their two blocks sit SIDE BY SIDE in one grid row, so the screen costs max(left, right) rather than left + right, and a single scalar summing them would be wrong in both directions at once. They also grow on unrelated axes, which is the part measurement changed about the plan: the alive board grows with the ENTRANT COUNT and nothing else, and the on-deck stack does not grow with the roster at all, because it is a capped slice whose height is set by the band rather than by the night. So each ladder is the TIGHTER of two sub-ladders, one per column, each monotone in its own input, which keeps the pair monotone and is asserted as such. The on-deck sub-ladder is costed at the ROOMIEST slice rather than the band's own, because the band's slice depends on the band and asking that question first would be circular.
- THE BOARD IS TUNED AGAINST THE WORST STATE A COUNT PASSES THROUGH, not the state it is in (2026-08-15). The alive board has two groups, then three, then two again as people are knocked out, and the third group is worth a flat ~168px at every count measured. Taking the band off the LIVE group count would re-lay the screen out mid-night, at the exact moment somebody looked up to see who just went out. Rounding up to the worst state is `band.ts`'s rule for optional blocks applied to a block that turns itself on and off, and it costs the roomier states a rung they would not have needed.
- THE TYPE FLOOR IS THE MONEY BOARD'S, REUSED RATHER THAN RE-ARGUED (2026-08-15). 2.2vmin on the shell TV and 1.25vw on Beerio are the same physical size, 24px on a 1920x1080 screen, and it is what `.cg-tv[data-band="full"] .cg-tv__nm` has put a player's name at since 2026-08-02. This app now has three televisions and one answer to how small a NAME goes on one, which is worth more than three separately defensible answers. **IT APPLIES TO NAMES ONLY**: round titles, vote counts, seed numbers and group labels are recognised from a couch rather than read, and they go smaller in every band. **AND IT IS ASSERTED AGAINST THE STYLESHEETS**, because it is the one number in the pass a person has to be trusted with: a future band tuned to make something fit must spend gaps, padding or the QR code, and cannot buy the room out of somebody's name. The hard constraint it serves is that every entrant stays on the board at sixteen: no "+3 more", no scroll, no collapsing the field into a count, because a board that answers "who is left" for eleven of sixteen people has stopped answering it.
- BEERIO'S HEADER IS ON ITS LADDER, WHICH THE SHELL'S DID NOT NEED (2026-08-15). 561px of that screen's 1080 went on chrome before a single racer was drawn, most of it the BEERIO KART wordmark at 5.5vw with a QR beside it, and no amount of chip density recovers half a television. The wordmark, the LIVE pill, the page padding and the QR all shrink with the band. The QR is the odd one: it is a React prop on `<QRCodeSVG>` rather than a CSS property, so a variable override cannot reach it and the ladder hands the pixel size over explicitly, floored at 96px because that still scans from across a room. **THE REAL CLIFF ON THAT SCREEN WAS NOT TYPE AT ALL**: at sixteen racers with the 24-character name the pack allows, a base-metrics chip is wider than half its column, so the flex row wraps to ONE chip per line and the board doubles to 1182px. Getting two per line back is worth more than any font change, which is why the tight band spends horizontal padding hardest.
- A FIT HARNESS THAT ONLY REPORTS THE FIT IS HALF A CHECK (2026-08-15). `scripts/tv-fit.mjs` has asked two questions since the rail shipped and only the second one, is anything COVERED, could ever fail the run. A case could print "OVER by 468" in the first column and the script still exited 0. That is the same shape as the `rendered` flag being computed and never read, which let Ping Pong measure a waiting screen for four days, and it is how four bracketed cases were added in one session, all four over a 1080p screen, with the exit code silent about it. The fit is now enforced, with the two logged pack overflows still exempted by name. **AND THE RUN CARRIES A NEGATIVE CONTROL**, which is the discipline this repo keeps arriving at: before reporting that the ladder works it re-injects the PRE-LADDER metrics and requires the binding cases to go over. That control caught itself on its first run. Its stylesheet tied on specificity with the band rules and lost to a pack sheet arriving on a lazy chunk, so Beerio's control silently did nothing and reported a pass; the selectors are doubled now and the shell's control was pinned to the wrong baseline (the current roomy rung, already denser than what shipped) which made it 18px over instead of 229.
- THE ROUND COMPARATOR IS SHARED, AND IT TAKES TWO NUMBERS SO IT CANNOT BE TWO RULES (2026-08-15). Beerio's on-deck list was in insertion order and `TvPage` already had the fix, so the obvious move was to copy the five lines across. THAT IS HOW THE TWO SCREENS GOT HERE: they are two renderings of the same thing that were written apart, and a second copy of a sort rule is a third disagreement waiting for the session that tunes one of them. `compareRoundOrder` lives in `packages/shared/src/bracketboard.ts` and takes `{ side, depth }` AND NOTHING ELSE, which is the load-bearing part of the design rather than minimalism: with no ids, titles or match counts in the signature neither caller can sort by something the other cannot see, and both have to state their coordinates explicitly. `TvPage` maps in from the round metadata its payload carries; Beerio maps in from `roundOrderFromKey(def.grp)`. **BESIDE `bracket.ts`, NOT IN IT.** The new module derives nothing about a bracket: no match, no bye, no reset, no placement. It reads what a `compute()` already produced, reduced to seeds, so the engine's behaviour is untouched and a session that changes the boards cannot change the tournament. The parse returns NULL for an unknown key rather than a silent 0, because a zero depth would sort an unrecognised round to the front of the night.
- THE RESULTS COLUMN WAS GIVEN UP, AND WHAT REPLACED IT IS NOT A NICER VERSION OF IT (2026-08-15). Both bracketed TVs spent half the screen on what just finished. That is the one thing in the room nobody needs a television for: the result happened three feet away and was cheered. What a bracket night actually leaves open across two hours is WHO IS STILL IN IT, and specifically who is one loss from going home, which is not derivable at a glance from a list of recent scores. So the column now carries unbeaten, one-loss and out rather than the last six matches. **THE COST IS REAL AND WAS ACCEPTED**: a result recorded while nobody was watching (a phone in another room) no longer has a place on the big screen, and the strip's counts are the only trace of it. **SINGLE ELIM SHIPPED AS ITS OWN SHAPE** rather than as double elim with an empty middle group, because a "one loss, next one is out" heading on a single-elim night is a lie about the rules, and an empty group is how a lie like that survives review. **THE CHAMPION BEATS THE BOARD FOR THE SAME REASON THE BOARD BEAT THE RESULTS**, honesty rather than hierarchy: after the last race "next one is out" is false, and in double elim the champion themselves can be sitting in that group having come up through the losers bracket, so the board comes down and the strip (every round done, still true) stays.
- THE FULL BRACKET TREE ON TV IS REJECTED, ON MEASUREMENT (2026-08-15). It is the obvious missing feature and it will be proposed again, so it is written down. Drawn at EIGHT entrants, which is the friendly case, a tree fills the top third of a 1080p screen with four columns, fifteen slots and roughly 15px names, and most of those slots read TBD for most of the night. Sixteen is worse in every direction at once. THE ARGUMENT IS THE SAME ONE THE ORIGINAL TV COMMENT MAKES and it has been in `TvPage`'s header since it shipped: a tree is a reference document for someone standing at it, and a television is read from a couch. The bracket tree already exists where it belongs, on the phone (`BracketPage`), where the reader is 18 inches away and can scroll. What the TV owes the room is what is happening now, what is next, and who is still alive, which is what the three pieces of this session are.
- A TV VIEW JOINS BOTH BROWSER HARNESSES ON THE COMMIT THAT SHIPS IT, PACK OR NOT (2026-08-15). The ADDING A PACK checklist has said this since 2026-08-09 and it is scoped to packs, which is why it did not catch anything here: the two screens measured this session are OLDER THAN THE CHECKLIST AND OLDER THAN BOTH HARNESSES, so no step was skipped and nothing was anybody's fault. That is precisely the hole. A rule written as "when you add a pack, remember to" only ever covers what arrives after the rule, and this repo's whole harness history is the same lesson in different costumes (the money board measured to the footer, ping pong's payload going stale, Board Game unmeasured for five days). The checklist item is now phrased around the SCREEN rather than the pack, and the two oldest TVs are in both lists.

- SALEM MEANS SALEM 1692, THE CARD GAME, AND THE CATALOGUE HAD THE WRONG GAME (James, 2026-08-14, after playing a night and finding a Mafioso). Two different games share the name. TOWN OF SALEM is the online PC and mobile game and its roles are what shipped here on 2026-08-10; it runs in its own client with its own UI, so a crew playing it has no use for a moderator board or a TV and this pack cannot serve it at all. SALEM 1692 is the physical card game for parties and large groups, which is exactly what this pack is for. So Salem is now Townsperson, Witch and the Constable, and seven roles plus four factions belonging to a different game came out. **REMOVAL IS NOT THE SAME HAZARD AS RENAMING:** existing ledger rows keep their ids intact and the screens already fall back to the raw id for a role the catalogue does not have. **THE REAL HAZARD IS REUSE**, so it is written down and asserted: none of the removed ids may EVER be re-added to Salem meaning something else, because that silently relabels rows already written. If Town of Salem is ever wanted it is a DIFFERENT TITLE with its own label, not more roles under this one.
- THE SALEM LABEL NEVER CHANGES, AND AN ALIAS FIXES THE INPUT INSTEAD (2026-08-14). Renaming the title to "Salem 1692" was the obvious move and is the wrong one: `matches.label` carries the title and the crew's recents carry it, so a rename splits that title's history across two names with nothing erroring, which is the silent failure this file already catalogues for pack display names. `SD_TITLE_ALIASES` folds "Salem 1692" onto "Salem" before `canonicalTitle` runs, at all three title entry points, so a host typing either lands on the one label. "TOWN OF SALEM" IS DELIBERATELY NOT ALIASED: it is a genuinely different game rather than another name for this one, so it stays uncurated and opens the empty catalogue, which is the honest answer.
- THE WITCH KEEPS HER ID AND CHANGES ALIGNMENT (2026-08-14), which is the one place this correction changes meaning underneath a stable identifier. Under Town of Salem she was a SOLO third party; in Salem 1692 the Witches are a main EVIL faction. The role id still means "a witch" so it stays, and the faction id `witch` stays too because a faction id is written to `match_participants.side` and is exactly as permanent as a role id. What moves is the ALIGNMENT, from solo to evil, so any row recorded between 2026-08-10 and 2026-08-14 carries `meta.alignment: "solo"` and now means something different. NOTHING IN CODE CAN FIX THAT, so it was not guessed at: a read-only audit query shipped with this session for the Neon console, to be run BEFORE deploying, and what happens to any rows it finds is James's call rather than a migration written on spec.
- A GAME NOBODY PICKED IS NOT WEREWOLF (James, 2026-08-14). `SD_DEFAULT_DEF` was Village/Wolves with a `villager` and a `wolf`, returned for an empty title AND for every free-typed one. Both server routes already refused an empty title, so that half was only cosmetic (the deal card rendered a Werewolf catalogue before anyone had picked anything, so the game looked pre-selected). THE FREE-TYPED HALF WAS THE DAMAGING ONE: type "One Night Ultimate Werewolf" or anything uncurated and the app OFFERED those two roles, and a host who accepted them wrote `meta.role: "villager"` and `meta.faction: "village"` permanently for a game that has neither. Same class of false record as a Witch recorded as a Townsperson, except on the DEFAULT path, which is where it would happen most. An uncurated title now opens EMPTY and the host types the roles, which is exactly what typed roles were built for, so this was a deletion rather than a feature. The baselines became optional, `suggestComposition` returns nothing without them, and the two-faction floor still refuses a typed-only deal with nobody to find. **THE TEST THAT ASSERTED THE OLD BEHAVIOUR WAS THE BUG WRITTEN DOWN AS A TEST** ("anything else opens the default shape") and was changed rather than deleted, so the next reader sees what it used to claim.
- EVERY EXISTING ROLE ID IS PERMANENT: ADD ONLY, NEVER RENAME (James, 2026-08-10: "keep the existing roles"). Role ids are written into `match_participants.meta.role` on shipped ledger rows, so renaming one orphans that history with NOTHING ERRORING, exactly like a `ledgerKey`, a `gameName` or a pack's display name: the row stays, the lookup stops matching, and a win-rate-by-role surface quietly disagrees with the night it describes. The guard is mechanical rather than a note: every role id that shipped before this pass is transcribed by hand into `SHIPPED_ROLE_IDS` and asserted to still resolve, in the same title, with a faction. Three of them do not match their own name's slug (`alpha` for Alpha Werewolf, `servant` for Loyal Servant, Avalon's `minion` for Minion of Mordred); those are named exceptions rather than a reason to loosen the rule, because they predate typed roles and nothing can ever have been typed against them.
- A FREE-TYPED ROLE CANONICALIZES TO THE ID THE CURATED ROLE WOULD HAVE (2026-08-10), and this is the load-bearing line of the whole pass. If a host types "Witch" tonight and a later session adds Witch to Salem's catalogue, those must be THE SAME ROLE IN THE LEDGER. Anything else splits one player's history across a typed row and a curated row, and the split is silent. There is deliberately NO separate namespace like `custom:Witch`: that is the split, dressed as tidiness. `canonicalRole` matches a typed name against the curated NAMES first (which is what keeps the three legacy ids reachable), then against the ids, and only a genuine miss mints `sdRoleSlug(name)`. The slug STRIPS rather than hyphenates because "Serial Killer" already ships as `serialkiller`. Every role added from now on must slug cleanly, which is asserted, or a role typed before it was curated lands somewhere the curated entry cannot reach. Canonicalization happens SERVER-SIDE, because the id is what reaches the record forever and a client that minted its own would be one refactor from the split.
- A TYPED ROLE MUST CARRY A FACTION (2026-08-10), and it is structural rather than fussy. `factionsFromRoles` derives every player's side from their role and the result form ranks FACTIONS, so a role belonging to nothing cannot be ranked and the game it was dealt in could not be recorded at all. So typing a role means also saying who it wins with, chosen from the title's existing factions, or a new SOLO faction for a role that wins alone, which mints a faction named after the role exactly as the Jester and the Tanner already are in the catalogue. The same rule is why Blood on the Clocktower's Traveller needed TWO entries rather than one role with a floating alignment.
- SHARED WINS ARE 1, 1, 3, NOT 1, 1, 2 (2026-08-10). Two placement rules live in this app and both are correct, as teams.ts says at its head: a TEAM result ranks sides 1..N so a 2v2 is 1,1,2,2, and a GENUINE TIE uses competition ranking, which leaves a gap. Two factions sharing a win is a TIE BETWEEN SIDES, so with three factions on the table the loser is THIRD: there was no second place, there were two firsts. Closing the gap to 1,1,2 would quietly rewrite what a placement means in every row the other rule already wrote. This is the first result in the app where two sides win together, so nothing exercised the path before; `placementsFromRankedSides` turned out to handle it already, and what this pass added was the control on the record form (its label used to read "lost with the one above", written when the top faction always won alone) and the tests through the ranked order, the placements, the ledger lines and the night summary.
- TEST FILES ARE NOT IN THE TYPECHECK SCOPE, AND A FIXTURE THAT DRIFTS MAKES A GUARD LIE (found 2026-08-10). Both packages' tsconfigs are `include: ["src"]`, so a test that hand-builds a payload can quietly omit a field the real code now always sets. That happened to the secrecy suite's pinned deal-key assertion the moment typed roles added two fields: the test kept passing against a shape the server never serves. The fix is not to widen the typecheck, it is to make the fixtures build their payloads THE WAY THE ROUTE DOES, field for field, which is now written at the top of the fixture. Worth recording because the failure mode is a green test that is checking nothing, which is the one thing a redaction guard cannot afford.
- THE TV IS A PROJECTION, NOT A FILTER, AND THAT IS WHY IT IS SAFE (2026-08-10). Social Deduction's TV is the only screen in this app with a secrecy constraint: it is public, UUID-keyed and unauthenticated, so anybody with the event link can open it, including a player at the table on their own phone while the game is running. The rule is that an unrevealed role must be ABSENT FROM THE PAYLOAD, not hidden by CSS, not rendered face down with the value in the DOM, not filtered on the client, because all three are readable by anybody who opens a console. `sdTvView` does not spread the board: it REBUILDS each player out of seven named fields and never touches the secret store, so there is no code path that has an unrevealed role in a variable it could accidentally serialize, and a role planted on session state is dropped on the way OUT rather than filtered on the way IN. **THAT PROPERTY WAS FOUND RATHER THAN DESIGNED IN,** which is worth recording honestly: the first negative control planted a role on the board and could not make it leak, because the projection had already eaten it. It is now asserted as its own test, and the negative controls plant in the projection's OUTPUT instead, which is where a real regression would come from (a future session editing `sdTvView` to "just include the role so the TV can colour the tiles").
- NO GENERIC READER OF game_sessions MAY SELECT THE state COLUMN (2026-08-10), and it is now a test rather than a property nobody had written down. Part A's secrecy rests on the secret row being invisible to everything that reads the table generically: `events.ts` and `tv.ts` both read by eventId ALONE, across every pack's row including the secret one, and both project `{ pack, status, updatedAt }` and drop a row `PACK_BY_LEDGER` cannot resolve. That was true and unpinned. A future session adding `state` to either select would hand the deal to the event detail payload AND EVERY EXISTING TEST WOULD STAY GREEN, because they all scan the pack's own payloads. The check walks every server source, finds each `.from(gameSessions)` and reads the projection immediately before it; a bare `.select()` (all columns, including state) fails too, and the two files allowed to read state are named because both key by PACK as well as by event. It is the difference between safe by construction and safe until somebody edits a select. **WRITING IT CAUGHT A BUG IN ITSELF FIRST:** a forward lazy regex matched from the first `.select(` in the file and swallowed three hundred lines, reporting a comment as a state read, so the check searches BACKWARD from each read to the nearest select and has its own negative control.
- THE LIVE BOARD IS OPT-IN, OFF BY DEFAULT, AND FLIPS MID-SESSION BOTH WAYS (James, 2026-08-10: "we always lead with low friction tracking"). Blackjack's tracker is the precedent and the rule comes with it: THE BOARD BEING OFF NEVER LOSES A STAT THE RESULT FORM COULD HAVE CAPTURED. Off is a real mode rather than a degraded one, and a night moderated entirely on paper still records the title, the factions and the winner, which is everything win rate as village versus as wolf needs. Turning it ON partway through opens at Night 1 with everybody alive rather than inventing a day count for a game the app was not watching; turning it OFF discards nothing already recorded, because each game bakes `survived` and `votedOutFirst` onto its own lines at the moment it is recorded. Those two stay ABSENT rather than zero for any game played with the board off, and there is deliberately NO BOX TO TYPE THEM INTO: roulette's `bestStreak` settled that, and a `false` here would claim everybody died. The host's PREFERENCE outlives a game and the board itself does not, so a deal opens a fresh board without anybody re-flipping a toggle.
- REVEAL ON DEATH IS ONE WAY, PER PLAYER (2026-08-10). Putting somebody out reveals their role on the TV and bringing them back does NOT un-reveal it, which looks like a missing undo and is the honest behaviour: the room has already read it off the big screen, and a screen that took it back would be telling the table something untrue. A mis-tap is survivable (the player comes back alive, out of the exit order, and the ledger reads them as having survived), and the button says out loud that the reveal is not part of what comes back. The client never NAMES the role either: it sends `reveal: true` and the server looks it up in the secret store, so a client cannot reveal a role that was never dealt and does not have to know one to ask.
- THE BOARD IS HOST ONLY, EVEN WITH OPEN SCORING ON (2026-08-10). Open scoring lets a member record a RESULT, which is a claim about a game everybody just watched and argued about. The board is different in kind: it is the instrument the moderator is holding while they run the game, and its reveal route reaches the secret store, so whoever can tap it is whoever can learn the table. Dealing was already host-only for the same reason in part A. Stated on the screen rather than left implicit, because a toggle labelled "let members record results" reads like it covers everything on the page.
- A PACK'S STYLESHEET IS TOKEN-FIRST FROM THE COMMIT THAT CREATES IT (2026-08-10). `deduction.css` joined `copy-rules.test.ts`'s TOKENISED_CSS list on the day it was written rather than waiting for stage 4's per-pack conversion pass, which is what the other nine are queued for. It costs nothing on the day: every colour goes in a token block, every structural rule reads `var()`, and an alpha is `color-mix()` rather than `rgba()`. Stage 4 therefore has nothing to do for this pack, and the list finishes one pack sooner. **VISUAL IDENTITY WAS JAMES'S CALL** and was made before any of it was written: the MOONLIT NIGHT (deep indigo ground, moon silver for the living, cold slate for whoever is out, ember red only on a revealed evil role), chosen against a candlelit alternative that sat too close to Board Game's amber and Card Table's red.
- ROLES NEVER ENTER THE SHARED SESSION STATE (James, 2026-08-10). The rejected alternative was making `sessionView` VIEWER-AWARE, and it is worth saying why, because it is the obvious fix and it is wrong: it touches the shared plumbing all ten packs sit on, and it makes every future pack responsible for REMEMBERING to redact, which is a rule that holds right up until somebody adds a field. Instead the secret lives in its own store and the session payload is public-safe BY CONSTRUCTION rather than by filtering. Same instinct as `sideIdFor` holding the null rule in one place: make the wrong thing unrepresentable, not merely filtered out. Concretely, the shared state a pack page renders carries the roster, the title, the log and the standings, plus the DEAL SUMMARY (how many wolves are in play), which is public because every game in this genre opens with the moderator saying the setup out loud and a screen that hid it would be hiding what the room already knows. Who has what is not there for anybody, ever, while a game is live, and becomes public at REVEAL, where it moves onto the recorded game, which is exactly where it is supposed to be. Guarded by `apps/server/tests/deduction-secrecy.test.ts`, which scans the payload the real runtime produces, pins its exact key list so a new field is a deliberate act, and is negative-controlled twice over. THE WEBSOCKET HUB IS NOT A SECOND LEAK VECTOR, checked rather than assumed: packs broadcast `{ type, eventId }` only, a ping that triggers a refetch, so fixing the one function fixed the wire with it. The roomless-hub item in BUGS stays a SCALE concern, not a secrecy one.
- OWN STATE, SHARED RULES: SOCIAL DEDUCTION DOES NOT RIDE THE TITLE-NIGHT LAYER (2026-08-10). Investigated against the real files rather than assumed, because this file got exactly that wrong about Card Table once (2026-08-05, below). It looks like a title night and it is not one, for two structural reasons and the second settles it: (1) the layer's sides are STABLE FOR THE NIGHT with a reshuffle log keyed by the game index it took effect from, and Card Table partners genuinely persist across hands, while deduction factions are RE-DEALT EVERY SINGLE GAME, so a reshuffle would fire before every game and the log would carry one entry per game, which is the model telling you it is the wrong model; (2) `recordTnGame` reads `currentTnSides(state)` internally, so riding it REQUIRES factions to sit in session state during play, which is precisely what the decision above forbids. `titlenight-runtime.ts` is declared `PackRuntime<TnSessionState>` all the way down, so once the state shape differs its routes do not transfer either. WHAT IS REUSED, AND IT IS A REGRESSION NOT TO: `placementsFromRankedSides` for the faction result, `sideIdFor` for the `side` column, `validateSides` for the structure, `validateFfaSize` for the cap, and the three PURE title functions (`normalizeTitle`, `canonicalTitle`, `tnTitleSuggestions`), which have no state dependency and carry over free. Reuse the RULES, not the state shape.
- THIRD-PARTY SOLO ROLES ARE IN SCOPE FOR v1 (James, 2026-08-10). Tanner, Jester and the Serial Killer are in the box and a crew that plays one has played it whether the app models it or not, so they are factions like any other, with a third `alignment` value rather than a boolean. The arithmetic was checked before committing to it: sides `[Tanner, Village, Wolves]` with a tie flag on Wolves gives placements 1, 2, 2, which says the Tanner won and the other two lost together, and because VILLAGE HOLDS SEVERAL MEMBERS `isTeamPlay` is true, so the Tanner gets a REAL side id rather than null. That last part is the whole reason it has its own named test: a solo winner is the shape that WOULD go null if nothing else at the table had multiple members, and a null `side` makes `meetingOutcome` misread the row forever with nothing erroring. The `alignment` travels onto the ledger row alongside the faction id, because Secret Hitler's evil faction is `fascists` and Werewolf's is `wolves` and a lifetime stat still wants to count them together without maintaining a table of which ids are evil.
- THE LIVE BOARD IS OPTIONAL AND OFF BY DEFAULT, AND THAT SPLITS THE STATS (James, 2026-08-10: "we always lead with low friction tracking"). Blackjack's tracker is the precedent and the rule comes with it: THE BOARD BEING OFF MUST NEVER LOSE A STAT THE RESULT FORM COULD HAVE CAPTURED. So win rate as village versus as wolf comes off the FACTION on the ledger row, which the result form captures, and it is free with the board off. Survival rate and first voted out REQUIRE the board, and they follow roulette's max-consecutive-winning-spins precedent exactly: ABSENT rather than zero when the board was off, and deliberately NO BOX TO TYPE THEM INTO, because offering one invites a guess into the ledger. A `false` there would claim everybody died. Asserted in `packages/shared/tests/deduction.test.ts`, including that a value smuggled in through a typed field is ignored, the way roulette asserts it for `bestStreak`.
- THE ROLE READS DO NOT GO THROUGH THE CLIENT CACHE, and the session brief asked for the opposite (2026-08-10). `useCachedApi` writes through to LOCALSTORAGE, and a role is a secret with a lifetime of about twenty minutes: persisting it would leave the answer to "who was the wolf" on the device after the game, after the night and across packs, and a re-deal would be served the previous game's answer until a revalidation landed. That is a second, stronger version of the reason `usePackSession` already bypasses the cache for a pack's live session (a cached roster means tapping a player who is no longer in it). So both role routes are plain on-demand fetches, nothing about them is stored anywhere, and the fetch happens on the TAP rather than on mount so a role is not sitting in component state through the argument that follows. Written down because it is a deliberate deviation from a written instruction rather than an oversight.
- THE TITLE AUTO-APPLIES ITS SHAPE, WITH TWO GUARDS THAT KEEP IT OUT OF THE WAY (2026-08-09). The rule below it (a title sets a default, 2026-08-05) was a decision with no mechanism; this is the mechanism. Tapping Euchre puts the table into pairs and tapping Hearts puts it back, server-side, on the now-playing route only. THE POINT IS THAT NOBODY HAS TO KNOW IT EXISTS: a default that has to be found in a menu is a default that does not get used, and the alternative is a crew recording four hands of Euchre as a free-for-all and noticing in the stats a month later. **GUARD ONE: IT FIRES ONLY WHEN THE SIDE COUNT DIFFERS.** A host who has put four people into two specific pairs and then taps Euchre keeps their pairs, because the title wanted two sides and there are two sides. Free-for-all counts as one side per player, so its count is the roster size, and a nice thing falls out rather than being special-cased: WITH TWO PLAYERS partnerships and free-for-all are the same arrangement and the table is left alone. **GUARD TWO: GOING BACK TO FREE-FOR-ALL IS DETERMINISTIC**, `singletonSides` in roster order and never a shuffle, because the arrangement that means "no teams" is the only one there is, so randomising it would be theatre and Hearts-then-Euchre-then-Hearts gives the host the same screen back. A CLEARED TITLE CHANGES NOTHING, because "between games" is where a title night spends most of its evening. It runs on the CANONICAL title, and it never runs on the record route: the record form can carry its own title, and rearranging the table at the moment a game is submitted would change the sides the game was just played under. Both guards fail SILENTLY if they are wrong, because a table quietly reshuffled between two hands looks exactly like a table somebody rearranged on purpose, which is why each has a test named for it.
- A LIGHT PACK SURFACE BREAKS SHELL COLOURS THAT NOTHING CHECKS, AND CARD TABLE IS THE FIRST ONE (2026-08-09). Every other pack in this app has dark cards, so every shell colour that lands on one was tuned for a dark surface. Card Table's cards are cream, and THREE things would have failed silently on it: `--gn-danger` is a pale pink that all but vanishes on paper, the shell's confirm teal is a neon that fights it, and the HOST TOGGLE is the shell's own component reading `--gn-yes` / `--gn-no` directly, so it is invisible to a pack's token list entirely and measured 3.4 and 3.1 against the cream. The first two became layer tokens (`--tn-danger`, `--tn-go`) that default to the shell's values, so the pack that shipped first is unaffected; the toggle is re-pointed on `.ct-root .gn-toggle` rather than on the pack root, so nothing else can pick up a re-pointed shell token by accident. **THE NUMBER IS THE POINT.** The bottle green was `#1d7a45` first and measured 4.47 against the cream, three hundredths under AA: that is the exact miss eyeballing produces and a measurement does not. Every pair is now asserted in `theme-contrast.test.ts` in both themes, including the toggle's two inks, which is the first entry in that file for a component the pack does not own. THE GENERAL SHAPE, for the next light pack: a shared component that reads shell tokens directly is the thing to look for, because a pack's own token list cannot see it and neither can a reviewer reading the pack's stylesheet.
- A TITLE SETS THE DEFAULT SHAPE AND THE HOST CAN ALWAYS OVERRIDE IT (James, 2026-08-05). Euchre and Spades are partnership games and should open that way; Hearts, Rummy and the rest should not. But house rules are real: three-handed euchre exists, partnership rummy exists, and a default that could not be overridden would be the app refereeing somebody's kitchen table. So the table is a STARTING POSITION, never a rule, and it lives in the pack's config rather than the engine. A FREE-TYPED TITLE HAS NO DEFAULT and opens free-for-all, because there is nothing to look it up in. The same rule is meant to reach any pack where a title implies a shape, which is why it is in the shared layer rather than in Card table.
- A SCORE HAS A GRAIN, AND THE ROW HAS TO SAY WHICH (2026-08-05). `match_participants.score` is per participant, so a PER-SIDE score is written onto every member of that side and a crew-wide sum reads a partnership score once per partner. Ping Pong accepted exactly that double count on 2026-08-05 and this does the same thing for consistency, but goes one better: the grain travels in `match_participants.meta` alongside the score. A row that says its score was a side score can be divided by the side size later; a row that does not say is a number nobody can interpret in a year. Cheap now and impossible retroactively, which is the same shape as the decision to store the title as a display name rather than an id. A row written before this carries no grain, which reads as per-player, and that is what every one of them was.
- THE SECOND EXAMPLE IS WHEN YOU EXTRACT, AND THE BACKLOG WAS WRONG ABOUT WHICH SIDE OF IT WE WERE ON (2026-08-05). This file said Card table was cheap "because it shares Board Game's whole implementation". It did not: Board Game's implementation was Board Game's, and the sharing was an extraction nobody had done. The estimate had quietly priced the far side of work that had not happened, which is the most expensive kind of wrong estimate because it looks like a small item. Worth recording as a shape rather than an incident: when this file says a queued pack is cheap because it reuses another one, check that the reuse EXISTS before believing the price.
- MARIO KART TEAM VS WAS CONSIDERED AND NOT QUEUED (James, 2026-08-05), so a later session does not rediscover it as a fresh idea. It is a real Mario Kart mode, but it is the odd one out among the team retrofits: every team result the app records today comes from a TAPPED finish order, while a Team VS side's rank comes from its members' SUMMED POINTS. That would be the first team placement the app COMPUTES rather than RECORDS, which is a different claim about what this app does and deserves its own decision rather than riding in on a batch. Not refused, not scheduled.
- NO TEAMS ON THE CASINO PACKS (2026-08-05). Blackjack, roulette, craps and poker are individual by construction: each player's row is their own buy-in and their own net, and there is no shared result to put a side on. Casino Run is the co-op exception and already writes one shared side, which is what `side` was built for in the first place. Written down so nobody completes the set for symmetry's sake.
- BEERIO KART IS EXEMPT FROM THE TEAM RETROFITS, as it is from the theme work, and for the same reason: it is a vendored 1:1 port and the port is the point.
- A TEAM PLACEMENT RANKS SIDES; A TIE RANKS PLAYERS. BOTH RULES ARE CORRECT (2026-08-05). A 2v2 writes 1,1,2,2, not the 1,1,3,3 competition ranking would give it, because there were two SIDES and therefore two places to finish: a losing pair did not come third in a field of four. A genuine tie keeps competition ranking, because those players finished level with each other as individuals. The two rules sit next to each other in `teams.ts` with this reasoning, because the next session to read them will be tempted to unify them, and doing so silently rewrites what a placement means in every row already written under the other rule.
- TEAM RESULTS WILL NEVER COUNT TOWARD ANYONE'S LAST-PLACE STAT, and that is right (2026-08-05). `countLastPlace` derives last place by comparing a placement against the participant count of that match, so a 2v2 loser is placement 2 of 4 and never qualifies. Last place is a FREE-FOR-ALL idea: in a two-sided match there is a loser, not a last, and calling the losing pair "last" would be counting two people as the worst of four when they were half of a two-horse race. Logged as a decision rather than left as an accident, because the alternative reading (that this is a bug in `countLastPlace`) is the one a future session would reach for.
- A CREW THAT PLAYS A LOT OF DOUBLES WILL SKEW TOWARD 1sts AND 2nds, ACCEPTED (2026-08-05). The placement distribution and `avgPlacement` do not know how many sides a match had, so a night of 2v2 contributes only 1s and 2s to both. That is not a distortion, it is the truth: there genuinely were only two places to finish. Recording the field size per match would fix the arithmetic and cost a column plus a migration, and it would still leave "average finish" meaning two different things depending on the format. Left alone deliberately, and written down so the skew is a known property rather than a discovery.
- A DOUBLES WIN IS A WIN, so `feedAgg` and `finishAgg` were not touched (2026-08-05). This is the 2026-08-02 `side` decision carried forward unchanged: a teammate game is still a game you played and belongs in both players' lifetime totals. Only the RIVALRY treats a shared side specially (the fourth `together` outcome, which the streak skips), because that is the one place where "what happened between these two people" is the question being asked. Everywhere else the question is "what did this player do", and they won.
- TABLETOP STAGE 4 IS DEPRIORITIZED BECAUSE OF THE IMPLEMENTATION, NOT THE IDEA (2026-08-04, James's call: "I have not been happy with tabletop's implementation so it will be our lowest priority for now"). Stage 4 is the conversion of the remaining nine PACKS to the theme, and the thing he is unhappy with is the SHELL the packs would be converted to match. Converting nine packs to a treatment that is going to be revisited is nine packs of rework, so the order of operations is the whole point: the shell has to be right before anything is copied off it, and until then every pack that stays unconverted is a pack that costs nothing to change its mind about. That also reframes what the pilot bought. Ping Pong is not one ninth of the way through a conversion, it is the WORKED EXAMPLE that made the mechanism explicit and testable, and a worked example is exactly the right amount of a thing to have built when the direction is still in question. Nothing about the mechanism is in doubt (the per-pack checklist, the token contract, the sweep), and none of it expires while it waits. WHAT THIS DOES NOT MEAN: it is not a reversal of the theme, and the entries behind it stand unchanged. The felt still does not reach the packs, the casino group's identity-under-a-walnut-shell problem is still unsolved, and Board Game shipped unconverted and is the sharpest version of that problem. They are simply not urgent, and the file should stop implying they are: stage 4 sat at slot 3 for four consecutive passes and each map narrative wrote it up as next, which is how a queue quietly stops describing anybody's actual intent.
- A BOARD GAME'S PLACEMENT IS TAPPED, NEVER DERIVED FROM A SCORE (2026-08-04, James's design). Board games disagree about direction: Catan is high-wins, Hearts is low-wins, and plenty have no score at all. So any engine that derives placement from a number has to know each title's direction, and a wrong direction does not error, it silently INVERTS that title's leaderboard forever. Tapping the finish order removes the question entirely and costs the host nothing, since they already know who won. The score is kept as an OPTIONAL NOTE in `match_participants.meta` because crews like the number, and it is explicitly not a source of truth: a typed score that contradicts the tapped order is allowed to stand and the app must not "fix" either one. That is written at the function somebody would otherwise add a sort to, and asserted by a test that feeds a contradicting scoreline in both directions, so it cannot pass by being right about one of them. Note the score is deliberately NOT on `match_participants.score`: that column is a ranking input where packs use it (Mario Party's stars decide its winner), and a note that lands in a column other code ranks by is a bug waiting for its second reader.
- FREE-TEXT TITLES NEED CANONICALIZATION OR THEY SPLIT HISTORY THE WAY `games.name` DOES (2026-08-04). Per-title stats read `matches.label`, so "Catan", "Settlers of Catan" and "catan" are three titles, three champions and three histories, and nothing anywhere errors. Same silent-registration family as the `ping_pong` ledger typo and the `beerio` emoji key, one level further down. TWO DEFENCES, in this order: the crew's OWN RECENTS offered first, which is the one that actually works because it is the path most nights take, and case-fold matching on entry as the backstop, done SERVER-SIDE so a stale client cannot write a third spelling. Trim and collapse internal whitespace first, match against the crew's recents AND the curated starter list, and let an existing spelling win. Store the DISPLAY NAME rather than an id, because `label` is a display string everywhere else it is used and the leaderboard reads it directly. The app matches spellings and does not guess at synonyms: "Catan" and "Settlers of Catan" stay two titles unless the crew's own history makes one of them the match, which is the honest behaviour, since guessing wrong merges two real titles and there is no undo for that.
- THE FFA ROSTER CAP IS PER PACK, BECAUSE SMASH'S 8 IS LOAD-BEARING (2026-08-04). Board games seat more than eight and Smash cannot: Ultimate seats 8, and Smashdown's battle cap is `floor(rosterSize / playerCount)` arithmetic against exactly that number. Raising the global to seat a board game would have quietly changed every Smashdown cap in the app, which is a wrong number on a setup screen rather than an error. So `validateFfa` gained a `maxPlayers` argument defaulting to 8, the size rule moved into a shared `validateFfaSize` that names what it is capping, Board Game passes 12, and every existing caller stays exactly where it was. The tests assert both ends: 12 accepted and 13 rejected here, 8 accepted and 9 rejected in Smash. A default-valued argument is the cheap version of this; the expensive version is remembering, next time, that a shared constant with one pack's number in it is not a shared constant.
- CARD TABLE AND BOARD GAME ARE TWO PACKS SHARING ONE IMPLEMENTATION (2026-08-04, James's call, recorded before either half is built). Recording the FINISHED GAME ONLY (rather than the running score, which was considered and rejected as the most input of any pack in the app) makes the two packs' mechanics identical. So this is an IDENTITY split rather than a mechanical one, made on the same grounds as the casino group's four packs: "good at card games" and "good at board games" are different claims, and one `games` row per pack is what keeps a leaderboard tab meaningful. THE RISK TO WATCH IS THE OBVIOUS ONE: two packs that are one pack in disguise will drift if they are written twice, so the second to ship must reuse the first's runtime and screens, the way roulette reused blackjack's. What should differ is the registry entry, the title list, the copy and the theme. Nothing else.
- A FIT CHECK MUST ASK "IS IT SEEN", NOT ONLY "DOES IT FIT" (2026-08-03). The rail is a fixed overlay, so it cannot move an element and every TV fit number was unchanged the day it shipped. That reading was correct and useless: a fixed frame paints ON TOP of whatever the layout put at the edge, so the honest question is not where the element was placed but whether anything still covers it. This is the same family as measuring the money board to the footer instead of to the back button, and as running the sweep's rule pass on a route where the stylesheet was not loaded: a real measurement of the wrong thing. `scripts/tv-fit.mjs` now reads the overlay's own width off the live document and reports coverage separately from fit, so the next overlay of any kind is caught by the check that already exists. WORTH RECORDING SEPARATELY: the specific overlap this was written to fix did not reproduce. Every fitting TV case clears the timber by 43px or more; the only covered control is Ping Pong's back button past six players, which is off screen before any rail exists.
- A CARD IS A DARKENING OF THE SURFACE, NOT A COLOUR ON TOP OF IT (2026-08-03, from the approved render). On felt, an opaque card reads as a hole cut in the table; a translucent one lets the weave and the lamp through and belongs to what it sits on. THE ALPHA IS JAMES'S CALL AND IT IS `.24`, the render's own number, taken after the measured alternative (`.5`, which held every pair at parity) was on the table. What .24 buys is a card that is visibly cloth rather than a dark panel; what it costs is three inks lifted to clear AA and eight accents dropping 2.5 to 4.5 points against Arcade while staying above the floor. The cost is recorded as a named exemption list rather than a widened tolerance, because a tolerance hides which pairs paid and an exemption list does not. Two more consequences are easy to get wrong and are both now enforced. THE ALPHA IS A CONTRAST DECISION: what a person reads text against is a COMPOSITE, so `theme-contrast.test.ts` resolves any `rgba()` surface over the theme's crown before measuring, and the render's own `.24` fails five pairs at this felt while `.5` holds every one. THE BASE IS THE CROWN, NOT THE FELT, because the cloth is not uniform: a card under the lamp sits on a lighter background than one at the foot of a long page, and lighter is the worse case for every ink in a dark theme. And the model has one edge it does not cover, stated rather than discovered later: a translucent control nested inside a translucent card composites DARKER, not lighter, so "raised" only reads correctly while nothing nests.
- A TRANSLUCENT TOKEN NEEDS AN OPAQUE COMPANION, AND IT IS ALSO THE FREEZE FOR UNCONVERTED PACKS (2026-08-03). Once `--gn-surf` can be an `rgba()`, three use sites break quietly: the far stop of a cabinet gradient (the tile fades out instead of settling), a full-bleed TV background wash (a background is not an object on the table) and a native `<select>` option list (which paints on the compositor's backdrop, not on the page). They read `--gn-surf-solid`, which under Arcade is the same colour and therefore computes identically. THE MORE USEFUL HALF WAS UNPLANNED: the casino group reads the shell's surface through `--cg-surface`, and pointing that at the solid companion froze all four packs at exactly what they shipped until their own Tabletop pass converts them deliberately. A pack that has not been converted should not be converted by a shell token quietly changing shape underneath it, and one token is a cheaper guarantee than a rule about who may edit what.
- SPEND THE HEADROOM WHERE IT IS CHEAPEST, NOT WHERE THE COMPLAINT IS (2026-08-03). Tabletop's lamp was too weak and the reason was written down as a hard ceiling: `--gn-dim` on the lit crown, under AA if the lamp went brighter. True, and the wrong place to stop. The ceiling belongs to the PAIR, and a pair has two sides: paling `--gn-dim` from `#c6b99f` to `#d4c9b1` bought a 70% stronger lamp at the same measured contrast, and the same move on `--gn-place` is what made a translucent card affordable at all. The limit is real; which side of it you pay on is a choice. It has a stopping point too, and it is a design one rather than a numeric one: `#ddd3bd` would buy another six levels of light and starts closing the gap to `--gn-ink`, and a theme needs its text hierarchy more than it needs a brighter table.
- A PARITY TOLERANCE HAS TO BE PROPORTIONAL ABOVE SOME HEIGHT (2026-08-03). The flat "within 2.0 of Arcade" rule fired the day the card became translucent, on `--gn-p2` (9.97 to 7.64) and `--gn-gold` (11.26 to 9.01), both still nearly double AA. Two points off a pair at 4.6 is the difference between readable and not; two points off one at 11.3 is invisible to anybody. A pair now has to breach BOTH the flat tolerance and a quarter of its own Arcade value, which can only ever be more permissive above 8, and the AA floor is what stops that being a licence.
- A SWEEP CANNOT CATCH A CORRECT IMPLEMENTATION OF A WRONG SPEC (2026-08-03, and it is the third variation on the same lesson in this repo). `theme-sweep.mjs` compares computed CSS values. Through the entire flat-felt build it was green and RIGHT to be green: the tile was loaded, the tint was set, the blend mode was `overlay`, every value matched what it had been told to be. The screen was flat because the specification said to tint the cloth with the page's darkest colour, and `overlay` against a base that dark multiplies a texture into nothing. THE OTHER TWO ARE THE SAME SHAPE: the casino money board measured against the footer, which is a real element that is not the last one; and the stage 4 sweep running its rule pass on a route where the pack stylesheet is not loaded, which is a real pass over an empty set. Each time the check was correct and was measuring the wrong thing. So the rule for anything with a LOOK: alongside the value check there has to be one measurement taken from rendered pixels. `scripts/felt-variance.mjs` is that measurement here, and its floor was set from a negative control rather than picked, because the first floor chosen by eye was one the known-broken build still cleared.
- A BOUND MUST APPLY TO THE SURFACE THAT ACTUALLY HAS THE PROPERTY (2026-08-03). The felt tile's amplitude was capped so that no measured contrast pair moved, and the list of pairs was every pair the theme test knows about. Most of them are never rendered on cloth: cards, inputs and buttons paint above the texture layer (`.gn-app::before` is z-index 0, `.gn-app > *` is 1), and a pack root is not inside the shell's texture host at all. So surfaces the tile never touches were setting how much weave the theme was allowed, and the answer they gave was "almost none". The corrected bound gates on the two surfaces the cloth genuinely covers, the felt and the felt under the lamp, and the lit one is the binding case because every text colour here is light and a brighter surface is a smaller gap. Same failure as measuring the board against the footer: a real constraint, applied to the wrong object.
- A GEOMETRY TOKEN, A COLOUR AT THE USE SITE, AND THAT IS NOW THE HOUSE PATTERN (2026-08-03). The lamp is generalised across the shell and the packs as `--gn-lamp-geom` and `--gn-lamp-fade`, never as a token holding the whole gradient, because a custom property containing `var()` resolves where it is DECLARED: a `--gn-lamp` holding `radial-gradient(..., var(--gn-felt-lit), ...)` declared at `:root` would bake in the shell's green and Ping Pong could never light its own table with it. That is the bug `--gn-bevel-btn` shipped with in stage 3 and the reason `--pp-bevel-btn` is geometry-only today. THE PAYOFF IS THAT THE SHARED THING IS THE SHAPE: Arcade's values for the two tokens are Ping Pong's exact old literals, so nothing moved under Arcade, and Tabletop re-aims every lamp in the app at once.
