Following the instruction literally would have made your lifetime stats quietly shrink the day you left a crew. The actual defect, the missing attendance, was fixed directly.
- Route splitting needed a runtime check, so one was built rather than skipped (2026-07-28, cleanup phase 4): the phase's own risk note is that a missing Suspense boundary throws at runtime and typecheck cannot see it, so "click every route" was the acceptance criterion and there was no way to click anything from a Claude Code session. Playwright is not in node_modules and adding it would have meant regenerating the lockfile that Render installs frozen, which is exactly the dependency trap the test harness already avoids. Chromium IS pre-installed and Node 22 ships a global WebSocket, so a throwaway script in the scratchpad drove it over the DevTools Protocol against vite preview: navigate, wait for the load event, poll until React puts something in #root, then read the console. Worth recording how it went wrong first, because the failures were all in the harness and none in the app: a hand-rolled static server never executed the module script, a fixed sleep reported a different random set of routes as blank on every run, and evaluating during navigation read a destroyed execution context. The tell each time was that an EAGER route like / appeared in the failures, which a lazy-loading bug cannot explain. Only once the harness waited for the load event did it settle to all 23 green. The script was not committed: it is a one-off for this phase, it depends on a browser path that is an environment detail rather than a repo fact, and a test that needs a running preview server does not belong in the tsx unit-test glob. If route smoke-testing is ever wanted permanently it deserves a real decision about a browser-test dependency, not a leftover script.
- Deferred the mutation-side state threading to the pack-runtime pass (2026-07-27, cleanup phase 1): phase 1 asked for sessionView to take an already-loaded session AND for that to be threaded through every mutation handler, saving a read per tap. The mechanism shipped and the guaranteed double-read is gone from the four authed GETs, where loadState ran once for the role check and again inside respondState. The mutation sites did NOT get threaded, on purpose: there are roughly seventeen per pack, each would need the status literal restated by hand at the call site, and the clean version is for saveState to return the view-ready object, which cannot happen yet because the four saveState signatures still disagree (Smash's takes groupId, the others do not). Phase 3 unifies exactly those signatures, so doing it now means writing sixty-eight careful edits that phase 3 immediately rewrites, with a real chance of a wrong status literal surviving typecheck. Better to take the free win now and get the rest for nothing when saveState becomes one function. On the dedupe: batching the participant inserts forced a decision about the same (matchId, userId) appearing twice in ONE statement, which is reachable when two guests share a name and both link to one member. Rather than rely on the conflict target to sort that out inside a single command, callers dedupe by userId first. That also fixed a quiet miscount, since the old loop incremented `recorded` for an insert that ON CONFLICT had discarded.
- Deleted the third source of truth (2026-07-27, cleanup phase 0): GAMEPLAN.md, README.md and BACKLOG.md had drifted into disagreeing about the same facts, and GAMEPLAN was the stalest of the three while README sent readers to it twice. Rather than reconcile three files forever, there is now one: BACKLOG.md, which MAP PROTOCOL already declared the source of truth. Before deleting, every claim in GAMEPLAN was checked against this file, README and RENDER.md; all of it was already covered except the single-instance rule, which existed only in GAMEPLAN and a render.yaml comment and is now a standing rule here. attached_assets/ went the same way for a sharper reason than clutter: it held stale duplicates of the very files sessions edit, and a search tool cannot tell a working file from a snapshot of it, so the directory was actively feeding wrong context into every session. Deleting the working copy loses nothing, since git history keeps it, and .gitignore now stops it coming back. On testing: the harness deliberately did NOT gain a framework. The bracket tests already ride the server package's tsx precisely because adding a devDependency without regenerating pnpm-lock.yaml fails Render's frozen-lockfile install, so `pnpm test` is the same runner pointed at a glob. Test files are not in any tsconfig include, matching what the bracket test already did; they are typechecked by tsx at run time, not by `pnpm -r typecheck`, which is worth knowing before trusting a green typecheck to prove a test compiles. The first server test is async-safe rather than a throwaway placeholder, because a placeholder that asserts nothing real cannot tell a working runner from a broken one.
- Depth added to the shared aggregate, not to the pages (2026-07-27): the profile, your own stats, the friend view and both sides of a rivalry are all finishAgg over the same Agg, so placement depth, time/history and character depth were added THERE and every one of those surfaces got them at once. Writing them per page would have been four implementations that drift, which is the same trap the crew leaderboard was in before it was unified. One split was necessary though: the crew leaderboard calls finishAgg once per player, so anything costing a query cannot live in it. finishAgg stayed sync and pure, and finishAggDeep (last-place count + best night, one query each) is used only by the four personal endpoints. LAST PLACE was the interesting one: field size is not stored, so "came last" is not directly knowable. It is derived from one grouped count of participant rows per match id, compared against the placement, which keeps it to a single query for a whole history instead of one per match, and a field of one is skipped because its winner would otherwise be its last place too. Placement distribution deliberately EXCLUDES results with no placement and reports that count separately, because packs that only record a winner would otherwise pile into fourth-or-worse and read as a player who never places. Empty months are kept in the games-per-month series for the same honesty reason: a gap is part of the shape and dropping it draws a lie. The h2h streak is signed rather than labeled so one number carries both whose run it is and how long, and ordering-dependent h2h items use dated meetings only. Everything new sits behind a closed-by-default "More stats" disclosure on both the profile and the rivalry tab, which is how the minimal-by-default rule survives adding this much: the visible tiles did not change at all. Opponent/nemesis aggregates and the meta-column pack extras (Mario Party bonus stars, series records) were explicitly held for a later session.
- One player row for every pack tab (2026-07-27): asked to unify how Mario Party, Mario Kart and Smash display on the crew leaderboard. The split was structural, not cosmetic: three packs had bespoke panels built before the generic per-game leaderboard carried enough to describe a player, so each drew its own list with its own headline number and ordering, and only the generic list expanded. Now that the shared aggregation returns characters, form and nights per player per game, those bespoke player lists had no reason to exist, so they were replaced by one renderer and each panel kept only its genuinely pack-specific sections. The rule applied: a PLAYER reads the same everywhere, a PACK keeps what is unique to it. Pack-only per-player numbers were preserved rather than dropped, via a subline extras map, because unifying layout is not a licence to delete stats: Mario Party stars, Ping Pong game wins, and Smash fighter variety plus its night-scoped hot streak, which is deliberately kept alongside the lifetime streak because a run inside one night and a lifetime run are different claims. Two visible consequences accepted on purpose: Ping Pong now leads with match wins (its ledger unit) instead of game wins, and each pack tab ranks by the shared wins/win-rate/played order instead of its own. The expand affordance is the app's existing ▾ flipping to ▴, added to every leaderboard row and to the Friends and Past game nights cabinets, which had only a text hint; GamePicker and the install hint already used it, so nothing new was invented.
- Crew leaderboard reuses the profile aggregation (2026-07-27): asked to put the new character and time stats on the crew Stats page, the choice was to copy the tallies into the crew endpoint's own loop or to delete that loop and share the profile's. Shared it. The crew endpoint had been maintaining a parallel copy of the same played/wins/placement logic, which is exactly how two screens start quoting different numbers for one player, and it would have needed a third and fourth copy as stats keep landing. One real consequence, worth stating rather than burying: the two aggregations had already drifted on avgPlacement (crew divided by games played, profile divided by results that carried a placement), so unifying them CHANGES the crew page's avg finish for any pack that records unranked results. The profile's definition won because averaging placements over games that had no placement is simply wrong. Rows became expandable on every tab, not just the overall one, because the per-game character breakdown is the most useful thing here and it only exists inside a game tab. Kept compact deliberately: pips plus two summary lines inside the existing expand, not a second full stats page nested in a list.
- matches.playedAt added, time-based stats on the profiles (2026-07-27): streaks and recent form were blocked on one missing fact, WHEN a result happened, logged as a limitation twice before (the rivalry decision of 2026-07-18 and the Home stats block sweep). One column fixes it: timestamp NOT NULL DEFAULT now() on matches. NO BACKFILL, deliberately: the app has not been in real use, so the rows that exist are test data and letting them take the migration timestamp costs nothing. That is the whole reason this could be one column and no migration script. Capture is automatic rather than hand-set, and that was CONFIRMED before relying on it, not assumed: every one of the eight matches insert sites writes status "completed" inline at the moment the game finishes, and nothing anywhere updates a matches row after creation, so defaultNow() is play time by construction. The guest-link backfill reuses the existing match row and only adds a participant, so linking a guest never restamps a night. If a pack ever starts creating a pending or live matches row to fill in later, that pack must set playedAt explicitly at its completion write, or its results will be stamped when the row was created instead of when the game was played. Two shape calls. (1) NIGHTS PLAYED counts distinct eventIds off the ledger, NOT the attendance rollup, which is what the session prompt suggested. Attendance measures whether someone confirmed showing up, so a crew that never taps the check-in prompt would read "0 nights" next to 40 games, which is worse than not showing it. Having a recorded result on a night is proof you played it, needs no extra query, and is what the label actually claims. (2) FIRST-PLACE FINISHES was not added: placement 1 is isWinner in every pack, FFA and head-to-head alike, so it would be the wins number under a second name. Streaks are computed in one ascending pass, since the run ending at the newest result is by definition the current streak; results with no timestamp are excluded from the ordering rather than guessed at, and the response carries `tracked` so the client can tell "no wins yet" from "nothing ordered yet". feedAgg moved to a single ResultRow object because it had grown to five like-typed positional args that the query rows already matched by name. Accurate from this deploy forward, which is all it needs to be.
- Per-character stats on profiles, podium stat removed (2026-07-27): the character data was already being written by every character pack and read by nothing, so this is a read-layer add with no schema change and no change to how games are recorded. Three calls worth keeping. (1) UNIFY BY NAME, not by title: match_participants.character already stores a name, so grouping by it directly means Fox in Melee and Fox in Ultimate are one line, which is the existing standing rule for character stats rather than a new choice. (2) A FLOOR ON "BEST": MIN_CHAR_GAMES = 3, named and commented, because win rate over one game is 100% and would beat a genuine main every time; below the floor a character still appears in the list, it just cannot be crowned. When nothing clears the floor the card says what the floor is instead of silently showing no best. (3) NESTED, NOT FLAT: the rollup returns a `characters` object holding byCharacter / mostPlayed / best, because the character summary's `best` (best character) would otherwise collide with the response's existing `best` (best placement). The rollup lives in the shared Agg helpers, so it is pack-agnostic: any future character pack that writes the character column inherits this with no stats work. Packs with no characters write null and are skipped, and the card hides itself, so Beerio / Ping Pong / bracket-only profiles are unchanged. Scope was held to the personal profile views (MemberPage, MyStatsPage) with no per-character section on the crew leaderboard and no per-title matrix; James asked for the crew leaderboard the same day, so that half is superseded (see the crew leaderboard entry). The no-per-title-matrix call stands. Podiums were removed the same pass for the reason logged when the item was raised: a podium is a top-3 finish, and in the many 1v1 formats (Best Of, KOTH, Ping Pong singles) the LOSER places 2nd, so both players "podium" every game and the number is noise. Wins, win rate and avg placement already say it honestly. Removed from the aggregator and every stat view rather than redefined with a 4+ field-size rule, which was the alternative and costs an extra pass over match field sizes for a stat nobody asked to keep. The recap card's visual 1-2-3 podium is a layout, not this stat, and stays.
- Historical-data caveats removed from the UI (2026-07-27): the app has not been put to real use yet, in guest linking or anywhere else, so there is no pre-existing history for a warning to be about. The Beerio "forward-only, older nights cannot be credited" copy on LinkGuestPage (the intro line, the empty-preview parenthetical, and the sub-line under the credit count) was written for a history that does not exist, and warning about it would only make an admin doubt data that is fine. Removed all three, plus the now-dead beerioForwardOnly flag on the preview response (nothing consumed it). This is a copy change only: the mechanism is untouched and still reads matches.rawResult, so a Beerio night without a stored snapshot contributes nothing, silently and correctly. Standing rule going forward: do not add UI copy explaining gaps in data that predates real usage. If the app is ever seeded with genuine pre-change nights, that is when a caveat earns its place.
- Beerio guest linking is FORWARD-ONLY, fixed at the source rather than recovered (2026-07-27): the honest options were (a) parse the vendored beerio_sessions.state to reconstruct who raced on past nights, or (b) stop discarding the names going forward. (a) was rejected: that blob is the vendored engine's own opaque shape, it is keyed by a reusable live-session code with no link to the finished match, and a stale or replayed code could attach one night's racers to another night's result. That is not recovery, it is fabricating credits, and the whole feature's value is that a preview can be trusted. So the names are persisted at completion instead, and history is written off explicitly: every Beerio night finished before this deploy discarded its guest names and is unrecoverable. The UI said so at first; that copy was removed same-day once it was confirmed the app has no real usage yet, so there is no such history for it to describe (see the entry below). Storage is ONE nullable jsonb column, matches.rawResult, holding the completion snapshot { placements: [{ name, place }] }. A Beerio-only sidecar table was the alternative; a single nullable column is simpler, additive, and a future fire-and-forget pack could reuse it. It is documented as Beerio-only today because every other pack already persists its full session jsonb (brackets, smash_sessions, game_sessions) and has nothing to gain from it. The member-matching loop was extracted into one shared creditBeerioPlacements helper rather than duplicated, so the live completion path and the backfill can never disagree about what makes a name a member; the live route passes no link map and is behaviorally unchanged. One deliberate asymmetry: the backfill passes an EMPTY member map, so only the linked guest is ever credited. Passing the real member map would have let a member who joined the crew AFTER that night get silently credited by a link that previewed nothing of the sort, which would break the preview-is-the-contract guarantee. Idempotency is unchanged from the rest of the feature (reopen the existing match, skip via memberCreditedKeys, insert ON CONFLICT (matchId,userId) DO NOTHING). Schema deploy: db:push:ci; the column is nullable with no default, so existing rows get NULL and are correctly invisible to guestNamesBeerio.
- Link-a-guest shipped as a one-time additive backfill (2026-07-26, supersedes the 2026-07-25 deferral below): no schema change, no persistent link table, no forward auto-credit (once the guest becomes a member they are picked as a member, so forward auto-credit buys nothing, and a permanent name binding could miscredit a different real person later typed with the same name). Owner/admin only, enforced server-side. Preview-first is the safety net because nothing here is runtime-tested in CI (Render only typechecks; no node in this environment): a dry run returns exactly what would be written and writes nothing, confirm then writes. Mechanism: an optional linkMap (guest display name -> member userId) added to each of the five recoverable materializers; when absent the live path is byte-identical to before. When a guest slot's name is in linkMap, the row materializes under that userId into the match that ALREADY exists (reused, never recreated, since materialization is per-game on record) via ON CONFLICT (matchId,userId) DO NOTHING, so it is additive, idempotent, and never disturbs existing member rows. Confirm reuses the materializers; the preview reads the stored lines (placement is read, never recomputed) and skips units the member already has (memberCreditedKeys queries the member's existing external_keys per event; brackets check the bracket's one match directly). Beerio is excluded (opaque vendored jsonb). Unlink/retract deferred to v2 (a clean retract needs a column marking backfilled rows to tell them from natural ones). Exact-name matching from an admin picker, no fuzzy matching (a name that does not exactly match a member's display name is precisely what makes something a guest).
- Link-a-guest deferred after investigation (2026-07-25, design-first task): mapped where guest names live and how each pack materializes (see the FEATURES scope note). Core finding: match_participants.userId is NOT NULL + FK, so guests have zero ledger rows; crediting a guest is re-materializing their history from each pack's stored jsonb, not re-pointing rows. Recoverable in generic brackets (Entrant guest shape, idempotent by bracketId) and in the four session packs (SmashPlayer / PpPlayer rosters in jsonb, keyed {pack}:{eventId}:{sessionKey}:{idx}); Beerio is opaque (names matched at completion) and stays out. Recommended safe mechanism is additive backfill (insert the member's participant per already-materialized match, ON CONFLICT (matchId,userId) DO NOTHING), never delete+remat. NOT built this session: it spans five heterogeneous materialize paths plus a live stats-ledger mutation and is untestable in the Claude Code environment (no node; the Render build only typechecks). A brackets-only slice would be clean but a misleading partial (an admin would expect all history credited, not just tournaments), so the item is left whole in FEATURES rather than shipped half. Next session that picks it up should build the bounded owner/admin rebind (guest name + member, show affected games, additive backfill, Beerio excluded).
- Every ledger row carries its format; recap redesigned to Style A (2026-07-21): added matches.format (additive column) stamped by every pack at materialization (free / bestof / koth / ffa / grandprix / board), because label alone couldn't tell formats apart (Ping Pong free play and KOTH both label bo1, which is why KOTH counted as Free Play). This unlocked: (1) per-format lifetime stats, a "Wins by format" breakdown on each pack's crew Stats tab, correct across every pack; (2) the night recap grouping matches into the thing actually played (one Best Of run, one KOTH run, one Grand Prix cup) via a sessions array. The recap card was rebuilt to the James-approved Style A "Podium Night": MVP hero, a 1-2-3 podium, then a line per session with its winner and how dominant (won X of Y races/sets/games). No "best of 1" bucket: free play IS best-of-1, so the buckets are the formats people pick. Schema deploy: db:push:ci applies the column; fallback ALTER TABLE matches ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS format text. Pre-existing rows have null format (bucket "Other").
- Smash and Mario Kart stay TWO separate packs, sharing primitives only (2026-07-21, James): the queued "unify into one config-driven session pack" idea is dropped for good. They keep separate routers, pages, tables (Smash on smash_sessions, Mario Kart on game_sessions) and TV designs, but reuse shared building blocks: the best-of series engine (series.ts), the KOTH rotation (kothAdvance), the FFA placement path, and the Smash session factory. Mario Kart got its own MkSessionState (the Smash session shape with MK's own format union plus Grand Prix bookkeeping) so the two type systems never entangle.
- Explicit per-pack format pickers (2026-07-21): each pack now exposes its formats as one Format choice at session start, replacing ad-hoc mode flags. Ping Pong: Free Play / Best Of / KOTH. Smash: Free For All / King of the Hill / Best Of. Mario Kart: Free Play / Grand Prix / Best Of / KOTH. The picker value is stored in session state (PpFormat/SmashFormat/MkFormat) and expands to the engine's mode + bestOf; the ledger keys off mode/bestOf as before, so stats and history are unchanged. One games row per pack, never one per format (format lives in state and, where it must survive, in matches.label). Both entry points (Home quick-play and the crew event launcher) always carry the same formats via ?format=.
- Best Of is match-as-unit across every pack (2026-07-21): a best-of set is one ledger match (label bo{N}, winner placement 1 / loser 2, per-player game wins in meta), materialized once when the set is won, and finalized to the game leader if the night ends mid-set (a dead tie stays unrecorded). Smash and Mario Kart use the shared series primitive; Ping Pong keeps its own equivalent (left untouched since it works).
- Grand Prix cups are DERIVED, not stored (2026-07-21): a cup is raceCount consecutive races, computed by chunking the games log (race i belongs to cup floor(i/raceCount)+1). Each race still materializes as its own ledger match (game-as-unit) tagged with its cup id on matches.label; there is deliberately no cup-level match row, so a "win" in the lifetime ledger keeps meaning one race. Cups advance automatically every raceCount races and cup standings (cumulative MK8 points) are read-derived, which makes undo trivial (nothing to unwind but the race itself) and avoids a stored cup pointer that could drift.
- Starting a format over a live session is confirm-and-replace (2026-07-21): the start route 409s when a session is already in progress (has recorded games or series); the client shows a confirm and, on yes, resends with force to replace it. Never a silent clobber (standing rule 8). External-key namespacing per session (the sessionKey work) means the replaced and replacement sessions never collide in the ledger.
- Ping Pong ledger key is namespaced per session (2026-07-21): materializeMatch keyed each match pp:{eventId}:{idx}, and idx restarts at 0 every time a session starts (new state, matches:[]). So the SECOND session on the same event (the "That session wrapped. Set up another one below." replay a crew does on a recurring event) produced keys that collided with the first session's, and the dedup SELECT dropped every new match as a duplicate: matches silently stopped recording to the recap and leaderboard after the first night, while the first night's numbers lingered. Fix: PpSessionState now carries a sessionKey (set in newPingPongState), and the key is pp:{eventId}:{sessionKey}:{idx}; legacy sessions with no sessionKey keep the old shape and never collide with new ones. Verified three back-to-back sessions on one event all record (recap totalGames 3), and undo still deletes the right row. Smash, Mario Kart and Mario Party materialized with the same {pack}:{eventId}:{idx} shape and had the same latent bug on event replay; they were given the same sessionKey treatment on 2026-07-21 (Smash and Mario Kart share newSmashState, so one change covers both; Mario Party via newMpState). Verified with two back-to-back Smash sessions on one event both recording. Timely because best-of formats are coming to Smash and Mario Kart. This does not touch existing rows: any duplicate/stale ledger data already in Neon from earlier testing is separate and needs a manual cleanup, which I can't do without the production DATABASE_URL.
- Ping Pong finalizes an in-progress match on session end (2026-07-21): a best-of match only materializes to the ledger on a decisive finish, so a match abandoned mid-set (the host ends the night at, say, 2-1 in a bo5) was losing every game played in it, and those bo3/5/7 results never reached the night recap or the leaderboard. Free play never hit this because every single game completes immediately. Fix: the complete route now calls finalizeCurrent(), which awards the current match to whoever leads on games and materializes it; an exact game tie (2-2 in a bo5) has no fair winner so it stays unrecorded. The host controls show a hint that ending records the current match for the leader. No schema or Neon change: same materialize path, and the schema already auto-syncs on deploy via db:push:ci. Also made the recap card render the match length in words (Best of 7) instead of the raw bo7 token.
- Ping Pong match win vs game win are distinct stats (2026-07-20): because the ledger unit is the match, a best-of-seven win and a free-play single-game win both count as one match win, which is not the same achievement. So individual game wins are tracked too: a player's game wins total every game they won inside their matches (up to four in a won bo7) plus every free-play game. Match wins are additionally split by match length (free play / bo3 / bo5 / bo7), which only differs across sessions since a session is one length. Implementation reuses what the ledger already stores: the format lives in matches.label, and per-player game wins/played are written to match_participants.meta at materialization (the meta jsonb column already exists for Mario Party, so no schema change). Surfaced as a Ping Pong lifetime panel on the crew Stats page, same pattern as Smash and Mario Party. Note: ping pong matches recorded before this change carry no meta, so they contribute 0 game wins; there is no real ping pong data yet, so no backfill is needed.
- Ping Pong ledger unit is the MATCH (2026-07-20): one completed best-of-N match materializes one matches row plus two match_participants rows (winner placement 1, loser 2). The individual games and any optional points live only in the session jsonb. Rationale: in KOTH the challenger line rotates per match, not per game, so match-as-unit keeps win rate, streaks, and king reigns meaning what people say out loud. This deliberately differs from Smash/Mario Kart, which record each game. Match length rides matches.label (bo3/bo5/bo7); each player's optional points ride match_participants.score (their total points in games they lost, the only points captured; null when none entered). No schema change: additive use of game_sessions + matches/match_participants, same path Mario Kart uses. (This was the ledger-unit decision flagged for James to confirm; the interactive confirm was declined with "continue", so I proceeded with the proposed default and am recording it here.)
- Ping Pong doubles is explicitly out (2026-07-20): 2v2 needs a team model the per-player match_participants ledger does not have. Nothing in the pack builds toward it, no half a team model was added "for later". Doubles ping pong stays parked behind the Beer pong pack's team-model work.
- Ping Pong optional points are entered then tapped (2026-07-20): the loser-points input sits with the two one-tap winner buttons and is submitted together with the winning tap, rather than editing a game after it is recorded. This keeps recording to one endpoint and one tap with zero friction to skip (leave the field empty), and avoids editing a completed match's frozen games. Points are optional flavor and are not used in any stat (no point differential, no ELO, per scope). Minor deviation from a literal "tap first, then optionally enter" flow, flagged here.
- Night recap MVP rule (2026-07-20, confirmed by James): MVP of the night is most wins, tiebreak by best (lowest) average placement (the avg stat shown on the card). A player with no ranked placement sorts last on the tiebreak; a remaining exact tie falls to alphabetical name only as a stable, non-ranking fallback. Implemented in rankMvp() in apps/server/src/events.ts. (The games-played tertiary tiebreak from the original proposal is not used.)
- Night recap is ledger-based, members only (2026-07-20): the recap aggregates the materialized matches/match_participants ledger, which is the single cross-pack source every pack writes into on completion. Guests are never materialized (they have no userId to credit, existing rule), so they do not appear on the recap. Showing guests would require reading each pack's live jsonb session (smash_sessions, game_sessions, beerio_sessions) with three different shapes; deferred rather than built this session. Flagged as scope, not silently dropped.
- Event share routes through the existing invite/join flow (2026-07-20): rather than a new public event route or a schema change, the share link is the crew invite link with an event id query param (/join/:code?event=:id). JoinPage reads ?event= and, after the idempotent join, redirects (replace navigation) to /e/:id; the event param is carried through login so a logged-out user returns to the join page and then the event. The invite code already identifies the crew, so no token schema change was needed.
- Login email sends from a sending SUBDOMAIN (2026-07-20): FROM is login@mail.gamenightos.app, on mail.gamenightos.app rather than the root gamenightos.app. A login flow generates bounces and spam complaints (mistyped addresses, "not me" reports); keeping that on a dedicated sending subdomain means the bounce reputation can never drag down the deliverability or trust of the root domain the app itself is served from. FROM and the optional reply-to are both env-overridable (MAIL_FROM, MAIL_REPLY_TO) so the addresses can change on Render without a deploy, and reply-to is omitted entirely when unset rather than pointed at an invented mailbox.
- 6-digit code login (2026-07-20): added a typed 6-digit verification code as the primary passwordless path, because an installed iOS PWA runs in a standalone browser context whose cookie jar is separate from Safari's. A magic link tapped in Mail opens Safari and sets the session cookie there, leaving the installed app still logged out; a code typed into the app keeps the cookie in the app's own context. One magic_link_tokens row now carries BOTH the code and the existing link token (additive columns code + attempts, index on (email, code); the token column and the tap-through link flow are untouched, so links in flight keep working). Decisions: code TTL 10 minutes (both code and link on a row share this expiry, down from the link's old 15); the email shows the code large and monospace above the fold with the link kept below under "Or log in on this device", both working per decision 2; 5 wrong attempts per row burns the code (row marked used); 60 second resend throttle enforced server-side and mirrored by the UI countdown. verify-code returns one generic "Invalid or expired code" for every failure mode (wrong / expired / capped / no such email) with no account probing; request-link returns { ok: true } regardless of account existence and silently no-ops within the throttle window, though a malformed-format email still gets a 400 so a typo is caught (that reveals nothing about accounts). find-or-create + session start was extracted into a shared loginVerifiedEmail() used by both POST /verify and POST /verify-code. Code generation uses crypto.randomInt (not Math.random), zero-padded so 004217 is valid. This is code only; DNS and Resend domain verification are handled separately by the owner. Navigation on code success reuses the existing password path's finish() (a same-origin window.location reload, which stays inside the installed PWA scope); the "use the router" guidance was noted but reusing finish keeps the two paths identical and avoids threading login state through App/Home, flagged in the session summary.
- Mutation latency pattern (2026-07-19): interactions were paying three trips (POST -> load() refetch -> WS-echo refetch) with buttons locked throughout. New pattern, applied to EventPage RSVP/attendance/date first: (1) optimistic local update on tap with snapshot rollback + alert on failure; (2) mutations return the FULL updated detail payload (shared eventDetail() builder serves the GET and every mutation), which the client applies directly — no load() after a write; (3) every request carries a per-tab X-GN-Client id, broadcast() stamps it as msg.origin, and the acting tab skips messages bearing its own origin while all other clients reload as before. A useRef request-sequence guard drops out-of-order mutation responses under rapid taps. Measured: 0 redundant refetches after a write (was 2) and ~40ms perceived repaint. Extended app-wide the same day: Smash, Mario Kart, and Mario Party mutations return the full session view via a shared sessionView() (same shape as the GETs, so they can never disagree) with saveState() threading origin; fighter/character picks are optimistic; record/undo/start apply the response with busy kept as double-tap protection. Brackets: score/undo/settings return the re-derived view and BracketPage's raw socket skips own-origin frames. groups.ts, events create/delete, and beerio-gn stamp origin so GroupPage (which already applies its writes locally) skips its own echoes. Beerio's vendored pack is deliberately untouched — it has its own polling sync. Verified live: 0 extra GETs on start/pick/record in Smash, ~20ms optimistic fighter pick, cross-client broadcast convergence intact everywhere.
- Flake definition (2026-07-19, James): a flake is RSVP yes + never confirming arrival, not just an explicit "didn't show" answer — nobody opens the app to confess a no-show. Silence after a yes counts once the event is 24h past its date (grace so the prompt can still be answered the night of); an honest "no" answer counts immediately; a yes on a dateless (TBD) event never counts, and shows always count the moment they're confirmed. Implemented entirely in the stats read path (attendanceFor), so the definition can be tuned without touching stored data.
- Friends live behind one button on Home (2026-07-19, James): the list will grow unbounded, so the section renders as a single "Friends (n)" cabinet button that expands to the list on tap.
- UI cleanup pass details (2026-07-18): rivalry color convention is YOU = teal, opponent = red/coral, applied on the rivalry page and the share card (the card previously had it the other way around). RSVP collapses to a one-line status + "Update RSVP" once answered; show-up confirmation will collapse the same way when built. The game picker skips the format expansion for any game with exactly one format (Mario Party today; also a Tournament tile once its bracket is live), so those are one tap. Delete event moved inside the event tile (stops navigation via preventDefault/stopPropagation). On the crew page, Members sit directly under the schedule form and the invite link collapsed to a single copy row; the raw URL only appears if the clipboard is blocked, preserving the long-press fallback. Event RSVP names now link to /g/:id/member/:userId like the crew list does.
- Map cadence is counter-driven, not memory-driven (2026-07-19): "every third session" is unenforceable across chats, since a fresh session cannot know the count. The MAP STATUS block at the top of this file holds the tally and the last redraw date; every shipping session increments it, and the session that tips it to 3 redraws the map before doing its feature work. The counter lives in the repo, so the rule survives losing a chat, the drawing, and the project settings all at once.
- Backlog structure mirrors the project map (2026-07-19): headings were reorganized so each one renders as exactly one zone on the Excalidraw map (SHIPPED — FOUNDATION, SHIPPED — GAME PACKS, NEXT UP, FEATURES TO ADD, BUGS, IDEAS). MAP PROTOCOL at the top pins the layout, colors, and cameras so any session redraws the same map from this file alone, with or without the previous drawing's checkpoint. Renaming or reordering these headings means updating MAP PROTOCOL in the same commit.
- Event date editing + arrival confirmation are ONE unit of work (2026-07-19): arrival confirmation is gated by the event date, so the date has to be authoritative and mutable before the gate can be trusted. Changing a date re-evaluates the lock. Building them separately means writing the lock twice.
- Magic link failure is unconfirmed as a Render regression (2026-07-19): Resend's free tier only ever delivered to the account owner's address, which is indistinguishable from "broken" when a friend tests it. Check the Render logs for the generated link before treating this as a delivery bug. The verification-code fix is worth building regardless, because it also resolves the installed-app cookie split that was already on the backlog.
- Standalone web app pass (2026-07-18): added manifest + icons + apple meta tags so Add to Home Screen launches without browser chrome; status bar style is black-translucent with safe-area insets padded on every root shell (gn-app, gn-tv, beerio-root, mk/mp/sm roots and TVs) so content stays clear of the notch and home indicator while backgrounds bleed edge to edge. Shells that carried Tailwind padding utilities moved that padding to inline calc(base + env(safe-area-inset-*)) because un-layered CSS and utility classes fight over the same property. No service worker / offline mode: decided against for now (app is online-only by nature). Magic-link-in-standalone gap logged in NEXT.
- Mario Party titles, bonus-star accuracy, exclusivity (2026-07-18, revises the entry below): three titles added (Super Mario Party 2018, Mario Party 6, Mario Party 2), so the picker is Jamboree (default), Superstars, Super Mario Party, MP6, MP2. The original bonus-star sets were WRONG and are corrected: modern games renamed Coin Star to Rich Star/Rich Bonus and Happening Star to Eventful Star/Eventful Bonus, and Superstars/Jamboree support nine bonus stars each (the "On" setting), not three (three is the "Classic" setting). Verified per game against the Mario Party Overlay bonus list plus the Super Mario Wiki. Counts: Jamboree 22 chars/7 boards/9 bonus, Superstars 10/5/9, Super Mario Party 20/4/11, MP6 11/6/3 (MP5's ten plus Toadette; Toad is NOT playable in 6), MP2 6/6/3. Each title shows its OWN award names while recording (what's on screen), but lifetime stats aggregate by FAMILY via MP_BONUS_FAMILIES/bonusFamilyOf, so Coin Star + Rich Star + Rich Bonus all roll up to "Coins" and the crew's "who always wins the minigame star" holds across every Mario Party they own. Same one-identity-many-spellings principle as characters. Bonus stars are now EXCLUSIVE per board: the recording UI was flipped from per-player chips to one row per bonus star with a single owner (structurally impossible to double-assign, and far fewer taps: 9 rows instead of 36 chips on Jamboree), and rankMpLines rejects a duplicate award server-side too. One player can still hold several different bonus stars. Toggling a player out of a board releases any award they held. Mario Party also added to the home Quick Play picker.
- Mario Party pack (2026-07-18): a recorded "game" is one BOARD, not a minigame or a turn. Input is deliberately minimal (standing rule 9): board, each player's final star count, and optional bonus-star toggles. Coins are NOT tracked, so a tie on stars can't be auto-resolved; the host taps the winner instead (rankMpLines rejects a tapped winner who isn't tied at the top). Capped at 4 players, the game's own limit. Two new nullable columns rather than a pack-specific table: matches.label holds the board and match_participants.meta holds { bonusStars: [...] }, both generic enough for future packs (darts legs, poker tables). Stars ride on the existing match_participants.score. Title selection (Session 1's pattern) scopes three lists here, not just the roster: characters, boards, and bonus stars, since those differ per title (Jamboree awards Event Star, Superstars awards Happening Star). Jamboree is the default (newest, widest). A "Custom board" option is always available so a board we didn't pin, or a house variant, still records. Stats live on their own /groups/:id/marioparty-stats endpoint, leaving the generic aggregator untouched (same call as the Smash pack).
- game>format navigation (2026-07-16): the "start a game" surface on both the home quick-play screen and the event page is now a two-step "pick a game, then a format" chooser (apps/web/src/GamePicker.tsx, shared by both). Games: Mario Kart (formats: Beerio Kart, General tracking), Smash Bros (FFA, King of the Hill), Tournament (Single elim, Double elim — the "format is the game" case, so no distinct game name). Labels changed "Game modes"->"Games" (home) and "Tournament"->"Games" (event). The format choice carries via query (?mode= into Smash, ?format= into Quick Play); session packs (Beerio/Smash/Mario Kart) are plain links that gate hosting on their own pages, so only Tournament needs host/RSVP gating in the picker (an owner/admin with 2+ yes RSVPs sees single/double; members see "waiting", an existing bracket shows "open").
- Title-scoped character selection (2026-07-17): "From" means the specific title being played, not a character's origin franchise. Playing Brawl gives you Brawl's roster with no Ultimate newcomers; playing Mario Kart World gives that game's cast. A "Which game?" dropdown sits on each character pack's front page and scopes both the picker and random assignment (host asked for random to stay within the game being played). Implemented in packages/shared as GameTitle[] (SMASH_TITLES, MARIO_KART_TITLES) + rosterForTitle(); titleId lives in the session state, so no schema change. Smash earlier titles are subsets of the Ultimate roster so spellings and stats stay identical (Everyone-is-Here makes that exact); counts checksum to canonical totals (64=12, Melee=26, Smash 4=58). Mario Kart World = the 24 main characters only, excluding the 26 enemy/NPC transform racers as low-value noise (same reasoning as Smash's per-Pokemon cut, standing rule 9). Stats are NOT split by title (James chose the simple leaderboard); character is character across titles. Default title is newest/widest (Smash Ultimate, Mario Kart 8 Deluxe). The random helpers already took a pool arg, so scoping was just passing rosterForTitle(...). Per-title rosters are the most likely spot to want a small correction later; each is a one-line data edit.
- Mario Kart general tracking pack (2026-07-16): Beerio Kart now lives under a "Mario Kart" game heading alongside a new "General tracking" format. General tracking is FFA races built on the SAME pure session engine as Smash FFA (packages/shared session logic: roster, per-game placements/winner-only, night summary) pointed at a Mario Kart 8 Deluxe roster (packages/shared/src/mariokart.ts), race wording, no KOTH. Storage is a new generic game_sessions table keyed by (eventId, pack) — additive, so smash_sessions is untouched and Smash keeps working; new packs share game_sessions. Server routes in apps/server/src/mariokart.ts (adapted from smash.ts, FFA-only), pages in apps/web/src/mariokart/, own race-day red/blue theme (standing rule 3), own TV mode, materializes into matches/match_participants with the racer as `character` so it feeds the generic cross-game lifetime leaderboard (a dedicated character-stats endpoint like Smash's was skipped for now). Smash and Mario Kart deliberately don't share server/page code yet (kept Smash byte-stable since it's shipped and untestable locally); unifying them into one config-driven session pack is a future refactor. Quick play: POST /api/quickplay/{smash,mariokart} spins up a personal event.
- Async error boundary (2026-07-16): the crash-loop risk (a rejected promise in a route handler killing the process + WebSocket hub) is fixed without touching every handler and without a new dependency. apps/server/src/async-safe.ts monkeypatches the Express Router prototype's route-registration methods (get/post/use/…) to auto-wrap handlers so throws/rejections go to next(err); it is imported FIRST in index.ts so the patch is in place before any router registers. A top-level error middleware returns 500; process-level unhandledRejection/uncaughtException handlers are a last-resort net so anything outside a request handler (app-level middleware, ws callbacks, timers) still doesn't take the process down. 4-arg error handlers and mounted sub-routers (they carry a .stack) are left unwrapped so their arity/behaviour is unchanged. Verified in isolation: async reject → next(err), sync throw → next(err), wrapped arity stays 3, error handlers stay arity 4, sub-routers untouched.
- Arcade coverage complete (2026-07-16): the generic app is now fully Arcade. JoinPage, the App loading fallback, and the magic-link tap-through pages (auth.ts confirmPage/expiredPage, server-rendered so they pull the fonts in directly) were themed; the tournament recap/share card (recap.tsx) was redrawn in the Arcade palette (deep plum bg + coral glow, gold "GAMENIGHT OS" Luckiest Guy wordmark, gold champion, plum surfaces, teal accent tick, coral upset) and made format-aware (single vs double elim). The canvas waits on document.fonts before drawing so the webfonts (Luckiest Guy/Fredoka/Nunito) bake in instead of system-ui, with a fallback if they're unavailable. Branded packs (Beerio, Smash) stay independent by standing decision; the QR quiet-zone white stays white for scannability.
- Generic bracket readability (2026-07-16): the generic tournament view (BracketPage) and its TV view (TvPage) were redrawn. BracketPage now uses the SAME slot-height + connector layout the Beerio pack uses: each match is absolutely centered at (i+0.5)*slotH within its round column, slotH doubles per winners round (steps every second column in the losers bracket), so a later match lines up vertically between the two that feed it, with connector arms + vertical joiners tracing the path. Winners/Losers/Grand Final are labeled sections, each horizontally scrollable. Scoring is tap-a-name-to-win, tap-the-winner-again-to-undo (undo still confirms + cascades). The TV view stopped drawing the whole tree (unreadable across a room) and now mirrors Beerio's TV approach: big "On deck" (live matchups) + "Latest results" columns in vmin-scaled type, with the champion banner taking over when it's done. New CSS: gn-bkt-* and gn-tvm-* in index.css, reading only --gn-* tokens. BracketPage/TvPage are shared by BOTH the event flow and Quick Play (both route to /b/:id and /tv/:id), so there is no separate quick-play bracket — any bracket change lands in both by construction. Geometry was verified headlessly (card centers land exactly on feeder midpoints; columns tile without overlap).
- "Format" terminology (2026-07-16): the things you pick inside a pack (FFA, King of the Hill; Beerio's Bracket vs Grand Prix; single vs double elim) are called FORMATS in user-facing copy, not "game modes" ("modules" was considered and rejected as too software-y). Code-level type names (SmashMode etc.) were left alone; this is a copy decision. The Smash "End night" button became "End format" because completing a session returns the host to the format picker to start another run the same night.
- Double elim engine (2026-07-16): shipped in the shared engine as Session B. The declarative-graph pattern extended exactly as the seed app does it: a {t:"lose",m} slot source feeds losers-bracket matches, GF/GF2 are built up front with GF2 gated on GF going to slot B (the reset), and undo stays "delete a key + cascade" — downstreamOf() follows win AND lose edges plus an explicit GF→GF2 edge. Ids are stable per format (single elim keeps R{r}M{i}, so old brackets read fine; double uses W/L/GF ids). Placements moved into the engine (placements()): in double elim only losers-bracket and deciding-grand-final losses eliminate. No schema push needed: drizzle text enums are TypeScript-level, existing rows default single_elim. Format dropdowns: event bracket + Quick Play. Tests: packages/shared/tests/bracket.test.ts via `pnpm test:bracket` (rides the server package's tsx on purpose — adding a devDep without regenerating pnpm-lock.yaml would fail Render's frozen-lockfile install).
- Smash session UX polish (2026-07-16): FFA "Record a game" starts with every roster player checked (plus a check all / clear all toggle) since full-roster games are the norm; the KOTH TV view shows the next challenger and the rest of the line ("Up next" / "Then"), not just the current king.
- Smash leaderboard (2026-07-16): the crew stats page (/g/:id/stats) gains a Smash-specific panel on the Smash Bros tab, replacing the generic per-game list there. It surfaces everything the pack tracks: per-player wins/win-rate/main/variety/best-streak, per-fighter wins/win-rate, and member head-to-head. Head-to-head is derived from shared games (better finish wins the meeting; equal finish, e.g. two non-winners in a winner-only FFA, is a no-edge meeting). Best streak is the longest run of consecutive wins within a single night, ordered by game position, which reads as the KOTH king streak and as an FFA hot streak. All of it comes from the materialized ledger via /groups/:id/smash-stats; the generic aggregator stays untouched.
- Smash deploy schema miss (2026-07-16): the Session A deploy shipped code but the DB was missing match_participants.character and smash_sessions, so recording a game crash-looped the server on "column does not exist". Root cause: pnpm db:push:ci (drizzle-kit push --force) exited 0 without applying the additive change. push is introspection-diff based and its create/rename resolution expects a TTY; --force only bypasses the destructive-change confirmation, so in Render's non-interactive build it can no-op and still exit clean. Fix applied: idempotent ALTER/CREATE ... IF NOT EXISTS in the Neon console (no redeploy needed). Takeaway: for schema-changing deploys, confirm the build log shows "[✓] Changes applied", and if not, run the idempotent SQL directly. Additive CREATE/ALTER ... IF NOT EXISTS is always safe to run by hand.
- Smash scope split (2026-07-16): the pack ships as two sessions. Session A = FFA Night + King of the Hill + character system + TV + stats, with Tournament reusing the existing single-elim engine. Session B = double-elimination in the shared engine, surfaced as a format dropdown in both the generic Tournament tracker and the Smash Tournament option. Double-elim was split out because it touches non-Smash code and its undo (re-routing losers between brackets + grand-final reset) needs isolated testing.
- Smash storage model (2026-07-16): FFA and KOTH are session-based, not brackets. A night is a running log of individual games held in one server-side row per event (smash_sessions, jsonb state), so members join the host's session rather than a local copy (standing rule 2). Each completed game materializes into matches/match_participants immediately, keyed smash:{eventId}:{idx} for idempotent undo/redo. The jsonb is the live working state; the matches tables are the durable ledger.
- Smash character tracking (2026-07-16): added one nullable column, match_participants.character, so the fighter played survives into the lifetime ledger (otherwise "wins with <fighter>" is impossible). It is a generic per-participant label; brackets and Beerio leave it null. This is the only schema change in Session A besides the new smash_sessions table; both are additive and apply via the existing pnpm db:push:ci in the Render build.
- Smash roster (2026-07-16): one flat list of every fighter across all games (the complete Ultimate roster, static since Sora in Oct 2021), no per-game filtering. Echo fighters are separate entries and the three Mii Fighters are separate; Pokemon Trainer and Pyra/Mythra are single picks (tracking which Pokemon or twin is the kind of per-life granularity standing rule 9 says isn't worth the input). Pinned against a current source at build time, not from memory.
- Smash result detail (2026-07-16): winner-only is the default (one tap), with an opt-in full-placement toggle for FFA (1..N). Low barrier for entry, meticulous mode available. KOTH and Tournament are inherently winner/loser so the toggle only affects FFA.
- Smash character assignment (2026-07-16): host picks one mode per session: players self-select on their own devices (default), host taps once for random, or host-assigns everyone. Self-select writes are scoped so a member can only set their own slot; the host can set any.
- Smash stats endpoint (2026-07-16): character stats (wins by fighter, each member's main, variety) come from a dedicated /groups/:id/smash-stats endpoint reading the pack's ledger rows, leaving the generic /groups/:id/stats aggregator untouched. Keeps the character focus out of the cross-game leaderboard and lowers the risk of destabilizing a working endpoint (standing rule 9).
- UI direction (2026-07-15): Arcade chosen as the app's visual identity (deep plum, coral + teal "player" accents, chunky pressable buttons, Luckiest Guy wordmark). Tabletop was the runner-up and is saved as a future opt-in theme, not built now.
- Theming architecture (2026-07-15): the app moved off hardcoded Tailwind color utilities onto CSS custom-property tokens plus a small gn-* component-class layer in index.css. Reason: Tailwind color utilities compile to fixed values and cannot be re-themed at runtime; CSS variables can. A second theme (Tabletop) becomes another token block plus a switcher, with no component rewrites.
- Theme scope (2026-07-15): generic app screens get the Arcade theme; branded packs (Beerio Kart) keep their own styling by design. The generic tournament TV mode gets an Arcade big-screen variant; Beerio's TV mode is untouched.
- **Theming ships in THREE stages, not one (2026-08-02).** Stage 1: make Arcade swappable, adding no colours at all. Stage 2: the Tabletop palette. Stage 3: the packs. The reason is that the risk in a theming pass is not the new theme, it is silently wrecking the theme that is already shipped and in use, and those two things are only separable if one of the stages is a provable no-op. Stage 1 is that stage, and it is why `scripts/theme-sweep.mjs` compares COMPUTED colours rather than screenshots: a screenshot diff on this app is noisy enough (webfont swap, `.gn-pulse`, the skeleton shimmer) that it produces small differences on every run, and a check that cries wolf every run trains its reader to approve the one that matters. A computed colour either matches or it does not.
- **THE PACK CONVERSION MECHANISM, settled by the Ping Pong pilot (2026-08-02). The remaining eight packs follow this and should not re-derive it.** Four rules:
  1. **A pack's values become `--pp-*`-style tokens on that pack's OWN roots**, named for their job (`--pp-felt`, `--pp-edge`, `--pp-line`, `--pp-accent`), never for their colour. Identity lives with the pack, so its re-treatment lives with the pack, and `index.css` does not grow by a line per pack. The casino group's `--cg-*` block is the precedent.
  2. **Anything that is actually a SHELL value reads the shell token instead of getting a pack one.** Ping Pong had four hiding as literals: `#35e0c4` and `#1aa88f` are exactly `--gn-p2` and `--gn-p2-sh` (its Go button is the shell's confirm action wearing pack paint), `#fff` is `--gn-brand-ink`, and an rgba gold was `--gn-gold` at 16%. Check every literal against the shell token set before naming a pack token for it.
  3. **Structure comes from the stage 3 contract WHERE THE VALUE ALREADY MATCHES.** Ping Pong's pills, paddle-box radius and TV panel radii were already exactly `--gn-radius-pill`, `--gn-radius-tile` and `--gn-radius-card-tv`, so they read those. Where a pack's number DIFFERS it keeps a pack token rather than being rounded to fit, because rounding it moves Arcade and no stage-4 commit may do that.
  4. **The theme block lives in the pack's stylesheet**, as `:root[data-theme="tabletop"] .pp-root, :root[data-theme="tabletop"] .pp-tv { ... }`.
- **A PACK'S ACCENT IS IDENTITY AND IS NOT REDECLARED UNDER A THEME (2026-08-02).** Ping Pong's `--pp-accent` (#ff7a1a) is the same orange under Tabletop, where it reads as the rubber on the paddle. This is the same call the shell's cabinet inks make and it is what standing rule 3 is protecting: the packs have to stay tellable apart across a room, and a theme that repaints every pack's accent in its own palette is the uniform token swap stage 3's model was written to prevent. `theme-contrast.test.ts` asserts the accent tokens are absent from the pack's Tabletop block, so "tidying" them in fails the build.
- **A pack's TOKEN BLOCK is recognised by shape, not by selector name (2026-08-02).** The no-literals lint now treats any rule whose declarations are ALL custom properties as a token block. That definition is the honest one (such a rule cannot paint anything itself, so every literal in it is a token value) and it is the one that survives eight more packs without the test being edited eight more times.
- **A THEME IS ITS MATERIAL, NOT ONLY ITS PALETTE (2026-08-02).** Stage 2 shipped Tabletop as sixteen swapped hex values and it read as Arcade with a different scheme, which is exactly what it was. What makes a screen feel like an arcade cabinet is structure: a scanline raster, blurred light behind the letters, a hard zero-blur bevel that is moulded plastic, lozenge geometry and a cartoon display face. None of those is a colour and none of them moved. Any future theme brief that says "this is a palette" is scoped wrong for the same reason.
- **THE STRUCTURE TOKENS, as shipped in stage 3. Stage 4 sets these per pack, so this is the contract.** All twenty-one live in `index.css`, Arcade on `:root` and Tabletop on `:root[data-theme="tabletop"]`, and every one is named for its JOB rather than its value, so `--gn-glow-title: none` reads as a sensible instruction:
  - TEXTURE, the ambient overlay: `--gn-texture` (the whole background shorthand, so a theme swaps the GEOMETRY and not just its colour), `--gn-texture-tv` (coarser, because a 3px diagonal seen from a sofa is either invisible or aliasing), `--gn-texture-opacity`.
  - GLOW: `--gn-glow-title`, `--gn-glow-h2`, `--gn-glow-brand`, `--gn-glow-brand-tv`. All four are `none` or a hard drop under Tabletop, because print on card does not emit light.
  - BEVEL: `--gn-bevel`, `--gn-bevel-press`, `--gn-bevel-sm`, `--gn-bevel-sm-press`, `--gn-bevel-xs`, `--gn-bevel-inset`, `--gn-bevel-btn`, `--gn-bevel-btn-press`. Eight rather than the two the brief sketched, because the shipped depths genuinely differ (6px, 3px, 2px, an inset lip) and collapsing them would have moved Arcade, which that pass could not do.
  - GEOMETRY: `--gn-radius-pill`, `--gn-radius-card`, `--gn-radius-card-tv`, `--gn-radius-tile`.
  - TYPE: `--gn-font-display`, `--gn-brand-track`.
- **`--gn-bevel-btn` CARRIES GEOMETRY ONLY, and the use site supplies the colour (2026-08-02).** The obvious design was one token reading a `--gn-btn-sh` that each accent button set locally. It is invalid: a custom property whose value contains a `var()` resolves where it is DECLARED, so a token on `:root` cannot see a local set on a descendant, and it silently resolved to the fallback and painted every accent button's lip transparent. Write `box-shadow: var(--gn-bevel-btn) var(--gn-p1-sh)` instead. This is a general trap for stage 4, which will want exactly this shape for per-pack accents.
- **`--gn-radius-pill` is theme-invariant (2026-08-02).** Deliberately not redeclared under Tabletop, and asserted as such in `theme-contrast.test.ts` so it reads as a decision rather than an omission. The things that read it are toggles, chips and the live tag, and those genuinely ARE pills under any theme; a squared chip is just a small rectangle.
- **Felt is CROSSED GRADIENTS, never feTurbulence and never an image (2026-08-02).** A turbulence filter on a fixed full-screen overlay is a real GPU cost on a television left open all night, which is precisely the screen a felt texture most needs to work on, and a tiling image is another request plus bytes on a bundle that has been trimmed on purpose. Two fine diagonals at opposite angles give a woven tooth for nothing and composite once. The opacity has to come DOWN when crossing two layers: they ink roughly 55% of the pixels where a single repeat inks 33%, so holding the number would have made the weave nearly twice as heavy as the raster it replaced.
- **A second display face is affordable only while it is named in ONE place (2026-08-02).** A browser fetches a face the first time rendered text uses it, so Bevan sitting inside the `--gn-font-display` token in the Tabletop block costs an Arcade session nothing. Do not preload it, and do not reference it from any selector outside the theme block. Measured over CDP: each theme fetches exactly three faces and swaps Luckiest Guy for Bevan, so neither theme pays for the other. Stage 4 should hold the same rule for any pack face.
- **Tabletop is WARM DARK, not light (2026-08-02, James).** Felt, brass, low pub light. The dark half is the load-bearing part and is worth restating whenever stage 3 is planned: a LIGHT Tabletop would have forced a second treatment of all ten pack palettes at once and turned stage 3 from a design pass into a rebuild. Keeping the shell dark means every pack's identity hue keeps working as it does today, and it suits the casino group for free (felt green up, lacquer red down). The shift is temperature, not depth: Arcade's cool deep plum to warm walnut and low brass.
- **Brass and felt green are LIFTED from their physically accurate values, on purpose (2026-08-02).** A literal brass (#d9a94a) reads at 7.98 against --gn-surf where Arcade's gold reads 11.26, and a literal felt (#4aa86c) at 5.84 where the teal reads 9.97. Both are more accurate to the material and materially harder to read across a room, which is where half this app is looked at. The shipped #eec262 and #61cd8b land at 10.29 and 8.71. If a later pass wants them more muted that is a legitimate call, but it is a LEGIBILITY TRADE and the number belongs on the table when it is made, which is why the contrast test asserts parity with Arcade rather than only the WCAG floor.
- **TEN TABLETOP OBJECTS, not one theme with ten hues (2026-08-02, James, sharpening the stage 3 model).** Under Tabletop, Ping Pong is a green paddle table, Mario Party is a board game box, Smash is a lacquered arena, the casino group is felt and brass. This supersedes nothing in the identity-versus-treatment split below (identity is still the pack's, treatment still the theme's) but it does settle what stage 3 IS: per-pack design work, scoped one pack at a time with a pilot first, not a mechanical token swap. A uniform swap would flatten ten packs into one look and break standing rule 3, which is exactly the trap the split exists to avoid.
- **A theme's failure mode is a MISSING token, not a wrong one (2026-08-02).** A wrong colour is visible; a token the second theme forgot to override silently inherits the first theme's value and reads as a slightly odd shade rather than as a bug. That is why the contrast test asserts the token NAMES as well as the ratios, and why the only tokens allowed to be inherited are the ones a decision says should be. The same shape one level up: index.html carries two colours that are read before the stylesheet exists (the theme-color meta and the <html> background) and therefore cannot be tokens, so a theme can ship complete in CSS and still flash the wrong colour for a frame. There is an assertion for that too.
- **The pre-paint background lives on <html>, not <body> (2026-08-02).** The theme script runs in <head>, where <body> does not exist yet, so <body> is the one element it cannot correct. With the colour on <body>, Tabletop painted Arcade's plum over its own walnut for the width of the body box. The canvas takes its background from <html> anyway, so moving it there costs nothing and lets the script fix it before the first frame.
- **IDENTITY BELONGS TO THE PACK, TREATMENT BELONGS TO THE THEME (2026-08-02). Stage 3 does not get to relitigate this; it is the model stage 1's token names were chosen to anticipate.** Stage 3 is NOT "replace pack colours with shell tokens". That would flatten ten packs into one look and break standing rule 3, which puts every pack's TV in that pack's OWN design language, and it would destroy the casino group's entire point, which is that a blackjack money board and a roulette one read as different packs from across a room. Instead: a pack declares its IDENTITY once, as a hue, on its own root. Smash is red, Mario Kart blue, Mario Party purple, Ping Pong green, blackjack gold, roulette teal, and that does not change with the theme. The THEME owns the treatment: how dark the backdrop sits under that hue, how heavy the borders are, how deep the button bevel is, whether ink is light on dark or dark on light. The casino group's `--cg-*` block is already exactly this pattern and is the working precedent to generalise from. That split is the only thing that lets a light Tabletop exist at all: under it Smash is still red, but red on warm paper instead of red on black. Pilot it on ONE pack before rolling it across all of them.
- **Name a token for what it is FOR, never for what colour it is (2026-08-02).** `--gn-shadow-depth`, not `--gn-dark-purple`. A token named after its colour cannot survive a light theme, because under that theme it is neither the colour its name claims nor obviously wrong to a reader. This is why stage 1's 35 new tokens read `--gn-faint`, `--gn-p1-tint-ink`, `--gn-act`, `--gn-cab-smash-edge`.
- **The theme preference does NOT go through `cache.ts` (2026-08-02, James).** Per device, its own localStorage key, its own namespace (`gamenight.pref.theme`, deliberately not a near-miss of `cache.ts`'s `gn:` prefix). Every key in that module is namespaced by `__BUILD_ID__` and swept on boot, which is right for cached API payloads and wrong for a preference: a theme that silently resets to Arcade every time the app ships is worse than no switcher, because the person who set it cannot tell why it keeps changing back. Related: an unrecognised stored theme is ignored but NOT erased, so a rollback to a build that predates Tabletop renders Arcade without wiping the choice off every phone that had chosen it.
- **`color-mix()` is now a hard requirement of the shell, and its auto-generated fallback is wrong (2026-08-02).** Stage 1 replaced 50 rgba() literals in `index.css` with `color-mix(in srgb, var(--gn-x) N%, transparent)`. Tailwind v4 runs Lightning CSS over that file and splits each one into an opaque `var()` fallback plus the real value inside `@supports (color:color-mix(in lab,red,red))`. 39 of the 50 fallbacks therefore paint the token at FULL opacity on a browser without `color-mix`: chips become solid coral blocks, bevel shadows become solid black slabs. That is worse than no fallback at all, which is what the pack stylesheets already get (they are not processed by Lightning, and `casino.css` has used `color-mix` unguarded since it shipped). The affected browsers are Safari < 16.2, Chrome < 111 and Firefox < 113, all pre-2023, and the casino packs already do not render correctly on them. NOT CHANGED IN STAGE 1, because suppressing the fallback means setting a `browserslist` for the whole build, which would change Lightning's output well beyond colour, inside the one session whose entire claim is that nothing moved. James's call: either accept the floor, or set the browserslist in stage 2 and re-run the sweep.
- BACKLOG.md recovery (2026-07-15): the "Prepare for render" commit (94cc12b) accidentally overwrote BACKLOG.md with an old copy of the Beerio source. Restored from the last good copy (bef93b5) and brought current. If it ever happens again: `git checkout <good-commit> -- BACKLOG.md`.
- Hosting (resolved): deploys run on Render free tier with Postgres on NEON (Render's free DB is deleted after 30 days; Neon's is not). The Render build command MUST end with `pnpm db:push:ci` or deploys ship code without schema and crash-loop on missing columns. See RENDER.md.
- Beerio TV mode: reads the SAME public live-session endpoint the spectator view uses and renders with the SAME exported engine functions the host runs, so the big screen can never disagree with the phones. Polls every 3s (WebSocket hub is not wired into the beerio pack's own sync).
- Generic brackets materialize into matches/match_participants on completion. Undoing a result past completion RETRACTS the recorded rows, so the ledger always matches reality.
- Placement rule for generic brackets: champion 1st, then everyone ranked by how late they were eliminated. Guests are skipped (no identity to credit).
- Passwords added pre-v1 (revised from "magic links only"): Resend free-tier delivery limits made email-only login painful for testing and for friends pre-domain-verification. Signup does NOT verify email ownership; magic links remain the fallback and the only verified path. Revisit verification before any public launch.
- Bracket scoring defaults open to all members; group owners/admins can lock it per bracket.
- TV/spectator view is public read-only, keyed by the bracket UUID (unguessable link, like invite codes). No login on TVs. Revisit if brackets ever carry private data.
- Quick play (Option B, chosen over a parallel statless system): running a mode without a crew silently creates a PERSONAL crew (groups.is_personal, hidden from the crew list). One system: scoring, TV view, recap all work unchanged. Entrants can be typed guests; guests carry no lifetime stats until linked to a member.
- Bracket entrants are Entrant[] ({kind:"member",userId} | {kind:"guest",name}). Legacy rows holding bare userId strings are upgraded on read by parseEntrants(), so no data migration was needed.
- Leaving a crew: any member can leave; an owner cannot leave while others remain (no ownership transfer exists yet).
- Beerio divergence from the original (bug fix): champion celebration and Hall of Fame keys are seed-based, not name-based. Name-based keys re-fired the champion popup and wrote a HoF entry per keystroke when editing names on a completed bracket.
- Beerio prefill: launching from an event prefills setup with the yes-RSVP list ONLY when nothing is recorded yet; setup-screen names from an older saved night get replaced, an in-progress tournament never does. Fires once per event per device via a localStorage flag.
- Beerio stats binding: names match crew members case-insensitively at completion time; unmatched names are guests and unrecorded. A member renamed mid-night won't match; acceptable v1.
- Firefox back-button (bug fix): the auto event->s=CODE member bounce used push navigation, leaving a ghost history entry; switched to replace navigation, plus a "Connecting to the night" gate so members never see the host's setup screen mid-fetch.
- Recap card sharing (resolved, shipped): native share sheet first, download fallback.
- Mario Party tie placements (2026-07-18, bug fix): non-winners tied on stars now SHARE a placement (competition ranking: 1, 2, 2, 4) instead of getting an arbitrary order. The real in-game tiebreak is coins, which we deliberately don't track, so we don't invent one. The winner always holds 1st.
- Rivalry head-to-head definition (2026-07-18): an encounter is any completed match where both players have participant rows, across every pack; better (lower) placement wins it, equal placement ties. No "current streak" stat at the time: matches carried no timestamp, so encounter order could not be reconstructed. SUPERSEDED 2026-07-27 by matches.playedAt, which made streaks and recent form real; the rivalry compare itself still shows no streak, but both profiles do.
- Home "Your stats" aggregates across ALL crews (quick-play personal crew included, labeled "Quick play"); member profiles and rivalries stay crew-scoped because the ledger is group-scoped.

## OPEN DECISIONS
- **IN SMASHDOWN, DOES A PAIR BURN TWO FIGHTERS OR ONE SHARED FIGHTER?** (raised 2026-08-17 by the team-entrants session, for James.) This has to be answered before SMASH 2v2 TEAM BATTLES can be scoped, and it is not a detail: Smashdown's whole rule is that a used fighter is gone, so the cap the setup screen prints as a sentence is `floor(rosterSize / playerCount)` games. If a pair burns TWO fighters the cap halves and a 2v2 Smashdown night is half as long as the same crew's free-for-all; if they share ONE, the arithmetic is unchanged and the pair picks together. Both are defensible as a house rule and they produce different screens, so the session that builds it should ask rather than pick. The generic bracket's team entrants needed none of this, because a bracket slot burns nothing.
- Whether to reskin Beerio Kart to match Arcade later, or keep packs visually independent forever. Currently: packs stay independent.
